# NBA Hub

A full-stack NBA analytics platform built as a final-year project: real historical data (1950 through the current season for career/season stats; 2009-10 through the current season for the deepest advanced-stat coverage), trained and backtested prediction models, era-normalized player comparison engines, a live betting-market cross-reference, real play-by-play win-probability modeling, and a suite of stat-driven daily games — all built on top of one PostgreSQL database (plus an optional read-only cloud mirror — Layerbase, full parity including shot charts/play-by-play — for remote/cloud sessions) and served through three FastAPI microservices to a React dashboard.

The project's guiding rule, followed throughout: **never fabricate data**. Where a real number exists, it's used and its source is traceable to a real endpoint or a real database column. Where the project doesn't have real data for something (player height/wingspan, a trade-history dataset, a real referee-bias claim), that gap is disclosed in the UI rather than invented — several features explicitly say what they *aren't*, and small real samples get an explicit warning instead of being presented as comprehensive.

![Dashboard](docs/screenshots/dashboard.png)

---

## For an AI assistant picking this project up

Read this whole section before touching anything. It exists so you don't need the owner to re-explain context that's already been established over many previous sessions.

**Environment gotchas — get these wrong and everything else breaks:**
- Plain `python3` on this machine resolves to an Anaconda install missing this project's dependencies. Always use `/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3` explicitly, for every script run and every `pip install`.
- Each of the three API files has its own `if __name__ == "__main__"` uvicorn fallback port, but **always pass `--port` explicitly anyway** — don't rely on the fallback, and don't assume the fallback is correct (it has been wrong before and gets fixed reactively; the ports the frontend actually calls are the ones in `frontend/src/services/api.js` — currently 8000/mvp, 8001/similarity, 8002/impact).
- DB credentials live in `api/.env` (gitignored) as `DB_HOST`/`DB_PORT`/`DB_USER`/`DB_PASSWORD`/`DB_NAME`, read through `api/db_config.py` and `scripts/db_config.py` (two separate small modules, each resolving `api/.env` by an absolute path so they work regardless of current working directory). Every script and service does `from db_config import DB_CONFIG`. **Never hardcode the password back into a file** — it used to be hardcoded everywhere and was deliberately pulled out; the old password is still in git history from before that cleanup, so treat it as not-really-secret and don't reuse it for anything real.
- Postgres DB name: `nba_analytics`.
- `DB_TARGET` in `api/.env` picks local (default, always use for dev), `layerbase` (a full-parity Postgres mirror — every table including `player_shots`/`pbp_events`, see the Layerbase entry near the bottom of the Roadmap section), or `cloud` (the older Supabase read mirror, missing `player_shots`/`pbp_events` due to its 500MB free-tier cap — kept for now, but Layerbase is the one with full parity). If you ever need to refresh a cloud copy after local data changes meaningfully: `cd scripts && python3 migrate_to_layerbase.py` (full drop-and-reload per table, chunked streaming for the two big tables, recreates non-PK indexes too) or `python3 migrate_to_supabase.py` for the Supabase copy (no `pg_dump`/`psql` needed or available on this machine, either way).
- Scripts are normally run as `cd scripts && python3 whatever.py` (their docstrings say so), but don't assume — check the top of the script; some things (like the test suite) are written to work from any cwd.
- `nba_api` hits the real stats.nba.com over the network. It's rate-limit-sensitive: existing fetch scripts use `time.sleep()` between calls (0.4–1.0s for reliable endpoints like `PlayByPlayV3`/`LeagueGameFinder`, more conservative 5–30s backoff for the flakier `shotchartdetail` endpoint) plus retry-with-backoff. Match the existing pattern in whichever script is closest to what you're building; don't invent a new rate-limiting scheme.
- Season encoding: `player_season_stats.season` and most other tables use the **end year as an int** (e.g. `2026` means the 2025-26 season). Convert to the NBA's own label format with `f"{season - 1}-{str(season)[-2:]}"` when calling `nba_api` — this exact expression appears throughout the codebase, copy it rather than reinventing it. `pbp_games`/`pbp_events` now span real seasons 2021-2026 (previously just one sampled season, see C8 below) and agree with `player_season_stats`'s latest season — but still query each table's own `MAX(season)` rather than assuming any two tables agree on "the latest season" (this exact bug was found and fixed once already in `/games/wp-replay/list`, back when they didn't).

**The "never fabricate data" rule in practice — how to apply it to new work:**
- A composite/index metric (Heliocentricity Index, Impact Score) is fine as a simple, fully-disclosed equal-weighted average of real percentile ranks. It is never presented as a trained/validated model unless it actually is one.
- A predictive/simulated feature (e.g. "what would this trade do to team chemistry") needs either a real trained model with real validation metrics, or it gets scoped down to something that only uses real data that actually happened (see Lineup Chemistry below — the honest substitute for a "Trade Chemistry Simulator" that would have needed an invented usage-redistribution formula).
- Every "what if" / counterfactual feature (see Game Replay) must say in its own API response and its own UI copy that it's a counterfactual, not a prediction of what actually would have happened.
- Small real samples get a disclosed warning/cutoff (e.g. Playoff Drop-off Forecaster's `small_sample_warning` under 10 games, Lineup Chemistry's `min_minutes` cutoff with `lineups_qualified`/`lineups_total` both returned) rather than being silently included or silently hidden.
- If a feature needs data the project doesn't have (a real API key, a real dataset), say so to the owner directly and ask, rather than fabricating placeholder data or silently declining. Registering third-party accounts / submitting signup forms with the owner's info is an action that needs the owner's explicit go-ahead, not something to do autonomously.

**Verification discipline expected on every feature before it's considered done:**
1. Test the actual data source (nba_api endpoint, SQL query, whatever) directly with real inputs *before* writing application code around it — this has caught real bugs early several times (empty-string score fields that needed forward-filling, a `CalibratedClassifierCV` API quirk, non-unique `action_number` values).
2. Run the backend, `curl` the new endpoint(s), confirm real data and the expected JSON shape.
3. Start the frontend dev server, actually click through the new UI in the browser pane (`preview_start` with name `nba-frontend`), check the browser console for errors, screenshot if it's visual.
4. Run the smoke tests: `/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m pytest api/tests` (works from repo root or `api/`). Add a test for any new endpoint.
5. Do a real "sniff test": does a leaderboard/ranking match genuine basketball knowledge? (e.g. DeMar DeRozan's real reputation as a clutch scorer landing near the top of the Clutch WPA leaderboard was treated as evidence the whole WPA pipeline was working.)
6. `git status --short` and a `grep` for any literal secret values across changed files, every time, before staging — `api/.env` must never get committed (it's gitignored; verify with `git check-ignore -v api/.env` if in doubt).

**Commit style:** one commit per feature, detailed message explaining what's real about it and what was verified, ending with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` (or whichever model actually did the work — check the current session's own attribution instructions rather than assuming). Only commit when the owner asks; `git status` and read the diff before staging.

**Current status as of this README's last update (2026-09-30):** everything in "What's in it" below is built, verified, and pushed, including all five features of the owner's advanced-analytics plan (Garbage-Time Deflator, DAD Index, Exploit Guide v1+v2, Gravity Index & Spacing Lab, Contract Value) and three rounds of the owner's "analyst's best friend" list (round 2: shareable links, player profile page, Role Player Finder, Trade Impact, Stat Stability, Era Translator, Stat Line Finder, clutch vs. non-clutch, shot mix over a career, Pair Chemistry, Watchlist, referee crews; round 3: Game Log + Game Finder, On/Off, team page, Hot Streak Checker, Situational Splits, next-season projections, Aging Curves, Expected FG% / shot-making, Luck & Schedule, chart export + tooltips, Saved analyses, Data Coverage) and all eleven phases of round 4 (five-man stints from play-by-play, RAPM, rotation charts, assist network, Play Finder, season simulator, best games and upsets, shot quality map, rim deterrence, known-facts tests, report builder). 100 smoke tests, 27 known-facts tests and 50 paper tests (177 in all, `pytest api/tests`) passing against the local database; the smoke and known-facts tests also pass against Layerbase (re-synced after round 4; the paper tables of round 5 are not synced yet, and their tests skip where a table is missing). The only open roadmap item is B5 (parked — costs API money). See **Roadmap / what's next** near the bottom, and **Known real gaps** for everything disclosed rather than hidden.

**Local-only data you may need to restore:** Contract Value's salary CSVs live in `nba_data/salaries/` and are gitignored (third-party, no stated license) — see Known real gaps for where to get them. A full-parity cloud mirror now exists on Layerbase (`DB_TARGET=layerbase`), created 2026-09-26 with the owner's go-ahead (all 62 tables at the time, including `player_shots`/`pbp_events`, every row count verified matching local exactly: 6,318,078 and 3,609,582 rows respectively) and last re-synced 2026-09-29 after round 4 (147 tables, 0 differences, ~2,516MB of the 5GB free tier); shot charts and Game Replay were checked live through the app against it and both work. The older Supabase mirror (`DB_TARGET=cloud`, all tables except the two big ones) is left as-is for now — see the Layerbase entry near the bottom of the Roadmap section for the full story, catches (scale-to-zero idle sleep, 14-day archive), and re-sync instructions.

## What's in it

**Core stats & browsing**
- **Shareable links** (`frontend/src/utils/useUrlState.js`) — the open page is in the URL (`?page=builder`), so a reload, a bookmark or a pasted link opens the same page, and Back/Forward move between pages. Seven tools also keep their inputs in the link and have a **Copy link** button: Leaderboard Builder (`stat`, `from`, `to`, `gp`, `mpg`, `att`, `team`, `order`, `n`), its Build-your-own-metric mode (`mode=metric&w=pts:1,ts_pct:2` plus the same filters), Regression Explorer (`x`, `y`, seasons, floors, `within=0`), Breakout Detector (`season`, `dir`, `stats`, floors, `n`), Player Comparison (`season`, `a`, `b`), Shot Charts (`player` + `pid`, the NBA id that picks him since names aren't unique, `season`, `games=playoffs|all`, `view=heatmap`) and Trade Analyzer (`season`, `ta`/`pa`, `tb`/`pb`; a full trade runs by itself on open). Changing an input rewrites the link without adding history entries; a value that's missing or no longer valid falls back to the default. Analytics tabs keep their `#hash`.
- **Player profile page** (`?page=player&id=<NBA person id>`, `pages/PlayerProfile.jsx`, `GET /player-profile/{id}`) — one linkable page per player, opened by clicking any player's name or photo (`PlayerName`), a player result in the Ctrl+K search, or **Open full profile** in the player modal (the modal stays). Header: photo, bio (position, height, weight, birth date, college, Hall of Fame; Basketball-Reference), draft pick, last team, latest role (`player_roles`) and Greats of the Game entry. Then, each only when that player has data: season-by-season table (a traded season lists games per team; seasons under 10 games greyed), awards and honours (every MVP / DPOY / ROY / Sixth Man / MIP / Clutch POY vote finish, All-NBA / All-Defense / All-Rookie teams and All-Star selections, 1946-47 on), shot zones by season against the league (regular season, `league_zone_mix`) plus the Shot mix over a career chart, the Exploit Guide scouting report, DAD Index defense, Gravity, contract value, clutch WPA, the five most similar seasons by other players (similarity service, looked up by player id), and Breakout Detector flags (same ranking as that page, with a link to the list). Whatever a player lacks is listed under **Not on file** with the reason (e.g. matchup tracking starts in 2017-18; salary data starts in 2005-06), never shown as an empty box. Every block carries its season and sample size; **Copy link** shares the profile.
- **Team page** (`?page=team&abbr=<code>&season=<end year>`, `pages/TeamProfile.jsx`, `GET /team-profile/{abbr}?season=` in `api/routers/team_profile.py`) — the team twin of the player profile, for every team-season 1946-47 to 2025-26. `abbr` takes any code a team has used (PHX or PHO, BKN/BRK/NJN) or its franchise's current code; a franchise plus a season it played under another name opens that team with a note (OKC + 2005 = the 2004-05 SuperSonics). Blocks, each stating its coverage and, when empty, listed under "Not on file" with the reason: season summary (record, Pythagorean record, margin, SRS/SOS, ORtg/DRtg/pace, the four factors on both ends, league averages and ranks among that season's teams; Basketball-Reference, every season), franchise history chart (win % every season, playoffs marked, click to open), game by game (margin chart, last 10 or all games, home/away and rest records; 2009-10 on, real scores), luck & schedule (2009-10 on), roster with roles and part-season rows for traded players (from 2020-21 the play-by-play decides who played for whom, and players the season rows list under a team they never played for are named as left out), payroll and best/worst contract value (salary seasons only), best/worst stored five-man lineups with minutes coverage and a Pair Chemistry link (2013-14 on), on/off leaders and the top-usage player (2020-21 on), and shot mix vs. the league for the team and its opponents (1996-97 on). New tables: `team_seasons` (`scripts/build_team_seasons.py`, franchise map in `api/teams_lib.py`) and `team_zone_mix` (`scripts/build_team_zone_mix.py`). Every team logo/name in Standings, the dashboard standings, Team Comparison, Ctrl+K team results, the player profile (hero chip and season table), Game Log, Game Finder, On/Off, Luck & Schedule's team history, Player Stats, Stat Leaders, Lineup Chemistry and Schedule Fatigue links to it through `common/TeamLink.jsx` (with the season where the table has one). Smoke test.
- **Watchlist** (`utils/watchlist.js`, `?page=watchlist`) — click the star next to a player's name anywhere `common/PlayerName.jsx` renders it to add or remove them; the list lives in `localStorage` only (per-browser, nothing sent to the server). The Watchlist page shows each starred player's most recent season's PTS/REB/AST/STL/BLK/TS%/Net Rtg side by side (`GET /players/history/{name}`, the same endpoint Trend Analysis uses), exports to CSV/JSON, and stays in sync live everywhere the star appears via `useSyncExternalStore`.
- **Saved analyses** (`utils/savedViews.js`, `?page=saved`) — a **Save** button next to every tool's **Copy link** button (Leaderboard Builder incl. its Build-your-own-metric mode, Regression Explorer, Breakout Detector, Player Comparison, Shot Charts, Trade Analyzer, Trade Impact, Stat Stability, Stat Line Finder, Era Translator, Role Player Finder, Pair Chemistry, and the player profile) saves the exact current URL — page, tab and every input already in the query string — with an auto title (the tool's nav label plus its inputs, or the player's name on a profile). Saves live in `localStorage` only, nothing server-side. The Saved page lists them newest first with an editable title and note, **Open** (loads the exact saved view via a new `openFullUrl()` in `utils/useUrlState.js`, which replays the URL verbatim rather than rebuilding it), **Delete**, and JSON **Export**/**Import** (import merges by URL, so re-importing the same file twice never duplicates a row).
- **Report builder** (round 4, phase 10, 2026-09-29; `?page=report&r=`, `pages/Reports.jsx`, `common/AddToReport.jsx`, `utils/reportStore.js`, `styles/report.css`; in the nav under **Saved › Report builder**) — a **Report** button next to the PNG/SVG buttons of every chart (`common/ChartExport.jsx`) and the CSV/JSON buttons of every table (`common/TableExport.jsx`, so every chart and table with an export toolbar has it, with no per-page wiring) stores a snapshot of it: the chart's SVG (colours inlined by `chartExport.js`; a chart seen in Ink is captured as Paper so it prints on white) or the table's rows exactly as rendered, plus the URL it came from, the page's title and the date. The Report page keeps several named reports; each is a list of items with editable captions, free-text sections between them (**Add text**, or **Text below** on any item), Up/Down buttons to reorder (keyboard accessible, focus follows the moved item), Remove with Undo, and an **Open live view** link. **Print / Save as PDF** uses an A4 print stylesheet (chrome hidden, Paper colours forced, each chart or table starts a page unless a text section leads into it, table headers repeat, source and date under every item). JSON **Export**/**Import** (one report or all; import matches by report id so a second import adds nothing, and every imported item is re-validated: only `<svg>` markup, same-app links, capped sizes). Storage is IndexedDB in this browser only (`nba-hub-reports`, one record per report; SVG snapshots can pass localStorage's ~5 MB); the page says so; if the browser blocks storage the button shows why instead of failing silently. No backend change, no table.
- **Chart export + hover tooltips** (`utils/chartExport.js`, `common/ChartExport.jsx`, `utils/useChartCrosshair.js`, `common/ChartTooltip.jsx`) — every hand-built SVG chart with real data (about 35 charts across ~28 components) gets a **PNG**/**SVG** download button: it walks the live, rendered SVG with `getComputedStyle` and writes every element's actual paint (fill, stroke, opacity, stroke-width/dasharray, font) back as an explicit attribute before serializing, since this app's colors come from CSS custom properties and stylesheet classes that mean nothing in a file opened outside the app — verified by exporting a chart and confirming zero `var(--...)` or `class=` left in the file (since 2026-10-03 a value a node would inherit unchanged from its parent isn't written again, which makes a file ~3× smaller with identical rendering, checked element by element; see Just shipped for round 7 step 4); PNG renders the same markup through a canvas at 2x with the current theme's page background painted first, so nothing is transparent. Decorative/non-data SVGs (icon previews, sparkline accents, the Learn-the-Game court diagrams, Guess the Game) are left out on purpose. A shared `useChartCrosshair` hook adds nearest-point hover (mouse move anywhere over the plot area, not a precise hit on a 2-3px dot) plus keyboard support (Tab to focus, Left/Right/Home/End to step through points) to seven time-series charts that had no tooltip at all or only an exact-circle one: Trend Analysis, Stat Stability's reliability curve, Era Translator's pace chart, the Game Log rolling-average chart, and Projections' backtest-by-season chart got the full hook; Trajectory Forecaster and Game Replay's win-probability line keep their own layered hover (band/line/point regions that a single nearest-x overlay would break) but gained keyboard focus. Charts with an existing working hover (Schedule Fatigue's rest chart, Shot Mix, Shot-making) kept their own interaction and just got the export button. No new table, no backend change.
- Live scores, standings, full player-stats browser with sortable/filterable tables
- **Stat Line Finder** (Players menu, `?page=statline`, `GET /similarity/stat-line?line=pts:25,ts_pct:0.6,ast:8&season=` on the similarity service, options at `/similarity/stat-line/options`) — type a stat line (any of 16 stats, up to 10: per-game box score, 3P%/FT%/TS%, usage, assist %, rebound %, net rating, minutes, age) and get the closest real player-seasons. Same idea as Season Similarity's matrix (each stat z-scored within its own season over every player-season that has it), but the typed line is read as a standing in a season the user picks, and matches are ranked by the root-mean-square gap in z-scores on the typed stats only (levels, not cosine shape). Shooting percentages use the Leaderboard Builder's attempts floors; each stat is usable from its first well-recorded season (same catalogue; a smoke test checks they agree). The page echoes the line (season average, standing, n), shows every match's values with its gap per stat and what drives the distance, greys seasons under 40 games, warns when nothing is within one standard deviation, has example lines, a season range, games floor and one-per-player toggle, and a shareable link (`line`, `season`, `from`, `to`, `gp`, `one`, `n`). Sniff tests: Curry 2015-16's, LeBron 2012-13's and Wilt 1961-62's own lines return those seasons at distance 0; 3 blocks + 12 rebounds today gives Olajuwon, Camby, Mutombo, Wembanyama, Robinson.
- **Game Log + Game Finder** (`api/routers/game_log.py`: `GET /games/player-log/{id}?season=`, `GET /games/finder`, options at `/games/finder/options`, player search at `/games/finder/players`) — every regular-season player-game 2020-21 to 2025-26 (`player_game_lines`, rebuilt from ESPN play-by-play; there is no game-level data before 2020-21 and both pages say so). The **player profile** gets a Game log block: every game he played in a chosen season (date, opponent, home/away, result and margin, minutes, box line, TS%, days of rest / back-to-back), newest first, with a hand-built SVG chart of one stat game by game plus its 5- or 10-game rolling average (shooting % are ratios of the window's totals) against the season average, and a warning when the play-by-play has a different number of games than NBA.com lists. **Game Finder** (Players menu, `?page=gamefinder`): up to 8 stat conditions (`f=pts:gte:30,fga:lt:15`; 19 stats incl. minutes, FG%, 3P%, FT%, TS%), season range, team, home/away, team result, minimum minutes and one player; sortable, paginated (25-200 a page), CSV/JSON export, copy link. Streak mode finds the longest runs of games in a row that all meet the conditions (gaps-and-islands over each player's games that pass the other filters; games he sat out don't break a run, runs can cross seasons; longest per player or every run; an Active tag when it's still going at his last game on file). Opponent, result and rest come from `team_game_fatigue`, joined on (team, date) because ESPN game ids aren't NBA ids; that join also leaves out the three NBA Cup finals (they don't count in regular-season stats or the standings), which the pages list. Filters are whitelisted (stat keys, operators, sort keys, teams), values are bound parameters. No new table. Checked against real box scores: Dončić's 73 (25-33 FG, 8-13 3P, 15-16 FT, 10 reb, 7 ast), Embiid's 70 (24-41, 21-23 FT, 18 reb), Mitchell's 71 in overtime (22-34, 7-15 3P, 20-25 FT, 11 ast), Lillard's 71 (22-38, 13-22 3P), Curry's 56 at Orlando (12-19 3P); streaks: Curry's 11 straight 30-point games (Mar 29 to Apr 19, 2021) and Embiid's 22 in 2023-24; Jokić's games per season 72/74/69/79/70 = NBA.com's.
- **Hot Streak Checker** (Players menu, `?page=hotstreaks`; `api/routers/hot_streaks.py`: `GET /games/hot-streak/{id}?season=&stat=&window=&as_of=`, `GET /games/hot-streaks`, options at `/games/hot-streak/options`; shared definitions in `api/hot_streaks.py`; `scripts/build_hot_streak_persistence.py` -> `hot_streak_persistence`) — "is his last 5, 10 or 20 games real?" for 14 stats (per-game counts, minutes, FG%/3P%/FT%/TS%, usage), 2020-21 on. Two separate answers: **how unusual** (4,000 random N-game sets from his own season so far; the share at least as hot or cold, plus the run's percentile among his actual stretches) and **how much carries on** (the share of a run's gap from baseline that showed up again in the next N games, measured on every player-season window 2020-21 to 2025-26). Baseline = this season's games before the run plus his previous season (`player_season_stats`) counted as a set number of games, chosen per stat and window on held-out seasons (half a season to a whole one for 3P%/TS%/FT%, 10-20 games for points and usage). On the **player profile** it's a card inside the Game log (same stat and window as the chart, any as-of date, and what he actually did next when there are later games); the **league page** ranks everyone's run as of a date by how far it sits from his own random sets, greys p >= 0.05, shows minutes before and during (role changes), counts how many would look significant by chance alone, and, for a past date, what the listed players did next. Findings: shooting runs are mostly noise (3P% keeps 8/12/28% of a 5/10/20-game gap; as of January 15 of three seasons, 9-11 players had a significant 10-game 3P% run against 9.6-10.8 expected by chance); minutes (56/66/77%), usage and points carry on far more. Stat Stability's between-player reliability isn't used for this: for points it would say 90% of a 5-game run lasts, against 34% measured. Methodology card added. **The "carries on" share is not a share of real change (measured 2026-09-29, round 5 step 6):** shuffling every player's games within each season, so that nothing carries on by construction, still gives a slope of 7-86% depending on the stat and window (the baseline is estimated from the same finite season, and both gaps subtract the same noisy baseline); net of that null centre, shooting-percentage runs carry on about nothing and points about 19% over 5-10 games. The page still shows the uncorrected (predictive) share; the card and Known real gaps say so, and `paper_beliefs_summary` holds the null centre per stat and window.
- **Situational Splits** (Players menu, `?page=splits`; `api/routers/situational_splits.py`: `GET /splits/situational/leaderboard?season=&split=&stat=&sort=`, `GET /splits/situational/player/{id}?season=`, options at `/splits/situational/options`; shared definitions in `api/situational_splits.py`; `scripts/build_situational_splits.py` (~80 s) -> `player_situational_splits`, `situational_split_league`) — every player-season 2020-21 on, four splits: home vs. away, back-to-back (second nights he also played the first night of) vs. 1+ days of team rest, 1,000+ miles traveled vs. under 300, that season's top-10 vs. bottom-10 teams by final-score margin (`game_scores`); 11 stats (minutes per game; points, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks, turnovers, 3PA per 36; TS%, 3P%, usage). Each gap has a game-clustered 95% interval and is shown against the **average player's gap** that season (weighted mean, team-season bootstrap). The honesty checks are on the page: how many players stand out against how many do when each player's own games are shuffled between the two sides, and the year-to-year correlation of a player's gap. On the **player profile** it's a block with every stat for a chosen season and split (greyed within noise, a Repeats column). Findings: home court +0.23 points per 36 and +0.9 TS points (smallest in fan-less 2020-21); top-10 opponents cost 1.09 points per 36 and 2.2 TS points; players who play both nights of a back-to-back get 0.65 more minutes and shoot the same (stars rest); 41 of 44 split/stat year-to-year correlations are under 0.1, so individual splits are mostly noise. Travel is confounded with home court (long trips end at home 38% of the time, short ones 77%): the page also shows the effect with home/away held equal. Methodology card added.
- **Breakout Detector** (Players menu, `GET /explore/breakouts?season=&direction=up|down&stats=`) — the biggest season-over-season rises or drops in a player's standing within the league: each chosen stat (default points, TS%, usage, assist %, rebound %, BPM; up to 8) is z-scored within its season among players with 30+ games and 15+ minutes, and the score is the average change in standing from the season before (both seasons must qualify). Every row shows each stat before → after with its change in standing. A historical line measures regression to the mean with the same stats and filters: the 20 biggest breakouts of each season 1978-79 to 2024-25 kept a median 73% of their jump the next year (847 players; 59% for three-point stats alone). Sniff test: 2016-17 breakouts include Giannis (that year's Most Improved Player), Jokić and James Johnson; declines are led by Chandler Parsons and Curry after his unanimous MVP season.
- **Stat Stability** (Players menu, `?page=stability`, `GET /leaderboard/stability`, `scripts/build_stat_stability.py`) — "when does a stat mean something": for every Leaderboard stat, the sample at which a player's number is half his own level and half luck. Split-half reliability: each player-season's games split into odd and even, both halves cut at the same sample (attempts, games, possessions, rebound chances...), centred by season, correlated across players; Spearman-Brown gives reliability = n / (n + M) and M is fitted over every sample size with 100+ player-seasons (bootstrap 95% range). Data: new `player_game_lines` (every regular-season game 2020-21 to 2025-26, rebuilt from ESPN play-by-play including minutes from substitutions: season totals within 0.3% of NBA.com's, three-point attempts included since 2026-09-29, minutes within 0.3% on average) and every stored shot since 1996-97 for FG%/3P%/eFG%. Results: 3P% needs 476 attempts (95% range 422-534; a typical rotation season of 155 attempts is 0.25 reliable), FT% 27 attempts, rebound % 51 chances, TS% 208 shooting attempts, net rating about 1,000 possessions; per-game counting stats settle in a game or two (minutes and role barely change), so they also get a per-minute version (points per minute: 48 minutes; steals: 289). Year-to-year correlation for every stat alongside (the only measure for BPM/VORP/impact score): 3P% 0.39, rebound % 0.96. Table + reliability curve (hand-built SVG), shareable (`stat`, `v=per_minute`). Fed back: the Leaderboard Builder adds a Reliability column and greys rows under 0.5 (e.g. 20 of 2025-26's top 25 3P% seasons), and the Breakout Detector marks stat changes where either season's sample is under 0.5 (`api/stat_samples.py` turns season totals into the same sample units).
- **Era Translator** (Players menu, `?page=era&player=&season=&to=`, `api/routers/era.py`: `GET /era/translate?player_id=&season=&target=`, `GET /era/players?q=`) — restates any player-season 1949-50 to 2025-26 in another season's environment, two ways side by side: **pace only** (each per-game count × target league pace ÷ source league pace; percentages unchanged) and **pace + league** (each count × target ÷ source league average per team game for that stat, i.e. the same share of an average team's output, which for points folds in scoring efficiency; percentages shifted by the change in league average; threes stay pace-only). Plus the era-free comparison: z-score, percentile and rank within the player's own season among 30+ game, 20+ minute players (the builders' attempt floors for shooting %). League pace chart with estimated seasons dashed, a small-sample warning under 30 games or 20 minutes, CSV/JSON export, Copy link. League data: `scripts/build_league_averages.py` → `league_season_averages` (80 seasons, Basketball-Reference league-average rows; pace for 1950-51 to 1972-73 is Basketball-Reference's estimate, and 1949-50 plus the BAA seasons are estimated here from FGA + 0.44 × FTA × 0.994, the 1950-51 to 1952-53 ratio). Sniff test: Wilt's 1961-62 50.4 points is 39.7 at 2025-26's pace (49.0 at the same share of league scoring); Oscar's 1961-62 30.8/12.5/11.4 becomes 24.3/9.8/9.0, against 24.5/9.9/9.1 from Neil Paine's published per-100 line (26.7/10.8/9.9) at the same pace and minutes; Walt Bellamy, not Wilt, ranks first in 1961-62 FG%. Uses league pace, not the team's (Wilt's Warriors played at 131.1, so on team pace his 39.7 would be 38.2).
- **Projections** (Players menu, `?page=projections&stat=&team=&q=`, `api/routers/projections.py`: `GET /projections?stat=`, `GET /projections/backtest`, `GET /projections/player/{id}`, `scripts/build_projections.py`) — next season's line for every current player (501 of 582 in 2025-26; the 81 under 250 minutes over three seasons get a reason instead) from a transparent Marcel-style baseline: the last three seasons weighted 5/4/3 and by sample, regressed toward the league average with the weight on his own numbers N / (N + M) where M is Stat Stability's half-signal sample (year-to-year correlation for minutes and BPM), then an age step from the aging curves; minutes a game take a level-conditional step instead (bench / rotation / starter), because the population minutes curve is mostly role changes and under-projected 31+ starters by 2.6-3.5 minutes. Per-game counts = per-36 rate × projected minutes; 34 stats (per game, per 36, shooting %, rates, BPM). Every projection carries an 80% range from the same method's past errors in its reliability and level bin. Backtest 2000-01 to 2025-26 stored in `projection_backtest` (per stat and season: MAE against "same as last season", the league average, the weighted average alone and the projection without the age step; bias, calibration slope, and the range's leave-one-season-out coverage) and `projection_backtest_rows` (every past projection next to what happened). Result: beats "same as last season" on 31 of 34 stats (median 8% smaller error; points 2.32 vs 2.46 a game, BPM 1.33 vs 1.55, 3P% 3.4 vs 4.7 points), the league average on all 34; loses only on three-point volume, which keeps rising league-wide; the 80% range held 80% for every stat. Page: table per stat with team filter, name filter, Copy link and Save; backtest panel (error bars per method, coverage, bias, slope, error by season) and the full backtest table; profile block "Next season". Sniff tests: Dončić 30.6 points, Wembanyama 25.0 → 25.7 at 23, Jokić 27.7 → 24.3 at 31, LeBron 20.9 → 17.3 at 42, Kennard's 47.8% from three → 40.9%.
- **Aging Curves** (Players menu, `?page=aging&stat=&era=&player=`, `api/routers/aging.py`: `GET /aging/curves?stat=&era=`, `GET /aging/player?player_id=&stat=&era=`, `scripts/build_aging_curves.py`) — how a typical player's game changes from one age to the next, for 24 stats (per 36 minutes, minutes a game, shooting %, usage/assist/rebound/turnover %, BPM), by the delta method: every player with two consecutive 250+ minute seasons (shooting % also 100 FGA / 50 3PA / 50 FTA in both) gives one change from age a to a+1; each season is measured against that season's league average first (so the rise in three-point volume doesn't count as aging), changes are weighted by the harmonic mean of the two seasons' minutes (attempts for shooting %), averaged per age and chained, anchored at the average 27-year-old; ages under 30 pairs are left off, under 100 greyed; 95% ranges and the peak-age range from 300 bootstrap resamples of whole careers. Three eras side by side: every season the stat is recorded, the three-point era (1979-80 on) and 2009-10 on. Age is the age on February 1 of the season from `player_bio` birth dates in every season (the stored `age` switches convention in 2009-10, see Known real gaps). Survivor bias is measured, not hidden: per age, how many qualified players never had another qualified season and how they compared with those who did. Overlay any player's career on the same scale (his value minus that season's league average) with the typical curve moved to his level; plus a by-age table and a peak-age table for every stat, CSV/JSON export, Copy link. Sniff test: points per 36 peak at 26 (95% range 25-26), BPM at 25 (25-26); assists 28, free-throw % 28, three-point attempts 29; blocks and offensive rebounds 19, rebounds 22, steals 24. By 34 the typical player is 2.7 BPM below his 27-year-old self.
- **Role Player Finder** (Players menu, `?page=rolefinder`, `api/routers/role_finder.py`: `GET /roles/finder/options`, `GET /roles/finder?preset=&season=&positions=&relative=&max_usg=&max_salary=&weights=`) — "I need a 3-and-D wing" → a ranked list. Seven presets (3-and-D wing, rim protector, stretch big, secondary creator, point-of-attack defender, glass cleaner, floor spacer) plus custom; each preset is an explicit weight table shown on the page and editable there. Score = the sum of weight × z-score over 19 components from `defender_dad` (DAD, position-relative DAD, defended-FG% differential), `player_gravity` (3PA per 100, catch-and-shoot 3P%, guarded share, Gravity), `player_season_stats` (steals/blocks per 36, rebound, assist, usage and turnover rates, TS%, 3P%, free-throw rate, OBPM/DBPM), within one season among players with 500+ minutes; the DAD and Gravity z-scores are used as those tables store them, the rest are z-scored in the endpoint. Covers 2017-18 to 2025-26 (the first season every input exists; 339-379 players a season). "Compare with the chosen positions" re-z-scores everything within the selected positions (the stretch-big default, so a center's three-point volume is judged against centers). `player_roles` gives each row its role label; `scouting_splits` findings relevant to the role are shown as fit flags (▲ strength / ▼ weakness), not scored, because they only exist for ~180 players a season; the summary says how many listed players had splits tested. Optional usage cap (secondary creator defaults to 24%) and salary filter from `contract_value` (only 2017-18 to 2019-20 and 2024-25 have salaries; other seasons say so). A player missing a weighted component (under 1,000 tracked defensive possessions, or under 2 3PA a game for 3P%) is left out and counted, not treated as average. Sniff tests on 2024-25: rim protector Wembanyama, Holmgren, Kessler 1-2-3; point-of-attack Caruso, Ausar Thompson, Dyson Daniels; floor spacer Beasley, Isaiah Joe, Curry; glass cleaner Kessler, Adams, Duren; 3-and-D Dort, Finney-Smith, Caruso (Anunoby 2nd in 2025-26). Known misses, stated on the Methodology card: Gobert about 20th as a rim protector (few blocks, modest defended-FG% effect), Brook Lopez mid-pack as a stretch big on 2024-25 volume. Inputs live in the link (`p`, `w`, `s`, `pos`, `rel`, `usg`, `sal`, `n`); CSV/JSON export.
- **Regression Explorer** (Players menu, `api/routers/explore.py`, `GET /explore/regression?x=&y=`) — pick any two of the builder's stats and a season range: a scatter of player-seasons (a fixed sample of up to 2,500 drawn; the fit uses all), the fitted line, a plain-language verdict (strength, change per standard deviation with a 95% interval, r, R²) and the 8 player-seasons furthest above and below the line. By default both stats are compared within each season (season fixed effects) so league-wide trends don't count; standard errors are clustered by player (`scripts/stats_lib.wls_cluster`). Presets include usage vs. true shooting (2015-16 to 2025-26: essentially no relationship, r = 0.01) and assists vs. turnovers (r = 0.80). The page says it's an association and names the selection effect (older players in the pool are the ones good enough to stay).
- **Leaderboard Builder** (Players menu, `api/routers/leaderboard.py`: `GET /leaderboard/options`, `GET /leaderboard/custom`) — rank single player-seasons 1949-50 to today by any of 31 stats (per game, shooting, rates, impact), with a season range, minimum games, minutes and (for shooting %) attempts per game, team, order and size, plus presets; every row shows games, minutes, points, rebounds, assists and TS% for context, and exports to CSV/JSON. Each stat starts in the first season it's recorded for at least 90% of player-seasons (steals and blocks 1973-74, threes 1979-80, net rating/plus-minus/impact 2009-10); longer ranges are trimmed with a note. Shooting percentages default to 5 FGA / 2 3PA / 2 FTA a game. Traded players show under their last team from 2009-10 on and as 2TM/3TM before. A second mode, **Build your own metric** (`GET /leaderboard/composite?weights=pts:1,ts_pct:2`), ranks player-seasons by a weighted sum of up to 8 stats (weights −3 to +3 in the UI, −5 to +5 in the API): each stat is z-scored within its own season among players passing the games/minutes filters, lower-is-better stats are flipped, shooting percentages on too few attempts count as average, and every row shows each stat's value and z-score; five presets. Caveat shown on the page: in seasons where few players did something (threes in the early 1980s) the few who did can sit 5-8 SDs above the rest.
- **News** — real headlines from 6 public RSS feeds (ESPN, NBA.com, Google News, Yahoo Sports, CBS Sports, Sports Illustrated), with a team filter (adds a real team-scoped Google News feed), keyword search, and real keyword-based category tagging (Injuries/Trade Rumors/Game Recap/Player Watch) disclosed as a heuristic on the real article text, not a source-provided category — these feeds carry no category field at all
- **Team Comparison** — real per-game stat bars (existing), plus real advanced stats (Off/Def/Net Rating, turnovers), a real head-to-head record + recent-meetings list (`team_game_fatigue`), each team's real last-10-games form, and each team's real top-8 roster by points (`api/routers/team_comparison.py`)
- Shot charts (real shot-location data, live-fetched and cached per player/season), plus **Shot mix over a career** below the chart (`common/ShotMixHistory.jsx`, `GET /shots/player/{name}/zone-history`): each regular season's share of shots in the five zones as a 100% stacked column, with a dashed line where the league's own 3-point share starts that season (`league_zone_mix`), a per-season detail table (share and FG% vs. league) and an all-seasons table with export. Seasons under 200 tracked attempts are faded; careers that began before 1996-97 say how many seasons are missing. Reusable on any page: `<ShotMixHistory key={name} playerName={name} />`.
- **Expected FG% and shot-making** (Shot Charts › Shot-making tab, `common/ShotMaking.jsx`; `GET /shots/shot-making/leaderboard`, `GET /shots/player/{name}/shot-making`, `GET /shots/shot-making/model` in `api/routers/shot_making.py`; `scripts/build_shot_making.py` → `player_shot_making`, `shot_making_league`, `shot_making_validation`) — a gradient-boosting model scores every regular-season shot since 1996-97 (5.93M) by how often an average shooter makes a shot like it (court location, distance, angle, zone, two or three, period, seconds left in the period, season). Per player-season: shot quality (the expected eFG% of his own shots), shot-making (actual eFG% minus that, with a binomial 95% range), points above expected, 3P% and 2P% next to their expected values, and rank among players with 200+ attempts (fewer are shown faded, unranked). Every player is scored by a model trained on the other four-fifths of players (5 folds), so his own makes never raise the bar he is measured against. The tab shows one player's seasons (bars with 95% whiskers, hover for the season), a league leaderboard by season (shot-making, quality, points above, eFG%; best or worst first; `rank=`, `by=`, `order=` in the link) and the model check (held-out log loss against two baselines, reliability bins). The same seasons table is a block on the player profile, and the Methodology card loads the model numbers live. Regular season only; every response repeats what no shot on file carries: closest-defender distance and shot type, so shot-making also includes the defence faced and shot creation.
- **Shot quality map** (round 4, phase 9, 2026-09-29; Shot Charts › Quality map tab, `?page=shotcharts&view=quality&player=&qs=&qm=expected|league&vs=&vss=&qmin=`, `common/ShotQualityMap.jsx`, `styles/qualitymap.css`; `GET /shots/quality-map?player=&season=`, `/shots/quality-map/options?player=` in `api/routers/shot_quality_map.py`; the grid in `api/shot_hex.py`, shared with `scripts/build_shot_making.py`, which now also writes `player_shot_hex`, `shot_hex_league`, `shot_hex_meta` while its cross-fitted predictions are in memory) — where a player shoots and how he does from each spot. Every regular-season shot with a recorded location (1996-97 on) goes into a 2.08-foot pointy-top hexagon of the half court (765 cells; axial coordinates packed into one small integer); per qualified player-season (200+ FGA, 9,026 of them) the cell's attempts, makes and **expected makes** (the shot-making model's make chance summed over the cell's shots, each scored by a model that never saw that player) are stored as per-cell arrays (1,542,816 cells in 9,026 rows, 15 MB instead of ~100 as one row per cell), and the league's attempts, makes and expected makes per cell and season (18,774 rows, 1.3 MB). **Colour** = the player's FG% in the cell minus either the expected FG% or the league's FG% from the same hexagon that season (toggle; the league side needs 30+ league shots in the cell), shrunk toward zero as if 5 more shots had gone in at the reference rate so a one-shot cell can't look extreme; orange above, blue below; **size** = shots (a cell floor of 1, 2, 3, 5 or 10 shots); hover shows the raw counts, both references and the league's sample; a collapsible table lists the biggest gaps (cells with 10+ shots) as text; two players side by side ("compare with"), each with its own season, PNG/SVG export and CSV/JSON export of the gaps table. **Off the map** (counted per player-season, so cells + off-map = his attempts and makes, 0 of 9,026 differ): shots beyond half court (8,564, 0.14%) and, through 2009-10, the shots the NBA gave no location (664,345, stored at exactly (0, 0), 25-36% of a season's shots, nearly all at the rim); from 2010-11 on 99.9%+ of shots are in a cell and the page says how many aren't. Sniff tests off the stored cells: Curry 2015-16 shot 136 of 315 (43.2%) from 26-30 ft where an average shooter makes 33.0% on those shots (league 33.6%); Korver 2014-15 made 61 of 112 corner threes (54.5% against 39.3% expected); Gobert 2024-25 made 320 of 420 within 4 ft (76.2%, 70.7% expected, 66.5% league) and took 12 shots from beyond 10 ft; Jordan 2014-15 was at 72.6% within 4 ft against 75.0% expected. **`player_shot_making`, `shot_making_league` and `shot_making_validation` were rebuilt in the process and moved slightly (see Just shipped).** Not yet on Layerbase.
- **Shot Value Added** (round 6, step 8, 2026-10-02; Shot Charts › Shot value tab, `?page=shotcharts&view=value&player=&sv=&svby=&svdir=&svmin=`, `common/ShotValue.jsx`, `utils/shotValue.js`, `styles/shotvalue.css`; `GET /shots/shot-value/options`, `/shots/shot-value?season=&min_fga=`, `/shots/shot-value/player/{id}` in `api/routers/shot_value.py`; model in `scripts/shot_value_lib.py`, shared with `scripts/paper_xrapm.py`; `scripts/build_shot_value.py` (~20 min, 30 shot-model fits) → `shot_value_shots`, `shot_value_states`, `shot_value_added`, `shot_value_fit`, `shot_value_validation`) — every regular-season attempt from 2020-21 on priced **before its game** twice. *Average shooter*: build_shot_making's boosting model, but fitted only on the seasons before the priced one (and on none of the shooter's fifth of players), moved by the league's level so far that season (the shrunk running log-odds gap of every earlier attempt; opening night unmoved); a free throw at last season's league rate, moved the same way. *This shooter*: the same plus his skill as of the day before, a log-odds number for each of four kinds of attempt (rim, other twos, threes, free throws) that starts at his NBA debut near a rookie's average, keeps a share of itself between seasons, drifts, and is updated by every game (Laplace step), so no attempt's price uses its own game or a later one. The four settings per kind were estimated by **empirical Bayes on 2010-11 to 2019-20**, before any priced season (three optimiser starts plus a polish, all stored). A season's shooting points split into **skill** (this shooter minus average shooter: what his record said his shooting adds), **beyond** (points minus this shooter's price: what he made past that, luck included) and their sum, points above an average shooter on the same shots; **Shot Value Added** = FG + FT skill points. Page: the picked player's carried-in skill per season for each kind with its 95% band (and after the season), his seasons table, a sortable season leaderboard (skill, beyond, total, FT skill, SVA, per 100 shots, carried-in skills in percentage points; 100/200/500/1,000 FGA floors) with the league's own gap to its forecast that season, and the checks: out-of-sample log loss by season and kind with intervals, year-to-year correlations of the parts, the settings in plain units (numbers in Just shipped, below). Also feeds the RAPM page's **Shot-aware** version (`?page=rapm&version=shotaware&kind=prior|single`: the same regression on these prices, `paper_xrapm_players` versions `sa_prior` / `sa_single`, with the paper protocol's scores and paired intervals instead of the platform's validation tables; `api/routers/rapm.py`).
- **Greats of the Game** (`scripts/build_greats.py` → `greats`, `GET /greats`, Players menu) — the NBA 75th Anniversary Team (76) plus today's stars by a stated rule (played in 2025-26, not on the 75th team, 3+ All-NBA selections with one in the last three All-NBA seasons: Jokić, Dončić, Embiid, Tatum, Butler, Gilgeous-Alexander, Towns). Full-bleed hero: five rows of NBA headshots drifting sideways in an orange halftone treatment, faces colouring in near the cursor (a slow spotlight sweep on touch screens; still under reduced motion), a ticker of headline facts, then filter/sort and a card per player with a detail panel. Numbers are Basketball-Reference NBA/BAA regular-season totals (ABA seasons not counted; no championship data exists, so rings are never shown): MVPs, All-NBA (1st team), All-Defense, All-Star, Naismith Hall of Fame, Win Shares and best season. Facts are generated from the data (all-time ranks, times leading the league in season totals, top-3 MVP finishes, best season, draft pick, college, debut age; pick numbers omitted before 1966, the territorial-pick era). "Did you know?" trivia for 34 players (67 items) is either proven by league-wide records in the data (e.g. Curry's all-time and single-season three-point records and the only unanimous MVP) or a well-known fact checked against a linked Wikipedia article (e.g. Curry "credited with revolutionizing the game", Wilt's 100-point game); the check caught one stale claim (Kobe's 81 is no longer the second-highest game — Bam Adebayo scored 83 in 2026). Photos: 260x190 CDN images on the wall (~16 KB each), 1040x760 only in the detail panel; the CDN's blank silhouette (Kidd, Wilkens, Ewing) is detected by hash and replaced with an initials tile.
- Stat leaders, Draft Value Guide (Basketball-Reference Win Shares in each pick's first five NBA seasons, draft classes 1980-2021, plus every draft class 1947-2025 with career Win Shares and All-Star selections), Rookie Class Tracker (this season's rookies ranked by real ROY probability). Stat Leaders also includes a real **Hustle** stat group (deflections, contested shots, screen assists, loose balls, charges drawn, box outs — NBA's own real hustle-stat tracking, `scripts/fetch_hustle_stats.py`) alongside the traditional box-score stats.
- **Hall of Fame** — real all-time career leaders, single-season records, and career-longevity leaders, computed live from `player_season_stats`' full 1950-2026 coverage (`api/routers/hall_of_fame.py`). Named for "statistical greatness," not literal induction — this project has no real Naismith Hall of Fame membership dataset from any source it uses, and says so directly in the page's own `InfoTooltip` rather than implying verified induction data it doesn't have. Real, separate accolade layered on top: a gold "75" badge for the actual NBA 75th Anniversary Team (76 real players, announced 2021-10-21, `scripts/load_nba75_team.py`, `nba75_team` table) — sourced from Wikipedia and cross-checked name-for-name against `nba_api`'s own static player list.

**Player Comparison**
- Head-to-head statistical profiles with percentile ranks against a real qualified-player pool
- **Scouting Report ("Exploit Guide") v1 + v2** (`scripts/build_scouting_reports.py`, `GET /players/scouting-report/{player_name}`) — automatic, significance-filtered scouting keys for every player-season with 1,500+ real regular-season minutes (2,502 player-seasons, 2012-13 through 2025-26), shown as an "Exploit Guide" card (titled that way in the UI because Player Comparison already has an unrelated "Scouting Report" stat table) in every player's detail view and side by side on Player Comparison. Each real split is tested against a real baseline: FG% in each of 5 shot zones vs. the rest of the league in that zone (two-proportion z-test, 50+ FGA, regular-season shots only), and points per possession in each NBA Synergy play type vs. the rest of the league (z-test, 50+ possessions — Synergy publishes no per-possession outcomes, so the per-possession SD is estimated per season and play type from the real spread of player PPPs by method of moments, landing at a plausible ~0.8–1.6 points; Synergy's "Misc" catch-all isn't tested); and, from 2013-14 (v2, `scripts/fetch_shot_context.py`), 3PT FG% in each NBA closest-defender band (0-2 / 2-4 / 4-6 / 6+ ft), touch-time band (< 2 / 2-6 / 6+ s) and dribble band (0 / 1 / 2 / 3-6 / 7+) vs. the rest of the league in the same band (two-proportion z-test, 50+ attempts; 3PT only, because a first run showed 2PT bands just restating shot location — Giannis came out "strong" in every tight-defense, short-touch and 0-dribble 2PT band, i.e. his rim finishing again). Categories count as scouting keys only if their own findings persist: the lower bound of the 95% Wilson interval on the next-season same-direction rate must exceed 50% — decided by the data, not hand-picked. Only p < 0.05 findings are listed (up to 3 strengths, 3 weaknesses, strongest per category first so one skill isn't listed three ways) as template text with real numbers and n, alongside how many splits were tested and how many would clear p < 0.05 by chance. Also shows the toughest real defenders against the player (`player_matchups`, 20+ partial possessions and 10+ of his shots, with a p-value) and an explicit "what this report can't tell you" list (no pick-and-roll coverage type, no drive direction, no closest-defender distance yet, no help defense/scheme). **Real validation:** of 3,260 real findings whose player qualified again the next season, 84.3% point the same direction the following season (zones 87.0%, play types 82.8%, closest-defender 82.6%, dribbles 82.9%, touch time 83.8%; chance would be 50%). High-leverage FG% (from the Garbage-Time Deflator) is tested but deliberately *not* listed as a strength or weakness — the same check found it no better than chance (54.3% same direction, 5.7% significant again, n = 35), the well-known "clutch isn't a stable skill" result — so the reliability rule drops it to reference only. Sniff test: 2024-25 Giannis Antetokounmpo — restricted area 74.9% vs. 66.2% league (strength), spot-ups 0.75 vs. 1.06 PPP (weakness); 2025-26 Shai Gilgeous-Alexander — mid-range 55.0% vs. 41.4% league; 2024-25 Stephen Curry — 1-dribble threes 50.9% vs. 32.4% league and quick-release (< 2 s touch) threes 44.8% vs. 36.8% (strengths), threes after a 6+ s touch 25.7% vs. 33.7% (weakness).
- Any player's detail view (opened from Players, Stat Leaders, etc.) shows a real **Play-Type Profile**: real share of offensive possessions by real play type (NBA Synergy tracking, `scripts/fetch_playtypes.py`) with real points-per-possession for each, color-coded by percentile
- Fit Analysis: **Real Pair Synergy** — a real ridge regression (`scripts/train_pair_synergy.py`), trained on 23,381 real 2-man lineup pairs across all 17 real seasons and validated with real season-grouped cross-validation (real R² = 0.0148, disclosed honestly even though it's low — a real finding that pair chemistry isn't well predicted by box-score features alone, not a bug). Predicts a pair's expected real net rating above what their two individual real net ratings alone would suggest, and shows the pair's real observed net rating live-fetched from the NBA's own data when the two players have actually shared the floor. The older percentile-threshold heuristic (usage/spacing/role-overlap flags) is kept underneath as a simpler, unvalidated fallback.

![Player Comparison](docs/screenshots/player_comparison.png)

**Analytics** (grouped into four tab groups — Models, Player Analysis, Teams & Markets, Prospects — with the active tab kept in sync with the URL hash, e.g. `?page=analytics#wpa`, `#lineups`, `#replay` (old `/#wpa` links still open that tab), so any tab is directly linkable)

*Models*
- Awards Race, Impact Rankings, Model Validation — ROC curves, SHAP feature breakdowns, leave-one-season-out backtests against real historical winners for MVP/DPOY/ROY/All-NBA, **plus a Clutch WPA calibration tab**: real Brier score, log loss, and a 10-bucket reliability curve (predicted probability vs. real observed outcome) for the win-probability model, computed on real held-out games and shown for both "all events" and "real clutch time only" scopes, with an explicit note that the two scopes' aggregate scores aren't directly comparable (clutch predictions cluster near 50/50, which is a harder problem, not worse calibration — the reliability points are the real evidence).
- **Prediction Ledger** — real live model predictions (MVP/DPOY/ROY/All-NBA) logged with a real timestamp (`scripts/snapshot_predictions.py`), graded with a real Brier score once a season actually finishes and gets a real recorded winner/selection (`scripts/resolve_predictions.py`). Shows each award's current real favorites' probability trajectory over real logged snapshots, kept strictly separate from the honest historical backtest tables above — this is a live, growing record of what the model actually said and when, not a re-run.

*Player Analysis*
- Season Similarity & Player Archetypes — cosine-similarity and K-Means clustering, both **era-normalized**: every feature is z-scored within its own season before comparison. Season Similarity (2009-10 on, where usage and net rating exist) shows each match's full stat line next to the searched season with a plain-language reason ("closest on usage and assist %; differs most: lower true shooting"), and can be filtered to other players only, one season per player and a minimum games count (`GET /similarity/season-profile/{player}/{season}`). Player Archetypes includes a **League Evolution** panel: a real stacked-area chart of each archetype's share of the qualified pool across all 17 seasons, plus minutes-weighted real league-average trend lines for 3PA rate, True Shooting %, and a disclosed real pace proxy — cleanly reproduces the real, well-documented 3-point revolution and pace revival as a sanity check.
- **Player Roles** (Player Archetypes page, `scripts/build_player_roles.py`) — ten roles found by K-Means on what players do with their minutes rather than per-game totals: usage/assist/turnover/rebounding rates, steals and blocks per 36, free-throw rate, true shooting, and shot location shares (rim, paint, mid-range, corner three, above-the-break three, from `player_shots` via `shots_lib.classify_zone`), z-scored within each season, 5,386 player-seasons 2010-2026 (15+ mpg, 20+ games, 100+ located shots). K = 10 is the most roles whose grouping survives refitting on 80% subsamples (adjusted Rand 0.83; 11+ roles drop to 0.52-0.64); separation is low at every K (silhouette 0.10: styles are a continuum), which the page says. Names come from matching centroids to written role profiles (Hungarian assignment): Lead Creator (Harden, Dončić, SGA), Combo Guard, Mid-Range Scorer (Durant, DeRozan, Booker), Above-the-Break Shooter (Klay, Beal), Corner Spot-Up Shooter, Defensive Wing (Anunoby, 2015 Kawhi), Balanced Forward, Interior Finisher (Zion, Mobley), Rim-Running Big (DeAndre Jordan, Gobert, Jarrett Allen), Skilled Big (Embiid, Horford, AD). The older six-way archetypes (`cluster_players.py`, `player_clusters`) are unchanged because Pair Synergy's trained model and Trivia use those names; they show as each player's "family".
- **Offensive Style** (`scripts/cluster_playtypes.py`) — a second, separate K-Means clustering of the qualified pool by real offensive play-type frequency mix (NBA Synergy tracking: real isolation, pick-and-roll, spot-up, post-up, transition, and roll-man possession shares, era-normalized the same way as the stat archetypes), answering "how does this player's offense get generated" rather than "what does their production look like." Real silhouette score (0.21) is disclosed as lower than the stat archetypes' — play-type mixes genuinely overlap more than production profiles do. Sits right below Player Archetypes with its own scatter plot and table.
- Radar Compare, Trend Analysis, Career Trajectory Forecaster (CARMELO-style: real closest-age comps, weighted by similarity, overlaid against real future data when it exists)

![Career Trajectory Forecaster](docs/screenshots/trajectory.png)

- **Heliocentricity Index** — real touch/possession-share data (`nba_api`'s `LeagueDashPtStats`) combined with real usage%/assist% into a disclosed, equal-weighted percentile-rank composite (modeled on the Impact Score's own precedent). Explicitly not a predictive "slider" — just an honest index of who the real offense actually runs through.
- **Clutch-Time Win Probability Added (WPA) Tracker** — a real trained Logistic Regression win-probability model (same library/approach as the MVP/DPOY/ROY models), fit on real play-by-play from two real sources (`scripts/fetch_play_by_play.py` for nba_api, `scripts/fetch_pbp_espn.py` for real full-season ESPN coverage via sportsdataverse → `train_wpa_model.py` → `compute_wpa.py`), isotonic-calibrated, held out by *game* (not by row) to avoid leakage — 7,232 unique real games across seasons 2021-2026 (each real game counted once: the 420 nba_api games that ESPN also has are dropped in favor of the ESPN copy, `wpa_lib.PBP_DEDUP_WHERE`), real held-out ROC-AUC 0.830 (0.850 clutch-time-only) on 1,447 held-out games. WPA per play = the model's real win-probability output after the play minus before it, attributed to whichever player made the play, summed over the NBA's own real "clutch time" definition (final 5 min of regulation/OT, score within 5). The real sample size is always shown, not implied to be the full historical record.
- **Clutch vs. non-clutch** (Analytics › Clutch WPA, under the leaderboard; `GET /players/clutch-split?min_clutch_chances=50|100|200`) — does a player do better or worse in clutch time than in the rest of the game? A clutch play swings win probability 3.7× as much as an average play, so raw per-play WPA would call almost everyone clutch; `compute_wpa.py` divides each play's WPA by its leverage (the win-probability value of one point at that moment, from the same model: (WP(margin+2) − WP(margin−2)) / 4) and sums as a ratio, Σ WPA / Σ leverage, which puts it in points at equal leverage. Rates are per scoring chance (a shot, free throw or turnover), since rebounds, fouls and substitutions earn ~0 WPA and would mix usage into the rate. Clutch lift = clutch rate − non-clutch rate − the league's own change (0.843 vs 0.914 points per chance), with a 95% interval clustered by game (delta method for a ratio). Stored in new `player_wpa_totals` columns (`nonclutch_wpa`, `nonclutch_plays`, `clutch_chances`, `nonclutch_chances`, `clutch_pts_rate`, `nonclutch_pts_rate`, `clutch_lift`, `clutch_lift_se`) and a one-row `wpa_clutch_league`. The page leads with the honest result: with 100+ clutch chances, 6 of 186 players are better and 8 worse at 95%, where about 9 would clear the bar by chance alone. The model doesn't know who has the ball, so a miss or turnover costs nothing: this measures scoring, not defense.
- **Matchup Finder ("Kryptonite" defender finder)** — real player-vs-player defensive matchup data (`nba_api`'s `LeagueSeasonMatchups`, `scripts/fetch_matchups.py`: real partial possessions matched up, real FG% allowed, 571,608 real rows 2017-18 through 2025-26). Search any player as either the scorer (which real defenders have actually held them to the lowest FG%, and which have they torched) or the defender (which real offensive players do they actually shut down, and which torch them), reversible either direction. Disclosed sample-size guardrail: pairs under 20 real partial possessions are greyed out and marked "small sample" rather than silently ranked alongside reliable ones — a single defended shot is either 0% or 100%.
- **Garbage-Time Deflator** (`scripts/build_leverage_splits.py`, `GET /players/garbage-time`, `GET /players/garbage-time/player/{id}`) — splits every real player's real production by how much the game was on the line when it happened. Every real play in ESPN's full-season play-by-play gets a **Leverage Index**: the expected absolute win-probability swing of the next play at that game state (seconds left × score margin before the play), from the project's own deployed WPA model and the real league-wide mix of scoring outcomes, scaled so the average real play is exactly 1.0. Plays are bucketed garbage (pre-play WP > 99% either way, or 2nd half/OT with margin ≥ 18) → high (LI ≥ 1.5 or NBA clutch time) → low (LI < 0.3) → medium, first match wins (garbage is checked before clutch on purpose; ~17% of real NBA-clutch plays are already >99% decided, and that count is shown). Outputs raw / ex-garbage / filtered PPG, True Production Ratio, leverage-weighted PPG, and a "high stat-padding risk" badge only at ≥ 90th percentile garbage-time share among qualified (40+ game) players, with the percentile and n shown. UI: slope chart of the top 30 scorers, "Most empty calories" / "Most clutch-heavy" leaderboards (min-PPG filter, since deep-bench players dominate otherwise), a searchable per-player bucket card, and a methods/thresholds drawer. **Real validation:** rebuilt per-game scoring vs. the official per-game line, r = 1.000 and mean absolute error 0.025–0.031 PPG in every season 2020-21 through 2025-26 (n ≈ 325–367 qualified players each); 98.7–99.98% of real points credited to a matched player.
- **DAD Index (OBPM-weighted) — Defensive Assignment Difficulty** (`scripts/fetch_defend_dashboard.py` → `scripts/build_dad_index.py`, `GET /defense/dad`) — how hard each real defender's real assignments were: `DAD = Σ (share of the defender's real partial possessions vs. player i × OBPM of player i)` over `player_matchups`' real tracking data (2017-18 through 2025-26). OBPM is Basketball-Reference's published Offensive Box Plus/Minus (`load_bref_bpm_vorp.py`; until 2026-09-27 it was this project's own reproduction, which ran hot); EPM is proprietary and not used. Offensive players under 500 real minutes (or with no stored season line) count as replacement level, −2.0 (3.7–5.3% of real possessions per season). Qualified defenders: 1,000+ real partial possessions (317–344 per season); z-scored per season, plus a within-position (G/F/C) z-score, because qualified centers average +0.65 on the plain z (+0.95 with the old in-house OBPM), mostly because they guard other starting bigs. Plotted against the NBA's own real defended-FG% differential (`LeagueDashPtDefend`: defended FG% − the same shooters' normal FG%), with a real 95% sampling margin on every defender and anyone under 300 real defended shots faded. Quadrants (Lockdown vs. stars / Hidden / Targeted / Struggling vs. stars) split at z = 0 and a 0-point differential, and are labeled descriptive. **Real validation:** year-over-year stability r = 0.52–0.70 for the same real defenders qualified in consecutive seasons (DAD measures a persistent role, not noise); DAD vs. DFG% differential r = −0.25 to −0.38 (descriptive). Sniff test: known stoppers Lu Dort, Alex Caruso, Dillon Brooks, Jrue Holiday and Herb Jones all draw well-above-average assignments (z +0.61 to +2.16 across 2023-24 and 2025-26; Dort highest at +2.00 / +2.16), while Tyrese Haliburton (−1.75, 2024-25), Stephen Curry (−1.76, 2025-26) and Trae Young (−1.22, 2023-24) draw well-below-average ones.

*Teams & Markets*
- Vegas vs. Machine — real live championship-winner odds across multiple real sportsbooks, de-vigged with Shin's method, with per-book breakdown and a consensus-disagreement signal (coefficient of variation across books).

![Vegas Scanner](docs/screenshots/vegas_scanner.png)

- Playoff Drop-off Forecaster — real regular-season vs. real playoff advanced stats for the same player-season, with a small-sample warning under 10 real playoff games.
- **Lineup Chemistry** (Teams & Markets → Lineup Chemistry, `?page=analytics#lineups`; `GET /lineups/chemistry`, `api/routers/lineup_chemistry.py`) — the best and worst real five-man lineups of a season by net rating, with a season selector, a shared-minutes floor (default 100) and Best/Worst toggle, all in the link, plus Copy link / Save. Stored data only, two sources named on the page (`api/lineups_lib.py` picks by season): from 2020-21 every play-by-play stint (`lineup_seasons`, see **Five-man stints** below), before that `lineup_stats` (stats.nba.com's 2,000 most-used lineups a season). `lineups_qualified` of `lineups_total` is always shown since tiny real samples produce real but extremely noisy net ratings. Still the honest substitute for a "Trade Chemistry Simulator": real lineups that *have* played together, not an invented usage-redistribution formula. (Until 2026-09-28 this tab live-fetched stats.nba.com, unreachable from the build machine since 2026-09-26.)
- **Pair Chemistry** (Teams & Markets → Pair Chemistry, `?page=analytics#pairs`; `GET /lineups/pair-grid`, `api/routers/pair_chemistry.py`) — pick a team and season (2013-14 to 2025-26) and get a grid of its most-used players (8, 10, 12 or 15): each cell is how the team did with both players on the floor. **Two sources, named on the page and in the season list:** from 2020-21 every play-by-play stint (`pair_seasons` + `lineup_seasons` from `scripts/build_lineup_stints.py`; a pair's minutes, possessions and points are summed over every stint containing both, ratings are points per 100 possessions), and before that the stored 5-man lineups in `lineup_stats` (the 2,000 most-used a season; ratings weighted by possessions). Net / Offense / Defense toggle. Cells are coloured against the team's own figure over the same minutes (lower DRtg counts as better), hatched and greyed below a shared-minutes floor (default 100), and hover, tap or keyboard focus shows minutes, possessions, lineups and ratings. The diagonal is each player alone; row labels show a player's tracked minutes against their season total (all teams). Below the grid, the pairs that clear the floor are ranked, with export. **Coverage is stated per team:** from 2020-21 the page says how many of the team's games are fully tracked and lists, in a fold-out, the games excluded (play-by-play that didn't reconcile) and the minutes left out because a player on the floor had no id in the play-by-play (2023-24 Nuggets: 97% of minutes tracked, 110 minutes in 22 games left out; Jokić and Murray 1,410 shared minutes at +14.6); before 2020-21 `lineup_stats` covers 31–89% of a team's regulation minutes (median 65%), so bench units are under-counted and a team's same-lineup net rating runs above its real one (2015-16 Warriors, 73-9: 76% covered). Traded players appear under each team with only that team's minutes. Inputs are in the link (`season`, `team`, `m`, `min`, `n`) with Copy link and Save.
- **On/Off for every player** (Teams & Markets → On/Off, `?page=analytics#onoff`; `GET /lineups/on-off` and `/lineups/on-off/stars`, `api/routers/on_off.py`; table `player_on_off` from `scripts/build_player_on_off.py`) — every minute of every regular-season game 2020-21 to 2025-26, from the play-by-play `player_game_lines` (unlike Pair Chemistry's stored top-2,000 lineups, coverage is 99.1–100% of player-minutes a season). Per player-season-team: minutes, possessions, ORtg/DRtg/net with him on the floor; the same with him off (the team's game totals minus his on-court totals, game by game, over the games he played — games he missed belong to With/Without a Star); on − off for each; and a 95% interval for on − off net from a game-clustered bootstrap (his games resampled 2,000 times), plus his usage from the same lines. Team-game totals come from the same ESPN play-by-play with the same event rules (`team_game_totals`: points, FGA/FTA/OREB/TOV both sides, possessions = FGA + 0.44 FTA − OREB + TOV averaged over the two sides, overtime seconds, tracked share), so on + off equals the team total by construction. Three views: **One team** (every player, rows under the minutes floor greyed and marked, the top-usage regular's on/off in the summary), **League leaders** (players over the floor, with how many intervals clear zero against the 5% expected by chance: 11–15% a season do), **Stars: team with and without** (each team's top-usage player with 1,000+ minutes — the "fragility" half of the Heliocentricity idea, from real minutes rather than a simulation). Sortable columns, a range bar per interval, export, and inputs in the link (`season`, `team`, `view`, `min`, `sort`, `dir`). Also a block on the player profile. Honest framing everywhere: descriptive on/off, not RAPM — it credits a player with everything his lineups did and depends on his backups; ratings run about 3 points under NBA.com's scale because this possession estimate counts about 3% more possessions (differences are unaffected). Sniff-tested: 2023-24 Celtics 64-18, Nuggets 57-25, Pistons 14-68; league possession-weighted on-court net within ±0.07 of zero every season; Jokić has the biggest on − off among 1,000+ minute players in 2022-23 (+26.9), 2023-24 (+22.1) and 2024-25 (+23.3) and the Nuggets were a net negative without him every season from 2021-22 on.
- **Five-man stints from play-by-play** (round 4, phase 1, 2026-09-28; `scripts/pbp_lineups.py` + `scripts/build_lineup_stints.py`; tables `lineup_stints`, `lineup_stint_games`, `lineup_stint_seasons`, `lineup_seasons`, `pair_seasons`; `api/lineups_lib.py`) — every five-on-five stint of every regular-season game 2020-21 to 2025-26 (294,772 stints, ~41 a game), so lineup and pair numbers no longer depend on stats.nba.com's top-2,000 list. The on-floor tracking that built `player_game_lines` was moved verbatim into `pbp_lineups.py` (one `Game.walk()` replay drives both the player lines and the stints, so the two can't disagree; `player_game_lines` was rebuilt through it and diffed: 0 rows changed either way of 152,473). A stint is a stretch of one period with the same ten players; each row carries both sorted fives (NBA ids), seconds since tip-off, per-side points, FGA/FGM/3PA/3PM/FTA/FTM/OREB/DREB/TOV and possessions (FGA + 0.44 FTA − OREB + TOV, the `team_game_totals` credit rules), the running score, and an inclusive `action_number` range so any event can be mapped to its stint (`e.action_number BETWEEN s.action_from AND s.action_to` within the game). **Points:** ESPN's score fields are stale in a few hundred games of 2020-21 to 2022-23 (they drop, or jump two baskets at once), so crediting positive score changes double-counts there — it reconciles with the real final in only 512 of 1,080 games in 2020-21 (that is why `player_game_lines`' on-court +/- doesn't add up in about a quarter of team-games: 25% when re-measured 2026-09-29); the stints use the made shots and free throws themselves (7,052 games reconcile), falling back to the running maximum of the score fields (170 more). **Reconciled per game** (`lineup_stint_games`): 7,220 of 7,232 games match the real final score for both teams, the game length from `game_scores.periods` and `team_game_totals`' FGA/FTA/OREB/TOV exactly; the 12 that don't (3 NBA Cup finals not in `game_scores`, one game whose play-by-play stops after three periods, 6 missing their last few points, 2 with a rebound credited to the wrong side) are excluded from the aggregates and listed with the reason. Separately, a stint is used only when both sides have exactly five identified players: **93.7% (2020-21), 93.9%, 97.2%, 96.1%, 97.8% and 99.8% (2025-26) of each season's minutes are tracked**; the rest is time with a player ESPN gives no id to (two-way and 10-day players such as Mamadi Diakite, 100–155 names a season) whose name isn't in `player_season_stats`, the parser's only id source — adding an id fallback (`player_bio` has them) would change `player_game_lines` and everything built on it, so it is left for a separate, deliberate rebuild. Checks: every player's stint seconds equal his game-line seconds in all but 9 of 152,469 player-games (games with a substitution ESPN tagged to no team, where the lines credit him twice); each season's stint minutes equal 48 × games + overtime; each team's five most-used lineups match `lineup_stats`' minutes at r 1.000 (median gap 0.8–1.1 min, mean −0.8 to −2.7 from the minutes left out) and net ratings at r 0.98–0.99. Ratings use possessions averaged over both sides like On/Off (about 3 points under NBA.com's scale). Sizes: `lineup_stints` 98 MB, `lineup_seasons` 24 MB (114,496 lineups), `pair_seasons` 5.6 MB (29,125 pairs), `lineup_stint_games` 1.5 MB — 129 MB in all. Rerun after `build_player_game_lines.py` or `fetch_game_scores.py`; the API caches season lists per process (restart impact_api). Later round-4 phases (RAPM, rotation charts, rim deterrence) build on `lineup_stints`.
- **RAPM** (round 4, phase 2, 2026-09-29; Players menu, `?page=rapm`, `pages/Rapm.jsx`; `GET /rapm/options`, `/rapm`, `/rapm/validation` in `api/routers/rapm.py`; `scripts/build_rapm.py` → `player_rapm`, `rapm_fits`, `rapm_lambda_cv`, `rapm_validation`) — regularized adjusted plus-minus from the phase-1 stints: one row per side per tracked stint (the offence's points per 100 possessions, weighted by those possessions) regressed on +1 for the five offensive players' offence columns, −1 for the five defenders' defence columns, a season intercept and a home term, with ridge shrinkage λ chosen by 5-fold cross-validation grouped by game (λ = 3,000 every season; the curve is flat and the page shows it). Three versions, each stated on the page: **one season**; a **three-season window** (one rating per player, one intercept per season; windows ending 2022-23 to 2025-26); and **one season with a BPM prior** (shrunk toward 0.75-1× Basketball-Reference OBPM/DBPM instead of zero; λ kept at the single-season value because with λ free cross-validation slides to the top of any grid, i.e. to BPM itself — `rapm_fits` stores both `lambda_rule` and `cv_best_lambda`). 300 game-resampled refits give SEs and 95% intervals; rows under 1,000 possessions are flagged, not hidden.
