Page: version tabs, season / team / possessions floor, a verdict line (λ, held-out error vs everyone-average, home edge, how many intervals clear zero vs chance, r with BPM), a RAPM-vs-BPM scatter and the λ curve (hover + export), two validation tables, and a sortable leaderboard (ORAPM / DRAPM / RAPM, interval bar, BPM) with export, copy link and save; inputs in the link (`version`, `season`, `team`, `min`, `sort`, `dir`). Profile block (every version, league ranks) and a link from the On/Off page. **Validation, stored in `rapm_validation`:** held-out games within each season, and next season's stints predicted from the earlier season's ratings used as published (only the intercept and home term refitted): predicting 2025-26 game margins from 2024-25, BPM 15.33 points RMSE, three-season RAPM 15.46, BPM-prior 15.53, one-season 15.91, everyone-average 16.78, on/off as published 21.33 (best-fitting scale 0.09-0.20); predicting 2024-25 from 2023-24 the prior and three-season versions edge BPM (14.84 / 14.88 vs 15.12). **Honest result: RAPM with a prior or a window is about as good as BPM, plain one-season RAPM a little worse** — a known result, said on the page and the Methodology card. Year-to-year r: one-season RAPM 0.35-0.41, BPM 0.73-0.81, on/off 0.23-0.34. Sniff tests: home edge +0.95 per 100 in fan-less 2020-21 and +1.5 to +2.5 after; Jokić top-6 every season from 2021-22, 1st in every three-season window from 2023-24, 69th in 2020-21 (+1.8 ± 1.0: the Nuggets were +0.5 without him that season); r with BPM 0.56-0.60 among qualified players; 2024-25 Draymond Green 8th by RAPM vs 129th by BPM, Herb Jones 53rd vs 340th.
- **Rating Tracker** (round 6, step 7, 2026-10-01; Players › RAPM › Rating Tracker tab, `?page=rapm&version=tracker&season=&kind=filtered|smoothed&team=&min=&sort=&dir=`, in `pages/Rapm.jsx` + `styles/tracker.css`; profile block `common/RatingTrackerBlock.jsx` (career line with band); `GET /rapm?version=tracker&kind=` and the `rating_tracker` block of `/player-profile/{id}` in `api/routers/rapm.py` / `player_profile.py`; model in `scripts/rating_tracker_lib.py`, shared with `paper_eval.py`; `scripts/build_rating_tracker.py` (~8 min) → `player_rating_tracker`, `rating_tracker_fit`, `rating_tracker_curve`, `rating_tracker_validation`) — RAPM whose ratings carry across seasons. The same stint rows as RAPM, but each player's offence and defence rating is a hidden state: between seasons it keeps a share φ of itself and gains drift, a newcomer starts at average with the ordinary ridge spread, and each season his Basketball-Reference OBPM/DBPM (× a scale) are folded into the prior as noisy measurements before the season's stints are seen. A Kalman filter in information form (the full 1,852 × 1,852 covariance of 925 players' two ratings plus the season's intercept and home term, Cholesky; half a second a pass) gives the posterior after each season (**as of then**, nothing later), the Rauch-Tung-Striebel smoother the **with-hindsight** career line. **Hyperparameters chosen on 2020-21 to 2023-24 only** by the pooled next-season game-margin RMSE over the three tune pairs (the criterion every other RAPM version's λ was chosen by under the paper's protocol; Powell from two starts, 339 evaluations) and held fixed: newcomer λ₀ 4,918 (±1.63 per 100 per side), drift λq 3,424 (±1.96 per side per season), BPM weight λb 11,050 (a season's BPM counts like 11,050 possessions of stint evidence), BPM scale 0.76, carry-over φ 0.89. **The state-space model's own maximum likelihood is stored but not used:** it sends the BPM weight to the top of its range (995,501) because a season's BPM already contains that season's point differential (its team adjustment), and it predicts the next season worse (14.414 vs 14.333); a first version that scored the BPM rows as data was worse still (it "explained" them perfectly with scale 0 and infinite precision) and is why the BPM is conditioned on, never scored. Checks stored in `rating_tracker_fit`: with no carry-over the tracker reproduces the BPM-prior ridge to 3 × 10⁻¹³; leaving out any one tune pair moves φ between 0.61 and 1.00 but the held-out pair's RMSE by at most 0.03 (the criterion is flat; the choice is one defensible point); the stint noise is flat across stint lengths, so the posterior sds (1.20-1.26) are on the one-season bootstrap's scale (1.25-1.34). Page: a fourth version tab with an as-of-then / with-hindsight toggle, the five hyperparameters in plain words, the drift profile (both criteria along the drift grid, the choice marked), held-out and next-season tables with every version rescored on the same rows, a "Carried in" column (what he brought into the season) and posterior intervals; the profile block draws the with-hindsight line and band, the as-of-then dots and whiskers, and BPM. **Honest result** (`rating_tracker_validation`, next season's stints from the ratings as of the earlier season, every version on the same rows): the tracker beats every RAPM version in every season and BPM in four of five, tying it on 2025-26 (15.32 vs 15.33; three-season 15.46, BPM-prior 15.53, one-season 15.90); its fitted scale is 0.97-1.02 (right-sized; BPM's 0.62-0.75). It correlates 0.96-0.97 with BPM and 0.73-0.76 with one-season RAPM among qualified players: the BPM weight dominates a starter's 3,000-5,000 possessions, so the tracker is largely a rescaled BPM carried across seasons and nudged by the stints, and that carry-over is where its edge over BPM comes from. Sniff tests: Jokić 1st as of every season (2020-21 included, +6.3 ± 1.2, where one-season RAPM had him 69th); Wembanyama +4.3 carried → +8.7 as of 2025-26; Embiid +7.5 → +3.2 as of 2024-25; Draymond Green 73rd in 2024-25 (8th by one-season RAPM, 129th by BPM); hindsight revises an as-of-then rating by at most 1.0. Under the paper's protocol (`paper_eval.py`, `paper_tests.py`, model `rapm_tracker`) see Just shipped. Not on Layerbase (four tables, ~1.8 MB, for the round-6 sync).
- **Rotations** (round 4, phase 3, 2026-09-29; Teams menu, `?page=rotations&team=&season=&game=&m=own`, `pages/Rotations.jsx`, `common/RotationHeatmap.jsx`, `common/GameRotationChart.jsx`, `styles/rotations.css`; `GET /rotations/options`, `/rotations/games`, `/rotations/game/{id}` (ESPN or NBA game id), `/rotations/team` in `api/routers/rotations.py`; `scripts/build_rotations.py` → `rotation_closing_games`, `rotation_closing_stints`) — who was on the floor when, from the phase-1 stints. **One game:** both teams' players as rows, the game clock across (overtime included), a bar for every stretch on the floor, starters in bold, minutes on the right (the same as the Game Log's, same parser), a dashed "Unidentified" row where a side had fewer than five identified players, and the picked team's margin underneath, taken at every substitution and period break (the stints' own points, reconciled to the real final); hover or arrow keys step through the stints (both fives, score, time); a player table with minutes and on-court +/- (reconciled games only), previous/next game, and **Open in Game Replay** (Game Replay now opens `?page=analytics&game=<id>#replay`; without the parameter it behaves as before). **One team-season:** a heatmap of every player × every minute of regulation, shade = the share of the team's games (or, toggle, of his own games) he was on the floor in that minute, in seconds, so each column adds up to five players including an Unidentified row; games started beside it; the most common starting five; the most-used closing five; **closing lineups in close games**: the last five minutes of the fourth and all of overtime in games within five points at 5:00 left in the fourth (the NBA's clutch window and margin, `wpa_lib`, but the game is picked once at 5:00 rather than re-checked every moment), per five: close games, minutes, points for/against, +/-, net per 100 (greyed under 15 minutes) and the games it finished with their record; plus a starters and closers table (GP, GS, minutes, closing minutes and share). Only games whose play-by-play reconciled count (`lineup_stint_games.game_ok`, 7,220 of 7,232); the rest are listed with the reason and can still be opened as single games. A closing stretch counts for a five only while all five of that team's players were identified. `build_rotations.py` (~50 s) replays every game with the shared parser cut at 5:00 left in the fourth (`Game.stints(home, split_at=[2580])`, a new optional argument; without it the output is unchanged, checked on 600 games), and glued back together the cut stints equal `lineup_stints` in all 7,232 games; score at 5:00 plus the closing stretch equals the real final in all 7,220 reconciled games. **Since 2026-10-01 (round 6 step 3b) the replay runs on the corrected clock** (`pbp_event_clock`, `Game(..., clock=)`): ESPN stamped made shots a median 14 s late, so baskets just before 5:00 counted after the cut. Against the ESPN-clock build: the score at 5:00 changed in 3,354 of 7,220 reconciled games (+0.97 points on average), 352 close-game calls flipped (187 in, 165 out; 2,066 → 2,088 close games), closing-stretch seconds unchanged; the cut stints still glue back into `lineup_stints`' stints (same fives, counts and points) in 7,231 of 7,232 games, stint boundaries move by 2 s or less in all but 9 games, and 57 event-less stints of ≤ 2 s (two substitutions a moment apart by ESPN's clock) vanish. 27-31% of games a season are close at 5:00 left. Team page: a Rotation block (the heatmap's top 12, starting and closing fives, link to the page; "Not on file" before 2020-21). Sniff tests: Denver 2023-24 games played and started match the real ones (Jokić 79, Porter Jr. 81, Caldwell-Pope 76, Gordon 73, Murray 59) and the real five started 48 games together; Boston 2023-24 starts match (Tatum 74, White 73, Brown 70, Holiday 69, Porziņģis 57, Horford 33); Denver's four starters who played nearly every game are on the floor in the first minute of both halves in 88-99% of its games; opening night 2023-24 (DEN 119, LAL 107, LeBron 29 minutes) matches the box score; Brunson played 95% of the Knicks' closing minutes. (Boston 2023-24's most-used close-game closing five was Horford's on ESPN's clock, 36 minutes against 34 for the starting five with Porziņģis; on the corrected clock its close games change and the order flips, 27.1 against 22.7, though Horford still has more closing minutes, 64.7 against 60.6.) Copy link, Save, chart export (PNG/SVG) and CSV/JSON export on every table.
- **Lineup Predictor** (round 6, step 9, 2026-10-02; the "Try a lineup" panel at the bottom of Teams › Rotations' season view, `?page=rotations&team=&season=&lu=<five ids>`, `common/LineupPredictorPanel.jsx`, `styles/lineuppredictor.css`; `GET /lineup-predictor/options`, `/lineup-predictor/team?team=&season=`, `/lineup-predictor/predict?team=&season=&ids=` in `api/routers/lineup_predictor.py` (lru-cached: restart impact_api after a rebuild); model in `api/lineup_predictor_lib.py`, shared with `scripts/build_lineup_predictor.py` (~40 s) → `lineup_predictor_units`, `_players`, `_teams`, `_fit`, `_metrics`, `_tests`) — the net rating of a five-man lineup predicted from what was known **before its first game**, scored on the possessions it then played. Unit = one five on one team in one season, 2021-22 to 2025-26 (78,205; 2020-21 has no RAPM/tracker season before it); outcome = points per 100 for minus against over the counted possessions (round 6 step 3's `possessions`) that began with those five on the floor; weight = the harmonic mean of its offensive and defensive possessions. Features: the five pre-season ratings summed (BPM projection / last season's RAPM with prior / last season's Rating Tracker; the source chosen on tune), spacing (Gravity summed, season before), role counts and two fit flags (2+ bigs, no playmaker; Player Roles, season before), projected usage summed, and the team's and the five's on-court net so far, shrunk by n/(n+K). Models: plain sum, rescaled sum, + fit, + season so far; floors zero and team-so-far. Round 5's protocol (choices by grouped CV on 2021-22 to 2023-24, validate 2024-25, test 2025-26 refit on 2021-22 to 2024-25); scores are the **share of the real (noise-free) spread explained**, from a per-lineup noise model checked against a split-half estimate (105.2 vs 93.5 real variance, so shares may read ~11% high); paired bootstrap over team-seasons. Panel: pick any five of the team's players (the most-used five by default) → the prediction before the season and with the whole season, an 80% range of the real net and of what 100 possessions would show, each player's rating / role / Gravity / usage / on-court net, how the prediction adds up, and, if the five played, its record and what the model said before its first game; below, the team's 15 most-used lineups predicted vs actual ("Try" loads one) and the model's held-out scores (interval chart + tests table). **Honest result** in Just shipped: fit features add nothing; the season so far adds a little. Methodology card `lineuppredictor`, Data Coverage entry. Not on Layerbase (six tables, ~27 MB, for the round-6 sync).
- **Model Report Card** (round 6, step 10, 2026-10-02; Analytics › Model Report Card, `?page=reportcard&task=&metric=&v=&a=&b=`, `pages/ModelReportCard.jsx`, `styles/reportcard.css`, reuses `IntervalChart` from `common/CoachingCharts.jsx`; `GET /report-card/options`, `/report-card/task?task=&metric=&variant=`, `/report-card/pair?task=&a=&b=&metric=&variant=` in `api/routers/report_card.py` (lru-cached: restart impact_api after a rebuild); task catalogue, labels and the random-effects pooling in `api/report_card_lib.py`, shared with `scripts/build_report_card.py` (~9 min) → `report_card_units`, `_game_sums`, `_tests`, `_pooled`, `_choices`, `_meta`) — every model scored **season by season with a rolling origin**: each target season predicted by models rebuilt from the seasons before it only, by their own selection rules (pre-game constants, coefficients and the simulator's form 2012-13 to 2025-26; the simulator at three checkpoints vs the record carried forward and the standings; every impact λ / prior scale / BPM-on/off scale on the pairs finished before the season, the Rating Tracker re-estimated per season, 2022-23 to 2025-26, scored by game margin and **per possession**; Shot Value's look-ahead-free prices vs last season's FG% 2021-22 to 2025-26). Per season every score with a 95% cluster-bootstrap interval and every pair's difference (paper_tests' functions); pooled across seasons by DerSimonian-Laird with the Hartung-Knapp interval, τ, I² and a prediction interval for next season; flips counted per pair. Page: task pills, a season × model grid shaded by rank, every model pooled against the task's reference (interval chart), any two models season by season with the pooled and next-season rows, every pair by flips, and what was chosen before each season. Methodology card `reportcard`, two Data Coverage entries. Not on Layerbase (six tables, ~40 MB, for the round-6 sync).
- **Data Quality** (round 6, step 11, 2026-10-02; Analytics › Data Quality, next to Data Coverage, `?page=quality&v=classes|games|matter` plus `season=&level=&cls=&team=&sort=&pg=` (games) and `r=&scope=&ph=&pair=&metric=` (does it matter?), `pages/DataQuality.jsx`, `styles/dataquality.css`, reuses `IntervalChart`; `GET /data-quality/overview`, `/data-quality/check/{key}`, `/data-quality/games`, `/data-quality/sensitivity?result=` in `api/routers/data_quality.py` (overview and sensitivity lru-cached: restart impact_api after a rebuild; live checks cached per process with their time); class rules, live checks, plain-text sizes, drop sets and results in `api/data_quality_lib.py`, shared with `scripts/build_data_quality.py` (~10 min) → `data_quality_game_flags`, `data_quality_sensitivity`, `data_quality_meta`) — the paper's data-quality audit as a page. **Error classes:** all 18 classes of `paper_data_audit_classes` (ESPN's clock lag by event type added in round 6 step 12) with their size written from the stored audit, their handling, and a **live check**: 14 classes re-measured on request by independent SQL against the tables as they are now (110 numbers, each compared with the audit's at the precision the paper prints; slowest ~2.5 s); the other 3 (wrong player, tag/text, missed threes) need a replay of every game in Python and are checked by the build, which re-runs all of `paper_data_audit.py`'s checks and **stops unless all 223 stored audit numbers reproduce** (it imports the audit's functions; `identity_checks` / `shot_checks` now also return their per-event frames, audit output unchanged). **Games:** every ESPN game 2020-21 on (7,232, the three Cup finals included) with each per-game class's size there and a level, the worst handling among the classes that touch it: excluded (12) > flagged (an error left in the data: tag/text, team-less substitution, unidentified player, chart gap) > worked around > clean; filters, Replay links. **Does it matter?:** three headline results re-scored with each drop set (the flagged games, then each per-game class touching 1 game to half the result's games) against **30 random drops of as many games from the same seasons** (the control for "fewer games"). See Just shipped for the result. Methodology card `dataquality`, two Data Coverage entries. Not on Layerbase (three tables, ~6 MB, for the round-6 sync).
- **Workbench query layer** (round 7, step 1, 2026-10-03; read by the Workbench page since step 3, next entry but one; `GET /workbench/catalogue`, `POST /workbench/query` in `api/routers/workbench.py`, the catalogue in `api/workbench_catalogue.py`; catalogue and franchise lists lru-cached: restart impact_api after a rebuild) — every dataset and stat the Workbench can use, described once, and one endpoint that runs a validated query on it. **Datasets:** `player_season` (`player_season_stats`, 1949-50 on, 39 columns), `player_game` (`player_game_lines` joined exactly like the Game Log / Game Finder, so the three NBA Cup finals drop out; 2020-21 on, 26 columns), `team_season` (`team_seasons`, 1946-47 on, the franchise as the entity, 29 columns) and `team_game` (`game_scores` + `team_game_fatigue`, 2009-10 on, with NBA.com's team box score `game_team_box` for possessions and ratings from 2020-21, 26 columns). **Every column states how it combines over several rows:** counting stats as summed total ÷ summed games, minutes (per 36) or possessions (per 100), restricted to the rows that have that denominator; percentages as summed makes ÷ summed attempts (the season table's stored percentages weighted by their attempts, so one row gives back the stored value exactly); ratings weighted by possessions; BPM and usage-type rates by minutes; VORP, wins and attendance summed; Basketball-Reference's team four-factor rates and the impact score not combined at all (the table has the rate, not the counts behind it). Each column also has its first reliable season (the Leaderboard's 90% rule, tested for every column), its sources and a status. **Not offered, with the reason shown:** the season table's stored age (two conventions; the Workbench uses age on Feb 1 from `player_bio`, as Aging Curves do). Per-game on-court plus-minus and on-court points for/against were excluded in step 1 (the game lines' are wrong in about 1 team-game in 4) and come from `player_game_onfloor` since step 2 (next entry). **A query** names a dataset, players (ids) or teams (any code: NJN means the Nets franchise), up to 40 columns, a season range, row filters (stats in the row's own unit, or team / opponent / home / result / back-to-back / overtime / date / league / playoffs), a grouping (none, all, entity, season, team, opponent, home, result, or several), a per mode (per game, totals, per 36, per 100), filters on the combined value and a minimum of games, a sort and a limit. **Every row carries its n:** rows and games combined, and per column the sample behind that value in its own unit (games, attempts, possessions, minutes); player stats with a Stat Stability estimate also carry its reliability (n / (n + M), noisy under 0.5, as on the Leaderboard). **Safety:** a fixed request schema (unknown keys refused), every dataset/column/field/operator looked up in the catalogue, values only as bound parameters, a read-only transaction with an 8 s statement timeout, 5,000 rows at most; the heaviest query (all 23 game-line stats per player, every season, per 100) takes ~1 s. **The existing pages now read the same catalogue:** the Leaderboard Builder's `STATS` and attempt floors (and through them Composite, Stat Stability, the Regression Explorer, the Breakout Detector, the Era Translator and the player profile) and the Game Finder's `STATS` and base join are built from it; 184 saved responses of all those endpoints are byte-identical before and after. `api/tests/test_workbench.py` (15 tests, 38 cases).
- **Workbench charts** (round 7, step 4, 2026-10-03; the **Chart** block, `components/workbench/ChartBlock.jsx` (settings, requests, the data table), `PlotChart.jsx` (the only file that imports Observable Plot; lazy-loaded, so Plot and d3 (296 kB, 100 kB gzipped) download only when a board shows a chart), `chartSpec.js` (settings → requests); `POST /workbench/context` and `POST /workbench/trend` in `api/routers/workbench.py`) — six chart types on any verified catalogue stat of the four datasets: **scatter** (x, y, optional dot size, colour by the set's colours / one colour / any stat, "each dot is" any catalogue grouping, small multiples per member or per season / team / opponent / home-away / result), **line** (a stat over seasons, or game by game on the game datasets), **bars** (each member's value pooled over the seasons, optionally split by season / home-away / ...), **histogram**, **distribution** (boxes or every row as a dot) and **heatmap** (members × seasons or another split, value written in each cell). **League context:** the set is drawn in its colours over the whole population in grey, from the identical query for every player or team (same seasons, games floor and per mode): every matching row as grey dots on a scatter (up to the 5,000-row cap; past it the grey is left out and the block says why), and elsewhere a summary over **every** matching row with no cap (`/workbench/context`: 10th/25th/50th/75th/90th percentiles per season or split, numpy's linear rule, the same one the chart uses for the set's own boxes; or a histogram on round bin edges): a grey band (middle half, middle 80%, median) behind a line, a grey band and median line behind bars, an "All players" box, grey histogram bars with the set's rows as coloured ticks, an "All players (median)" heatmap row. **Trend line** (scatter, one panel): least squares over exactly the points drawn (the grey ones when shown), every point weighted equally; slope, its interval and the line's 95% band with errors clustered by player or team (`stats_lib.wls_cluster`, CR1), so five seasons of one player count as one player's evidence; r is Pearson's with a 95% interval from 2,000 resamples of whole players or teams (cluster bootstrap from summed per-cluster moments, seeded by the request, ~0.1 s). **Honest numbers:** values with Stat Stability reliability under 0.5 are hollow or faded; every tooltip carries the value's n in its own unit; the n of the chart, what the grey is and the fit's r, interval and cluster count are written *inside* the SVG (with the legend), so a PNG/SVG export or a Report snapshot carries them; a games floor applies only where rows combine games (each player-game row is one game, so a floor would empty it). Every chart has `ChartExport` (PNG/SVG/Report) and a **Data behind this chart** table (the set's rows with each value's n, and the grey population's percentiles) with `TableExport`. Colours: members `--wb-series-N`, grey `--wb-context` (≥ 3:1 on the surface in Paper and Ink), axes `currentColor`, so a chart follows the theme without redrawing; colour *scales* (a stat as colour, the heatmap) use viridis, or red-blue around zero for signed stats, readable on both themes. Methodology card `workbench`. No table; nothing to sync.
- **Workbench app tools + "Open in Workbench"** (round 7, step 5, 2026-10-03; the **App tool** block, `components/workbench/ToolBlock.jsx`, catalogue + per-tab fetch cache in `utils/workbenchTools.js`; the buttons in `common/OpenInWorkbenchButton.jsx`, boards built in `utils/openInWorkbench.js`) — ten of the app's own tools as Workbench blocks, each showing **one member of a set** (a player or a team, picked in the block; it follows the set like every block): for a player the **player card** (the profile's header and "at a glance" tiles, any season), **shot chart** (the Shot Charts court, dots or heat map, any season; regular season, playoffs and play-in, or all games, with the count left out said), **shot quality map** (the quality map's panel, FG% − expected or − league), **shot mix history**, **game log** (with its rolling chart and hot-streak card), **Rating Tracker**, **RAPM** and the **projection** (the profile's blocks); for a team its **rotation chart** and **assist network** (the team page's blocks, any season of the franchise, under that season's code). They are the same components the pages use, with their own data loading, tooltips, exports and Report button; nothing is recomputed. A player's blocks read `GET /player-profile/{id}` once per tab (every block showing him shares the request) and, where he has no data, show the profile's own "Not on file" reason (Bill Russell's shot chart: "Shot locations start in 1996-97; Bill Russell's career ended in 1968-69"); team blocks read `GET /team-profile/{code}?season=` and show the team page's reason (2014-15 rotations: "on file from 2020-21"). Add one from the **App tool…** menu: it binds to the newest set of its kind and, with none on the board, brings a new Set block with it. Inside a block the profile and team sections drop their card and page anchor (`ProfileEmbedContext` in `pages/playerProfileShared.js`, which also holds the profile's `missingReasons()`), so the page markup is unchanged: the Shot Charts page, three player profiles and two team pages render byte-identical HTML before and after (the court moved to `common/ShotCourt.jsx`, totals to `utils/shotTotals.js`). **"Open in Workbench"** on the Leaderboard Builder (single-stat mode: a set of the players in its shown rows in rank order, a note with its filters and what the table can't repeat, a table of their seasons sorted by the stat, bars for one season or lines over the range), Player Comparison (both players' cards for the compared season, their season side by side, BPM over the ten seasons up to it), the player profile (card, seasons table, points over his career, and the shot chart, game log, RAPM and projection where he has them) and the team page (the franchise's last ten seasons, net rating over them, that season's rotation and assist network where the play-by-play has them): a new board saved in this browser, checked by `cleanBoard()` like an imported file, and the Workbench opens on it. **Names aren't unique** (19 names belong to two players, e.g. two Brandon Williams and two Mike James), so the shot chart, quality map and shot mix blocks ask by NBA id: `/shots/player/{name}`, `/shots/player/{name}/zone-history` and `/shots/quality-map` take an optional `player_id` (`impact_core.resolve_player`); without it their responses are byte-identical to before (checked on 7 saved responses). Nothing new in the database; nothing to sync.
- **Per-game on-floor plus-minus** (round 7, step 2, 2026-10-03; `scripts/build_player_game_onfloor.py`, ~35 s → `player_game_onfloor` (152,461 player-games, 22 MB) + `player_game_onfloor_meta` (the build's checks); read by the Workbench's `player_game` columns `plus_minus`, `onfloor_pts_for`, `onfloor_pts_against`; Data Coverage entry) — a player's team and opponent points while he was on the floor in each game 2020-21 on, from the same lineups and points as `lineup_stints` instead of the game lines' stale score fields. **One rule differs from the stints, found by checking against box scores:** a free throw is credited to the players on the floor *at the foul*, not at the shot (substitutions happen between free throws; the official box score credits the lineup that was fouled). The "lineups at the foul" are those at the last earlier event that is a foul or names a player, other than a substitution or another free throw. **Checked against ESPN's box-score +/- (300 random games, 6,390 player-games, `--espn-check 300`, read 2026-10-03):** exact for 98.2%, within 2 for 99.7%; crediting free throws at the shot (the stints' rule) 42.5% exact; the game lines' `tm_pts - op_pts` 36.6%. All five 70+ point games since 2020-21 checked player by player (`test_known_facts.py`): every player exact in four, four Bulls one point off in Mitchell's 71 (overtime). **Build checks (it stops otherwise):** with free throws credited at the shot, every player-game's points for/against and seconds equal his `lineup_stints` rows; every one of the 12,874 team-games of a reconciled game whose side had five players on the floor all game adds up to 5 × the real final margin (the 1,566 with a stretch of four or six listed: 162). Values are shown only in the 7,220 of 7,232 games that reconcile (`game_ok`); `tracked_seconds` gives each row's share in fully tracked stints (96.9% of seconds). Same rows as `player_game_lines` except its 9 zero-second rows, plus one player on the floor only for a foul (Nicolas Batum, PHI vs CLE 2024-02-23: checked in and out at the same clock around a take foul, so he is credited the free throw and has 0 seconds).
- **Workbench** (round 7, step 3, 2026-10-03; its own top-nav tab, `?page=workbench&b=<board id>`, `pages/Workbench.jsx`, blocks in `components/workbench/`, `styles/workbench.css`, boards in `utils/workbenchStore.js`, grid rules in `utils/workbenchLayout.js`; backend `GET /workbench/entities` in `routers/workbench.py`) — boards you build yourself. A board is a page of **blocks** on a 12-column grid plus named **sets** of players or teams that any block can use; change a set and every block bound to it follows. Three blocks so far: **Set** (search players by name, accents ignored, so "jokic" finds Jokić; or pick from every franchise; add/remove members; each member gets a colour from an 8-colour palette defined as `--wb-series-0..7` tokens, separate for Paper and Ink, ≥ 4.6:1 against the block surface in both); **Table** (any verified catalogue column on any of the four datasets, all players/teams or a set, a season range, "each row is" = any grouping the catalogue offers, per game / totals / per 36 / per 100, a minimum of games, 25-1,000 rows; sortable by clicking a header (server-side, so the sort covers every matching row, not just the shown ones); every row shows its seasons/games, every value its n on hover (or under it, a setting) and greys values with Stat Stability reliability under 0.5; excluded columns are listed in the picker as "Not offered: <reason>"; the summary line says how many rows matched, the span, grouping and per mode, plus the `_source` badge; `TableExport` (CSV/JSON/Report)); **Note** (plain text, shown as text, never HTML). **Layout:** drag a block by its handle to move it and by its corner to resize it (pointer events: mouse, pen, touch), or focus the handle and use the arrow keys (Shift+arrows resize; the resize corner takes arrows too); a screen-reader live region says where it went; after every move the grid compacts upward and a block dropped on another pushes it down; new blocks take the first free spot; on screens under 760 px blocks stack in reading order with Up/Down buttons instead. **Storage:** like the Report builder, IndexedDB in this browser only (`nba-hub-workbench`, one record per board; every write re-reads the stored board inside its transaction; BroadcastChannel keeps tabs in step); the page says so. **Sharing:** *Share link* copies a link that carries the whole board (JSON without the random block and set ids, deflate-compressed with the browser's `CompressionStream`, base64url, `?page=workbench&share=…`; a board with a 3-player set, two tables and a note is 746 characters); a board whose link would pass 2,000 characters is refused with "use Export file" (chat apps and mail cut longer links). Opening a share link shows what the board holds and offers **Add to my boards** (a copy; later edits don't travel). **Export file / Import file** (one board or all; boards keep their id, so a repeat import adds nothing). Everything read from a link or a file is rebuilt field by field (`cleanBoard`: block types, grid positions, set kinds and member ids (player ids are whole numbers, team ids 2-4 capital letters), colour indices, catalogue keys by shape, caps of 40 blocks / 20 sets / 500 members / 40 columns), and the server checks every key again. **Save** (Saved analyses) saves `?page=workbench&b=<id>`; a link to a board this browser doesn't have says so and opens the latest one. `GET /workbench/entities?kind=player&q=|ids=` (players with their NBA id, first and last season and latest team, accent- and case-insensitive, LIKE wildcards stripped from the text, 25 at most) and `?kind=team` (every franchise in `team_seasons` with its latest name and code; lru-cached: restart impact_api after `build_team_seasons.py`). Nothing new in the database; nothing to sync. Charts came in step 4 and the app's own tools as blocks in step 5 (the entries before and after this one).
- **Season Simulator** (round 4, phase 6, 2026-09-29; Teams menu, `?page=simulator&season=&asof=&team=&sort=&dir=`, `pages/SeasonSimulator.jsx`, `styles/simulator.css`; `GET /season-sim/options`, `/season-sim?season=&as_of=`, `/season-sim/model` in `api/routers/season_sim.py`; math in `api/season_sim_lib.py`, shared with `scripts/build_season_sim.py` (~30 s) → `game_pregame_odds`, `pregame_model_fit`, `pregame_calibration`, `pregame_model_seasons`, `season_postseason`, `season_sim_seasons`, `season_sim_params`, `season_sim_backtest`, `season_sim_backtest_summary`, `season_sim_calibration`; `scripts/fetch_postseason_games.py` (~3 min, ESPN) → `postseason_games`) — playoff, play-in and seed odds from the morning of any date of any season 2010-11 to 2025-26. **Pre-game odds** for every game (19,118): a logistic regression of the home team winning on the expected margin (rating difference plus home court) and both sides' back-to-back flags (`team_game_fatigue.rest_days`), everything as of that morning; a team's rating is this season's SRS from the games so far (raw venue-adjusted margins until 30 games) blended with last season's final SRS as a Bayesian prior (prior mean 0.60 × last season, the slope over all 480 franchise pairs; prior variance 14.6, so the prior is worth 10-13 games; home court blended with last season's, worth 390 games). Four forms fitted **leave-one-season-out** and scored by log loss: prior + rest 0.6126 (used), prior 0.6142, this season's ratings only 0.6237, the Luck & Schedule page's own projection 0.6275; the prior matters most in a team's first five games (0.641 vs 0.766); calibration within 2 points in every decile with 300+ games; favourites win 65.8% (66.3% through 2019-20, 65.1% since), home teams 58.5% then 55.2%; a home team on a back-to-back against a rested opponent wins 50.5% (1,737 games) against 56.7% when both are rested and 61.8% the other way round. Every game's held-out probability under every form is stored (`game_pregame_odds`, for Phase 7's upsets: Sacramento beat Golden State on 2017-11-27 as a 5.6% shot, the Lakers beat the 2015-16 Warriors on 2016-03-06 at 9.9%). **Simulation**: 10,000 runs from the picked morning, each first drawing every team's true rating from its posterior (so a team's games are correlated through its rating), then every remaining game (the schedule as actually played) with those odds; conference standings by win%, ties by head-to-head, then conference record, then a coin flip (stated on the page; division winners and the NBA's further criteria are not applied); top 8 make the playoffs before 2020-21, from 2020-21 the top 6 go straight in and seeds 7-10 play the play-in (7 v 8, 9 v 10, loser v winner, higher seed at home, same odds) in every run. Seeded per (season, date), so a link always gives the same numbers; a simulation takes 0.2-0.5 s and is cached per process. **Page:** season, date (Opening day / Halfway / 60 games in / Final day buttons), a verdict line, two conference tables (record and position that morning, rating ± its uncertainty, games left, mean and 10th-90th percentile wins, playoff / top-6 / play-in / 1st odds, a shaded finish strip for positions 1-15, and what actually happened: final record, finish, playoffs / out in play-in / out), sortable, exportable, with a cut line under the last playoff spot; click a team for its simulated win-total histogram (80% range shaded, real final marked); that date's games with their held-out pre-game odds and results; and a model section: the four forms, log loss by games played and by season, the rest table, a calibration chart, the backtest table and a predicted-vs-actual playoff chart per checkpoint (charts with hover + export). **Backtest** (`season_sim_backtest*`): every season simulated at opening day, the halfway date and 60 games in with coefficients fitted without that season, against the real playoff field, with two baselines at the same dates: the standings as they stood (playoff spot = 100%) and the record carried forward (log5, no home court, same tiebreaks). **Honest result:** by the halfway date the record carried forward predicts who makes the playoffs about as well as the model (Brier 0.089 vs 0.090; standings 0.121); the model wins on log loss (0.277 vs 0.293 vs 1.669), win totals (mean error 3.6 wins vs 3.9) and range calibration (75% of final totals inside its 80% range vs 60%); 60 games in, Brier 0.058 vs 0.058, win error 2.2, 78% inside; opening day, from last season alone, Brier 0.189, win error 7.7, 83% inside. The real playoff field comes from `postseason_games` (every play-in and playoff game 2009-10 on from ESPN's scoreboard, 1,458 games: stage, round, conference, scores; the 2025-26 field could not be checked any other way since the postseason shots aren't loaded), cross-checked in the script against the postseason shots in `player_shots` (2009-10 to 2024-25) and Basketball-Reference's playoff flag in `team_seasons` (2009-10 to 2023-24; its 2024-25 and 2025-26 flags list 20 and 3 teams, so they aren't used) — the script stops on any disagreement. Sniff tests: the 2015-16 Warriors (37-4 at halfway) and 2023-24 Celtics (32-9) are in every run, projected 67.3 [63-71] and 64.0 [60-68] wins (real 73 and 64); 2025-26 Detroit 60 games in (44-14) projected 60.6 [57-64], finished 60-22; playoff, top-6 and play-in odds sum to exactly 8, 6 and 4 per conference. Past dates only (no 2026-27 schedule is loaded; the page says so); 2019-20 runs on the schedule actually played as a plain top-8 season. Methodology card; Data Coverage entries; smoke test. Not yet on Layerbase. Since round 6 step 6 (2026-10-01) the day's games also show the odds with who played and open a lineup what-if (see Availability-aware pre-game odds).
- **Forecast Ledger** (round 6, step 1, 2026-09-30; Teams menu, `?page=ledger&v=both|roster|as_is&team=&sort=&dir=`, `pages/ForecastLedger.jsx`, `styles/ledger.css`; `GET /ledger/preseason`, `/ledger/games?team=`, `/ledger/roster/{team}`, `/ledger/hindcast`, `/ledger/lock.csv` in `api/routers/ledger.py`; frozen rules in `api/ledger_lib.py`; `scripts/ledger_lock.py` (--dry-run / --lock / --verify, ~30 s) with ESPN reads in `scripts/ledger_espn.py` → `ledger_meta`, `ledger_schedule`, `ledger_rosters`, `ledger_hindcast`, `ledger_forecasts`, `ledger_lock`) — the 2026-27 predictions, **locked on 2026-09-30 at 14:19 UTC, 20.2 days before the first tip** ESPN lists (2026-10-20, 19:00 UTC), so the season is a forward-looking test. **Two forecasts:** *as is*, the Season Simulator's opening day unchanged (0.604 × 2025-26 SRS, prior variance 14.63, the chosen pre-game form); *roster-aware*, 0.680 × the league-centred projected BPM of each team's ESPN roster at lock time + 0.187 × its 2025-26 SRS, prior variance 11.20. Team BPM: every rostered player's Marcel projection (`build_projections.py`'s own functions; they reproduce the stored `player_projections` exactly for all 501 players and add 106 who sat out 2025-26, whom the stored table leaves out), minutes by a **depth chart** (in order of projected minutes, each player gets his projection until 240 a game are used; the rest at replacement level, BPM −2.0; rookies and players under 250 minutes over three seasons get none). ESPN rosters: 607 players (training camp), 504 matched to NBA ids (name + birth date, then three stated fallbacks), 448 with a projection; injury status stored, not used. The weights come from a **hindcast** of 2010-11 to 2025-26 (480 team-seasons: each season's players with 200+ minutes on the team they started it with, projections made before it). Each forecast is played out 10,000 times on ESPN's 1,200 scheduled games (80 a team; the two the NBA adds after the NBA Cup group stage are played against a league-average opponent, one home, one away), with the simulator's tiebreaks and play-in, then the playoffs (new `season_sim_lib.simulate_playoffs`: fixed bracket, best of seven, 2-2-1-1-1). Stored: per team win totals with 80% ranges, playoff / top-6 / play-in / first-seed / each round / title odds; per game P(home wins) from opening-day ratings. **Proof:** the SHA-256 of a canonical CSV of all five locked tables (`c1a48402c1363db2f1e247f6931bad5c788a928d36717806aa5d8f89e8483d17`, 5.9 MB, 110,356 lines), stored in `ledger_lock` with the code commit and the tag `ledger-2026-27` (the in-season odds of round 6 step 2 must run the code at that tag); the page re-exports the stored rows and checks the hash on every load, offers the exact CSV for download and says how to check it with `shasum -a 256`. **Page:** the lock card, a verdict line (title favourite, the largest disagreements), a projected-wins chart with both 80% ranges (PNG/SVG export), conference tables (both side by side, or either forecast in full), click a team for its locked roster (projections, minutes given, what each player adds) and its 80 games' odds, the hindcast table and the rules as locked. Methodology card `ledger`; Data Coverage entries. **Honest caveat:** the hindcast knows who played for each team, so it flatters the roster-aware forecast; 2026-27 is the real test. Not yet on Layerbase.
  - **Live scoring** (round 6 step 2, 2026-09-30; tab `?page=ledger&tab=live&m=log_loss|brier&cal=<version>&tv=all|logged_before_tip`, `pages/ForecastLedgerLive.jsx`; `GET /ledger/live`, `/ledger/live/games?team=`, not cached; scoring helpers in `api/ledger_live.py`, shared with the script): `scripts/ledger_update.py` (~20 s; `--dry-run`, `--offline`, `--dry-run --today YYYY-MM-DD`) runs each morning (US Eastern date). It **imports `ledger_lib` / `season_sim_lib` / `luck_lib` from the tag** (`git archive ledger-2026-27` into a temp folder first on `sys.path`; blob ids checked against `ledger_meta.frozen_files`, tag commit against `code_commit`) and stops if the stored lock no longer reproduces its hash; reads ESPN's scoreboard for every date of the season (`ledger_results`: status and score of each regular-season event; the NBA Cup Championship doesn't count — Wikipedia "NBA Cup", read 2026-09-30); for every date up to today whose earlier counting games are all final, appends each game's odds to `ledger_game_log` under the locked in-season rule (`prior_for` → `ratings_on(final scores before the date)` → `game_odds` with the locked beta, back-to-backs from the dates actually played) for *as is*, *roster* and a *record-only* baseline (`season_sim_lib.record_p_matrix`: log5 of win % before the date), with `computed_at`, `before_tip` (vs ESPN's tip time), `games_used`, `last_result_date` and a digest of the results used; a row is only appended if that game/version/date/digest isn't there, so rerunning a night adds nothing, and a missed day is computed on the next run and labelled recomputed. `ledger_team_log`: per morning, forecast and team, record, rating and expected final wins (remaining win chances + the games owed to reach 82 vs an average opponent). `ledger_tests`: from 100 games scored under every version, `paper_tests.paired_rows` (task `pregame`: paired bootstrap by game, 10,000 resamples, sign-flip p, Diebold-Mariano p) for six pairs on Brier and log loss, on all games and on games whose odds were all logged before tip. `ledger_runs`: one row per run. The official number for a game = the earliest row logged for the date it was actually played. **Page:** status (games scored, last update, next date), a verdict line ("too early to tell" under 100 games), odds logged for games not yet final, the scoreboard on the games every version scored (the three logged versions and both sets of locked opening-day odds), the paired-difference table, a running log loss / Brier chart (record only's early spike clipped and noted), calibration in ten bins with 95% Wilson intervals, win totals so far against the locked 80% ranges, and every scored game. The page opens on this tab once a game has been scored. **No scheduled job (2026-09-30, owner's decision):** a daily LaunchAgent was set up, then removed at the owner's request (plist and log deleted; nothing runs in the background). Run `ledger_update.py` by hand on game days, ideally before tip-off, or that day's odds are labelled recomputed; don't set up a schedule again without the owner's OK. The removed setup was: a macOS LaunchAgent, `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.nbahub.ledger-update.plist` (outside the repo), runs `python3.14 ledger_update.py` in `scripts/` daily at 14:00 IST with `DB_TARGET=local` forced (it never writes to a cloud mirror, whatever `api/.env` says); a run missed while the laptop slept happens once on wake; output goes to `~/Library/Logs/nbahub-ledger-update.log`, and each run is a row in `ledger_runs`. Python is launched directly: through `/bin/bash` macOS refused to read `~/Desktop` from a background job ("Operation not permitted"). Run now: `launchctl kickstart gui/$(id -u)/com.nbahub.ledger-update`; stop (after the season): `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.nbahub.ledger-update`. It is a heavy-ish job only for ~20 s (ESPN reads), but mind it when timing a long build around 14:00.
- **Best Games & Upsets** (round 4, phase 7, 2026-09-29; Teams menu, `?page=bestgames&v=games|upsets&season=&team=&sort=&ot=&side=&mg=&n=&pg=`, `pages/BestGames.jsx`, `styles/bestgames.css`; `GET /best-games/options`, `/best-games?season=&team=&sort=&ot=&limit=&offset=`, `/upsets?season=&team=&side=&min_games=&sort=&limit=&offset=` in `api/routers/best_games.py`; formula and sorts in `api/best_games.py`, shared with `scripts/build_best_games.py` (~10 s; rerun after `build_play_finder.py` or a change to the win-probability model) → `best_games`, `best_games_meta`) — two lists. **Best games** (2020-21 on, where play-by-play exists; 7,229 games): each game is replayed on the reconciled score (the score before each play from `play_finder_events`, the real final at the buzzer; 7 games whose score doesn't reconcile keep their numbers but are left out of every ranking) and the home team's win probability is read at every play with the same model as Game Replay. Per game: `swing` (sum of |change in win probability| from play to play), `lead_changes` (the lead passing from one team to the other; ties skipped), `ties`, the winner's `largest_lead`, `comeback` (his biggest deficit) and `win_min_wp` (his lowest win probability), the single play that moved win probability most (`peak_event_id`, `peak_t`, `peak_dwp`) and `excitement = swing + 0.10 × lead changes + 1.0 × overtime periods − 0.05 × final margin`. **The three weights are a judgment call, not fitted** (nothing on file says which games fans found exciting); ranking by the plain swing gives a rank correlation of 0.986 and 41 of the same top 50, stored in `best_games_meta`. Overtime already adds swing on its own (mean 5.1 in regulation, 9.3 / 11.4 / 13.2 with one / two / three overtimes). Sorts: excitement, swing, biggest comeback, most lead changes, closest finish, most overtimes, newest; season, team and overtime-only filters; each row opens Game Replay at its biggest swing (`?page=analytics&game=&t=&ev=#replay`), shows the winner's pre-game chance and its rank among all ranked games. **Upsets** (2010-11 on; 19,118 games): the Season Simulator's held-out pre-game win chance for every game joined to the real result; an upset is an underdog win (34.2% of games) and the list runs lowest chance first, with the expected margin, both teams' games played, back-to-back flags, and the game's excitement and a Replay link from 2020-21 on; filters: season, team (franchise codes: NJN = BKN, NOH = NOP), team as the winner or as the favourite that lost, and "both teams N+ games in" to drop the weeks when ratings are mostly last year's; a collapsible calibration table by underdog chance. Team pages and links use `TeamLink`; both tables export. **Sniff tests, checked against outside sources:** the best game is Lakers 145, Warriors 144 in double overtime (2024-01-27; Curry's 28-footer in the second overtime is its biggest swing), Kings 176, Clippers 175 in double overtime (2023-02-24, the second-highest-scoring game ever) is 15th, the Clippers' 35-point comeback at Washington (2022-01-25, 116-115) is the biggest comeback of the six seasons (only the 1996 Jazz have a bigger one on record) and the Hawks' 30-point comeback over the Celtics (2024-03-25) is third; the 73-point Grizzlies win over the Thunder (2021-12-02) is last, and the best of the 437 games decided by 30+ is 3,010th of 7,222 (3,046th on ESPN's clock; since 2026-10-01 the swings are read on the corrected clock through `play_finder_events`: excitement correlates 0.9998 with the ESPN-clock build, same top five in the same order, 49 of the same top 50, swing −0.07 on average, the peak play changed in 871 games). Upsets: favourites won 65.8% of games (66.3% expected); games with the underdog at 10% or less (239) were won 8.8% of the time against 8.1% expected, at 15% or less (1,057) 11.2% against 11.7%; the biggest upset on file is the Kings at Golden State on 2017-11-27 (5.6%, 110-106) with Curry and Durant out, and the second is the Pacers at the Thunder on 2026-01-23 (6.3%) with Jalen Williams, Hartenstein and Caruso out: the odds know ratings, home court and rest, not who plays, and the page says so. Regular season only (no playoff play-by-play or pre-game odds on file); the three NBA Cup finals aren't in the best-games list. Methodology card; Data Coverage entry (`best_games`); smoke test. Not yet on Layerbase.
- **Rim Deterrence** (round 4, phase 8, 2026-09-29; Analytics › Player Analysis, `?page=analytics#rim`, `RimDeterrenceSection.jsx`, `common/RimBandChart.jsx`, `styles/rim.css`; `GET /defense/rim-deterrence` in `api/routers/rim_deterrence.py`; `scripts/build_rim_deterrence.py` → `rim_deterrence`, `rim_deterrence_seasons`) — what opponents shot, and from where, with each defender on the floor and off it, 2020-21 to 2025-26. Every field-goal attempt comes from the shared play-by-play parser (`Game.walk()`, no second parser) and is placed in its phase-1 stint by event number (every one of 1,283,689 attempts lands in exactly one stint, and every stint's attempts equal its stored FGA). **Distance comes from the NBA's own shot chart, not the text:** each ESPN attempt is matched to the same shot in `player_shots` by order within game, shooter and period when both feeds list the same make/miss sequence there (99.0% of attempts; the two feeds' clocks differ by up to ~26 s for the same shot, so clocks can't match them), distance from the x/y coordinates. Fallbacks, counted per season on the page: ESPN's "N-foot" text; a layup/dunk/tip/putback with no distance counts as 0-3 ft (the plan's rule, which the matched shots show is right only 85.8% of the time: the rest are 4-9 ft drives, so it is used for under 0.3% of attempts); any other two stays "distance unknown". Bands: 0-3 ft (under 4 ft, about the restricted area), 4-9, 10-15, 16 ft to the arc, threes (the NBA's shot type where matched). On = opponents' attempts with him among the five defenders; off = his team's other tracked stints in the games he played (the On/Off convention, same possessions). Per player-season-team: opponents' rim attempts per 100 possessions, rim FG% and rim points per 100 (2 × makes: both effects together) on, off and on − off with game-clustered bootstrap 95% intervals (2,000 resamples), every band's attempts/makes, his own blocks. Page: League / One team, season, all players or listed centers, minutes floor (default 1,000: at 500 the extremes are 600-minute bench stints); a verdict line (league rim rate, how many intervals clear zero vs chance, year-to-year r); a scatter of attempts gap × FG% gap (dot size = minutes, hover/keys, export); a detail panel per player (on/off table with intervals and league ranks, every-distance bar chart); a sortable table with interval bars, export, copy link and save; inputs in the link (`season`, `team`, `view`, `pos`, `min`, `sort`, `dir`, `sel`). Profile block (every season, league ranks, band chart), Methodology card, Data Coverage entry. **Honest result:** real signal, modest: 15-20% of 1,000-minute players a season have a rim-attempts gap whose interval clears zero (5% by chance), 10-16% a rim FG% gap; year to year the attempts gap correlates 0.34 with itself, the FG% gap only 0.17 — keeping opponents away is the repeatable half. Plainly on/off: teammates, backups and opponents move it. Sniff tests (rank in rim points among 1,000+ minute players): Gobert 2nd, 2nd, 1st, 3rd, 1st, 22nd from 2020-21 to 2025-26; Wembanyama 3rd in 2024-25 (2nd in rim attempts), 12th in 2025-26, 41st as a rookie; Lopez 5th in 2022-23 and 5th in rim FG% in 2023-24 but mid-pack in attempts most seasons; the league's rim share matches `player_shots`' own coordinates to 0.1 point every season.
- **Assist Network** (round 4, phase 4, 2026-09-29; Teams menu, `?page=assists&team=&season=&player=&n=&min=&v=duos&sort=`, `pages/AssistNetwork.jsx`, `common/AssistNetwork.jsx` (the chart), `utils/assistNetwork.js`, `styles/assists.css`; `GET /assists/options`, `/assists/team`, `/assists/pairs`, `/assists/player/{id}` in `api/routers/assist_network.py`; `scripts/build_assist_network.py` (~40 s) → `assist_pairs`, `player_assisted_share`, `assist_seasons`) — who sets up whom, from every assisted basket 2020-21 to 2025-26. ESPN names the passer on each made shot ("(X assists)"); the made shots and the name → id matching are the shared parser's (`Game.parse()` in `scripts/pbp_lineups.py`, no new matcher), so **every player-season's assists equal his `player_game_lines` assists (all but 4 of 3,172, each by one assist: the 4 made shots with data errors the network drops)** and are within 0.3% of NBA.com's season totals. Per pair and team-season: assists, games, assisted 2s/3s, points, and assists by shot kind from ESPN's shot type (layups & dunks, floaters & hooks, other 2-pt jumpers, threes). Per player-season-team: minutes/games (from the lines), made 2s/3s and how many were assisted (by kind), his assists and the points on them. The three NBA Cup finals are left out (not regular-season games; Game Log drops them too). Counted and disclosed, not hidden: 1,149 assisted baskets (0.31%) whose passer ESPN gives no id to (still assisted for the scorer, in no pair), assists to a scorer with no id ("Unidentified"), 4 data-error plays (3 self-assists). Page: team network (hand-built SVG, top 6-15 players by minutes around a circle, one curved arrow per passer → scorer, width = assists, circle = the player's assists; hover/focus a player to light his passes and the passes to him, hover or tap a line for its counts by shot kind; click a player for a panel with his top targets and feeders and his assisted shares against the league; PNG/SVG export), every pair (table, export), every player's assisted shares (greyed under 50 made 2s / 25 made 3s), and a League's top duos view (by assists, points, threes or layups/dunks). Profile block (a season's targets, feeders, assisted shares with league rank, every season) and team page block (network of the top 8 + top pairs), Methodology card, Data Coverage entry. Sniff tests: the league's top pair each season is Green → Curry (194, 2020-21), Young → Capela, Harden → Embiid (244), Haliburton → Turner (215), Harden → Zubac, Murray → Jokić (171, 2025-26); Green → Curry leads Golden State every season; Jokić → Gordon led Denver in 2022-23 (155, 131 at the rim). Creators: 12-19% of Harden's made 2s assisted, 7-22% of Gilgeous-Alexander's, 10-26% of Dončić's; shooters: 89-94% of Klay Thompson's made 3s, 88-100% of Duncan Robinson's (84% league-wide, so even 94% is only mid-pack among shooters: the 2s share separates creators from finishers better).
- **Possessions from play-by-play** (round 6, step 3, 2026-10-01; `scripts/pbp_possessions.py` (rules + the rebuilt clock, on `pbp_lineups.Game.walk()`) + `scripts/build_possessions.py` (~3.5 min); tables `possessions`, `possession_games`, `possession_seasons`, `possession_meta`; read by the Possession Explorer below) — every regular-season game 2020-21 to 2025-26 cut into possessions (1,442,608; ~100 a team-game). A possession ends on a made field goal (or the last free throw of an and-one), a made last free throw (flagrant, clear-path, transition-take-foul and away-from-play trips keep the ball), a defensive rebound (player or team; a dead-ball team rebound between two free throws doesn't count), a turnover, a held ball lost, or the period's end; the next one's `start_type` is that ending (`made_fg`, `made_ft`, `dreb`, `dreb_ft`, `team_dreb`, `steal`, `dead_tov`, `jump_ball`, `period_start`, `other` = the other team acted with no ending logged, 0.14%). Each row: offence/defence, start and end (seconds since tip-off), length, time to the first attempt, `transition`, event range, the `lineup_stints` stint at its first and last event (`stint_no`, `stint_no_end`; the ten players come from the stint), the score at its start, points, FGA/FGM/3PA/3PM/FTA/FTM, player and team offensive rebounds, turnovers, second-chance points, technical points by either side (kept out of `pts`), `and_one`, `empty`, `clock_ok`, `tracked_ok`. **The clock is rebuilt** (see Known real gaps: ESPN logs made shots a median 14 s late): matched shots take the NBA shot chart's clock (99.0% of field goals), free-throw trips their first free throw, rebounds 2 s after their miss; against NBA.com's own play-by-play of the 418 twin games of 2024-25, 93.9% of events land within 2 s (ESPN's own clock: 31.9%), and the lags measured on the odd and even halves of those games are identical. Turnovers can't be fixed (ESPN stamps them at about the time of the next play), so possessions that start after one have no time-to-first-attempt and no transition flag, and 72.6% of possessions have both ends on an anchored clock (`clock_ok`). **Transition** = first attempt within 7 s of a start on the anchored clock (after a defensive rebound the early-shot premium fades from ~1.55 points to the ~1.31 plateau by 7-8 s; the curve is stored in `possession_meta`; 99.3% of 270 transition take fouls fall inside the window; ESPN's text has no fast-break label): 14.8% of timed possessions, 1.40 points each. **Reconciled per game** (`possession_games`): 7,220 of 7,232 games — the same set as `lineup_stints` — add up to the real final score (made shots and free throws, else ESPN's running score maximum in 170 games, like the stints) and to `team_game_totals`' FGA, FTA (with technicals), OREB and TOV exactly; 94.5% of possessions sit inside one stint (85% of the rest include a free-throw trip, where substitutions come between the shots). **Counted possessions run 2.3 a team-game under the lines' estimate** (FGA + 0.44 FTA − OREB + TOV) because ESPN logs ~3.6 team offensive rebounds a team-game that continue a possession and the estimate only subtracts player rebounds; with them subtracted the count is 1.2-1.4 above it (the 0.44). **Sniff test against ESPN's Hollinger team stats** (espn.com/nba/hollinger/teamstats/_/year/2021 and /2025, read 2026-10-01; box-score estimates): pace r 0.98 (2020-21) and 0.97 (2024-25), offensive efficiency r 0.997 / 0.993, defensive 0.990 / 0.995; our pace 1.5-1.7 lower and efficiency 1.9-2.1 points per 100 higher (the team-rebound effect); fastest team Washington in both for 2020-21, best offence Brooklyn (2020-21) and Cleveland (2024-25) in both. League points per possession by start (all seasons): after a steal 1.31, after a defensive rebound 1.16, after a made shot 1.11, after a made free throw 1.10, after a dead-ball turnover 1.09; second-chance points 13.8 a team-game. `possession_seasons` (2,033 rows) has these per season × team (and `ALL`) × start type (and `all`), with the same allowed. Rerun after `build_lineup_stints.py` or a `player_shots` reload. Data Coverage entry.
- **Possession Explorer** (round 6, step 4, 2026-10-01; Teams menu, `?page=possessions&team=&season=&start=&v=off|def&sort=&dir=`, `pages/PossessionExplorer.jsx`, charts in `common/PossessionCharts.jsx`, `styles/possessions.css`; `api/routers/possessions.py`: `GET /possessions/options`, `/possessions/league?season=`, `/possessions/team/{abbr}`, `/possessions/player/{id}?season=`; profile block `common/PossessionBlock.jsx` with `possessions.seasons` in `/player-profile/{id}`; Data Coverage entry `possessions` now links here; no Methodology card: nothing is modelled) — the possessions table above, read by how each possession began, for the league and every team, offence and defence, 2020-21 to 2025-26. League view: points per possession by start type with 95% intervals; every team ranked on a chosen start type (click a team to open it); a sortable team table (possessions a game, PPP, after a make / defensive rebound / steal / dead-ball turnover, transition share and PPP, second-chance points and points off steals a game; green/red only where the team's interval clears the league's value); transition (PPP by second of the first shot after a defensive rebound, the 7-second window shaded; transition vs settled by start type); **"is it a team trait?"** per start type (spread between teams vs the spread chance alone gives, and year-to-year r over five season pairs); the league by season. Team view: the same by start type against league ticks, plus every season. Profile block: the team's possessions with the player on the floor when they began vs off, by start type, offence and defence, with an interval on each difference (greyed under 150 possessions). Everything reads `possession_seasons` except three cached live reads: points-per-possession variance (for the intervals), transition vs settled and second-chance points allowed per season, and the player split (`possessions` ⨝ `lineup_stints`, ~0.2-0.8 s). Intervals treat possessions as independent (variance pooled by season and start type), so they run a little narrow; the page says so. Defence view names start types from the offence's side (a "steal" start is the team's own live-ball turnover). Six seasons pooled: after a steal 1.306 points a possession, after the other team scores 1.106 (+0.20), after a dead-ball turnover 1.089; after a defensive rebound 1.155. **Honest result:** a team's overall efficiency is mostly a team trait (83% of the between-team spread beyond chance on offence, year-to-year r 0.47), its efficiency after steals isn't (25%, r 0.10): 2025-26's spread from Atlanta's 1.396 to Brooklyn's 1.223 is mostly luck. Transition after a defensive rebound (2025-26): 33% of those possessions, 1.458 points vs 1.031 settled. Restart impact_api after `build_possessions.py` (every read is cached).
- **Coaching Decisions** (round 6, step 5, 2026-10-01; Teams menu, `?page=coaching&v=timeout|challenge|foul_up3|twoforone|tests&team=&season=&sort=&dir=`, `pages/CoachingDecisions.jsx`, chart `common/CoachingCharts.jsx` (`IntervalChart`), `styles/coaching.css`; `api/routers/coaching.py`: `GET /coaching/options`, `/coaching/decision/{name}?team=&season=`, `/coaching/tests`; estimator and labels shared in `api/coaching_lib.py`; `scripts/build_coaching_decisions.py` (~4 min) → `coaching_decisions`, `coaching_decision_tests`, `coaching_decision_summary`, `coaching_decision_meta`; Methodology card `coaching`; two Data Coverage entries) — four things coaches act on, each tested with round 5's popular-beliefs machinery (`paper_beliefs.py`'s permutation p, second stage and Benjamini-Hochberg, imported): the decision is compared with matched moments where the coach did the other thing, the effect is the stratified average gap on the treated, its null comes from shuffling the decision within the strata (2,000 draws, 20,000 when p ≤ 0.02), its interval from a game-clustered bootstrap. Games whose possessions reconcile (7,220 of 7,232, 2020-21 to 2025-26), every time on the corrected clock through `possessions`. **Timeouts:** the first moment a run reaches 8-0 (unanswered, same period) right after the score, the other team inbounding next, 2+ minutes left, no timeout by that team during the run; a timeout by it before its next counted play (by the log's order: ESPN stamps about one timeout in five late, checked against NBA.com's log) vs none; "Full Timeout" within 3 events of a coach's challenge is the challenge's own (5,611 of 77,867); matched on season × quarter group × time left × margin × run size; outcome net points over the next 6 possessions (also 2, 12, scoring on the next one; sensitivity: 6-0 and 10-0 runs, timeouts surely at the dead ball). **Challenges:** ESPN's outcome events ("Overturned" = won, timeout kept; "Stands" / "Supported" = lost, timeout charged; "replaycenter" = no outcome); a plain "Challenge" next to an outcome of the same team is a second record (1,485 of 1,510); outcome the challenger's win probability (Game Replay's model) from the possession in progress to the end of the next one, won vs lost, matched on season × quarter group; per team, success rate vs the league's. What was challenged is not on file (ESPN rewrites the log after an overturn). **Up 3 late:** the first possession of a game where the trailing team has the ball down 3 with ≤ 24 s in the 4th/OT; a non-shooting foul before its first shot (shared parser's event kinds) vs not; outcome the leader winning; matched on time left × regulation/OT. **2-for-1:** possessions starting 28-40 s from the end of quarters 1-3 (not those whose first event is a free throw on a non-shooting foul or technical: the defence timed those, 964); first shot / shooting foul / turnover with > 24 s left vs not (the valley of the bimodal timing); outcome net points to the end of the quarter, split into the possession itself and after it; matched on season × quarter × start time (2 s) × how it began. **Results (all six seasons):** timeout −0.13 net points over 6 possessions (95% −0.26 to +0.01, p 0.056; 3,535 vs 6,699) — no sign it stops a run, and the team scored on its next possession less often (47.0% vs 50.2%); a won challenge leaves the challenger 4.2 points of win probability better off than a lost one (3.9 to 4.5; +1.39 net points); 59.3% of 6,786 decided challenges won (48.7% in 2020-21, 64.9% in 2025-26); fouling up 3: leader won 93.5% vs 92.1% (+1.3 points, −3.5 to +5.9, p 0.57, 153 fouls in 688 situations; threes made on the possession 2.6% vs 13.5%); 2-for-1 +0.42 net points (0.32 to 0.50; +0.21 on the early possession itself, +0.21 after it). League family 2 of 4 survive; per team, timeouts 0 of 30, challenges 2 of 30 (Toronto −11 points, Memphis +10), 2-for-1 6 of 30 (all positive). Restart impact_api after rerunning the script (every read is cached).
- **Availability-aware pre-game odds and the lineup what-if** (round 6, step 6, 2026-10-01; on the Season Simulator page: the day's games table gains "With who played" and "Sat" columns and a What-if button from 2020-21, `?page=simulator&season=&asof=&game=&out=&add=` (out / add = player ids), `common/AvailabilityOdds.jsx` (`AvailabilityWhatIf`, `AvailabilitySection`), `styles/availability.css`; `api/routers/pregame_availability.py`: `GET /pregame/availability/model`, `/pregame/availability/games?date=`, `/pregame/availability/game/{game_id}?out=&add=`; math in `api/availability_lib.py`, shared with `scripts/build_pregame_availability.py` (~20 s) → `pregame_availability_odds`, `pregame_availability_players`, `pregame_availability_fit`, `pregame_availability_tests`; Methodology card `simulator` extended; two Data Coverage entries) — how much better are the pre-game odds if you know who played? Each team's **lineup strength** in a game = 5 × the minutes-weighted mean rating of its rotation players who played (players expected to play 10+ minutes), with **expected** minutes (his mean in his earlier regular-season games for the team; else that season; else last season's minutes a game; else 6.4, the tune seasons' median for no-history appearances), never the minutes he played; minutes they don't cover go to a replacement-level player and a total over 240 is scaled down. **Ratings are fixed before the season**: the Projections page's Marcel-style BPM projection (`projection_backtest_rows`, made from earlier seasons only) or last season's RAPM with prior (2021-22 on); unrated players (15% of minutes for BPM) get the minutes-weighted same-season rating of such players on the tune seasons (BPM −2.30, RAPM −1.69). The **reference** is what the team rating already knows, blended with the prior's own weight: last season's rotation (minutes per team game, this season's ratings) and this season's earlier lineups. Home deviation minus away deviation enters the pre-game model's log-odds with **one coefficient**; the pre-game model is an offset, never refitted, so the comparison is the lineup term alone. The app's odds hold each season out (coefficient fitted on the other five; 0.108 ± 0.007 all seasons, 0.105-0.114 by season: one point of lineup strength = 0.78 points of expected margin); the what-if uses the game's own season's coefficient, so with nothing changed it returns the stored odds, and its 80% range draws the coefficient and the players' ratings 4,000 times (the team ratings' own uncertainty is the same for any lineup and isn't in it). **Under round 5's protocol** (tune 2020-21 to 2023-24 with the coefficient leave-one-season-out, rating source picked on 2024-25, 2025-26 scored once; base = `paper_eval_predictions`' prior_rest) with `paper_tests`' paired bootstrap by game, sign-flip and Diebold-Mariano: log loss 0.6352 → 0.6177 (tune), 0.6046 → 0.5876 (validate), **0.5975 → 0.5802 on the test season (−0.0173, 95% −0.0276 to −0.0068, p 0.001)**; Brier −0.007 to −0.008 in every phase; RAPM gains −0.0151 / −0.0151 / −0.0138, BPM minus RAPM on the test season −0.0034 (−0.0096 to +0.0026, no real difference; BPM picked on 2024-25, 0.5876 vs 0.5896). For scale: the four pre-game forms are 0.004 apart on the test season. Most of the gain is in games with big lineup changes (|change| ≥ 6 points per 100: 817 games, 0.650 → 0.569, odds moving 18 points on average) and after 30 games; counting every player who appeared instead of rotation players changes nothing (−0.0003, −0.0012 to +0.0007; deep-bench appearances correlate 0.36 with the absolute margin, which is why they're left out). Of the 10 biggest upsets since 2020-21, 8 become less surprising (Washington at Minnesota 2025-02-01, Edwards, Randle and DiVincenzo out: 7.9% → 19.3%; Indiana at Oklahoma City 2026-01-23: 6.3% → 10.5%), one is unchanged, one more surprising (Washington at Detroit 2026-02-05: 8.8% → 6.7%). **Who played is known at tip-off, not when a forecast is usually made: every gain is an upper bound on what injury news is worth** (the page, the card and the Data Coverage entry say so). The simulator doesn't use it (future games have no lineups). Restart impact_api after rerunning the script (the model summary is cached).
- **Play Finder** (round 4, phase 5, 2026-09-29; Players menu, `?page=plays&player=&cat=&from=&to=&df=&dt=&game=&team=&opp=&home=&per=&cmin=&cmax=&mmin=&mmax=&clutch=&dmin=&dmax=&sort=`, `pages/PlayFinder.jsx`, `styles/playfinder.css`; `GET /plays/finder/options`, `GET /plays/finder` in `api/routers/play_finder.py`, categories in `api/play_finder.py` (shared with the script); `scripts/build_play_finder.py` (~75 s) → `play_finder_events`, `play_finder_games`, `play_finder_seasons`) — every play of every regular-season game 2020-21 to 2025-26 (7,229 games, 3,287,148 player-plays), searchable by player, play (made/missed 2 or 3, assist, block, steal, turnover, foul, free throw, rebound, and groups of them), season or date range, one game, team, opponent, home/away, quarter, clock left in the period, the player's team's lead before the play, clutch (wpa_lib's definition) and shot distance; sorted newest, oldest or longest shot; paged (200 a page, up to 10,000 deep). Each result shows the date, clock, player, game, play, ESPN's text, the score after the play (player's team first) and the distance, and a **Replay** link that opens Game Replay at that moment. The clock is the corrected one (`pbp_event_clock`, since 2026-10-01; every other column is unchanged, checked row by row): it moved in 80% of rows, made shots a median 14 s earlier, so end-of-period searches change most (made threes in the last 5 s of the fourth or overtime: 947 on ESPN's clock, 500 now; made shots in a period's last second 11,443 → 4,836). Built once from the shared parser (`Game.parse()`, no new name matcher), because ESPN's rows alone can't answer it: the passer, blocker and stealer are only names in the text, half of the made field goals don't say two or three, and the score fields are stale in a few hundred games. So **every player-game's made and missed shots, twos and threes, free throws, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks and turnovers equal his `player_game_lines` line (0 of 152,428 player-games differ)**, and Bam Adebayo's 83 on 2026-03-10 comes back as 20 field goals (7 threes) and 36 free throws. Two or three: makes as the parser scores them (the NBA shot chart agrees on 99.97% of matched makes); misses as the NBA shot chart calls them where the shot is matched, like the Game Log since 2026-09-29 (ESPN's text calls 8,688 of the chart's missed threes twos, median 26 ft, and 1,545 twos threes). The score before each play is reconciled to the real final (game_scores) the same way as `lineup_stints` (made shots and free throws, else ESPN's running maximum; the per-game choice agrees with `lineup_stint_games` in all 7,229 games; 7 games with bad play-by-play keep ESPN's own). Distance: the NBA shot chart's coordinates for 99.0% of shots (Rim Deterrence's order-within-game/shooter/period match), ESPN's text for most of the rest, none for 0.35%. Disclosed: team rebounds left out (not anybody's play, the `team_game_totals` convention; team turnovers and fouls kept), 10,434 rows (0.32%) name a player ESPN gives no id (shown by the name in the text, not findable by player), the three NBA Cup finals left out. Game Replay now also reads `t=` (seconds since tip-off) and `ev=` (event id) next to `game=` and marks that play on the chart (dashed line, ring and a "Linked play" line); without them nothing changed. No index was added to `pbp_events`: the finder reads its own table (partial btree on player, BRIN on game), then `pbp_events` by primary key; EXPLAIN ANALYZE: 2 ms for a player's plays, 40-110 ms for team or clutch filters over all seasons, 0.35-0.65 s for an unfiltered page. Sniff tests: Jokić sets up the most Nuggets baskets (708 in 2023-24; Murray 384); the 2020-21 to 2025-26 blocks leaders are Turner (819), Gobert (813) and Lopez (731); 2025-26's most clutch threes are Durant, Curry and Murray (15 each); the longest make on file is Ziaire Williams's 79-footer (2023-01-22). Data Coverage entry; not a model, so no Methodology card.
- **Luck & Schedule** (Teams & Markets → Luck & Schedule, `?page=analytics#luck`; `GET /teams/luck-schedule?season=&as_of=`, `/teams/luck-schedule/model`, `/teams/luck-schedule/team/{abbr}` in `api/routers/luck_schedule.py`; math shared with the script in `api/luck_lib.py`) — every team-season 2009-10 to 2025-26 from the real final score of every regular-season game (`game_scores`, `scripts/fetch_game_scores.py`, ESPN's scoreboard matched to NBA game ids; season points for/against equal Basketball-Reference's for all 510 team-seasons). Expected wins from points (Pythagorean, exponent 13.94 fitted on all team-seasons; beat a normal curve and a straight line in margin on leave-one-season-out error, 3.06 wins vs. 3.15/3.25), luck = actual − expected wins, records in games decided by ≤3 / ≤5 points and in overtime, and SRS/SOS (least-squares ratings with a home-court term per season, neutral-site games including the 2020 bubble get none). An **as-of date** (or the Halfway button) refits everything on the games before that morning, shows each team's remaining schedule strength and projects the rest (ratings shrunk toward average by the share of their spread that's noise, each game a win chance with that season's home court and game SD), next to what actually happened. Click a team for its franchise's luck in every season (NJN/BKN and NOH/NOP joined). A scatter of expected vs. actual win% for all 510 team-seasons shows the fit, and a checks table answers "does luck carry over?" (`scripts/build_luck_schedule.py` → `team_luck_schedule`, `luck_schedule_seasons`, `luck_model_fit`, `luck_schedule_validation`; the router caches them per process: restart impact_api after rerunning either script). Honest result: luck mostly doesn't carry (franchise luck correlates 0.14 with next season's, 95% interval 0.05-0.22, vs. 0.59 for margin), but the small part that repeats is clear of zero, and next season's win% correlates about equally with this season's record (0.600) and expected win% (0.605). Methodology card; smoke test.
- **Gravity Index & Spacing Lab** (`scripts/fetch_spacing_data.py` → `scripts/build_gravity_index.py`, `GET /spacing/gravity`, `GET /spacing/lineup`) — a **disclosed composite proxy** for shooting gravity (not player-tracking gravity, which needs raw defender coordinates this project doesn't have), 2013-14 through 2025-26. Three real components, each z-scored within the season against 500+ minute players and summed: 3PA per 100 possessions; catch-and-shoot 3P%; and the share of 3PA taken with a defender within 6 ft (the NBA's own closest-defender bands). The last two are shrunk with a 250-attempt prior toward the real rate of players with similar 3-point volume that season — a disclosed departure from the plan's "shrink to the league mean", made because the league-mean prior rated non-shooters as average (Rudy Gobert, 0 3PA in 2024-25, landed above a fifth of real shooters; after the change he's at the 7.5th percentile and Stephen Curry at the 99.7th). Tracking data covers 98.8–99.8% of real box-score 3PA every season. Lineup spacing = the five players' summed Gravity.
