The Spacing Lab lets you pick any five players and shows their spacing percentile among that season's real 100+-possession lineups, a half-court drawn to real court dimensions and tinted by that percentile (labeled as a visualization of the index, not player positions), whether those five really played together (and their real ORtg), and a predicted ORtg change vs. a median-spacing lineup — only when the validation is significant **and** the lineup's spacing is within the range of real lineups that season (the five highest-Gravity players sum to ~+26, far beyond any real lineup's ~+15, so that combination gets an explicit "outside real data" message instead of an extrapolated number). **Real validation:** 4,256 real 5-man lineups with 100+ possessions: weighted least squares of real ORtg on lineup spacing + the five players' summed OBPM + season fixed effects, standard errors clustered by team-season (390 clusters; `scripts/stats_lib.py`, checked by simulation — reported SE 0.0618 vs. empirical 0.0615, 96% CI coverage). Result with Basketball-Reference's OBPM (2026-09-27): +0.074 ORtg per spacing point (95% CI 0.014–0.134, p = 0.016); R² 0.3595 vs. 0.3586 without spacing — a small effect (25th→75th percentile spacing ≈ +0.4 ORtg). With the old in-house OBPM it was +0.163 (p ≈ 2e-6): better-measured lineup quality absorbed about half of what looked like a spacing effect. Sniff test: the real 2024-25 Knicks starting five scores +5.82 (81st percentile), predicted +0.3 (95% CI +0.06 to +0.6) vs. a median lineup; their real ORtg together was 117.7 over 1,939 possessions.
- **Contract Value ("Moneyball Engine")** (`scripts/load_salaries.py` → `scripts/build_contract_value.py`, `GET /contracts/value`, `GET /contracts/player/{id}`) — real production priced in dollars vs. real salary, for 2005-06 through 2016-17, 2018-19, 2019-20 and 2024-25. Fair value = max(WAR, 0) × that season's real cost per win + the real league minimum; surplus = fair value − real salary. **Cost per win** = (real salaries of the players who actually played − players × the real 0-years league minimum) ÷ (real league wins − what .200 teams would win), from real salaries, real team records (`Team Summaries.csv`) and the real CBA minimum salary (sourced per season from Larry Coon's CBA FAQ and Hoops Rumors, stored with its source in `league_minimum_salary`) — $2.08M/win in 2005-06 up to $6.11M in 2024-25, never assumed. **WAR** = VORP × 2.7 (Basketball-Reference's conversion) on Basketball-Reference's published VORP, then rescaled per season so positive WAR sums to the real wins above replacement (raw positive WAR sums to 1.25–1.31× them; scale factor 0.77–0.80, shown in the UI). With the old in-house VORP that ratio was 1.27–2.00× and priced 2024-25 SGA at a $279M "fair value", which is how the reproduction's problem was first spotted. Only players who actually played are priced (see Known real gaps for why). UI (Teams & Markets → Contract Value): the season's cost per win with its full real calculation, a salary-vs-WAR scatter with the fair-value line, Top 10 bargains (500+ minutes), Top 10 cap liabilities (paid $25M+), and a methods drawer; the Trade Analyzer also shows both traded players' real salary, WAR, fair value and surplus when the season has salary data. "Rookie-scale years" is labeled only from real draft data (first-round pick, seasons 1–4). **Checks:** salaries matched to real players for 96.8–100% of each included season's real minutes (seasons under 90% are excluded — 2017-18, at 88.2%, is missing LeBron, Durant and others in the source file). Sniff test: the biggest bargain of 2015-16 is Stephen Curry (unanimous MVP on $11.4M, +$45.1M surplus); 2019-20's top bargains after Giannis Antetokounmpo are rookie deals (Luka Dončić, Jayson Tatum, Pascal Siakam, Bam Adebayo); 2024-25's worst liabilities are Bradley Beal, Joel Embiid and Paul George.
- **Trade Impact** (Teams menu, `?page=tradeimpact`; `GET /trade/impact`, `api/routers/trade_impact.py`) — one screen for a 1-for-1 trade, before and after for both teams: projected win% and wins from the Trade Analyzer's model (minute-weighted roster net rating + TS%; players carry their own season stats, so role and minutes changes aren't simulated), starting-five spacing from the Gravity Index summed over each team's most-used real five-man lineup (the incoming player takes the outgoing player's spot, or the lowest-minute starter's spot if the outgoing player wasn't in that five; the page says which rule applied), and priced payroll and contract surplus from Contract Value (players with matched salaries who played for the team; not the cap sheet). Nothing new is estimated. Each block states its coverage (wins 2009-10 onward, spacing 2013-14 onward, payroll 2005-06 to 2016-17, 2018-19, 2019-20 and 2024-25) and shows the reason instead of zeros when a season lacks it; 2024-25 is the latest season with all three. The Spacing Lab's lineup logic moved into `spacing_lab.lineup_report()` so both endpoints share it, and Trade Analyzer and Trade Impact hand a trade to each other through link parameters (`onNavigate(page, hash, params)`). Exportable "both sides at a glance" table; Methodology card. Sniff test (2024-25): the Knicks' most-used five is their real starting five (Towns, Anunoby, Hart, Bridges, Brunson; 1,939 possessions, 117.7 ORtg), the roster model projects them at 51.4 wins (they won 51), and Josh Hart for Duncan Robinson lifts that five's spacing from the 81st to the 97th percentile (+5.96 on the index) while New York's priced payroll moves by the real salary gap (+$1.26M) and its surplus by −$45.6M (Hart +$28.6M vs. Robinson −$17.0M).
- **Game Win-Probability Replay** — replays one real game's entire real play-by-play through the same real WPA model, charting home win probability over the whole game (top 5 real plays by |WPA| marked, hover for details), plus a clickable "what if this real missed shot had gone in" counterfactual (dashed line) that recomputes the rest of the game's win probability assuming the shot's points landed and every later real play happened exactly as it did — clearly labeled as a counterfactual, not a re-simulation.
- **With vs. Without a Star** — pick a real team, season, and player: real record, win%, and average point differential split by whether that real player actually played in each real game that season, live-fetched from the NBA's own full-season game logs. Labeled explicitly as a real association, not a causal claim (other absences in the same games aren't controlled for) — a real sniff test on the 2023-24 Sixers/Embiid split (79.5% win rate with him vs. 37.2% without, across 39 and 43 real games) reproduces that season's well-documented real storyline exactly.
- **Schedule Fatigue** — real rest days, real back-to-backs, real travel miles (haversine between each real consecutive game's real arena location, `scripts/arenas.py`), and real time zones crossed for every real team-game 2010-2026 (`scripts/build_schedule_fatigue.py`, 40,696 real rows). A real win%-by-rest-bucket study (44.4% on 0 real rest days vs. 51-53% on 1-3, a clean real reproduction of the NBA's well-documented back-to-back fatigue effect) and a real team schedule-difficulty ranking by total real travel miles. Live Scores also gets real "B2B" / rest-disadvantage tags per team, computed from this same real stored schedule.
- **Referee Tendencies** — real officials (`BoxScoreSummaryV2`) matched to the real fouls/FTA/pace each of their real games produced (`LeagueGameFinder`), season-adjusted against the real league average for those same real seasons, with a real 95% confidence interval on the difference (`scripts/fetch_referee_officials.py`, `scripts/build_referee_tendencies.py`). Deliberately descriptive, not diagnostic: it compares real games-called totals to a real baseline and says nothing about cause — crew assignment, opponent style, and era are real, unmeasured confounders — and officials under 25 real games worked are explicitly flagged as a small sample rather than ranked alongside officials with a real full season on file. `fetch_referee_officials.py` is a real one-call-per-game pipeline (nba_api has no bulk officials endpoint) — resumable and safe to re-run; it skips every game_id it has already attempted, so real coverage grows each time it's run rather than needing to finish in one sitting. A **By Official / By Crew** toggle (`GET /referees/crew-tendencies`, `referee_crew_tendencies`) groups the same real games by the exact real 3-official crew that worked them (games with any other official count excluded so crew identity stays unambiguous) and runs the identical season-adjusted comparison per crew. **Honest finding, not a data gap:** real crew assignments are close to random from game to game — of 5,373 real distinct 3-official crews across 2021-2026, only 514 ever worked together more than once (max 4 real games), so the small-sample flag (below 10 real games together) applies to nearly every crew; any single crew's numbers are a curiosity about that handful of real games, not a reliable tendency estimate.

*College & Draft*
- Draft Prospect Comp Finder — ~105,000 real D1 college player-seasons (CollegeBasketballData.com), era-normalized, matched by name to real NBA rookie outcomes wherever a real match exists (~55% of NBA rookies since 2015 — international/G-League/draft-and-stash players never appear in US college data). Optional "include measurements" toggle adds real wingspan-minus-height and real standing reach (NBA Draft Combine, `scripts/fetch_draft_combine.py`, 1133 real player-measurements 2010-2026) to the comp vector, narrowing to real combine attendees only. A **"Does Length Matter?"** card real-correlates wingspan-minus-height against real career-average DBPM and real steals+blocks per 36 (n=565, Pearson r shown, scatter plot) — a real, weak-to-moderate positive correlation, not a causal claim. Real body measurements also show in the Player Comparison profile when a player has them on file.
- **College → NBA Pipeline** — every NBA pick 2013-2025 who came from a US college (613 of 615; one junior-college pick and Shaedon Sharpe, who never played a college game, are left out), linked to that college team's season in Bart Torvik's ratings (national rank, opponent-adjusted efficiency margin, NCAA tournament run), and to Basketball-Reference Win Shares in the first four NBA seasons against a draft-slot expectation (`WS4 = 17.42 − 3.78 × ln(pick)`, fitted on all 598 picks 2013-2022). Only classes through 2022 (478 college players) have four NBA seasons and get a verdict. **Finding: once you know the pick, college team strength barely matters** — +0.38 WS per +10 points of team margin, 95% bootstrap range −0.30 to +1.04, r = 0.05; every team-rank tier and tournament-run group's interval crosses zero. Picks with no US college come in at −1.41 WS vs. slot (−2.44 to −0.25), mostly the draft-and-stash effect (31% played no NBA games in four seasons vs. 5% of college picks). Also a school table (≥5 picks, with ranges) and a searchable player table with headshots. `scripts/load_college_teams.py` → `scripts/build_college_pipeline.py`, `GET /college/pipeline`, Analytics tab `#pipeline`.
- **March Madness Model** — pre-tournament odds for every NCAA bracket 2013-2026 (no 2020 tournament), from inputs known before the first tip: an opponent- and venue-adjusted scoring margin fitted here (ridge least squares on every D1-vs-D1 game before the tournament, margins capped at ±25), CollegeBasketballData.com Elo going into the tournament, and seed. Logistic regression on the difference between two teams, then 20,000 simulations of the real bracket. Five input sets were compared by leave-one-season-out backtest on 2013-2025 (801 tournament games) and the best held-out log loss was kept: **adjusted margin alone** (log loss 0.557, 70.4% of games called; seeds alone 0.581 / 69.4%; margin+Elo 0.557 / 70.8%; Elo alone 0.597 / 66.8%). Honest result: better-calibrated than seeds game by game (round-by-round log loss 0.216 vs 0.229), but **not better at finding the champion** — the champion was the model's favourite in 2 of 12 backtest seasons and in its top 4 in 7, while it was a 1 seed in 9. **2026 was held back entirely as the final test:** Michigan won; the model had them 3rd (18.9%) before tip-off; it called 71.6% of games vs 72.4% for seeds (same-seed games count as half for seeds), with log loss 0.473 vs 0.517. Torvik's end-of-season ratings are deliberately not used (they include the tournament). `scripts/fetch_cbb_games.py` → `scripts/build_ncaa_model.py`, `GET /college/madness?season=`, Analytics tab `#madness`.

![Draft Prospect Comp Finder](docs/screenshots/draft_prospects.png)

**Games** (daily, stateless, all built on the same real qualified-player pool)
- Guess the Player, Blurred Player (real headshot proxied through the backend so the URL can't just be read off the page), Higher or Lower, Trivia (5 daily questions, decoys always other real players)
- **Guess the Game** — a real completed game's real win-probability curve (from the same WPA model as Clutch WPA/Game Replay), no teams or date shown; up to 3 guesses at either team, each wrong guess revealing the next real clue (season → final margin → one team)

![Games hub](docs/screenshots/games_hub.png)

**Methodology** (`pages/Methodology.jsx`, content in `pages/methodologyContent.js`, Analytics menu) — one page for every model and index: what it answers, how it works, how it was checked and its known limits, grouped as predictive models, ratings and indexes, similarity and clustering, and descriptive studies, each linking to its tab. The award backtests (`/backtest`, `/backtest/allnba`), the win-probability validation (`/validation/wpa`) and the current MVP probabilities load live; everything else was checked on the date shown. An "Open issues" list names problems found and not yet fixed.
- **Data Coverage** (`?page=coverage`, Analytics menu next to Methodology, `pages/DataCoverage.jsx`, `GET /meta/coverage` in `api/routers/meta.py`) — every important table this app reads, in one place: a live row count and season span for each (computed with `COUNT`/`MIN`/`MAX` every time the page loads, cached per backend process, never hand-typed so they can't drift from what's actually in the database), plus a hand-maintained source and known-gap description per table (`COVERAGE_MAP` in `meta.py`, reusing the same wording as this README's Known real gaps and the Methodology page rather than inventing new claims). 25 tables tracked across Core, Shooting, Games, Teams, Models, Draft, College and Players groups, filterable by group, with a "Used by" column linking each table to the page(s) that read it. A missing table (in the map but not the database) is called out with an alert banner instead of silently showing zero. Smoke test: every table in `COVERAGE_MAP` must actually exist and return a nonzero count.

**Learn the Game** (`LearnTheGame.jsx`, `GET /learn/basics` in `api/routers/learn.py`)
- A beginner's guide in a blueprint style: the half court draws itself with its official dimensions (rule facts: 10 ft rim, 23 ft 9 in arc, 22 ft corners, 16 ft lane, free-throw line 15 ft from the backboard), how scoring works, the clock, and how to read a stat line.
- The data lesson is real: latest-season league FG% and share of shots for the five official zones, turned into points per shot (2025-26: rim 1.34, corner three 1.16, above-the-break three 1.05, paint 0.89, mid-range 0.83), which is why teams hunt layups and threes. Zones come from `shots_lib.classify_zone()` over all 218,443 real 2025-26 shots, and the page shows the classifier's measured error against the NBA's own zone totals (`zone_classifier_check`: shot counts within 1.3%, make rates within 0.3 points, 2023-24 and 2024-25).
- Also real: pace (101.8 possessions per team per game over 1,230 games, `game_team_box.poss_est`), points per team per game (≈116, summed from every player's per-game average, so labelled approximate) and the league's top scorer's real stat line.

**Dashboard**
- Awards Race cards show real seed and "won it before" streak badges — informational context from real standings/award history, never folded into the model's own probability number

---

## Architecture

```
                       ┌─────────────────────┐
                       │   React / Vite SPA   │  localhost:5173
                       └──────────┬───────────┘
                                  │ axios
         ┌────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┐
         ▼                        ▼                        ▼
┌─────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐
│   mvp_api.py     │    │ similarity_api.py│    │  impact_api.py    │
│  localhost:8000  │    │  localhost:8001  │    │  localhost:8002   │
│  awards models,  │    │  similarity,      │    │  everything else: │
│  backtests, SHAP,│    │  clustering,      │    │  games, dashboard,│
│  WPA calibration │    │  trajectory       │    │  odds, live data, │
│                  │    │                   │    │  WPA/lineups/replay│
└────────┬─────────┘    └────────┬──────────┘    └────────┬─────────┘
         └────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┘
                                  ▼
                     ┌─────────────────────────┐
                     │  PostgreSQL: nba_analytics│
                     └─────────────────────────┘
```

Each service is independent (its own CORS setup, its own DB connection pool built from the shared `db_config.py`) and can be restarted without touching the others. The frontend's `services/api.js` hardcodes which base URL each feature calls. `scripts/wpa_lib.py` is a small shared module (model loading, win-probability scoring, the clutch-time constants, a game-clock-elapsed conversion) imported by both `scripts/compute_wpa.py` and `api/impact_api.py`'s Game Replay endpoints, so there's exactly one real implementation of the win-probability math rather than two that could drift apart.

## Database schema (all tables in `nba_analytics`)

| Table | What it holds |
|---|---|
| `player_season_stats` | Core real per-player-season stat line, every season 2009-10–present |
| `all_nba_seasons`, `mvp_seasons`, `dpoy_seasons`, `roy_seasons`, `award_winners` | Real historical award ballots/winners |
| `model_backtest_summary`, `model_backtest_seasons`, `all_nba_backtest_summary`, `all_nba_backtest_seasons`, `win_model_backtest` | Leave-one-season-out backtest results (honest, historical — not live predictions) |
| `shap_explanations` | Cached SHAP breakdowns for the Random Forest award models |
| `season_similarity`, `career_similarity` | Precomputed cosine-similarity pairs |
| `player_clusters`, `cluster_archetypes` | The six broad K-Means archetypes (used by Pair Synergy, Trivia, Player Comparison) |
| `player_roles`, `role_archetypes` | Ten player roles per player-season (role, family, PCA coordinates, the 14 role features) and per role (centroid, description, most typical players, top scorers, silhouette, stability) (`build_player_roles.py`) |
| `draft_history` | Every BAA/NBA draft pick 1947-2025 (8,383), with the NBA player id where it can be matched safely and `nba_id_status` (matched / never_played / played_unmatched; placeholder negative ids for the last two). Built by `load_draft_history_bref.py` from Basketball-Reference because stats.nba.com is unreachable; `fetch_draft_history.py` (NBA's own endpoint) writes the same table when it works |
| `greats`, `greats_meta` | Greats of the Game: one row per player (group, NBA/BAA career totals and per game, Win Shares, best season, MVP/All-NBA/All-Defense/All-Star counts, Hall of Fame, has_photo, facts JSON, trivia JSON with basis data/source and source URL) and the today's-stars rule text (`build_greats.py`) |
| `player_bio` | One row per BAA/NBA player with an NBA id: position, height, weight, birth date, colleges, Hall of Fame, first/last season (Basketball-Reference; `build_player_profile_data.py`) |
| `player_awards` | Every BAA/NBA award row per player-season: MVP / DPOY / ROY / Sixth Man / MIP / Clutch POY voting (winner, vote share, finish), All-NBA / All-BAA / All-Defense / All-Rookie teams, All-Star selections (detail notes a selection he didn't play) (`build_player_profile_data.py`) |
| `player_team_stints` | Games, minutes and points per team for every multi-team (traded) season, in the order played, 1946-47 on; team codes as in `player_season_stats` (`build_player_profile_data.py`) |
| `draft_pick_outcomes` | Per pick: Win Shares in the first five NBA seasons, career Win Shares, NBA seasons/games, All-Star selections (Basketball-Reference) |
| `defense_tracking_stats` | Real defensive tracking stats (DPOY features) |
| `player_shots`, `player_shots_cache_status`, `league_shot_zones` | Live-fetched, cached shot-location data |
| `college_player_season_stats` | ~105k real D1 college player-seasons (CollegeBasketballData.com) |
| `college_team_seasons` | Bart Torvik team ratings per D1 team-season 2013-2026 (4,967 rows; end-of-season for 2013-2025, pre-tournament snapshot for 2026), plus adj. margin and national rank (`load_college_teams.py`) |
| `cbb_games` | Every men's D1 game 2013-2026 from CollegeBasketballData.com (84,460 rows: scores, neutral site, Elo at tip-off, NCAA seeds; `fetch_cbb_games.py`) |
| `ncaa_bracket_odds`, `ncaa_model_seasons`, `ncaa_model_backtest`, `ncaa_tourney_games` | March Madness model: per team-season round odds (model and seed-only title odds) with the round actually reached; per season champion rank and game/round metrics; held-out metrics per input set and season; every tournament game with the model's pre-tournament probability (`build_ncaa_model.py`) |
| `college_draft_pipeline`, `college_pipeline_meta` | NBA picks 2013-2025 linked to their last college team-season, first-four-season Win Shares and draft-slot expectation; fit coefficients and the no-college comparison group (`build_college_pipeline.py`) |
| `pbp_games`, `pbp_events` | Real play-by-play, 7,652 stored rows = 7,232 unique real games across seasons 2021-2026 — a real sampled subset from `fetch_play_by_play.py` (nba_api, `source='nba_api'`) plus real full-season coverage from `fetch_pbp_espn.py` (ESPN via sportsdataverse, `source='espn'`); both real sources verified to share the identical seconds-remaining/score-margin convention before being combined. All 420 nba_api games (2024-25) are also stored under an ESPN game_id (identical final scores; 2 are neutral-site games whose nba_api copy has `home_team` NULL) — every reader filters with `scripts/wpa_lib.py`'s `PBP_DEDUP_WHERE`, which keeps the ESPN copy |
| `player_game_lines` | One row per player per regular-season game 2020-21 to 2025-26 (152k rows), rebuilt from ESPN play-by-play: points, FGM/FGA, 3PM/3PA (a missed shot's two or three from the NBA shot chart where matched), FTM/FTA, OREB/DREB, AST, STL, BLK, TOV, seconds played (from substitutions) and team/opponent FGM/FGA/FTA/OREB/DREB/TOV/points while on the floor (`build_player_game_lines.py`) |
| `hot_streak_persistence` | 42 rows (14 stats x 5/10/20-game windows): share of a run's gap from baseline that carried into the next N games (slope, bootstrap 95% range, intercept; also season-only-baseline versions), the previous-season weight chosen on 2023-24, held-out 2024-25/2025-26 errors of the rule vs. baseline-only and last-N-only, and what Stat Stability's reliability would predict. `scripts/build_hot_streak_persistence.py` (~20 s); rerun after rebuilding `player_game_lines`. |
| `player_situational_splits`, `situational_split_league` | Situational Splits (`build_situational_splits.py`): per player, season (2020-21 to 2025-26), split (home, rest, travel, opp) and stat, games/minutes/denominator and value on each side, gap, game-clustered SE and 95% interval, league gap, vs. league, z, `qualified` (120,967 rows, 73,306 qualified; rows need 3+ games a side). Per season (0 = pooled), split and stat: the league gap with team-season bootstrap interval, within-player fixed-effects fit with and without home/away, home share of each side, standouts vs. the shuffled-games count, year-to-year r (308 rows) |
| `team_game_totals` | One row per team-game 2020-21 to 2025-26 (14,464 rows) from the same ESPN play-by-play as `player_game_lines`: opponent, home/away, win, points for/against, FGA/FTA/OREB/TOV for both sides (OREB = player rebounds only, TOV includes team turnovers, matching the lines' credit rules), possessions, game seconds incl. overtime, and the share of player-seconds the rebuilt lineups tracked (`build_player_on_off.py`) |
| `player_on_off`, `player_on_off_seasons` | On/off per player-season-team (3,667 rows): games, minutes and possessions on and off the floor, points for/against on and off, ORtg/DRtg/net on and off, on − off for each, bootstrap SE and 95% interval for on − off net (2,000 game resamples), SE of on-court net, usage from the lines; per season the tracked share, league possession-weighted on-court net, league ORtg and how many 500-minute players' intervals exclude zero (`build_player_on_off.py`) |
| `stat_stability`, `stat_stability_curve`, `stat_year_to_year` | Stat Stability: per catalogue stat (and per-minute variant) the split-half sample for 0.5 reliability with bootstrap range, the per-sample-size curve, typical-season reliability and the scale to season-table units; year-to-year correlation per stat (`build_stat_stability.py`) |
| `aging_curves`, `aging_curve_summary`, `aging_league_average` | Aging Curves: per stat, era and age the chained delta-method level (vs. league average), change vs. age 27 with bootstrap 95% range, pairs, and survivors (qualified seasons at that age, share with another qualified season, leavers vs. stayers); per stat and era the peak age with its range, pairs, players and floors; each stat's weighted league average per season (`build_aging_curves.py`) |
| `player_projections`, `projection_backtest`, `projection_backtest_rows`, `projection_ranges`, `projection_stats` | Next-season projections: per current player and stat the Marcel-style projection with its 80% range, own-numbers weight, sample, seasons used, last value and age step; the backtest per stat and target season (season 0 = all pooled: n, MAE of the projection / last season / league average / weighted average / no age step, bias, slope, r, 80% coverage); every backtest projection next to its actual; the error percentiles per reliability and level bin (−1 = every bin); the catalogue with each stat's regression sample and its source (`build_projections.py`) |
| `player_wpa_totals` | Aggregated real Win Probability Added per player (`compute_wpa.py` output), plus the clutch/non-clutch split (leverage-neutral points per scoring chance, clutch lift and its game-clustered SE) |
| `wpa_clutch_league` | One row: the league's leverage-neutral points per scoring chance in and out of clutch time, and the clutch leverage ratio (`compute_wpa.py`) |
| `wpa_model_validation` | Real calibration validation (Brier/log-loss/reliability bins) for the WPA model, two scopes: all events and clutch-only |
| `prediction_ledger` | Real live-logged MVP/DPOY/ROY/All-NBA predictions, timestamped, graded once real outcomes exist (`snapshot_predictions.py` / `resolve_predictions.py`) |
| `draft_combine` | Real NBA Draft Combine measurements, 2010-2026 (`fetch_draft_combine.py`) — NULL for any test a player skipped, no row at all for players who never attended |
| `pair_synergy_validation` | Real season-grouped cross-validated R² for the 2-man pair-synergy ridge model (`train_pair_synergy.py`) |
| `team_game_fatigue` | Real per-team-game rest/B2B/travel-miles/timezone data, 2010-2026, 40,696 rows (`build_schedule_fatigue.py`) |
| `team_seasons` | One row per team-season 1946-47 to 2025-26 plus each season's league average (1,804 rows) from Basketball-Reference's team summaries/per-game files in the local Kaggle export (`scripts/build_team_seasons.py`): record, Pythagorean W-L, MOV, SOS, SRS, ORtg/DRtg/NRtg, pace, four factors both ends, age, arena, attendance, playoffs; `abbreviation` = the code the rest of the database uses that season, `bref_abbreviation`, `franchise` (`api/teams_lib.py`). W-L equals `team_game_fatigue`'s for all 510 team-seasons 2009-10 on, and every code in the player tables has a row |
| `team_zone_mix` | Shot attempts/makes per team-season, zone and side (`team` / `opponent`), 1996-97 to 2025-26 (`scripts/build_team_zone_mix.py`, ~15 s): each shot placed on a team game by game (game teams from `team_game_fatigue` 2009-10 on, else the shooters' teams; a player's team = the game team he appears with most that season). 99.98% of 5.93M regular-season shots placed, agreeing with the play-by-play's team in 99.82% of player-games 2020-21 on; 97.3-100% of Basketball-Reference's team and opponent FGA every team-season |
| `game_scores` | Real final score of every regular-season game 2009-10 to 2025-26, one row per team-game (40,696 rows, same rows and abbreviations as `team_game_fatigue`): points for/against, periods, neutral-site flag, ESPN id (`scripts/fetch_game_scores.py`, ESPN scoreboard, ~7 min). Season totals equal Basketball-Reference's for all 510 team-seasons. Every per-game margin in the app comes from it (Luck & Schedule, team page, Game Log/Finder, Schedule Fatigue, Team Comparison, Situational Splits' opponent ranks), never from `team_game_fatigue.plus_minus` |
| `team_luck_schedule`, `luck_schedule_seasons`, `luck_model_fit`, `luck_schedule_validation` | Luck & Schedule (`scripts/build_luck_schedule.py`, ~1 s): per team-season record, points, expected wins, luck, close-game/OT records, SRS, SOS (510 rows); per season home court, game SD, close-game share, halfway date; the three expected-win curves with leave-one-season-out errors; the carry-over, mid-season and Basketball-Reference checks |
| `game_pregame_odds` | P(home wins) for every regular-season game 2010-11 to 2025-26 (19,118 rows, `scripts/build_season_sim.py`): the held-out probability under the form used (`p_home`) and under every form (`p_baseline`, `p_current`, `p_prior`, `p_prior_rest`), the as-of ratings, home court, expected margin, both back-to-back flags, games played so far, the result and score |
| `ledger_forecasts` | Forecast Ledger (`scripts/ledger_lock.py --lock`, once per season before its first tip; kind `ledger`, never rebuilt): one row per forecast (`as_is`, `roster`) × team (prior mean and SD, win totals with 10th/50th/90th percentiles, playoff / top-6 / play-in / first-seed / round / title odds) and per forecast × scheduled game (P(home wins) and expected margin from opening-day ratings); every row stamped `locked_at` (2027: 2,460 rows) |
| `ledger_results` | Forecast Ledger live scoring (`scripts/ledger_update.py`, each morning; kind `ledger`, LIVE: counts grow between manifests): ESPN's status and final score of every 2026-27 regular-season event, `counts` (both teams known, not the NBA Cup Championship), `in_lock`, `first_seen_at` / `final_seen_at` (2027: 1,206 events) |
| `ledger_game_log` | Append-only: one row per game × version (`as_is`, `roster`, `record`) × computation, with `computed_at`, `before_tip`, ratings, expected margin, P(home wins), `games_used`, `last_result_date` (always before the game's date) and `results_digest`; empty until 2026-10-20 |
| `ledger_team_log` | Per morning × forecast × team: record, in-season rating and SD, expected final wins |
| `ledger_tests` | Paired tests of the scored versions (`paper_tests` columns + `as_of`), replaced per morning, from 100 games on |
| `ledger_runs` | One row per `ledger_update.py` run: what was read, logged and waited for |
| `ledger_schedule`, `ledger_rosters` | ESPN's regular-season schedule (1,206 events, 1,200 with both teams) and 30 rosters (607 players, matched NBA id, Marcel projection, minutes the depth chart gave, injury status) exactly as read at lock time |
| `ledger_hindcast`, `ledger_meta`, `ledger_lock` | The 2010-11 to 2025-26 roster hindcast per team-season (480 rows); every locked parameter, rule text, check and source (`key`/`value`/`note`); the SHA-256 of the canonical CSV of the five tables above, its size, lock time, first tip, code commit and tag |
| `pregame_model_fit`, `pregame_calibration`, `pregame_model_seasons` | The four pre-game forms (coefficients, held-out log loss / Brier / favourite win rate, chosen), predicted vs actual by decile per form, held-out scores per form per season |
| `season_postseason`, `season_sim_seasons`, `season_sim_params` | Per team-season 2009-10 on: conference, play-in, playoffs, top-6, final record and finish by the simulator's ranking rule (510 rows); per season: dates, halfway and 60-game checkpoints, last season's home court and game SD, notes; the prior constants, run count and chosen form |
| `season_sim_backtest`, `season_sim_backtest_summary`, `season_sim_calibration` | Every season 2010-11 on simulated at opening day, the halfway date and 60 games in (3,360 rows: per team the odds and what happened, for the model and the two baselines); Brier / log loss / win error / 80% coverage per checkpoint and method; playoff and top-6 calibration bins |
| `postseason_games` | Every play-in and playoff game 2009-10 to 2025-26 from ESPN's scoreboard (1,458 rows, `scripts/fetch_postseason_games.py`): stage, round, conference, home/away, score, winner, ESPN's headline |
| `player_playtypes` | Real per-player-season play-type frequency/PPP/percentile, NBA Synergy tracking, 2013-2026, 43,681 rows (`fetch_playtypes.py`) — mid-season-traded players poss-weighted-averaged across teams |
| `player_hustle` | Real per-player-season hustle stats (deflections, contested shots, screen assists, loose balls, charges, box outs), 2016-2026, 5,602 rows (`fetch_hustle_stats.py`) |
| `playtype_clusters`, `playtype_cluster_archetypes` | K-Means "Offensive Style" cluster assignments and labels, from real play-type frequency mix (`cluster_playtypes.py`) |
| `player_matchups` | Real player-vs-player defensive matchup data (partial possessions, FG% allowed, etc.), 2017-18–2025-26, 571,608 rows (`fetch_matchups.py`) — rows below 5 real partial possessions dropped at fetch time as noise |
| `game_team_box` | Real per-team-game FTA/PF/FGA/OREB/TOV plus a derived possessions estimate, seasons 2021-2026 (`fetch_referee_officials.py`, bulk via `LeagueGameFinder`) |
| `game_officials`, `game_officials_fetch_log` | Real official assignments per real game (`BoxScoreSummaryV2`, one real call per game) and a fetch-attempt log so re-runs skip games already tried |
| `referee_tendencies` | Real per-official fouls/FTA/pace averages vs. the real season league average, with a real 95% CI and small-n warning (`build_referee_tendencies.py`) |
| `referee_crew_tendencies` | Same real comparison as `referee_tendencies`, grouped by the exact real 3-official crew instead of one official; 5,373 real distinct crews, almost all small-sample by construction (`build_referee_tendencies.py`) |
| `leverage_index_grid` | Leverage Index on a regular (minute of regulation, margin −25..25) lattice, for display (`build_leverage_splits.py`) |
| `player_leverage_splits` | Real per-player-season stats (pts, FG/3P/FT made-attempted, reb, ast, tov, leverage-weighted pts) split by leverage bucket, 2020-21–2025-26 |
| `player_leverage_summary` | Real per-player-season raw/ex-garbage/filtered PPG, True Production Ratio, leverage-weighted PPG, garbage/high shares, percentile + stat-padding flag, official PPG/GP for comparison |
| `leverage_validation` | Per-season real attribution rates, bucket shares, clutch-vs-garbage overlap count, rebuilt-vs-official PPG r/MAE, 3PA-rule accuracy |
| `defender_dfg` | Real per-defender-season defended FG% vs. the shooters' normal FG% (`LeagueDashPtDefend`, 2017-18–2025-26, 4,968 rows; mid-season-traded players' stints combined exactly — `fetch_defend_dashboard.py`) |
| `defender_dad` | DAD Index per defender-season: DAD, season z, within-position z, qualified flag, replacement share, top-3 real assignments (JSON), joined DFG% differential (`build_dad_index.py`) |
| `dad_validation` | Per-season year-over-year DAD stability r, DAD-vs-DFG% r, qualified counts, replacement possession share |
| `scouting_splits` | Every real split tested for every qualified player-season (zone FG% / play-type PPP / high-leverage FG%): value, baseline, n, z, p, significant, direction (`build_scouting_reports.py`) |
| `scouting_validation` | Next-season persistence of real findings per category (same-direction and significant-again rates) |
| `zone_classifier_check` | The shot-zone classifier's real league zone totals vs. the NBA's own `league_shot_zones` (2023-24, 2024-25) |
| `league_zone_mix` | League shot attempts and makes per season by the five zones, regular season only, 1996-97 to 2025-26, 150 rows (`build_league_zone_mix.py`, zones from `shots_lib.classify_zone`; rerun after reloading `player_shots`) |
| `player_shot_making`, `shot_making_league`, `shot_making_validation` | Expected FG% / shot-making per player-season (14,513 rows, 9,026 with 200+ FGA, 1996-97 to 2025-26), the league's actual vs. expected eFG% per season (30 rows) and the model check (held-out and cross-fit log loss, Brier, ROC-AUC, reliability bins, year-to-year stability; `build_shot_making.py`, ~5 min, rerun after reloading `player_shots`) |
| `player_shot_hex` | Quality-map cells per qualified player-season (9,026 rows, 1,542,816 cells, 15 MB, `build_shot_making.py`): player_id, season, and parallel arrays `cells` (hexagon id, `api/shot_hex.py`), `fga`, `fgm`, `xm` (expected makes) per cell, plus `off_fga`/`off_fgm`/`off_xm` for shots in no cell (beyond half court; through 2009-10 the unlocated ones at (0, 0)) |
| `shot_hex_league`, `shot_hex_meta` | The league's attempts, makes and expected makes per season and hexagon (18,774 rows, 1.3 MB), and the grid constants (size, half-court limit, id packing, last unlocated season, attempts floor) |
| `player_shot_tracking` | Real per-player-season catch-and-shoot 3PM/3PA and 3PA by closest-defender band (0-2 / 2-4 / 4-6 / 6+ ft), 2013-14–2025-26 (`fetch_spacing_data.py`, league-wide `LeagueDashPlayerPtShot`, 5 calls per season) |
| `lineup_stats` | Real 5-man lineups per season (possessions, off/def/net rating, player ids), 2013-14–2025-26 — the endpoint's 2,000 most-used lineups per season, which always includes every lineup with 100+ possessions (checked: the 2,000th has ~20). Since 2026-09-28 used only before 2020-21 |
| `lineup_stints` | Every five-on-five stint of every regular-season game 2020-21–2025-26 from ESPN play-by-play (`scripts/build_lineup_stints.py`): both fives, seconds since tip-off, per-side points / shooting and possession components / possessions, running score, event range, `tracked_ok` (294,772 rows, 98 MB) |
| `lineup_stint_games` | One row per game: reconciliation against `game_scores` (points), `game_scores.periods` (seconds) and `team_game_totals` (possession components), which point-crediting method was used, incomplete-lineup minutes, `game_ok` / `tracked_ok` and the reason |
| `lineup_stint_seasons` | Per season: games, reconciled and fully tracked games, stints, tracked share of minutes |
| `player_game_onfloor` | One row per player-game 2020-21 on (152,461, 22 MB, `scripts/build_player_game_onfloor.py`): seconds on the floor, seconds in fully tracked stints, team / opponent points while on the floor and on-court plus-minus (free throws credited to the lineup at the foul, as the box score does), `game_ok` from `lineup_stint_games` |
| `player_game_onfloor_meta` | The build's checks (stint reproduction, 5 × margin identity, row set vs `player_game_lines`) and the free-throw rule |
| `possessions` | Every possession of every regular-season game 2020-21–2025-26 (1,442,608, 281 MB; `scripts/build_possessions.py`, rules in `scripts/pbp_possessions.py`): key (`game_id`, `poss_no`), offence/defence, `start_type`/`end_type`, start/end seconds since tip-off on the rebuilt clock, `clock_ok`, `first_attempt_sec`, `transition` (NULL after turnovers), event range, `stint_no`/`stint_no_end` (join `lineup_stints` on game + stint for the ten players), score at the start, points and box components, team offensive rebounds, second-chance and technical points, `and_one`, `empty`, `tracked_ok` |
| `possession_games` | One row per game: possessions per side against the box-score estimate and team offensive rebounds, points method, reconciliation (`points_ok`, `comp_ok`, `stints_ok`, `game_ok`), possessions spanning two stints, starts with no logged ending, the share of shots on the chart's clock, parser oddities, reason |
| `possession_seasons` | Per season × team (`ALL` = league) × start type (`all` = every start), from reconciled games: possessions, points, points per possession, average length, timed / transition possessions and points, possessions with an offensive rebound, second-chance points, FGA/3PA/FTA/TOV, and the same allowed (`d_*`) |
| `possession_meta` | Key/JSON: the rules and constants, the clock check against NBA.com's play-by-play (per event class, odd/even halves; copied from `pbp_event_clock_meta`), the transition-window check, parser counts |
| `coaching_decisions` | One row per decision point of Coaching Decisions (40,520; `scripts/build_coaching_decisions.py`): decision (`timeout`, `challenge`, `foul_up3`, `twoforone`), game, the deciding team and its opponent, home, seconds left in the period, margin and win probability (Game Replay's model) for the decider, the value of one point there (`lev`), pre-game expected margin (balance check), `stratum` / `team_stratum`, `treated`, `outcome` … `outcome_d` (meaning per decision in the script's docstring), `detail`, run size, the anchor event, `later` (a control that called a timeout later), `flag` (a timeout within 2 s of the score on the corrected clock) |
| `coaching_decision_tests` | One row per test (league / season / team; 124): matched effect, game-clustered bootstrap interval, treated and matched control means, the permutation null's mean / SD / 2.5-97.5%, p, draws, Benjamini-Hochberg q and flag (families only) |
| `coaching_decision_summary` | Per family: tests, p < 0.05, 0.05 n, survivors at FDR 5% (`coaching:league`, `timeout:team`, `twoforone:team`, `challenge:team`) |
| `coaching_decision_meta` | Key / value / note: parameters, every exclusion count, balance checks (treated minus matched control on pre-game margin, margin, time left), challenge counts by outcome, runtime |
| `pregame_availability_odds` | One row per regular-season game 2020-21 on (7,230, 2.5 MB; `scripts/build_pregame_availability.py`): the app's base odds (`p_base` = `game_pregame_odds.p_home`), per rating source (`bpm`, `rapm`) the lineup feature `avail_*`, both sides' lineup strength `s_*` and reference `ref_*`, rotation players counted, the held-out odds `p_*`; the every-appearance check (`*_all`); missing rotation minutes per side, minutes by players ESPN gave no id; `phase` and the protocol's odds (`p_eval_base` = paper_eval's prior_rest, `p_eval_bpm`, `p_eval_rapm`, `_all`); `lineups_ok` (false for CHI-LAC 2026-01-20 only) |
| `pregame_availability_players` | Per game and side, the rotation players who played and those who sat while still on the team (185,758 rows, 34 MB): expected minutes and where they came from (`team` / `season` / `last_season` / `default`), minutes played, both ratings with their SD and whether the player is rated (else replacement level); read by the what-if |
| `pregame_availability_fit` | Long table (kind / source / phase / season / name / value / se / n / detail): constants from the tune seasons, the lineup coefficient per held-out season and phase, metrics per phase, the rating-source choice (on 2024-25), breakdowns by games played and size of the lineup change, checks, the 10 biggest upsets with who sat |
| `pregame_availability_tests` | `paper_eval_tests`' columns: per phase, log loss and Brier of each lineup version vs the pre-game model, BPM vs RAPM, every-appearance vs rotation, with paired cluster-bootstrap intervals (10,000 resamples), sign-flip and Diebold-Mariano p |
| `lineup_predictor_units` | One row per five-man lineup × team × season, 2021-22 to 2025-26 (78,205, 26 MB with indexes; `scripts/build_lineup_predictor.py`): `player_ids` (sorted), phase, first date and team game number, `later` (first used after game 20), games, possessions and points for/against, net rating, weight, noise variance, the features (three rating sums and unrated counts, spacing, role counts and flags, usage, season so far at the chosen K) and every model's prediction made before the first game (`pred_*`) |
| `lineup_predictor_players` | Per team-season player who was in a lineup (3,073): the three pre-season ratings, Gravity (season before), projected usage, each filled at replacement level when unknown (`*_known`), role family (season before), on-court possessions and points for this team; read by the panel |
| `lineup_predictor_teams` | Per team-season: games, last date, possessions and points for/against (the panel's "whole season") |
| `lineup_predictor_fit` | Long table (fit_on `tune` / `tune_validate` / `app`, model, name, value, detail): intercepts and coefficients (with the standardisation), each model's chosen penalty / K / rating source with every CV candidate, constants (replacement levels, per-season points-per-possession variance, the panel's residual SDs, the split-half noise check), protocol meta |
| `lineup_predictor_metrics` | Per model × phase × subset (`later` / `all`): lineups, possessions, weighted MSE, mean noise, weighted variance, real variance, real RMSE, share of the real spread explained (`r2_true`) |
| `lineup_predictor_tests` | `paper_eval_tests`' columns, task `lineup`: per phase and subset, `r2_true` of every model with a 95% interval, and 11 paired comparisons in weighted MSE (bootstrap and sign-flip p) and in `r2_true`; clusters team-seasons, 10,000 resamples, seeds from the row key |
| `report_card_units` | One row per scored unit of the Model Report Card (`scripts/build_report_card.py`): task (`pregame` / `sim_playoffs` / `sim_top6` / `sim_wins` / `impact_next`), model, variant (simulator checkpoint), target season, unit (game id or season-team), pred, actual, lo/hi (80% win range), date (161,959, 26 MB) |
| `report_card_game_sums` | Per game for the per-possession (`impact_poss`: possessions, sum of squared errors) and shot (`xfg`: shots, Brier sum, log-loss sum) tasks (95,753) |
| `report_card_tests` | Per task × metric × variant × season: every model's score with a 95% cluster-bootstrap interval and SE (model_b = ''), and every pair's difference with interval, SE, bootstrap p and sign-flip p (2,072) |
| `report_card_pooled` | Per task × metric × variant × pair: DerSimonian-Laird random-effects pooled difference, Hartung-Knapp interval and p, τ², τ, I², Q p, prediction interval for next season, seasons each model was ahead (and clearly), flips (260) |
| `report_card_choices` | Every choice per target season (constants, coefficients, the chosen form, λ, prior scales, BPM/on-off scales, the Rating Tracker's five settings, the possession level factor, the shot baselines): value, seasons it was chosen on, criterion, candidates (210) |
| `report_card_meta` | The design, the checks against paper_eval / rating_tracker_fit / shot_value_validation, the run |
| `data_quality_game_flags` | One row per ESPN game 2020-21 on (`scripts/build_data_quality.py`, 7,232): ids, teams, the size of every per-game error class of the data-quality audit (wrong-player and tag/text events, unidentified events and untracked seconds, team-less substitutions and over-credited player-games, stale/backward score fields, last score ≠ final, missed-three calls, chart attempts / matched / missing, clock events outside their period, shots 5+ s from the chart and the game's median offset, unreconciled + reason, zero-distance threes, plus-minus field ≠ margin), `classes` (text[], `data_quality_lib.PER_GAME`'s rules), `n_classes`, `level` (excluded / flagged / worked_around / clean) |
| `data_quality_sensitivity` | `paper_eval_tests`' columns behind result (impact / possessions / availability), drop_set, scope (everywhere / scoring), games_dropped, games_in_scope: every model's score and every pair's difference per phase with the paired bootstrap, and the random-drop control on each difference (`rand_draws`, `rand_lo`, `rand_hi`, `rand_p`) |
| `data_quality_meta` | key → JSON: the audit check, every live check's agreement, level and flag rules, each result's drop sets with sizes, the hyperparameters held fixed, the reproduction checks, the run |
| `pbp_event_clock` | The corrected game clock for every ESPN play-by-play event (3,401,830 rows, 273 MB; `scripts/build_event_clock.py`, ~1.5 min, rules in `pbp_possessions.corrected_clock()`): key `event_id` (= `pbp_events.id`), `period_t` (seconds into the period, what scripts pass to `Game(..., clock=)` via `load_espn(conn, clock=True)` + `game_clock()`), `seconds_remaining` (pbp_events' convention, what SQL readers use), `source` (chart / ft_trip / rebound / lag / espn), `anchored`, `bounded`. Read by possessions, Rotations' closing stretch, the Play Finder (and so Best Games) and Game Replay |
| `pbp_event_clock_meta` | Key/JSON: lag constants, per-season counts by source, the check against NBA.com's play-by-play of the 418 twin games, and the win-probability check (Game Replay's model on ESPN's vs the corrected times) |
| `lineup_seasons` | Per team-season five-man lineup from tracked stints: games, stints, minutes, possessions, points, ORtg/DRtg/net (114,496 rows) |
| `pair_seasons` | The same per pair of teammates (29,125 rows) |
| `player_rapm` | RAPM per version (`single` / `multi` / `prior`), season and player (9,088 rows, `scripts/build_rapm.py`): teams, games, minutes, possessions by end, ORAPM / DRAPM / RAPM with bootstrap SEs and a 95% interval, the prior used, BPM for comparison, `qualified` (1,000+ possessions) |
| `rapm_fits`, `rapm_lambda_cv`, `rapm_validation` | Per version-season: λ, prior scale, `lambda_rule`, cross-validated error vs everyone-average, cross-validation's own minimum, season intercepts, home edge, sizes (16 rows); the full cross-validation curve (680 rows); held-out-games, next-season and year-to-year tests of RAPM vs BPM vs on/off vs everyone-average (92 rows) |
| `rotation_closing_games` | One row per game 2020-21 on (7,232, `scripts/build_rotations.py`): score and margin at 5:00 left in the fourth, `close_game` (within 5), overtimes, final, closing-stretch seconds, `game_ok`, `matches_stints` (the cut stints glue back into `lineup_stints`' stints: fives, counts, points), `stint_shift` (largest move of a stint boundary against `lineup_stints`' ESPN times), `empty_stints_skipped`; on the corrected clock since 2026-10-01 |
| `rotation_closing_stints` | Every stint piece in the closing stretch (last 5:00 of the fourth and overtime; 37,849 rows; corrected clock): both fives, per-side points and possessions, score at the piece's start |
| `rim_deterrence` | Per player-season-team 2020-21 on (3,646 rows, `scripts/build_rim_deterrence.py`): minutes/games/possessions on and off, opponents' attempts and makes per distance band (rim, 4-9, 10-15, 16 ft-arc, 3PT, unknown) on and off, rim attempts/100, rim FG%, rim points/100, rim share and opponents' FG% on/off/diff, bootstrap SE and 95% interval for the three rim differences, his own blocks |
| `assist_pairs` | Per season, team, passer and scorer 2020-21 on (40,681 rows, `scripts/build_assist_network.py`): assists, games with one, assisted 2s and 3s, points on them, assists by shot kind (rim / floater / jumper; threes = `ast3`); `scorer_id` 0 = a scorer with no id |
| `player_assisted_share` | Per player-season-team (3,667 rows): minutes and games (from `player_game_lines`), made FG / 2s / 3s and how many were assisted, the same by shot kind, assisted makes from a passer with no id, his assists, points on them and assisted threes given |
| `assist_seasons` | Per season (6 rows): league made shots and assisted shares by kind, unidentified passers/scorers, dropped data errors, pairs, and the checks (player-seasons whose assists differ from `player_game_lines`: 0; parser assists ÷ NBA.com's) |
| `play_finder_events` | One row per player per play, every regular-season game 2020-21 on (3,287,148 rows, 201 MB with indexes, `scripts/build_play_finder.py`): `pbp_events.id`, player_id (NULL = team play or a name with no id), game_no, cat (`api/play_finder.CATS`: made/missed 2 or 3, assist on a 2/3, block, steal, turnover, foul, made/missed free throw, offensive/defensive rebound), period, clock (tenths of a second left in the period), score_for/score_against before the play (player's team's side, reconciled to the real final), dist (feet, shot rows), is_home. Partial btree on (player_id, game_no), BRIN on game_no |
| `play_finder_games` | The 7,229 games behind it: game_no (date order), ESPN and NBA ids, season, date, teams, real final, score source (`shots`/`score`) and score_ok, plays |
| `play_finder_seasons` | Per season: games, rows, shot distances by source (coordinates / text / none), misses retyped by the NBA shot type, unidentified rows, and the check against `player_game_lines` (player-games checked, differing, differing on 3PA only) |
| `best_games` | One row per regular-season game 2020-21 on (7,229 rows, ~1.9 MB with indexes, `scripts/build_best_games.py`): ESPN and NBA ids, season, date, teams, final, periods, events, score_ok (7 false: left out of rankings), swing, lead_changes, ties, largest_lead, comeback, win_min_wp, final_margin, excitement, and the play that moved win probability most (peak_event_id = `pbp_events.id`, peak_t seconds since tip, peak_dwp) |
| `best_games_meta` | The excitement formula's weights and how much they matter (rank correlation and top-50 overlap with the plain swing), game counts |
| `rim_deterrence_seasons` | Per season (6 rows): league attempts, makes and rate per band, how many attempts each distance source placed (coordinates / text / rule / unknown / three), matched-to-shot-chart count, the no-distance rule's accuracy on matched shots, stint-mapping check, qualified players and how many intervals clear zero |
| `player_gravity` | Gravity components, shrunk rates, z-scores, Gravity and OBPM used, per player-season (`build_gravity_index.py`) |
| `gravity_validation` | Lineup ORtg regression results (coefficient, clustered SE/CI/p, R² with and without spacing, spacing percentiles) |
| `gravity_tracking_coverage` | Real tracked 3PA vs. real box-score 3PA per season |
| `player_shot_context` | Real per-player-season 2PT/3PT makes/attempts by closest-defender band, touch-time band and dribble band, 2013-14–2025-26 (`fetch_shot_context.py`, league-wide `LeagueDashPlayerPtShot`, 12 calls per season) |
| `player_salaries` | Real per-player salaries loaded from the local salary CSVs (seasons in scope only), with the matched `player_id` and how it matched (exact / fuzzy / ambiguous / unmatched) (`load_salaries.py`) |
| `league_minimum_salary` | Real 0-years-of-service league minimum per season, with its citation |
| `contract_value` | Per player-season: real salary, VORP, raw and calibrated WAR, fair value, surplus, rookie-scale flag (`build_contract_value.py`) |
| `contract_value_seasons` | Per season: on-court payroll, league minimum, real wins/games/replacement wins, cost per win, minutes coverage, WAR scale factor, included flag |
| `paper_eval_predictions`, `paper_eval_metrics`, `paper_eval_choices` | The conference paper's evaluation protocol (round 5 step 2, `scripts/paper_eval.py`, not read by the app): every unit scored under one split (tune 2020-21 to 2023-24, validate 2024-25, test 2025-26) with its prediction and outcome (~1.95M rows, mostly the per-shot rows of the expected-FG families in 2024-25 and 2025-26; games for the impact estimators and pre-game odds, players for year-to-year reliability, team-seasons for the simulator), the summary metrics per task, phase, model, variant and season, and every hyperparameter and model choice with what it was chosen on |
| `paper_eval_tests` | The paper's significance tests (round 5 step 3, `scripts/paper_tests.py`, not read by the app): for every comparison the paper states, the paired difference of two models on the same units with its 95% paired cluster-bootstrap interval, bootstrap p, sign-flip permutation p and, for game series, a Diebold–Mariano p; plus a bootstrap interval on every single stored metric and the shot model's calibration error (559 rows, 200 kB) |
| `shot_xfg` | The shot-making model's cross-fitted P(make) per regular-season shot from 2020-21 on (1,281,583 rows, 90 MB with index; `shot_id` = `player_shots.id`, season, fold, `p_make`; `build_shot_making.py`, round 5 step 4). Read by `scripts/paper_xrapm.py`; no app page reads it yet |
| `shot_value_shots`, `shot_value_states`, `shot_value_added`, `shot_value_fit`, `shot_value_validation` | Shot Value Added (round 6 step 8, `scripts/build_shot_value.py`; Shot Charts › Shot value, RAPM › Shot-aware): per regular-season chart shot 2020-21 on its class, made, protocol game fold and three pre-game prices (`p_blind` the look-ahead-free location model, `p_lf` + the league level so far, `p_sa` + the shooter's skill; 1,281,583 rows, 111 MB); per priced season × player × class the skill carried in and after (log-odds mean and sd; 13,596 rows); per player-season the points split into skill / beyond / total for field goals and free throws, carried-in and end skills, ranks among 200+ FGA (3,399 rows); the skill model's settings per class with every optimiser start and the 30 fits' iterations; out-of-sample log loss / Brier / calibration by season and class with intervals, and year-to-year correlations (260 rows) |
| `paper_data_audit`, `paper_data_audit_classes` | The paper's data-quality audit (round 5 step 5, `scripts/paper_data_audit.py`, not read by the app): every error class of the public feeds with how it was detected, its size re-measured from the database (key, season, value; the macro and format `paper_numbers.py` prints it with) and its handling (repaired / worked around / excluded / disclosed). |
| `paper_beliefs`, `paper_beliefs_summary`, `paper_beliefs_meta` | The paper's popular-beliefs tests (round 5 step 6, `scripts/paper_beliefs.py`, not read by the app): one row per unit and family (a player's clutch lift, a player's hot-streak persistence slope per stat and window, a player-season's situational gap per split and stat, a franchise's mean luck, an official's or crew's season-adjusted fouls / FTA / pace) with its statistic, the platform's own additive number (`platform_stat`), the permutation null's mean, SD and 2.5/97.5 percentiles, the two-sided permutation p, the draws it used and the Benjamini–Hochberg q and flag (~89k rows, 19 MB); one row per family with n, the p < 0.05 count, 0.05 n, the FDR survivors and the aggregate (league slope / null centre / null-centred share; the luck persistence r and its null); key/value meta (seeds, draws, the checks against the stored platform tables, runtime). |
| `paper_ablation_predictions`, `paper_ablation_shot_games`, `paper_ablation_metrics`, `paper_ablation_tests`, `paper_ablation_meta` | The paper's ablations (round 5 step 7, `scripts/paper_ablations.py`, not read by the app): one row per scored unit of every ablated model and of the full model it is compared with (next-season games and year-to-year player pairs for RAPM, games for the pre-game model, team-seasons for the simulator at the halfway date; 437,955 rows, 54 MB, no primary key: the test checks the key is unique); per (ablation, season, game) shot counts and summed log loss / Brier / P(make) / makes for the expected-FG ablations (17,192 rows, 3.3 MB; per-shot rows not stored); summary metrics (987 rows); paired tests against the full model in `paper_eval_tests`' columns (372 rows); key/value meta (what each ablation removes, every re-chosen λ and prior scale with its candidates, the checks that the full models reproduce `paper_eval_predictions`, the paper table's row spec). |
| `paper_xrapm_stints`, `paper_xrapm_players`, `paper_xrapm_fits`, `paper_xrapm_lambda_cv`, `paper_xrapm_meta` | Expected-points RAPM for the paper (round 5 step 4, `scripts/paper_xrapm.py`; since round 6 step 8 the RAPM page's Shot-aware version reads its `sa_*` rows): per stint and side the attempts, matched attempts, expected FG and FT points, event points, stored points, residual and expected points, plus (round 6) the look-ahead-free shooter-blind and shooter-aware targets and `fold_xpts`, the two targets recomputed with each held-out fold excluded (294,772 rows, 76 MB); platform-style fits per version and season with the actual-points RAPM beside each rating (18,954 rows: round 5's single / prior, round 6's lf_* / sa_*); λ, prior scale, cross-validated error, home edge and the correlation/spread against `player_rapm` (36 rows); the CV curve (1,836 rows); counts and constants per season (152 rows) |

## Tech stack

- **Backend:** Python, FastAPI, psycopg2, pandas, scikit-learn, scipy, `nba_api`, requests, python-dotenv, pytest + httpx (smoke tests)
- **Database:** PostgreSQL (`nba_analytics`)
- **Frontend:** React 19, Vite, Framer Motion, axios, Lenis (smooth scroll, landing page only), three.js (landing page's 3D shot court only, lazy-loaded), Observable Plot 0.6.17 (ISC; Workbench chart blocks only, lazy-loaded with them) — outside the Workbench no charting library; every chart (radar profiles, ROC curves, reliability diagrams, win-probability replay, trajectory cones, archetype scatter plots) is a hand-built SVG component
- **Models & statistics:** Logistic Regression (MVP/DPOY/ROY, ROC/SHAP-validated; win-probability model, isotonic-calibrated), Gradient Boosting comparisons, K-Means clustering (archetypes, offensive styles), cosine similarity (season/career comps), ridge regression (pair synergy), weighted least squares with team-season cluster-robust standard errors (`scripts/stats_lib.py`, Spacing Lab validation — no statsmodels dependency), two-proportion z-tests with Wilson-interval persistence checks (Exploit Guide), method-of-moments variance estimation (Synergy play types), empirical-Bayes shrinkage (Gravity)

## Data sources

Every number traces back to one of these (each feature's `_source` badge names the exact tables):
- **stats.nba.com via `nba_api`** — season stats, advanced/tracking stats, Synergy play types, hustle stats, shot charts, player-vs-player matchups (`LeagueSeasonMatchups`), defended FG% (`LeagueDashPtDefend`), shot-tracking splits by closest defender / touch time / dribbles / catch-and-shoot (`LeagueDashPlayerPtShot`, league-wide calls), 5-man lineups (`LeagueDashLineups`), officials (`BoxScoreSummaryV2`), game logs, draft combine, play-by-play.
- **ESPN play-by-play via sportsdataverse** — full-season play-by-play for the WPA model, Garbage-Time Deflator and Game Replay.
- **Kaggle "1947-present" Basketball-Reference-derived export** (`nba_data/kaggle_1947_present/`, gitignored) — pre-2010 season stats, team records (`Team Summaries.csv`), draft history.
- **Kaggle "College Basketball Dataset"** (`nba_data/college_teams/`, gitignored; `cbb13.csv`–`cbb26.csv`, team ratings from barttorvik.com) — college team strength for the College → NBA Pipeline. The combined `cbb.csv` in the same download was checked against the per-season files (identical) and isn't needed.
- **Kaggle salary datasets** (`nba_data/salaries/`, gitignored, no stated license) — player salaries for Contract Value, checked against real contracts (see Known real gaps).
- **Larry Coon's NBA Salary Cap FAQ and Hoops Rumors** — per-season league minimum salaries (cited per row in `league_minimum_salary`).
- **CollegeBasketballData.com** — D1 college player stats (Draft Prospects) and every D1 game with scores, Elo and NCAA seeds (March Madness Model; 84 API calls per full refetch). **The Odds API** — live championship odds (Vegas Scanner). **Public RSS feeds** — News.

## Running it locally

### Prerequisites
- PostgreSQL running locally with a `nba_analytics` database already populated (see `scripts/` for the fetch/build pipeline)
- Node.js for the frontend

### Python interpreter — read this first
```bash
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```
- `requirements.txt` pins the 17 packages the code imports at the versions the paper's numbers were made with; `requirements-lock.txt` pins every package of that interpreter (`pip freeze`). `api/tests/test_reproducibility.py` fails if an import is missing from it or an installed version drifts from its pin.
- **macOS only, one-time:** `sportsdataverse` transitively imports `xgboost`, which needs the OpenMP runtime: `brew install libomp`. Without it, `import sportsdataverse` (or anything under `sportsdataverse.nba`) fails with `XGBoostError: Library not loaded: @rpath/libomp.dylib` even though nothing in this project actually calls xgboost.
- **macOS python.org/Python.framework builds only, one-time:** if any live HTTPS fetch (`sportsdataverse`, `nba_api`, the odds/CBBD APIs) fails with `SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED`, run `/Applications/Python\ 3.14/Install\ Certificates.command` — the framework build doesn't wire itself up to the system/certifi trust store by default.

### `.env` setup
Create `api/.env` (gitignored):
```bash
DB_HOST=localhost
DB_PORT=5432
DB_USER=postgres
DB_PASSWORD=your_local_postgres_password
DB_NAME=nba_analytics

# Optional — see "Optional live data" below
ODDS_API_KEY=your_odds_api_key_here
CBBD_API_KEY=your_cbbd_api_key_here
```

### One command: start everything
```bash
./start.sh
```
Starts all three backends (8000/8001/8002) and the frontend in one terminal, each output line prefixed with its server (`[impact_api] …`, `[frontend] …`); **Ctrl+C stops everything it started**. Any backend port already in use is skipped (assumed already running). Tested in a real terminal: all four come up and respond, and Ctrl+C stops all of them with nothing left behind. Local Postgres still has to be running.

### Start the three backend services (manually)
Always pass `--port` explicitly:
```bash
cd api
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m uvicorn mvp_api:app        --port 8000 --reload
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m uvicorn similarity_api:app --port 8001 --reload
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m uvicorn impact_api:app     --port 8002 --reload
```

### Optional: live championship odds and college data
Two Analytics tabs need their own free API keys. Without them, every other feature still works — those two tabs just report the key is missing.
- **ODDS_API_KEY** (Vegas Scanner) — [The Odds API](https://the-odds-api.com/), free tier ~500 requests/month.
- **CBBD_API_KEY** (Draft Prospects) — [CollegeBasketballData.com](https://collegebasketballdata.com/key), free, one-field email signup. Only needed to re-run `scripts/fetch_college_stats.py`; the Draft Prospects tab itself just reads from Postgres once that script has run.

### Start the frontend
```bash
cd frontend
npm install
npm run dev
```
Opens at `http://localhost:5173`.

### Run the backend smoke tests
```bash
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m pytest api/tests -v
```
Real integration tests (FastAPI `TestClient` against the live local DB, no mocks) — skipped with a clear reason if Postgres isn't reachable. Add a test here for every new endpoint.

### Known-facts test suite
`api/tests/test_known_facts.py` (34 tests, ~2 s; `pytest api/tests` runs it with the smoke tests, or alone with `pytest api/tests/test_known_facts.py`; `DB_TARGET=layerbase` works too and skips what a mirror doesn't have yet). The smoke tests check that endpoints answer; this checks the numbers underneath, in two clearly separated kinds:
- **Real-world facts** — each carries its outside source URL in a comment and was read from that source before it was added: the five 70-point games since 2020-21 (Adebayo 83, Dončić 73, Embiid 70, Lillard 71, Mitchell 71; points, FG, 3P and FT lines against ESPN box scores / Wikipedia, and that there are exactly those five), the 1995-96 Bulls 72-10, 2015-16 Warriors 73-9 and 2023-24 Celtics 64-18 (also counted from `game_scores`), Wilt's 50.4 in 1961-62, LeBron's 2003-04 line (79 games, 20.9/5.5/5.9), Curry's 402 threes in 2015-16, and that season point totals, records and games equal Basketball-Reference's for all 510 team-seasons 2009-10 to 2025-26 with SRS correlating ≥ 0.999. Basketball-Reference itself blocks automated fetches (HTTP 403), so sources are ESPN, Wikipedia and NBA.com pages.
- **Internal invariants** — `game_scores` and `team_game_fatigue` are the same rows; every game has two mirrored rows; play-by-play team totals equal the real finals (all but ~43 bad-feed games); stints add up to game length and to the real final in every reconciled game; on-court plus off-court games and possessions equal the team's over the games a player played; the league's on-court net is ≈ 0 each season; `player_game_lines` season totals are within 0.5% of NBA.com's; assists match the Game Log; pre-game favourites win 60-72% and the fan-less 2020-21 home edge is the smallest.
Where a small understood gap exists the test pins its size and says why (Adebayo's 44 vs official 43 FGA: ESPN logs a Q2 buzzer heave as a miss; the on/off points gap in 26 mis-tracked 2022-23 Pistons/Bulls player-seasons; 4 assists). **Adding a fact:** read it from an outside page first (never from memory), put the URL and the date read in the test's comment, add it to the relevant list (`SEVENTY_POINT_GAMES`, `TEAM_RECORDS`, or a new function), and call `need(cur, "<table>")` first so it skips where the table isn't built. If a fact fails, treat it as a data bug until proven otherwise. What it found on its first run: Adebayo's 44 vs 43 FGA, an overstated "0 of 3,172" assist claim (it is 4, off by one), and that Layerbase still holds the pre-fix three-point attempts (Adebayo 19 vs the real 22) until the owner's sync.

### Paper numbers (conference paper, round 5)
`scripts/paper_numbers.py` (~4 s, read-only) writes every number the conference paper states to `paper/numbers.tex` as LaTeX macros (`\pnRapmNextPriorRmse` …), each with its source table or query in a comment; the paper (`paper/nba_hub_paper.tex`, untracked on purpose, like everything in `paper/`) types no number by hand. Rerun it after any rebuild and recompile. Formatting is decided once in the script (half-up rounding on the stored value, `{,}` thousands, `\ensuremath{-}`, seasons as 2025--26, small counts as words). Sentences that depend on an ordering or threshold ("λ lands at 3,000 in every single-season fit", "the record-only baseline wins on Brier") are checked as claims: if a rebuild breaks one, the script stops and names the sentence instead of writing a number that contradicts it. Data-quality counts come from `paper_data_audit` (round 5 step 5, below): the audit names each number's macro and format, `paper_numbers.py` prints them and checks the audit against the tables its other sections read, so a stale audit stops the run (the five counts that were copied from this README before step 5 are now re-derived there). `--check` also verifies every `\pn` macro the paper uses is defined, including those in the tables it `\input`s (`paper/tables/*.tex`). `api/tests/test_paper_numbers.py` (6 tests): formatting rules, two runs byte-identical, no broken claim, every used macro defined (skips if `paper/` is absent).

### Paper evaluation protocol (conference paper, round 5 step 2)
`scripts/paper_eval.py` (~7 min; `--only impact,xfg,pregame,sim` reruns some stages) scores every model the paper compares under one split: every hyperparameter tuned on 2020-21 to 2023-24, every choice between model families made on 2024-25, and one pass over 2025-26 with everything frozen. Models that need history before 2020-21 (the pre-game model and simulator from 2010-11, the shot model from 1996-97) use it as training data only. It reuses the platform's own code (`build_rapm.py`'s design matrix and fits, `build_shot_making.py`'s features and models, `season_sim_lib.py`) and touches none of the platform's tables: it writes `paper_eval_predictions` (one row per scored game / shot / player pair / team-season with prediction and outcome, so every comparison can be resampled), `paper_eval_metrics` (summary metrics per task, phase, model, variant and season, plus a pooled row for the tune phase) and `paper_eval_choices` (what was chosen, on which seasons, by which criterion, with the candidates' scores). Judgment calls are in the script's docstring and stored beside each choice: RAPM's λ by pooled next-season game RMSE over the three tune pairs (it lands at the platform's 3,000), the prior version keeps that λ by rule and tunes its scale (0.5; the free grid minimum is λ 12,000, scale 0.5, recorded but not used), the three-season λ has one tune pair with a full window, BPM and on/off get one least-squares multiplier each (0.71 and 0.19) beside the published scale-one values, and the scored season contributes nothing but outcomes (the intercept and home term are the earlier season's). The shot model's family is chosen on 2024-25 (the platform chose it on 2025-26 itself) after a three-configuration check on 2023-24 (they differ by < 0.0001 in log loss); the pre-game form is chosen on 2024-25 (the same form as leave-one-season-out within the tune seasons) and the prior's constants are fitted only on the seasons before the one scored (the platform fits them on all seasons). `api/tests/test_paper_eval.py` (8 tests) checks the split, that nothing was chosen on the test season, and that every stored metric recomputes from the stored per-unit rows. Rerun after `build_lineup_stints.py`, `load_bref_bpm_vorp.py`, a `player_shots` reload or a `game_scores` / postseason refresh, then `paper_numbers.py`.


### Paper significance tests (conference paper, round 5 step 3)
`scripts/paper_tests.py` (~75 s; `--only impact,xfg,pregame,sim`; `--resamples 500` for a quick look) puts an interval and a p-value on every comparison the paper states, from the per-unit rows `paper_eval.py` stored, and writes `paper_eval_tests`. For two models on the same units the difference is metric(A) − metric(B); a paired cluster bootstrap (10,000 resamples; clusters = games, the game a shot belongs to, players, or team-seasons) recomputes both metrics on the same resample and gives the 95% percentile interval and a two-sided bootstrap p; a sign-flip permutation on each cluster's summed loss differential gives a second p for mean-type metrics (equal MSE is equal RMSE, so it tests the RMSE difference too); where the units are a time-ordered game series (next-season and held-out margins, pre-game odds) a Diebold–Mariano test with a Newey–West long-run variance (Bartlett, bandwidth ⌊4(n/100)^{2/9}⌋) is stored as well. Single numbers get the same cluster bootstrap; the shot model's calibration (ECE over ten fixed-width bins, and the largest gap over bins holding ≥ 1% of the shots — the 0.9-1.0 bin holds 7 shots in 2025-26) is computed here since step 2 stored none. Judgment calls are in the docstring: team-seasons in one season are treated as exchangeable (16 of 30 make the playoffs, so those intervals run a little narrow); Brier follows the stored metric's clipping convention; percentile intervals without bias correction. Every row's seed is an md5 of its key, so reruns are bit-identical. `api/tests/test_paper_tests.py` (9 tests) checks the helpers on synthetic data (DM ~ N(0,1) under the null, sign-flip p uniform, bootstrap spread = closed form), that every stored value equals `paper_eval_metrics`, that intervals contain their point estimates and agree with the p-values, and that one row reproduces bit for bit from its stored seed. Rerun after `paper_eval.py`, then `paper_numbers.py`.

### Paper expected-points RAPM (conference paper, round 5 step 4)
Since round 6 step 8 it also fits two more targets from `scripts/build_shot_value.py`'s pre-game prices (look-ahead-free shooter-blind `lf_*`, shooter-aware `sa_*`; Just shipped). `scripts/paper_xrapm.py` (~90 s) refits RAPM on each stint side's **expected** points instead of the points scored, so that whether the shots went in drops out of the target: Σ over its field-goal attempts of P(make) × shot value + Σ over its free-throw attempts of the shooter's expected FT%, plus the few points no recorded attempt accounts for. P(make) is the shot-making model's own cross-fitted probability, now stored per shot by `build_shot_making.py` in `shot_xfg` (1,281,583 regular-season shots 2020-21 on, keyed by `player_shots.id`, 90 MB with its index; the five other shot-making tables hash identically before and after, `shot_making_validation` differs only in the fit timings its notes record). Play-by-play attempts are matched to the chart with `pbp_lineups.match_coordinates()` (which now also returns `nba_shot_id`): 99.4% of the 1.24M tracked attempts (≥ 99.8% every season but 2025-26 at 97.5%, see Known real gaps); an unmatched attempt takes the shooter's mean P(make) for that value and season, else the league's (7,393 attempts). Expected FT% is leave-one-out and shrunk toward the league rate with M = 27.3 pseudo-attempts (Stat Stability's split-half `stable_n` for FT%). Checks: 0 attempts outside every stint's range, every tracked stint's parsed FGA and FTA equal `lineup_stints`', attempt-accounted points + residual = stored points on every tracked stint (the residual is 309 absolute points in 192 stints, all but two in the 170 games whose points come from the score fields), expected points within 0.8% of actual in every season, and the possession-weighted spread of a side's points per 100 across stints falls from 61-63 to 34-37 (42-44% less). Writes `paper_xrapm_stints`, `paper_xrapm_players` (platform-style fits, CV λ 2,000-3,000, versions single/prior, no bootstrap), `paper_xrapm_fits`, `paper_xrapm_lambda_cv`, `paper_xrapm_meta`; `paper_eval.py`'s impact stage then scores `xrapm_single` / `xrapm_prior` under the protocol (λ 1,500 and prior scale 1.25 chosen on the tune pairs' *actual* margins; the carried intercept and home term refitted on actual points with the ratings fixed, as for BPM) and `paper_tests.py` pairs them with their actual-points twins, BPM and zero. **Result (honest): it does not help.** The one-season expected-points version is behind one-season RAPM on next-season margins by 0.46 [0.32, 0.59] points on the tune pairs and 0.84 [0.59, 1.10] on 2024-25, and level on 2025-26 (+0.06 [−0.21, 0.33]); with the prior +0.19, +0.63 and −0.04 [−0.34, 0.26], never ahead of BPM. It is more stable (SD of the ratings 0.55-0.73 of RAPM's; with the prior, year-to-year r 0.67 vs 0.55 on the test pair, outside the interval in every phase, partly a heavier prior) because a shooter-blind expected-points model removes shooting *skill* along with luck: the change in a player's rating correlates −0.44 with his shot-making (−0.54 with the prior) over 1,984 qualified player-seasons; Durant, Curry, Jokić, Gilgeous-Alexander and Wembanyama are the biggest fallers. The paper reports it as a tested negative result (Methods "Expected-points RAPM", Results subsection, xRAPM rows in Tables rapm and tests, four Crossref-/page-verified references on shot-based and expected-goals plus-minus). `api/tests/test_paper_xrapm.py` (5 tests).
