Order: `build_shot_making.py` → `paper_xrapm.py` → `paper_eval.py --only impact` → `paper_tests.py --only impact` → `paper_numbers.py`. Not synced to Layerbase.
### Paper data-quality audit (conference paper, round 5 step 5)
`scripts/paper_data_audit.py` (~95 s, one play-by-play parse and one shot match; read-only on every other table; two runs identical) re-measures every error class found so far in the public feeds, with the check that finds it, and writes `paper_data_audit` (267 key/season/value rows since round 6 step 12, 112 kB; each row the paper prints carries its macro name and format), `paper_data_audit_classes` (18 rows: feed, class, how detected, size as LaTeX with `\pn` macros, handling kind and handling) and `paper/tables/data_audit.tex` (the paper's Table audit). Nothing is copied from this README: Known real gaps is only the checklist. Classes: ESPN play-by-play (nba_api twin copies, NBA Cup finals, same-surname wrong-player tags, single tag/text disagreements, players with no id, team-less substitutions and the 'NaN' line, stale/backward score fields and the on-court +/- they break, last score ≠ final, missed threes worded as twos, clock errors and the two feeds' clock gap, games that don't reconcile), NBA shot chart (missing games and match rate, `shot_distance` = 0 on threes, shots at (0, 0) before 2010-11) and season tables (`team_game_fatigue.plus_minus`, season rows with a wrong team, the two age conventions). Two measurements are new rather than re-measured: the wrong-player count is re-derived by replaying the fetch's original season-only matcher on the stored names (it gives exactly the 66 repaired events in 9 games; the repair rule itself finds nothing left), and the tag/text rule is stated exactly (the tagged name is absent from one event's text but present in the same player's other events of that game: 64 events, 56 naming another player of the game, 54 a teammate). Found on the way: ESPN's clock shows less time left than the chart's for the same shot by a median 4 s (over 5 s for 40% of shots, 26 s at the 99th percentile; the few largest, up to ~2 minutes, are order-matching slips or ESPN clock errors), and 1,168 regulation events in 1,038 games carry an ESPN clock outside their own period (mostly end-of-quarter markers). Seconds are summed as numeric in the audit's SQL: a float sum's last digits depend on row order and once made two runs differ. Rerun after a play-by-play re-fetch or repair, `build_lineup_stints.py`, `build_player_game_lines.py` or a `player_shots` reload, then `paper_numbers.py`. `api/tests/test_paper_data_audit.py` (6 tests): no size cell types a measured number, every printed row has a known format and a defined macro, the audit's counts equal live queries (a rebuild without rerunning the audit fails), the pre-audit README counts re-derive exactly, and the table file is the one the class list generates.

### Paper popular beliefs (conference paper, round 5 step 6)
`scripts/paper_beliefs.py` (~22 min with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=4`; `--only clutch,streak,split,luck,referee`; `--perms 200 --perms2 2000 --streak-perms 50` for a quick look; deterministic: every family's generator is seeded from an md5 of its key, and two full runs printed identical aggregates) puts five of the platform's "is it real?" pages under one test and writes `paper_beliefs` / `paper_beliefs_summary` / `paper_beliefs_meta` and `paper/tables/beliefs.tex` (the paper's Table beliefs, every number a `\pn` macro). For every unit the statistic is the platform's own number, recomputed and checked against the stored table (the run stops if any differs), and the null comes from shuffling that unit's own data: **clutch** (unit a player with 50+/100+ clutch chances; clutch labels shuffled within each game *and kind of play* — free throw, turnover, two- or three-point attempt, because clutch chances are free-throw heavy and a free throw varies far less than a field-goal attempt, so a shuffle across kinds made the null too wide: 3 of 186 at p < 0.05 against 9 expected); **hot streaks** (unit a player with 40+ windows in seasons with a previous season on file; the persistence slope per stat and window; game order shuffled within each season; the same shuffles give the league slope's null centre); **situational splits** (unit a player-season; games shuffled between the two sides; the average player's gap taken out *proportionally*, see below); **team luck** (unit a franchise; luck shuffled across the 30 teams within each season; the persistence r gets the same null); **referees** (unit an official with 25+ games, or a 3-official crew with 2+ games; the games' values shuffled within each season, i.e. random assignment). Each unit gets 2,000 draws, a two-sided p around the null's own mean with Phipson & Smyth's +1, and units at p ≤ 0.02 a fresh 20,000 draws; Benjamini–Hochberg at 5% within each family; every family is reported as n, the p < 0.05 count, 0.05 n and the FDR survivors. `api/tests/test_paper_beliefs.py` (9 tests) re-derives every summary count from the stored rows, checks every p is a valid (1 + r)/(B + 1), checks every unit's platform number against `player_wpa_totals`, `hot_streak_persistence`, `player_situational_splits`, `team_luck_schedule` and `referee_tendencies`, and checks the table file uses only defined macros. **Results (2026-09-29):** clutch — 12 of 186 players at p < 0.05 (9.3 expected), 18 of 314 at the 50-chance floor (15.7 expected), none survives; splits — 3,929 of 73,319 player-season tests at p < 0.05 (3,666 expected), none survives in any of the 44 split × stat families; luck — the franchise persistence r of 0.14 is outside its shuffled range (−0.09 to 0.09, p 0.003), the between-franchise variance of mean luck is above its null (p 0.002), and 1 of 30 franchises survives (MIN, −3.0 wins per 82 against its Pythagorean expectation over 17 seasons); referees — 20 of 84 officials survive on fouls per game (28 at p < 0.05 against 4.2; the largest, Tony Brown, +3.1 fouls per game over 39 games), 10 on free throws, 0 on pace, and 0 of 514 crews on fouls (the null is random assignment, so this cannot separate a tendency from an assignment policy). **The Miller–Sanjurjo check:** the hot-streak persistence share is not zero when nothing carries on — shuffling every player's games within each season still gives a slope of 7-86% depending on stat and window, because both gaps are measured against the same noisy baseline (and, with the prior-season blend, against last season's level); net of that null centre, shooting runs carry on nothing (3P% over 10 games: 12% measured, 13% null centre, p 0.72; TS% 15% vs 15%; the null-centred share is 0-6% across the 12 shooting-% families and the observed slope is inside its shuffled range in 11 of them), points keep 19% over 5 and 10 games and 7% over 20, minutes 32% over 5 games and −9% over 20 (with games in their real order the baseline tracks the current role, which a shuffled season cannot); per player, 406 of 13,628 tests survive FDR (minutes over 5 games 156 of 443, points 67 of 443, usage 40 of 443; the 12 shooting-percentage families 2 of 3,079; FT% over 10 games has 18 at p < 0.05 against 8.5 expected and none surviving; 26 eligible tests dropped as untestable), which we read as role changes and injuries. Two things learned the hard way, both recorded in the script's docstring: taking the league gap out *additively* (as the platform's `vs_league` does) turned a non-shooter's home games into negative attempts that no shuffle can reproduce, so 68 spurious "survivors" appeared in 3PA and blocks (25 of the 29 home-3PA ones centres with 0-3 attempts a season) — the script takes it out proportionally (side-A numerators ÷ (1 + league gap / league side-B rate)), which leaves a zero at zero, and stores the platform's additive number beside it (`platform_stat`; same sign for 493 of 500 clutch rows and 98% of split rows); and a shuffle that leaves a near-floor player with no windows to fit yields no draw — an earlier version counted such draws as non-exceedances, which handed ~150 players the floor p-value (7 of 8 "survivors" for FT% over 10 games), so now a draw that is not a number counts in neither B nor r and a unit with under 90% of its draws defined is dropped (counted in `paper_beliefs_meta`). Then rerun `paper_numbers.py` (`\pnBl…` macros; claims guard the sentences). Not synced to Layerbase.

### Paper ablations (conference paper, round 5 step 7)
`scripts/paper_ablations.py` (~11 min with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=4`, most of it 13 boosting fits; `--only impact,xfg,sim`; `--resamples 200` for a quick look; two full runs give identical tables) takes one part out of a model at a time, re-estimates it under the paper's protocol (tune 2020-21 to 2023-24, validate 2024-25, test 2025-26) and pairs it with the full model on the same units with `paper_tests.py`'s machinery (paired cluster bootstrap, 10,000 resamples, sign-flip and Diebold–Mariano p where they apply). The full models are paper_eval's: the script reproduces `paper_eval_predictions` exactly (max |difference| 0) before it scores anything, including a refit of the test-season shot model. **RAPM** (next-season margins and year-to-year reliability): λ over the whole grid for one-season RAPM and RAPM + prior; the prior's scale 0 (= one-season RAPM) to 2; no possession weights (each side-row the season's mean possessions, λ re-chosen on the tune pairs: 8,000); no home column in the fit, with the home edge added back at prediction or not at all. **Expected FG** (validate and test; the tune season chose the configuration): coordinates; distance and angle; zone and three-point flag; all seven location features; clock and period; season. **Pre-game model and simulator** (halfway date, the full model's seeds): no carry-over (prior mean 0, prior variance the ratings' mean square around zero), no shrinkage (prior variance 10^12), no back-to-back flags, and for the simulator no per-run rating draw; prior constants and logistic coefficients re-estimated for each exactly as `paper_eval.py` does. **Results (2026-09-30):** without the box-score prior next-season RMSE rises 0.10 / 0.18 / 0.28 points (tune / validate / test, all outside their intervals); its weight is unsettled (scale 1 is worse on tune, +0.11, and better on test, −0.10 [−0.18, −0.01]); the tune grid's free minimum λ 12,000 (set aside by the rule) would have been better on the test season by 0.16 [0.04, 0.27] and still behind BPM; possession weights help one-season RAPM in every phase (+0.18 [0.03, 0.33] on test without them); the home term matters only for prediction (ratings without it correlate ≥ 0.999 with the full ones). Shot model: without any location feature log loss +42 ×10⁻³ (AUC 0.65 → 0.52), without any one location group ≤ 0.1 ×10⁻³ (redundant encodings), without the season +4.8 / +5.1 ×10⁻³, without clock and period +0.8. Pre-game: shrinkage matters in every phase (+17.6 / +9.6 / +10.8 ×10⁻³ log loss without it); carry-over and back-to-backs help on tune (and b2b on validate), not on test. Simulator: without carry-over or shrinkage the test-season playoff log loss is *better* (−21 and −25 ×10⁻³, intervals below zero; inside the interval on tune and validate); without the per-run rating draw the 80% ranges cover 65% of win totals over the tune seasons instead of 75% (−9.5 points [−12.4, −6.9]) at no gain in win-total error. `api/tests/test_paper_ablations.py` (7 tests) checks the full models equal paper_eval's, every metric re-derives from the stored rows, every test row carries the metrics, every re-chosen hyperparameter is the tune minimum of its stored candidates, and the table file uses only defined macros. Then rerun `paper_numbers.py` (`\pnAb…` macros, 916 in all; claims guard every sentence of the Ablations subsection). Rerun after `paper_eval.py`. Not synced to Layerbase.

### Paper figures (conference paper, round 5 step 8)
`scripts/paper_figures.py` (~10 s, read-only; `--only pipeline,forest,calibration,rapm_sens,streaks`; `--png DIR` for 300 dpi previews) draws the paper's five figures into `paper/figures/*.pdf` (untracked, like the rest of `paper/`) from the same rows as its tables: the pipeline and the protocol's seasons (numbers are the `\pn…` macros' own text); a forest plot of every paired difference the Results state, all three phases, with its 95% paired cluster-bootstrap interval (`paper_eval_tests`; the simulator's panel is scaled to the record baseline's own value, coverage shown as misses, so every row reads "negative favours the simulator"); the expected-FG and pre-game models' test-season calibration (`paper_eval_predictions`, same ten fixed-width bins as `paper_tests.py`; Wilson intervals on the pre-game bins); RAPM + prior's next-season RMSE over its prior scale and λ grids per phase against BPM (`paper_ablation_tests`); and every hot-streak family's observed persistence share against its shuffled null (`paper_beliefs_summary`, the Miller–Sanjurjo check). It builds the macros in-process with `paper_numbers.build()` and stops if any plotted number the paper also prints doesn't round to its macro (204 checked) or a sentence a caption states no longer holds, then checks that the paper includes every figure and references every figure label. Vector PDF at 3.5 in / 7.16 in, 8 pt STIX (Times-like) embedded as TrueType, grayscale-safe (phases differ by marker shape and fill), no creation date: two runs are byte-identical. `api/tests/test_paper_figures.py` (4 tests). Rerun after `paper_numbers.py` (any rebuild of the `paper_*` tables).
### Reproducibility package (conference paper, round 5 step 9)
Five pieces:
- **`requirements.txt` / `requirements-lock.txt`**: the pinned environment (see Python interpreter above).
- **`scripts/rebuild_all.sh`**: every pipeline in dependency order, one `step` line per script, in five stages: `fetch` (network: ESPN, stats.nba.com, CollegeBasketballData.com, each tagged), `load` (local raw files, each gitignored input named), `derived`, `paper`, `paper-inputs`.
  - With no argument it prints the plan and runs nothing. `--dry-run`, `--from <script>` and `--offline` do what they say.
  - `scripts/rebuild_all.sh paper-inputs` is the one command that regenerates the paper's inputs: manifest, `numbers.tex`, figures, `SHA256SUMS`. It takes ~1 min and is read-only on the database.
- **`scripts/paper_manifest.py`**: the row count, schema md5 and an order-independent content md5 of every table (~40 s for all 167, read-only), each table's kind (source / derived / paper / cache / legacy) and producing script (`PRODUCERS`; an unlisted table stops the run), and a database digest. Output goes to `paper/manifest.json` and `.tsv`. `--files` writes `paper/SHA256SUMS` for the generated inputs. `paper_numbers.py` prints the table count, row count and digest (`\pnMan…` macros) and stops if the manifest no longer matches the database's tables, schemas and row counts.
- **`docs/DATASHEET.md`**: sources, terms, coverage, known errors, and what is and isn't redistributed.
- **`docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md`**: environment, stages and runtimes, what differs after a rebuild, every seed, and the plan for an anonymous review artifact.

`api/tests/test_reproducibility.py` (9 tests) checks that:
- the runner parses and prints its plan;
- every step exists and every producer runs;
- no step reads a derived or paper table whose producer runs later (reads in the script and in every local module it imports; a swapped pair is caught);
- every table has a producer;
- the table hash ignores row order;
- the manifest is current and a tampered copy is caught;
- `SHA256SUMS` matches;
- `player_season_stats`' 2009-10 to 2024-25 rows are exactly the committed season CSVs' players with 200+ minutes (the rows loaded by a script that isn't in the repository);
- the requirements cover the imports and pin the installed versions.

**Found on the way:** 2 tables have no loader in the repository (`mvp_seasons`, `mvp_winners`). Neither does the 2009-10 to 2024-25 base of `player_season_stats`, whose loading rule is now recovered and tested. The provenance of the bulk shot files (`shots_data/pbp_shots_1997_2026/`) isn't recorded anywhere; the owner should add it to the datasheet.

### Paper references (conference paper, round 5 step 10)
The paper cites through BibTeX: `paper/refs.bib` (untracked, like the rest of `paper/`; IEEEtran.bst, built into Overleaf). 36 entries, each checked before it went in: 30 against their Crossref DOI record (title, authors in order, volume, issue, pages, year), the other 6 (a web article, a conference page, a forum thread, a book, the scikit-learn paper, an arXiv preprint) against the page itself. The line above each entry, `% verified: <url> <date> (...)`, says where, and for every work the text makes a claim about, what the paper relies on it for, checked against its abstract. `scripts/paper_refs_check.py` (no database; also a step of `rebuild_all.sh paper-inputs`) checks that every `\cite` has an entry, every entry is cited, every entry has its verified line and required fields, and no hand-written `thebibliography` is left. `--online` re-compares every DOI entry with Crossref (run it after adding or editing an entry). `api/tests/test_paper_refs.py` (4 tests). Rules: never cite from memory, open the DOI first; Basketball-Reference, ScienceDirect and some publishers return 403 to fetches, so use Crossref, OpenAlex, the arXiv API or an author's copy.

Rerun `paper-inputs` after any rebuild.

### Paper rewrite, trim and anonymisation (conference paper, round 5 step 11)
`paper/nba_hub_paper.tex` (untracked, like all of `paper/`) is restructured around its four contributions: the data-quality audit (Section III), one evaluation protocol with a test on every comparison (IV), under which the four model sections (V–VIII) and the ablations (IX) report, expected-points RAPM as a tested negative result (VI), and popular beliefs under one test (X). Each model's methods now sit in its own section, the award-models section is gone (its eight `\pnMvp…`/`\pnRoy…` macros stay defined but unused; `paper_numbers.py --check` only lists unused macros), and Platform is one paragraph. Two switches in the preamble: `\ifanon` (`\anontrue` drops the author block, affiliation, repository link and acknowledgment; `paper/nba_hub_paper_anon.tex` is the same file with it on, regenerate it with `sed 's/^\\anonfalse$/\\anontrue/'`) and `\ifappendix` (`\appendixtrue` attaches four appendices, protocol details, every ablation row, the data-quality lessons, hot streaks by family; every cross-reference into them is inside `\ifappendix`, so the main text stands alone with it off). **The paper compiles locally now:** `brew install tectonic` (XeTeX + BibTeX in one binary, the TeX bundle downloads on first run) and `brew install mupdf-tools` (`mutool draw -r 60 -o pg%02d.png nba_hub_paper.pdf` renders pages to look at) were installed on 2026-09-30; `cd paper && tectonic nba_hub_paper.tex` builds the PDF. IEEEtran 10 pt two-column: 13 pages with the appendices, 12 without, the anonymised build the same, with no overfull box and no undefined reference or citation. Layout fixes live in the table generators (`paper_data_audit.py`, `paper_beliefs.py`, `paper_ablations.py`, so a regeneration keeps them): every float is `[!t]`, because the audit table is taller than IEEEtran's top-of-page fraction and with a plain `[t]` could never be placed, which held every later table back to the end of the paper; the audit table uses the full text width with 4 pt column padding; the beliefs table is a `table*`; the ablations label column wraps. Also fixed: `\\` followed by `[City, India]` in the author block was read as an optional length (the paper had never been compiled), and long bibliography URLs may break at `/` and `-`. Overleaf upload: `paper/nba_hub_paper_overleaf.zip` (both .tex, numbers.tex, refs.bib, figures/, tables/). **Three lengths, both review states (owner's choice, venue not yet picked):** `paper/nba_hub_paper.tex` is the long version (12 pages; 13 with the appendices), `paper/nba_hub_paper_8p.tex` an 8-page version (every section, the audit, tests, RAPM and beliefs tables and the forest, calibration and streaks figures; the shot, simulator and ablations tables and the pipeline and RAPM-sensitivity figures folded into prose; 24 references cited) and `paper/nba_hub_paper_6p.tex` a 6-page version (audit, tests and RAPM tables, forest and calibration figures, 17 references, the beliefs and ablation results in prose). `scripts/paper_build.sh [long|8p|6p]` writes each version's `_anon.tex` (`\anontrue`) and compiles both with tectonic, printing pages, overfull boxes and undefined references per build: 13/13, 8/8 and 6/6 pages, all clean. Every version uses only macros from `numbers.tex` (`paper_numbers.py --check --paper <file>` passes for each) and only keys in `refs.bib`; the short versions cite fewer entries, which BibTeX simply omits. The Overleaf zip holds all six .tex files. Still open: the AI-use sentence stays as written until a venue's rule says otherwise; the review artifact (docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md) is built once a double-blind venue is chosen.

## Project structure

```
api/
  .env                  DB creds + API keys — gitignored, never committed
  db_config.py           Shared DB_CONFIG dict, read from api/.env
  mvp_api.py             Awards models, backtests, SHAP, WPA calibration (:8000)
  similarity_api.py      Similarity, clustering, trajectory (:8001)
  impact_api.py          Everything else — games, dashboard, odds, WPA/lineups/replay (:8002).
                          Thin app entrypoint only (FastAPI app + CORS + mounts every router) —
                          route bodies live in routers/, shared setup in impact_core.py (A6).
  impact_core.py          DB pool, loaded models, every fetch_*/find_player/get_db-style helper
                          and module-level constant shared across impact_api's routers.
  routers/                One file per impact_api feature (e.g. referee_tendencies.py,
                          matchup_finder.py) — each just its @router.get route(s), moved verbatim
                          out of the old single impact_api.py so paths stayed byte-for-byte
                          identical; verified via the full smoke suite plus a live sweep of all
                          59 real endpoints after the split (zero 5xx, only pre-existing gaps).
  shots_lib.py           Shared shot-chart fetch/cache logic
  lineups_lib.py         Which lineup source covers a season (play-by-play stints from 2020-21, lineup_stats before)
                          and a team's stint coverage; used by Pair/Lineup Chemistry and the team page
  workbench_catalogue.py Every dataset and stat the Workbench can query (how each combines, first season, status);
                          the Leaderboard's and Game Finder's STATS are built from it (round 7)
  tests/test_smoke.py    Real integration smoke tests, one per shipped endpoint
  tests/test_known_facts.py  Sourced real-world facts + internal invariants over the stored data (see Known-facts test suite)
scripts/
  db_config.py           Shared DB_CONFIG dict for scripts/, resolves api/.env by absolute path
  wpa_lib.py              Shared WPA model loading/scoring, used by compute_wpa.py and impact_api.py
  pbp_lineups.py          The one play-by-play lineup parser (who was on the floor when): build_player_game_lines.py
                          and build_lineup_stints.py both replay games through Game.walk()
  pbp_possessions.py      Possessions on top of Game.walk() (rules, and corrected_clock(): ESPN's clock rebuilt from
                          the shot chart); build_possessions.py writes them, later steps reuse possessions()
  build_event_clock.py    Stores corrected_clock() for every ESPN event (pbp_event_clock); scripts read it with
                          load_espn(conn, clock=True) + game_clock() -> Game(..., clock=), SQL readers join on event_id
  build_coaching_decisions.py
                          Coaching Decisions: decision points from possessions + ESPN's timeout / challenge events,
                          matched tests on paper_beliefs.py's machinery; estimator shared with the API in api/coaching_lib.py
  build_pregame_availability.py
                          Availability-aware pre-game odds: lineup term from who played (math shared with the what-if
                          endpoint in api/availability_lib.py); protocol scoring with paper_tests' functions
  stats_lib.py            Shared statistics helpers (WLS with cluster-robust SEs) — used instead of statsmodels
  fetch_*.py, build_*.py, train_*.py, compute_*.py, precompute_*.py, upgrade_*.py
                          The data pipeline: fetch from nba_api/CBBD → build/write Postgres tables →
                          train/backtest models → precompute derived tables. Each script's own
                          docstring says what real data it uses and how to run it.
  *.pkl                   Saved trained model artifacts (committed — matches project convention)
frontend/
  src/
    components/
      pages/              Top-level routed pages (Dashboard, Player Comparison, Games hub, ...)
      common/              Shared components (PlayerHeadshot, TeamLogo, InfoTooltip, Icon, ...)
      workbench/           Workbench blocks (Set, Table, Chart, Note, App tool), the board grid, the table's and chart's spec builders, PlotChart.jsx (the only Observable Plot import)
      *.jsx                Analytics sub-tab components (one per feature)
      pages/AnalyticsSection.jsx   Tab-group registry — add new Analytics tabs here (TAB_GROUPS array)
    services/api.js        Every backend call, one file, grouped by feature, one fetch function each
    utils/workbenchStore.js  Workbench boards in IndexedDB, file export/import, share links, cleanBoard()
    utils/workbenchLayout.js The Workbench grid: compaction, placing new blocks, reading order
    utils/workbenchTools.js  The app's tools as Workbench blocks (catalogue, sizes) and their per-tab profile fetch cache
    utils/openInWorkbench.js "Open in Workbench": boards built from the Leaderboard Builder, Player Comparison, profile and team page
    utils/useAutosave.js   Text boxes that save on blur and after typing stops (Report builder, Workbench)
    styles/dashboard.css   Single stylesheet for the whole app
nba_data/                  Raw CSV inputs; kaggle_1947_present/ and salaries/ are gitignored (third-party)
docs/screenshots/          README images (not all newer features have one yet)
PROJECT_DETAILS.md         Deep-dive on the ML methodology (predates several features in this README —
                          the modeling-methodology sections are still accurate; treat the feature list
                          in THIS file as the current source of truth)
```

## Established conventions & patterns (follow these, don't reinvent)

- **Era-normalization**: any feature comparing players across seasons z-scores each stat within its own season's qualified pool first (`cluster_players.py`, the similarity engines, the Draft Prospect bridge pool). Never pool raw stat values across eras.
- **Headshots/logos**: `<PlayerHeadshot playerId={} playerName={} size={} />` and `<TeamLogo abbreviation={} size={} />` (both in `components/common/`) — they fail gracefully to initials/a fallback, never a broken image icon.
- **Tooltips**: `<InfoTooltip label="..." title="...">` for any "how does this work" disclosure — the project's standard place to put honest methodology explanations rather than burying them in a modal or leaving them unexplained.
- **Source badges**: every main Analytics endpoint's JSON includes a `_source` object (`api/source_badge.py`'s `make_source(tables, upstream_api, as_of=None, live=False)`) disclosing which real Postgres table(s) backed the response, which real upstream API/dataset those tables came from, and — for the handful of endpoints that live-fetch rather than read a precomputed table — that it's live. The frontend renders it with `<SourceBadge source={data?._source} />` (`components/common/SourceBadge.jsx`) next to the section's `InfoTooltip`; it renders nothing if `_source` is absent, so it's safe to add to a new endpoint at any time without touching the frontend contract.
- **Page links**: pages are listed in `PAGES` in `frontend/src/App.jsx` (ids match `navConfig.js`) and live in `?page=<id>`. Navigate with the `onNavigate(page, hash)` prop App passes to every page (TopNav, CommandPalette, Landing and Methodology use the same function); it pushes a history entry. Never set `window.location.hash` after navigating: pass the Analytics tab as `hash` instead. From components that don't get `onNavigate` (e.g. `PlayerName`), use `openPage(page, params)` / `openPlayerProfile(id)` from `utils/useUrlState.js`; App re-reads the URL on its `nbahub:navigate` event. Link to a player with `<PlayerName>` (it links to the profile by itself) or put `playerProfileHref(id)` on an `<a>` with an `isPlainClick` handler, so cmd-click still opens a new tab.
- **Tool inputs in the link**: read them once when the component sets up its state (`useInitialParams()` + `parseParam.int/num/oneOf/str/list`, each returning null for a missing or bad value so you fall back to the default) and mirror them back with `useUrlSync({ key: value })` (null/''/[] removes a key; pass null while options are still loading). Keep keys short and readable, and add `<CopyLinkButton />` (`components/common/`) to the tool's title row.
- **Analytics tabs**: registered in `TAB_GROUPS` in `frontend/src/components/pages/AnalyticsSection.jsx`, grouped into Models / Player Analysis / Teams & Markets / Prospects. The active tab syncs to `location.hash` via `history.replaceState` (no back-button spam) so any tab is directly linkable, e.g. `http://localhost:5173/#wpa`.
- **Add to report**: nothing to wire. `ChartExport` and `TableExport` already contain the button (`common/AddToReport.jsx`); a new chart needs `<ChartExport svgRef name />` and a new table `<TableExport name />` with a meaningful `name` (it becomes the item's caption). A chart's colours must come from CSS variables or classes (as everywhere here), not from JS theme state, or the light-theme capture in `chartExport.snapshotChart()` won't reach them.
- **Table export**: put `<TableExport />` directly before any new data table (or its `.table-wrapper`); `DataTable` already has it. It reads the rendered table, so there's nothing to wire to the data. Mark decoration inside cells with `data-export-skip`, or give a badge plain words with `data-export-as="..."`. Pass `name="..."` when the nearest heading above the table isn't its title.
- **Signed numbers** (2026-10-02): format with `signed(v, d)` from `frontend/src/utils/format.js` and colour with `bySign(v, d, 'x-pos', 'x-neg')` at the same decimals (after the same ×100 for percentage points), never a local `` `${v > 0 ? '+' : ''}${v.toFixed(d)}` ``: the sign has to come from the rounded value, or −0.04 prints "−0.0", 0.03 prints "+0.0" and a cell reading 0.0 is tinted. Pass `'-'` as the third argument where a page has always printed a hyphen minus. `withSign(v, body)` puts the sign on an already formatted magnitude ("$1.2M", "4.0 pts"); `plain(v, d)` is `toFixed` without "-0.00".
- **Live external fetches** in the API layer are cached in an in-process `_CACHE` dict with a TTL (see `impact_api.py`'s `_CACHE`/`_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`) rather than hitting `nba_api`/external APIs on every request.
- **New feature checklist**: pipeline script in `scripts/` (if new data needed) → Postgres table → endpoint in the right API file (awards/validation → `mvp_api.py`; similarity/clustering → `similarity_api.py`; everything else → a new or existing file under `api/routers/`, sharing helpers from `api/impact_core.py`), with a `_source` object on the response → `fetchXyz()` function in `services/api.js` → `XyzSection.jsx` component with a `<SourceBadge>` → registered in `AnalyticsSection.jsx`'s `TAB_GROUPS` → smoke test → verified in browser → committed.
- **Workbench blocks** (round 7): a block type is an entry in `BLOCK_INFO` (`components/workbench/workbenchShared.js`), a branch in `cleanSettings()` (`utils/workbenchStore.js`, which rebuilds its settings from scratch for imported boards and share links), a body in `renderBody()` of `pages/Workbench.jsx`, and its type in `BLOCK_TYPES`. A block that shows players or teams reads them from a set (`settings.setId`), never a list of its own, and colours them with `seriesVar(member.color)` (the `--wb-series-N` tokens), never a hex. Block data comes from `runWorkbenchQuery(spec)` (catalogue keys only) or, for the app's own tools (step 5), their existing endpoints. **A new app tool** is an entry in `TOOLS` (`utils/workbenchTools.js`: label, icon, player or team, size) and a branch in `ToolBlock.jsx` that renders the page's *own* component (export it from its page if needed; never copy it), with that component's "Not on file" reason when the member has no data; give profile/team sections `ProfileEmbedContext` handling instead of new markup, and pass a player's NBA id to any endpoint that otherwise looks him up by name. A page gets **Open in Workbench** with `<OpenInWorkbenchButton build={() => …} />` after its Save button and a builder in `utils/openInWorkbench.js` that only names players, teams, stats and settings (never copies a number). Board edits go through `updateBoard(id, change)`, which re-reads the stored board inside the transaction and compacts the layout. **Charts** (step 4) are drawn only in `components/workbench/PlotChart.jsx` (lazy-loaded; never import `@observablehq/plot` anywhere else, or every page grows by ~300 kB); colour marks with CSS variables (`seriesVar()`, `var(--wb-context)`, `currentColor`) so the theme and `chartExport.js` keep working, and draw the legend, the n and any fit's numbers inside the SVG so exports carry them. A chart's grey population is the same spec with `entities: 'all'`; for anything but raw points use `/workbench/context` (no row cap) rather than fetching rows.
- **Text boxes that save themselves** use `utils/useAutosave.js` (saves on blur and 0.7 s after typing stops, and only text the user typed).
- **Stats are defined once, in `api/workbench_catalogue.py`** (2026-10-03, round 7): a new player-season stat for the Leaderboard Builder, a new per-game stat for the Game Finder, or anything the Workbench should offer is a `Column` there (label, SQL, how it combines, first season, sources, status), not a new literal in a router. A stat whose source is known to be wrong goes in with `status="excluded"` and a `reason` the UI shows. SQL fragments in the catalogue are code (no `%`); user input only ever reaches a query as a bound parameter through `routers/workbench.py`'s `compile_query()`.
- **Possessions and time on the clock** (2026-10-01): possessions come only from `pbp_possessions.possessions()` (on `Game.walk()`), never a second set of rules; anything that needs *when* something happened within a possession (time to shot, end-of-quarter decisions, the last N seconds) uses `pbp_possessions.corrected_clock()` or the stored `possessions` times and their `clock_ok` / NULLs, not ESPN's `seconds_remaining` (made shots are logged a median 14 s late). Possessions map to stints by event number, never by time.

## Known real gaps (disclosed, not hidden)

- **scipy 1.18's `cho_factor` corrupts memory on macOS 27.0.1 (found 2026-10-02, worked around):** `scipy.linalg.cho_factor` goes through the batched C++ module `_batched_linalg._cholesky`; on this Mac (Apple M5, Accelerate) it overwrote memory in the Rating Tracker's ~1,850 × 1,850 factorisations, so `paper_eval.py --only impact` crashed (SIGBUS / SIGILL / SIGSEGV) or ran on with corrupted arrays. Guard Malloc on a tracker-only script faults inside Accelerate's `dpotrf$NEWLAPACK` entered from the batched module, every run; the same Accelerate routine through scipy's f2py wrapper runs clean, and `VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1` alone does not help. `scripts/rating_tracker_lib.py` (the only `scipy.linalg` user) now calls `dpotrf`/`dpotrs`/`dpotri` directly; don't reintroduce `cho_factor` / `scipy.linalg.cholesky`. After the move to macOS 27 the RAPM-family protocol numbers differ from macOS 26.6's in the last digits (≤ 4e-13 points); no number the paper prints moved. `docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md` has the proof.
- **Shot Value Added's prices are a forecast, so they lag (2026-10-02):** a shooter's skill moves with the evidence, not at once, so a real in-season change is only partly kept and partly counted as beyond; the league level is a running estimate, so a season whose accuracy rises puts the whole league above its forecast (0.4-1.5 FG points per 100 FGA a season, shown on the page); a rookie or a player who never took a kind of shot is priced near the rookie average; free-throw history before 2020-21 comes from per-game season averages (off by a few attempts). As with shot-making, skill includes the defence a shooter usually faces and the shots he creates.
- **Forecast Ledger 2026-27 (locked 2026-09-30):** the rosters are ESPN's training-camp rosters of that day (18-21 players; a veteran cut before opening night still counts for the team that had him), there is no injury model (13 players listed out, 46 day-to-day at lock time, stored and not used), rookies and players new to the NBA take no minutes, and the two games each team was still owed are simulated against a league-average opponent. The roster weights' hindcast knows who actually played for each team, so it flatters the roster-aware forecast. `fetch_game_scores.py` can't fetch 2026-27 (it matches ESPN finals to `team_game_fatigue`, which comes from stats.nba.com and can't be refreshed from this machine), so `game_scores` ends with 2025-26 and the ledger scores 2026-27 from its own ESPN read (`ledger_results`, keyed by ESPN id). **Live scoring:** it is only as current as the last `ledger_update.py` run, which needs the laptop awake; odds for a day it missed are computed afterwards by the same rule from the same earlier results and labelled "recomputed", and only odds logged before tip-off are a forward-looking record (the tests are stored both ways). The record-only baseline is worse than a coin flip for the first weeks (log5 of 1-0 against 0-1 is 95%).
- **The Hot Streak Checker's "carries on" share includes regression toward a noisy baseline (measured 2026-09-29, not changed):** `hot_streak_persistence.slope`, the share of a run's gap that showed up in the next N games, is fitted against a baseline from the earlier games of the same season (blended with last season), so it is positive even when every player's games are shuffled within each season: 7-86% depending on stat and window (`paper_beliefs_summary`, `scripts/paper_beliefs.py`). It is the right coefficient for the page's expected-next-N line (validated out of sample) and the wrong number for "how much of the run was real": net of the shuffled null centre, shooting-percentage runs carry on 0-6% and points about 19% over 5-10 games. This is the finite-sample effect Miller & Sanjurjo (2018) describe for streak statistics, measured by shuffling. The Methodology card and its open issues say so; fixing the page means storing the null centre beside each slope in `hot_streak_persistence` (a `build_hot_streak_persistence.py` change) and showing the net share.
- **Four 2025-26 games are missing from the NBA shot chart (found 2026-09-29, not changed):** `player_shots` has no rows at all for three games of 2025-11-19 and one of 2025-11-20 (NBA ids 0022500259-0022500261, 0022500265), although their ESPN play-by-play parsed and reconciled. Anything built on the chart (shot-making, quality map, the expected-points RAPM's pricing) simply lacks those games; in `paper_xrapm_stints` their attempts fall back to the shooter's season mean. Beyond those four, 2025-26's ESPN-to-chart match rate is 97.5% against ≥ 99.8% in every other season (about four unmatched attempts a game, spread over the season), cause not yet found. Re-fetch the chart for those games and re-check the match rate when stats.nba.com is reachable again.
- **Report snapshots are frozen and use fallback fonts (2026-09-29):** a chart in a report is a picture of the chart when it was added (it does not follow the data; **Open live view** goes to the current one), and it is shown through an `<img>`, where the page's web fonts (Space Grotesk, Space Mono) are not available, so its text falls back to the system font. That is the same limit as the SVG/PNG downloads. Print layout was checked on the live page but not in a real print run (headless Chrome would not start here).
- **A few player-seasons carry a team the player never played for (found 2026-09-28, not changed):** from 2020-21 on, 3-5 rows a season in `player_season_stats` list a later team (2024-25: Desmond Bane under ORL, Cole Anthony and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope under MEM, Brandon Ingram under TOR, Maxi Kleber under LAL), checked against the teams in `player_game_lines`. The team page's roster and payroll use the play-by-play's teams and name who was left out; tools that display a player's team from the season row still show the listed one (Methodology open issue). Before 2020-21 there are no per-game teams to check against.
- **`team_game_fatigue.plus_minus` isn't the final margin in 160 games (found 2026-09-28; no stored-data tool reads it since 2026-09-28):** it is stats.nba.com's summed player plus-minus ÷ 5 (values like 33.6), and it differs from the real final score in 160 of 20,348 games 2009-10 to 2025-26 (134 by a point or more; in 109 the two teams' margins don't add to zero, in 14 the sign of a nonzero value disagrees with the stored win flag (re-measured 2026-09-29; was written as 13)). Wins and losses are right (they match Basketball-Reference for every team-season). The column is kept, but every tool now takes margins from `game_scores` (joined on game_id + team): Luck & Schedule, the team page, Game Log and Game Finder (margin column and sort), Schedule Fatigue's rest study, Team Comparison (recent meetings, last-10 form) and Situational Splits' opponent ranks (plus_minus had tied NYK and DAL for 10th in 2020-21, so that season's "top 10" held 11 teams). Still affected: **With/Without a Star** fetches the same field live from stats.nba.com (`impact_core._fetch_team_game_log`), so its average point differential can be off by a fraction of a point; fixing it means moving it to stored data (team games from `game_scores`, who played from `player_game_lines`, 2020-21 on only). It's on its Methodology card. Separately, the ESPN play-by-play's last score (`team_game_totals.pts_for/pts_against`, used by On/Off) differs from ESPN's own final score in 43 of 7,229 games 2020-21 on (e.g. 87-85 for a 113-119 game on 2023-01-12), while `game_scores` sums to Basketball-Reference's season totals exactly.
- March Madness Model data quirks, all handled in code and disclosed on the page: CollegeBasketballData.com is missing two 2017 First Four games (the Tuesday pair), so those winners enter at the Round of 64 and the losers aren't listed (66 teams); 2021's Oregon-VCU no-contest is stored as a 1-0 "scheduled" game, kept in the bracket but excluded from training and scoring; LIU's 2017-18 season is split across two names ("LIU Brooklyn", "Long Island University"), merged by one alias after checking every tournament team for a low pre-tournament game count; VCU 2021 (no NCAA game played) takes its Elo from its last pre-tournament game. The API's `endDateRange` is exclusive (checked live), which the fetcher's month windows account for.
- **stats.nba.com has been unreachable from this machine since 2026-09-26** (every endpoint times out), so `draft_history` is built from Basketball-Reference (`load_draft_history_bref.py`). NBA ids come from `player_id_map` (built by `scripts/bref_nba_ids.py`, see the player-id entry below). 3,642 of 3,747 drafted players who played are matched; 105 aren't: they keep a placeholder id and have no photo, but their Win Shares still come from Basketball-Reference.
- **Pre-2010 players Basketball-Reference and NBA ids can't settle (129 of 3,354):** `player_id_map` was rebuilt 2026-09-27 with `scripts/bref_nba_ids.py` (it used to be exact names only, leaving out Ewing, Payton, Hardaway, Glen Rice, the 1991 #1 Larry Johnson and 228 others, and giving four NBA ids two players' careers). The 129 left out, 407 player-seasons, are mostly nicknames with no shared first-name form and same-name pairs the checks can't separate (the two Mike Davises, Freddie Lewises and Sam Williamses, whose single NBA id the old map had shared); they're skipped rather than guessed. ABA seasons are still out of scope.
- College → NBA Pipeline: a player's last college season is checked against CollegeBasketballData.com player stats, which start in 2013-14, so the 2013 draft class (45 players) is linked by draft year without that check. Torvik's 2013-2025 ratings are end-of-season (tournament games included); fine for describing team strength, not usable as pre-tournament inputs.
- Real height/weight/wingspan/draft-combine data now exists (`draft_combine` table, `scripts/fetch_draft_combine.py`) but only covers players who were actually invited to and measured at a real NBA Draft Combine — not every drafted player attends (e.g. Zion Williamson and Victor Wembanyama both have a real combine row but every measurement field is NULL, since they didn't complete physical testing), and undrafted/international players who skipped it entirely have no row at all. NULL is stored, never guessed, for any test a player skipped. Fit Analysis and archetypes still use statistical clustering as a stand-in for a scouted role, since combine data doesn't cover skill/role.
- No real trade-transaction-history dataset exists for a genuine "Trade Chemistry Simulator" — Lineup Chemistry is the honest, real-data substitute (see above); a true trade simulator remains blocked on data, not effort.
- No real MVP/DPOY/ROY betting-futures market exists on the odds provider used here — checked directly against the live API before building the Vegas Scanner, which is why that feature compares championship odds instead.
- The odds API free tier is quota-limited (~500 req/month); the scanner caches live odds for 6 hours rather than fetching on every page load.
- The WPA model is trained on 7,232 unique real games across seasons 2021-2026 (real full-season ESPN/sportsdataverse coverage; the ~420-game nba_api sample turned out to be entirely games ESPN also has, so after de-duplication every game used is an ESPN copy) — always disclosed via `sample_size_games`/`n_games_train`/`n_games_test` in the relevant API responses rather than implied to be comprehensive. 42,570 of ~3.15M real ESPN-sourced player-attributed events (1.4%) couldn't be matched to a real nba_api `person_id` by name and so carry no per-player WPA attribution — a real, disclosed gap in player-level totals only, not in the model itself (training never uses `person_id`). De-duplicating removed 22 fringe 2024-25 players (two-way/end-of-bench, 2–34 plays each) from `player_wpa_totals` entirely: their only matched plays were in the dropped nba_api copies, and their ESPN plays fall in this unmatched-name gap.
- Live `nba_api`/`stats.nba.com` calls are occasionally flaky (shot-chart data, live player search) — external network reliability, handled with retries/backoff, not a code bug.
- `/games/by-date` (`fetch_nba_games_by_date`) is built around the live scoreboard endpoint, which works fast for today's real games but still returns an empty list for an older historical date — a real, pre-existing limitation of that endpoint discovered while building Schedule Fatigue (which doesn't depend on it — Live Scores' rest tags come from the separately-computed `team_game_fatigue` table). The 45+ real seconds that limitation used to cost on every request is fixed (see below — short timeout + short cache), but the empty-list-for-old-dates behavior itself is unchanged; worth a look if historical date browsing on Live Scores becomes a priority.
- Referee Tendencies' real officials data comes from `BoxScoreSummaryV2`, which has no bulk endpoint (one real call per real game) — `scripts/fetch_referee_officials.py` is resumable and grows real coverage each time it's re-run rather than fetching every real game in one sitting, so `n_games`/season span shown in the UI reflects real coverage as of whenever it was last run, not necessarily every game in scope. Separately, nba_api's own `BoxScoreSummaryV2` is confirmed (checked directly against the live endpoint) to be missing officials for some real games on/after 2025-04-10 — those games are logged as attempted so they aren't retried forever, but contribute no officials rows, a real gap in very recent coverage specifically.
- Garbage-Time Deflator uses ESPN play-by-play only (2020-21 through 2025-26). The small nba_api play-by-play sample is excluded because all 420 of its games are the same real games already in the ESPN data (418 match on date + teams; the other 2 are neutral-site games with a NULL nba_api `home_team`, same date and final score), and the 3 real NBA Cup finals are excluded because the NBA doesn't count them in official regular-season stats. ESPN's text doesn't mark missed step-back/pull-up threes as "three point", so missed 3PA are identified by that phrase or a listed distance of 23+ ft — 99.7% accurate on real made shots, where the real score change gives the true answer (made shots use the real score change directly). Assists are matched by exact name within the same game: 92.7–95.4% matched per season, the rest carry no per-player credit.
- DAD Index describes who a defender guarded and how those shots went — not a full defensive rating (no help defense, rebounding, or scheme). Matchup pairs under 5 real partial possessions were dropped at fetch time, so DAD's possession shares are over ≥ 5-possession pairs.
- **Two age conventions in `player_season_stats` (found 2026-09-28, not changed):** 2009-10+ rows carry NBA.com's `AGE`, which counts age later in the season than Basketball-Reference (February 1); in every season about 45% of players are exactly one year older than on Basketball-Reference (e.g. 2025-26 Kawhi Leonard 35 vs 34). Pre-2010 rows come from Basketball-Reference. Each source is internally consistent, and the award and similarity models were trained on the NBA.com values, so ages were left as they are; age leaderboards spanning 2009-10 mix the two conventions by up to a year. The Breakout Detector's tooltip says so.
- **No EPM or other proprietary impact metric.** EPM is proprietary and isn't used or imitated anywhere (RAPM, since 2026-09-29, is this project's own ridge regression on the play-by-play stints, not an imitation of anyone's). Every "offensive quality" weight in the advanced-analytics features (DAD Index, Spacing Lab validation, Contract Value's WAR) comes from Basketball-Reference's published BPM/OBPM/VORP (since 2026-09-27; before that, this project's own BPM 2.0 reproduction, which ran hot). The reproduction survives only as `*_repro` columns feeding Pair Synergy's trained model.
- Scouting Report has no pick-and-roll coverage type or drive direction at all (not in any real dataset this project uses). Its closest-defender / touch-time / dribble splits (v2) cover 3PT only — 2PT bands mix layups with mid-range jumpers, so they'd mostly restate where a player shoots. At p < 0.05 about 1 in 20 tested splits clears the bar by chance; the card always shows that expected count. Play-type significance rests on an estimated per-possession SD (Synergy publishes no per-possession outcomes). "Toughest defenders" samples are small (10+ shots) and descriptive.
- Gravity is a composite proxy from tracking *splits*, not true gravity (how far defenders shade toward a player off the ball) — that needs raw defender coordinates, which aren't public. The Spacing Lab's predicted ORtg change is a real across-lineup association with a modest effect size, not a forecast for any specific lineup, and is withheld outside the range of real lineups. `lineup_stats` stores each season's 2,000 most-used lineups (the endpoint's cap), so very rare lineups aren't on file.
- **Contract Value salary data is third-party and imperfect — read before trusting a number.** The two salary CSVs are Kaggle uploads the owner downloaded (a 2000–2020 file and "NBA Player Stats and Salaries 2010-2025"), built by their uploaders from HoopsHype and Basketball-Reference, with no stated license — so they're kept local in `nba_data/salaries/` (gitignored, not redistributed); to rebuild, download them into that folder as `nba_salaries_2000_2020.csv` and `nba_player_stats_salaries_2010_2025.csv`, then run `load_salaries.py` and `build_contract_value.py` (the Contract Value smoke test skips if they were never loaded). Problems found by checking against real contracts: the 2010–2025 file's 2020-21 to 2023-24 salaries are inflation-adjusted (every player scaled by the same factor per season, e.g. ×1.156 for 2021-22), so those seasons are excluded rather than reconstructed; the 2000–2020 file's 2000–2005 payrolls are incomplete (excluded), it repeats stale rows for retired players (e.g. Cuttino Mobley at $9.5M through 2012-13 — why only players who actually played are priced, making cost per win the price of on-court production rather than total spending, with dead money and injured players left out), its 2017-18 is missing major players (excluded by the 90% minutes-coverage gate), and some individual rows are wrong (both files list Stephen Curry 2017-18 at his 2018-19 salary). Scraping the sources directly isn't possible: Basketball-Reference sits behind a Cloudflare bot challenge and its terms forbid automated collection, and HoopsHype's robots.txt blocks AI agents by name. WAR depends on this project's VORP reproduction (rescaled to real wins, not replaced by Basketball-Reference's numbers). No minimum-contract label is inferred (the full years-of-service scale isn't sourced for every season).
- `player_shots` includes real playoff (`game_id` `004…`) and play-in (`005…`) shots alongside the regular season (`002…`); anything computing regular-season shooting from it must filter on `game_id`. Separately, every bulk-loaded row has a NULL `shot_zone_basic`, so zones always come from `shots_lib.classify_zone()`'s coordinate rule — corrected 2026-09-26 (see Just shipped).
- **Shot-making has no defender or shot-type context.** No shot in `player_shots` carries a closest-defender distance or a shot type (catch-and-shoot vs. pull-up), so "shot-making" (actual minus expected eFG%) also includes the defence a player faced and the shots he created for himself; a rim-runner's finishing counts the same as a shooter's jumper. Before 2010-11 about a quarter of shots (nearly all at the rim) have no exact location and sit at (0, 0), and 11-17% of threes a season (1997-98 on) carry `shot_distance = 0`, so the model uses coordinates only and treats the (0, 0) rows season by season.
- **`player_game_lines` is rebuilt from ESPN play-by-play text, not box scores.** Players with no row in `player_season_stats` (fringe call-ups such as Mamadi Diakite or Kenneth Lofton Jr. in some seasons) can't be given an NBA id, so they have no line of their own (everyone else's minutes are unaffected). A missed shot is a two or a three as the NBA shot chart calls the same shot where the two feeds match (~99% of attempts), else as the text does ("three point", or 23+ ft); season totals are within 0.25% of NBA.com's, three-point attempts included (0.9996-1.0005 a season since 2026-09-29; the text alone left them 0.6-1.9% short). Rebound % chances exclude team rebounds, so they run about 13% below NBA.com's denominator (the Stat Stability page converts).
- **Per-game on-court plus-minus from `player_game_lines` isn't reliable, so the Game Log doesn't show it** (checked 2026-09-28; since 2026-10-03 the right numbers are in `player_game_onfloor`, which the Workbench reads; the Game Log and Game Finder still don't show +/-, and the lines' `tm_pts`/`op_pts` are unchanged). In 9,696 of 12,880 team-games (75%) whose player minutes add up to a full game (within 1 s; re-measured by `paper_data_audit.py` 2026-09-29, was 9,194 of 12,961 on 2026-09-28 before two rebuilds of the lines), the team's summed on-court margin (`tm_pts - op_pts`) is exactly 5x the final margin; in the rest it's off by multiples of 5, i.e. some stretches have the wrong number of players on the floor even though total minutes are right. Box-score lines themselves are unaffected (season totals above). The Game Log and Game Finder also can't show games a player sat out (no row), and a game where a player's name couldn't be matched is missing from his log: the profile warns when the play-by-play has fewer games than NBA.com lists for that season.
- **`lineup_stats` is capped at each season's 2,000 most-used lineups** (stats.nba.com's `LeagueDashLineups` limit when `scripts/fetch_spacing_data.py` stored them), 31–89% of a team's regulation minutes. Since 2026-09-28 it is used only for seasons before 2020-21 (Pair Chemistry, Lineup Chemistry and the team page say so per season); from 2020-21 every stint comes from play-by-play (`lineup_stints`), with its own disclosed gaps below.
- **One `player_game_lines` row has team `'NaN'` (found 2026-09-29, not changed):** Miye Oni, 2021-11-20 (`espn_401360071`), 195 seconds, no stats — the parser found no team for him in that game. Harmless for season totals; `build_assist_network.py` skips it. Fixing it is part of the same `player_game_lines` rebuild as the items below.
- **Availability-aware odds are an upper bound (2026-10-01, round 6 step 6):** they use who played, known at tip-off (injury reports, the inactive list) but not when a forecast is usually made; late scratches and a player hurt in the first minute count as playing. Player ratings are fixed before the season (rookies at replacement level all year), minutes are averages of earlier games, and "sat" on the what-if means played for the team earlier and not for another team since (long injuries are listed every game; a waived player stays listed). Before 2025-26 players ESPN gives no id look absent; games with such minutes (1,230) gain more than the rest because call-ups without ids play when regulars sit, and 2025-26, every player identified, gains as much as any season.
- **Timeouts and coach's challenges in ESPN's log (checked 2026-10-01, round 6 step 5):** one timeout type ("Full Timeout", always with a team), so a mandatory television break charged to a team can't be told from a coach's choice; ESPN's timeout times match NBA.com's own log to within 1 s for 77% of timeouts in the 418 twin games of 2024-25, but about one in five is stamped late (95th percentile 18 s), so Coaching Decisions places timeouts by the log's order, not its clock. ESPN rewrites the play-by-play after an overturned challenge (the overturned call disappears and the corrected call takes its place), so what a coach challenged can be read only from lost challenges; success by call type isn't reported. 527 challenges have no outcome ("replaycenter", and 25 lone "Challenge" records). Ref-initiated reviews (2023-24 on) are logged but not used.
- **ESPN's clock is late, by event type (measured 2026-10-01 against NBA.com's own play-by-play of the same 418 games of 2024-25):** made field goals a median 14 s (within 2 s for only 14% of them), made later free throws 13 s, rebounds 6 s, turnovers 5 s (steals) or 10 s (dead ball, bimodal: on time or 11-15 s late), misses 2 s; fouls called with the clock stopped and the first free throw of a trip are on time. It looks like ESPN stamps a live-ball event at about the time of the next thing that happens. The round-5 audit's pooled "ESPN trails the chart by a median 4 s" is this mix. The rebuilt clock (`pbp_possessions.corrected_clock()`, 93.9% of events within 2 s of NBA.com's) is stored once, since 2026-10-01 (round 6 step 3b), in `pbp_event_clock` (`scripts/build_event_clock.py`). **On the corrected clock:** `possessions`, Rotations' closing stretch (`rotation_closing_*`), the Play Finder (`play_finder_events`), Best Games (through the Play Finder), Coaching Decisions (`coaching_decisions`, through `possessions`) and Game Replay's timeline (ESPN games; nba_api games keep NBA.com's own clock). Clutch WPA (`compute_wpa.py`, and with it `paper_beliefs.py`'s clutch labels) since 2026-10-02 (round 6 step 12). **Still on ESPN's times:** the Garbage-Time Deflator / leverage splits (`build_leverage_splits.py`, its win probability and clutch labels: not rebuilt and not measured, expected negligible, since garbage time is a 99% win probability or an 18-point second-half margin, which a few seconds barely move); Guess the Game's curves; and, on purpose, `lineup_stints` and the player minutes (substitutions happen at dead balls, where ESPN is on time). Situational Splits read no clock. Game Replay's win-probability model was fitted on ESPN's times; on the corrected clock the same model scores slightly better, not worse (last minute of the fourth/OT: log loss 0.1652 → 0.1628, calibration error 0.0175 → 0.0158; `pbp_event_clock_meta.wp_check`), so it was not refitted (a refit on the corrected clock is optional, the owner's call). Two ESPN free throws logged "k of n" with no first free throw take the previous trip's time (2 of 142,259 later free throws); in one game (espn_401468511) that drags two substitutions back and an 11 s event-less stint disappears from Rotations' glue-back check. The time a turnover happened can't be recovered from ESPN at all, so `possessions` leaves time-to-first-attempt and transition NULL after turnovers.
- **`lineup_stints`' per-side 3PA still use ESPN's text call on misses (2026-09-29):** `player_game_lines` and the Play Finder take the NBA shot chart's two-or-three call (`Game(..., miss_threes=)`), the stints don't (their `home_fg3a`/`away_fg3a` are shown nowhere, and points, possessions and everything built on them don't depend on it). If a lineup three-point stat is ever shown, rebuild the stints with `miss_three_calls()` passed in first (then rerun RAPM, rotations and rim deterrence).
- **Best games' excitement weights are a judgment, and its swing isn't Game Replay's raw sum (2026-09-29):** `excitement = swing + 0.10 × lead changes + 1.0 × overtime periods − 0.05 × final margin` (`api/best_games.py`) has no ground truth to fit, so the three weights are stated choices (the ranking barely moves without them: ρ 0.986 with the plain swing). The swing is read on the reconciled score (`play_finder_events`), while Game Replay's chart reads ESPN's raw score fields, which step backwards in 1,978 games; in those games a game's swing here can differ a little from the sum read off the chart. The win-probability model has no possession, so a steal-and-score swings it like a made shot. Upset odds don't know injuries or rest decisions (see the Season Simulator's limits).
- **The Game Log overcounts minutes in 9 player-games (found 2026-09-29, not changed):** in 9 ESPN games (2020-21 to 2023-24) one substitution is logged with no team ("Luka Doncic enters the game for "); the player-lines replay (`Game.run()` in `scripts/pbp_lineups.py`) then opens a phantom lineup for "no team" and counts that player's minutes on both, e.g. Dončić 45.4 minutes on 2022-01-30 instead of 37.1. The stints (`Game.stints()`) only track the two real teams and are right, so the Rotations chart shows the right minutes there and differs from the Game Log in exactly these 9 player-games (+9 s to +503 s; the smoke test pins them). Fixing it changes `player_game_lines` and its chain (the same rebuild as the id fallback below), so it waits for the owner's OK.
- **ESPN names matched to the wrong player (found and fixed 2026-09-29):** `fetch_pbp_espn.py` matched each ESPN name to an NBA id through that season's `player_season_stats`, exact first, then fuzzy (WRatio ≥ 90), and stored the matched name over ESPN's. Two players with no row that season landed on a same-surname player: Keon Johnson (Nets 2023-24, 59 events in 5 games) became Keldon Johnson, and Jalen McDaniels (Wizards 2024-25, 7 substitutions in 4 games) became Jaden McDaniels, giving both phantom games for a team they never played for (Keldon's 2023-24 log read 74 games against NBA.com's 69). `scripts/repair_espn_player_ids.py` finds such events by rule (none of a game-team-id group's descriptions contain the stored name, and exactly one other known player's name, a fuzzy match for it, appears in all of them) and fixed exactly those 66 events; the fetch now tries an exact name in any season before fuzzy matching. Left alone: 64 single events whose text doesn't name the tagged player although that player's other events of the game do (56 name another player of the game, 54 of them a teammate, e.g. a Kevin Durant rebound tagged to Russell Westbrook; re-measured by `paper_data_audit.py`, 2026-09-29); one of the two is wrong and nothing on file says which. The smoke test checks no player has lines for two teams on one date.
- **Players ESPN gives no id to are invisible to the lineup parser (found 2026-09-28, not changed):** 8–9.5k events a season (100–155 names, two-way and 10-day players such as Mamadi Diakite, Kenneth Lofton Jr., Braxton Key) carry a name but no `person_id`, and `scripts/pbp_lineups.py` resolves names only through `player_season_stats`, where these players have no row. They get no `player_game_lines` row, and every stint they were on the floor for has four identified players and is left out of `lineup_seasons` / `pair_seasons`: 6.3% of minutes in 2020-21, 6.0% in 2021-22, 2.5%, 3.8%, 2.0%, and 0.001% in 2025-26 (ESPN now ids almost everyone). `player_bio` / `player_id_map` have their NBA ids, so a fallback there would fix it, but it changes `player_game_lines` and the chain built on it (`build_player_on_off.py`, `build_stat_stability.py`, `build_hot_streak_persistence.py`, `build_situational_splits.py`, `build_projections.py`), so it is a deliberate separate rebuild, not a quiet fix.
- **`player_game_lines`' on-court points (`tm_pts`/`op_pts`) double-count in games with a stale ESPN score field:** they credit positive score changes, which reconcile with the real final in only 512 of 1,080 games in 2020-21, 505 of 1,230 in 2021-22 and 547 of 1,230 in 2022-23 (nearly all from 2023-24 on). This is the cause of the known "on-court +/- doesn't add to 5× the margin in 25% of team-games" gap (29% before the lines' 2026-09-29 rebuilds). `lineup_stints` credits points from the made shots and free throws themselves (running-maximum score as fallback) and reconciles 7,220 of 7,232 games; the lines were left as they are so nothing built on them changed. In 9 player-games a substitution ESPN tagged to no team makes the lines credit a player's seconds twice; the stints credit them once.
- **A Workbench scatter's grey dots stop at 5,000 rows** (round 7 step 4): past the query cap the grey is left out (and so is a fit over it) rather than drawing an arbitrary first 5,000; the block says how many rows matched and to narrow the seasons or raise the games floor (player-games over more than about one season always pass it). Bands, boxes, histograms and heatmap rows summarise every row (`/workbench/context`, up to 200,000). Every row counts once in a fit and in the grey percentiles: a 20-game season weighs as much as an 82-game one.
- **Most tools that take a typed player name still look it up by name** (found 2026-10-03, round 7 step 5): `impact_core.find_player()` takes the first match, and 19 names belong to two players (two Brandon Williams, two Mike James, ...). Shot Charts (every tab, the quality map's comparison player too) and the Workbench pick players by NBA id; Player Comparison (and its shot zones, `/shots/player/{name}/zones`), Trend Analysis, Radar, Scouting Report, With/Without a Star and the other callers of `find_player(` under `api/` don't yet. Moving one means passing `player_id` through `impact_core.resolve_player()`, as `/shots/player/{name}`, `/zone-history`, `/shots/quality-map` and `/shot-making` do.
- **Workbench boards live in one browser** (round 7 step 3): IndexedDB, like reports; there are no accounts or server copies, so a board doesn't follow its owner to another device and clearing site data deletes it (the page says so). A share link carries a *copy* of the board (under 2,000 characters; bigger boards go by exported file), and its numbers are fetched live, so a shared board shows the data as it is when opened, not as it was when shared. On phones (< 760 px) blocks can be reordered but not resized (step 8 is the phone pass).
- **`lineup_stints` credits a free throw to the players on the floor at the shot; the official box score credits the players on the floor at the foul** (found 2026-10-03, round 7 step 2). Substitutions are made between free throws, so the two disagree in many games: per player-game, crediting at the shot matches ESPN's box-score +/- for 42.5% of player-games (300 random games), crediting at the foul for 98.2%. `player_game_onfloor` (the Workbench's per-game +/-) uses the box score's rule. The stints and everything built on them (lineup and pair ratings, RAPM, the Rating Tracker, expected-points RAPM) keep crediting at the shot: a team's totals are the same either way, only which five gets a free throw moves, and switching would mean rebuilding that whole chain (owner's call). The remaining ~2% of player-games off are mostly one free throw whose foul ESPN logs on the far side of the substitutions.

## Roadmap / what's next

This project is being built from a longer plan, executed feature-by-feature with full verification each time. Everything above is done, verified, and pushed. In priority order, what's left (originally scoped by a planning session, adjusted where building earlier items changed the scope of later ones):

1. **Advanced analytics plan (owner-provided, 2026-09-25)** — five features, built in this order, one at a time, full verification each. Plan rules: never fabricate data, show n on every stat, grey out small samples, label heuristics as heuristics, show a validation metric for every model.
   - ~~**F1 — Garbage-Time Deflator**~~ — shipped (see below).
   - ~~**F2 — Defensive Assignment Difficulty (DAD) Index**~~ — shipped (see below). Original plan: `DAD = Σ(share of a defender's partial possessions vs. player i × OBPM of player i)`, offensive players under 500 min = replacement (−2.0), z-scored per season, defenders qualified at ≥ 1,000 partial possessions; plus a new one-call-per-season `LeagueDashPtDefend` fetch for DFG% vs. normal FG%. Scatter DAD × DFG% differential with quadrant labels; name it "DAD Index (OBPM-weighted)". **Plan-vs-codebase notes, checked:** the plan says `season_matchups` — the real table is `player_matchups` (2017-18+, rows under 5 partial possessions already dropped at fetch time, so DAD's possession shares are over ≥5-poss pairs only — disclose). OBPM is the `obpm` column on `player_season_stats`, from `build_bpm_vorp.py` — the project's own disclosed *reproduction* of BPM 2.0, not Basketball-Reference's numbers.
   - ~~**F3 — Scouting Report ("Exploit Guide") v1 + v2**~~ — shipped (see below). Original plan: v1 from existing data (`player_playtypes` PPP, shot zones from `player_shots`, `player_matchups` best defenders, F1's leverage splits), significance-filtered (two-proportion z-test / CIs, p < 0.05, min n), template text with real numbers, and an explicit "what this report can't tell you" line (no pick-and-roll coverage type, no drive direction). v2 adds `PlayerDashPtShots` (one call per player, ~450/season: FG% by closest-defender distance, touch time, dribbles).
   - ~~**F4 — Gravity Index & Spacing Lab**~~ — shipped (see below). Original plan: disclosed composite proxy (z(3PA/100) + z(C&S 3P%, shrunk with a 250-attempt prior) + z(tight/open 3PA share)), reusing F3's `PlayerDashPtShots` fetch. Validation regresses real 5-man lineup ORtg (≥ 100 poss) on lineup spacing + summed OBPM; show a predicted ORtg change only if the coefficient is significant. **Plan-vs-codebase note:** the plan references a `lineup_stats` table — it does not exist; Lineup Chemistry live-fetches `LeagueDashLineups` per request. F4 needs a new pipeline script that stores multi-season 5-man lineups first.
   - ~~**F5 — Contract Surplus Value**~~ — shipped (see below), after the owner's go-ahead and a feasibility check. Original plan: ⚠️ **sensitive, needs the owner's explicit go-ahead in that session before starting** (scraping Basketball-Reference salary pages: check their terms/robots.txt first — `build_bpm_vorp.py`'s docstring already records that the site blocks automated fetches). WAR = VORP × 2.7, cost-per-win computed from real payroll/wins (never assumed), surplus = fair value − salary.
   - ~~Docs when done~~ — done 2026-09-26: every feature is in "What's in it", a **Data sources** section lists every upstream source, and Known real gaps covers no pick-and-roll coverage / drive-direction data, no EPM, and the salary data's real provenance (Kaggle uploads, not scraped — scraping was ruled out).
   - **Plan complete.** Across the five features, checking real output before shipping caught and fixed: a shot-zone classifier bug affecting every shot chart, stale 2025-26 season stats, 418 duplicate play-by-play games, a non-shooter bias in the plan's Gravity shrinkage, an extrapolating Spacing Lab prediction, 2PT context splits that just restated shot location, clutch splits that don't persist, inflation-adjusted and stale salary rows, and a VORP reproduction running ~1.3–2.0× hot against real wins.
2. **B5 — Ask the Database in plain English** (optional, costs Anthropic API spend, needs owner buy-in first): natural-language question → LLM-generated single read-only `SELECT` → real safety checks (reject anything but `SELECT`/`WITH...SELECT`, read-only transaction, statement timeout, row limit, ideally a dedicated read-only Postgres role) → results table shown alongside the exact SQL that ran, so the answer is verifiably real.

Everything else in the original roadmap, including A6, is shipped — B5 stays parked on purpose (real API spend, needs owner buy-in before starting).

**Decided:** Bklit UI was installed (commit 5b0960e) and then removed at the owner's request (2026-09-27): no page used it, its area chart labels dates only (most of this app is season data), and it added Tailwind, an alpha visx and a second copy of framer-motion. Charts remain hand-built SVG. The install commit documents how to add one Bklit component safely if a hard-to-build chart type (sankey, choropleth) is ever needed.

**Just shipped:** Shot Charts counts regular-season shots only and picks players by id (2026-10-03, after round 7 step 5; was in Known real gaps). **Games:** `/shots/player/{name}` returns every stored shot of a season, playoffs (`004…`) and play-in (`005…`) included, and the page drew and counted them all; it now has a Games choice beside the season, **Regular season** (default, like the quality map, shot-making and shot mix), **Playoffs and play-in** or **All games** (`games=` in the link, `SHOT_GAMES` in `utils/shotTotals.js`, now shared with the Workbench's shot chart block), and says how many of the season's shots it leaves out. Checked against `player_shots`: Curry 2024-25 regular season 1,258 shots, 44.8% (the shot mix's own count), playoffs and play-in 152 shots, 71 made (46.7%), threes 38 of 93. **Players by id:** the search suggestions now come from `GET /workbench/entities` (stored data, accents ignored) with each player's span and latest team, and picking one loads him by NBA id; a typed name (Enter) takes the latest player of exactly that name, otherwise the old name lookup; the link carries `pid=` beside the readable `player=`, and a bare-name link still opens (the latest player of that name). When another player has the same name the page says so with a link to him (`utils/playerChoice.js`). The id is passed to every tab: dots/heat map, shot-making (`/shots/player/{name}/shot-making` takes an optional `player_id` now; without it the response is unchanged, tested), quality map (also the "compare with" player, `vsid=`), shot value and shot mix; the profile's "Open in Shot Charts" passes it too. **Checked in the browser** (1280 and 375 px, no horizontal overflow): `?player=Brandon Williams` opens the one of 2021-22 on (617 regular-season shots in 2025-26) with "Another player is also called Brandon Williams: the one of 1997-98 to 2002-03", and that link opens him (7 shots in 2002-03); suggestions for "mike jam" list both Mike James with their spans; the quality map compared with the Mike James of 2001-02 to 2012-13 asked `/shots/quality-map?player_id=2229`. Still looked up by name elsewhere: Player Comparison and other tools (Known real gaps). Tests: the shot `player_id` test in `test_workbench.py` now also covers shot-making and the entity order the page relies on. No table; nothing to sync.

**Just shipped (previously):** The app's tools as Workbench blocks + "Open in Workbench" (2026-10-03, round 7 step 5; see What's in it): an **App tool** block with ten tools (player card, shot chart, shot quality map, shot mix history, game log, Rating Tracker, RAPM, projection; a team's rotation chart and assist network), each bound to one member of a set and rendered by the page's own component, and an **Open in Workbench** button on the Leaderboard Builder, Player Comparison, the player profile and the team page. **Checked in the browser** (1280 and 375 px, Paper and Ink; no horizontal overflow at 375): Jokić's board from his profile (shot chart 2025-26: 1,114 regular-season shots, 634 made, 56.9%, the profile's own counts; card 27.7 / 12.9 / 10.7 in 65 games; RAPM 2020-21 +1.8 ± 1.0, #69), the 2023-24 Celtics from the team page (64-18; rotation 82 of 82 games, 10-6 in 16 close games), the Leaderboard's 2024-25 3P% top 10 (table and bars equal the leaderboard: LaVine 44.6%, Durant 43.0%), Curry vs LeBron 2024-25 (Curry 70 games, 24.5 points); a block follows its set (adding Wembanyama to an empty set drew his 1,080 shots); "Not on file" reasons for Bill Russell (shots, game log) and the 2014-15 Celtics (rotation); an empty set, a set of the wrong kind; OKC 2024-25 assists 2,189 of 3,648 (60.0%, #27), equal to `/assists/team`. A share link with ten tool blocks (633 characters) gives back every position, setting and member; an imported board with an unknown tool loses that block (counted), a non-id member, an out-of-place view and a text season are cleared. **Found and fixed on the way:** the shot endpoints look players up by name, and 19 names belong to two players, so a set member could have been shown someone else's shots (the Brandon Williams of 2021-22 on would have got the 1997-98 to 2002-03 one's): they take `player_id` now; the Shot Charts page mixes playoff and play-in shots into "every shot" (Known real gaps; the page is unchanged, the block defaults to the regular season). **The pages are unchanged:** HTML of the Shot Charts page (dots, heat map, cards, zones, legend), three player profiles and two team pages rendered from HEAD and from this commit are byte-identical (apart from the new button), and the seven shot/profile responses saved before the backend change are byte-identical after it. Tests: 1 new in `test_workbench.py` (the id parameter on all three endpoints; by name unchanged). No table; nothing to sync.
