**Just shipped (previously):** Workbench charts (2026-10-03, round 7 step 4; see What's in it): a Chart block with scatter, line, bars, histogram, distribution and heatmap on Observable Plot 0.6.17 (ISC; its 53 new dependencies ISC/MIT/BSD-3/Unlicense; lazy-loaded with the block), the set in its colours over the whole population in grey, a clustered trend line with r and its interval, n and the legend inside the SVG. Two endpoints: `POST /workbench/context` (percentiles / histogram over every matching row, no row cap; reads past 5,000 rows, e.g. all 26,000+ player-games of 2025-26) and `POST /workbench/trend`; `/workbench/query`'s rows were moved into a shared `_run()` and its output checked byte-identical before/after on four specs (every dataset). `stats_lib.wls_cluster` also returns `cov` and `tcrit` (callers read named keys; nothing else changed). **Checked:** r and the slope equal numpy's corrcoef/polyfit on the same rows; player seasons 2021-22 to 2025-26 with 20+ games (2,171 rows, 735 players): usage vs true shooting r 0.01 (−0.05 to 0.08), threes attempted vs 3P% 0.43 (0.38 to 0.48), assists vs turnovers 0.85 (0.82 to 0.87); 2025-26 minutes vs points 0.88 (0.86 to 0.89, 452 players). Tooltips read SGA 2023-24 TS 63.6% and Curry 2024-25 24.5 points / 6.0 assists in 70 games (the season table's values). All six types drawn in the browser at 1280 and 375 px (no horizontal overflow at 375), Paper and Ink, from a 4-player and a 3-team set. **Found and fixed on the way:** r's interval first came from the clustered fit on standardised x and y, which matched a cluster bootstrap near r = 0 but ran 2-3 times too wide for strong relationships (0.82-0.93 for minutes vs points; bootstrap 0.86-0.89) because it treats both SDs as known, so r now gets a cluster bootstrap (a test keeps it within 2× of Fisher's z width when every player appears once); the heatmap's red-blue scale wasn't centred on zero (a −1 BPM cell was deep red); switching a chart to player games kept a "20+ games" floor that emptied it (a floor now applies only where rows combine games); the grey copy of a set member's season took its tooltip. **`utils/chartExport.js` exports ~3× smaller files:** it no longer writes a value a node inherits unchanged from its parent (a 2,171-dot scatter 1.06 MB → 346 KB, 3,738-element small multiples 1.80 MB → 599 KB); checked by rendering every export in a blank iframe and comparing each element's fill, stroke, opacity, stroke width/dash and font with the live chart: 0 differences on the 8 Workbench charts (7,041 elements) and on 6 charts of Era Translator, Aging Curves, Model Report Card and Possession Explorer; still no `var(--`, `class=` or `<style>` in any export, and Ink charts still snapshot as Paper. Tests: 6 new in `test_workbench.py` (context quantiles/histogram equal numpy and read past the cap; trend equals numpy, intervals cover, seeded, not too wide; refusals). No table; nothing to sync.

**Just shipped (previously):** The Workbench page (2026-10-03, round 7 step 3; see What's in it): boards of Set, Table and Note blocks on a 12-column grid, drag/resize with pointer and keyboard, boards in IndexedDB, share links, file export/import, Save. Checked in the browser at 1280 and 375 px, Paper and Ink: a 3-player set (Curry, LeBron, Jokić) bound to two tables gives LeBron 2021-22 30.3 points in 56 games and Jokić 2024-25 29.6 / 12.7 / 10.2 in 70 games (the season table's values), and a team set's table OKC 2024-25 68-14, Boston 2023-24 64-18; a share link gives back every block's position, size and settings and every set member exactly (new ids); a hand-made link with markup in names and notes, a bad block type, string player ids, an out-of-range colour and a SQL-looking dataset key comes back cleaned (markup shown as text, the bad parts dropped and counted); a cut-off link is refused with a clear message; exported files re-import with the same blocks and a repeat import adds nothing. **Found and fixed on the way:** the Report builder's autosave hook (moved to `utils/useAutosave.js`, now shared) saved a box's stale text when it unmounted even if nobody had typed in it, which overwrote a name changed elsewhere (a set renamed Teams 1 by its kind switch came back as Players 1); now only typed text is saved and an untouched box follows outside changes (Report builder checked: name and text still save on blur and after typing stops). **The top bar overflowed between 1,024 and ~1,200 px already** (by up to ~100 px), and the Workbench tab made 1,280 overflow by 37 px: the hamburger now takes over below 1,240 px (was 1,024) and items are tighter (8 px padding) up to 1,439 px; measured at 1,100 / 1,240 / 1,280 / 1,440 / 1,920 px, nothing past the right edge. Tests: 5 new in `test_workbench.py` (accent-insensitive search, ids lookup equal to the season table's span, wildcards and quotes as plain text, request checks, the team list equals the catalogue's franchises). No table; nothing to sync.

**Just shipped (previously):** Per-game on-floor plus-minus (2026-10-03, round 7 step 2; see What's in it). Step 1 had left per-game on-court +/- and on-court points out of the Workbench because the game lines' are wrong in 1 team-game in 4. Rebuilt from the stints first, they added up to 5 × the margin wherever a side had five players all game but still matched ESPN's box-score +/- for only 43% of player-games (95% within 2); placing points by the corrected clock didn't help (the late-logged made shots aren't the cause), crediting free throws to the lineup at the foul took it to 95%, and skipping team rebounds and player-less entries (timeouts) inside a free-throw trip to 98%. New table `player_game_onfloor` (22 MB) + `player_game_onfloor_meta`; Workbench columns `plus_minus`, `onfloor_pts_for`, `onfloor_pts_against` verified (only the two-convention season age stays excluded); Data Coverage entry; `paper_manifest.PRODUCERS` + a `rebuild_all.sh` step after `build_lineup_stints.py`; `paper-inputs` rerun (218 tables, digest `ba325e4fa0eedc8a`). Tests: 7 known-facts cases (every player's box-score +/- in the five 70+ point games, the 5 × margin identity, seconds equal to the stints) and a Workbench test. Not on Layerbase (two new tables, ~22 MB, for the round-7 sync).

**Just shipped (previously):** Workbench data catalogue + safe query API (2026-10-03, round 7 step 1; see What's in it). No table created or changed, nothing to sync. Checked against the data before writing: on-court possessions from the game lines add up to five times the team's in 11,559 of 11,560 fully tracked team-games (so per-100 on game lines is offered), while on-court points don't (so on-court +/- isn't); the play-by-play rebuild's team turnovers differ from NBA.com's box score in about half of team-games, so team-game possessions use NBA.com's box score (`game_team_box`, all 14,460 team-games of 2020-21 on); summing the players' lines doesn't give team totals (players with no id are missing), so no team box is built from them. Found and gated: `team_seasons.attend_g` holds per-game attendance before 1980-81 where the season total is missing, and those figures don't match known crowds (Knicks 1979-80: 1,432 a game), so attendance starts in 1980-81. Basketball-Reference's team turnover and rebound percentages are stored in percent units (11.2), so the catalogue divides them by 100: every percentage in it is a share. Wins from the final scores equal Basketball-Reference's for all 510 team-seasons of 2009-10 on (a test). The Leaderboard Builder's and Game Finder's catalogues now come from the Workbench catalogue: 184 saved responses (every Leaderboard stat three ways, Composite, Stability, Regression, Breakouts, Era Translator, four player profiles, Game Log, every Game Finder stat as sort, condition and streak) byte-identical before and after. `pydantic` added to `requirements.txt` (imported directly now; same version as installed).

**Just shipped (previously):** Round 6 into the paper (2026-10-02, round 6 step 12). **Clock follow-ups first:** `compute_wpa.py` now reads `pbp_event_clock` (`load_events()` joins it; every deduplicated event has a row), so Clutch WPA and `paper_beliefs.py`'s clutch labels (it calls the same loader) are on the corrected clock. Diffed against a snapshot (dropped after): league points per scoring chance at equal leverage clutch 0.843 → 0.840, non-clutch 0.914 → 0.917, clutch chances 70,897 → 69,647, leverage ratio 3.7 either way; players with 100+ clutch chances 186 → 184, clutch lift r 0.99998 with before, top-10 clutch WPA the same players (DeRozan, Jokić, Fox, Curry first). `paper_beliefs.py --only clutch` rerun (full permutation counts): 100+ chances 12 of 186 at p < 0.05 (9.3 expected) → 11 of 184 (9.2), 50+ 18 of 314 → 19 of 308, **0 survive FDR before and after: no clutch conclusion moved** (league shift −0.071 → −0.077). **Audit class 18, `clock_lag`:** `paper_data_audit.clock_lag_checks()` re-measures ESPN's lag behind NBA.com's play-by-play with `build_event_clock.clock_check()` on its own parse and shot match of the 420 twin games of 2024-25 and stops unless it equals `pbp_event_clock_meta`'s stored check (it does, exactly): made shots 14 s, later free throws 13, rebounds 6, steals 5, dead-ball turnovers 10, misses 2; 31.9% of 141,348 events within 2 s (corrected clock 93.9%); handling "worked around". `build_data_quality.py` calls it too (rerun: all 267 audit rows and every test row reproduce); in `api/data_quality_lib.py` the class is build-only and not per game (on every game). Clock-related Methodology cards (`clutch`, `wp`, `dataquality`) and the Data Coverage text updated. **Paper (`paper/`, untracked):** copies before the step in `paper/versions/*_2026-10-02_r6.tex`. `scripts/paper_numbers.py` gained nine sections (`possessions_clock`, `data_quality_effect`, `rating_tracker`, `shot_value`, `lineup_predictor`, `availability`, `ledger`, `report_card`, `coaching`; 1,348 macros now, every round-6 sentence pinned by a claim; the ledger section's claim *fails on purpose once a 2026-27 game is scored*, so the forward-test sentence gets rewritten with `ledger_tests`' intervals) and a shared `Pairs` reader for any table with paper_tests' columns. `scripts/paper_figures.py` gained `fig_possessions`, `fig_quality`, `fig_reportcard` and two rows in the forest plot (tracker − BPM, shooter-aware − shooter-blind xRAPM; 270 plotted numbers checked). Long paper: abstract and five contributions rewritten; new subsections Possessions and a corrected clock, Does it matter? (data quality), Ratings that carry across seasons (tracker), A shooter-aware price; new sections From Players to Lineups and Season by Season (report card); Who played and A forecast locked in advance in the pre-game section; Coaching decisions (with a table) under Popular beliefs; Tables rapm/tests gained the tracker and xRAPM-SA rows; limitations and conclusion updated (injuries now an upper bound; look-ahead fixed by the shooter-aware price; season-to-season variation measured; new: coaching is matched not randomised, lineup noise model, no forward game scored yet). Four references added after checking each on Crossref (+ OpenAlex abstracts; DerSimonian–Laird via the authors' 2015 revisit on PubMed): Kalman 1960, Rauch–Tung–Striebel 1965, DerSimonian–Laird 1986, Hartung–Knapp 2001 (`paper_refs_check.py --online` OK, 40 entries). Builds (`paper_build.sh`): long 18 pages (was 13), 8p 8, 6p 6, all six with 0 overfull and 0 undefined; the short versions only lost two clauses to absorb the audit table's new row (they don't carry the round-6 results: trimming waits for the venue). `rebuild_all.sh paper-inputs` rerun (216 tables, digest `3c69fc0ab3299472`). **Tables changed (for the round-6 Layerbase sync):** `player_wpa_totals` (880 rows), `wpa_clutch_league` (1), `paper_beliefs` (89,263, 19 MB), `paper_beliefs_summary`, `paper_beliefs_meta`, `paper_data_audit` (267), `paper_data_audit_classes` (18), `data_quality_game_flags`, `data_quality_sensitivity`, `data_quality_meta` (rewritten, same contents but the audit check). Note: `paper_beliefs_meta.runtime_seconds` now holds the clutch-only run's time.

**Just shipped (previously):** Data Quality (2026-10-02, round 6 step 11; see What's in it). The audit's 17 error classes on a page: 14 re-measured live by independent SQL (110 numbers, all equal to `paper_data_audit` at the paper's printed precision; slowest check ~2.5 s), 3 (wrong player, tag/text, missed threes) checked by the build, which re-runs every one of `paper_data_audit.py`'s checks and stops unless all 223 stored numbers reproduce. Per-game flags on all 7,232 ESPN games 2020-21 on: 12 excluded, 1,569 flagged (1,332 with an unidentified player, clustered in Dec 2021-Jan 2022 and in Aprils; 56 tag/text; 9 team-less substitutions; 203 chart gaps, mostly 2025-26), 5,650 worked around, 1 clean (zero-distance threes are on 7,209 games). Judgment calls (script docstring, Methodology card `dataquality`): any event of a class flags a game, except the chart gap (no rows or > 5% of identified attempts unmatched) and the clock (only an event outside its own period: ESPN's lag behind the chart is on every game, the game's median offset is stored instead); shots at (0, 0) are real rim shots in this era, so `unlocated` is not per game. **Does it matter? (paired bootstrap by game, 10,000 resamples; control = 30 random drops of as many games from the same seasons):** across every drop set, phase, scope, comparison and score (430 cells) **no conclusion flips sign**; 9 player-impact cells change clarity. RAPM + prior − BPM on next-season margins: every game tune −0.10 [−0.20, −0.01], validate −0.26 [−0.45, −0.07], test +0.30 [+0.11, +0.48]; without the 1,559 flagged games (refitted, hyperparameters held at the protocol's) −0.18 [−0.31, −0.05], −0.22 [−0.448, −0.003], **+0.13 [−0.09, +0.34]**, but random drops of 1,559 games give +0.17 to +0.43 on test (p 0.065) and −0.12 to +0.03 on tune (p 0.23): the move is what dropping any that many games does. Possessions: after a steal − after a made shot +0.200 [0.192, 0.207] → +0.195 [0.187, 0.203] (random +0.198 to +0.204, p 0.065); transition − settled +0.331 → +0.330; no drop set beyond random. Who played (log loss vs the pre-game model): test −0.0173 [−0.0276, −0.0068] → −0.0193; on tune the gain halves without the flagged games (−0.0175 → −0.0090 [−0.0150, −0.0030], p 0.03 vs random): the unidentified-player games are where knowing who played helps most. 35 of 266 controlled cells at p ≤ 0.05 vs random (≈13 if independent; they are nested and overlapping). **Paper-ready (the audit's downstream effect):** the known feed errors don't change any headline conclusion's direction; `data_quality_sensitivity`, `data_quality_game_flags`. Checks: every-game rows = `paper_eval_tests` / `pregame_availability_tests` bit for bit, impact predictions = `paper_eval_predictions` exactly, possessions = `possession_seasons`' league rows; `api/tests/test_data_quality.py` (7: one row per game and levels, flags add up to the audit, live checks agree and sizes write, every-game rows reproduce, drop sets drop their games, random control, endpoints); `paper-inputs` rerun (216 tables, digest `c3a214a22ff9a0b8`); browser-checked (three tabs, URL state, Replay link, light/dark, no overflow at 375 px). `paper_data_audit.py`'s `identity_checks` / `shot_checks` now also return their per-event frames (audit output unchanged: the build reproduces all 223 rows). New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `data_quality_game_flags` (5.1 MB), `data_quality_sensitivity` (0.4 MB), `data_quality_meta` (64 kB).

**Just shipped:** Model Report Card (2026-10-02, round 6 step 10; see What's in it). Every model on the platform scored season by season with a rolling origin (each season predicted by models rebuilt from the seasons before it, by their own selection rules), pooled across seasons with random effects. Checks (stop the build if they fail): 2024-25 and 2025-26 pre-game odds equal paper_eval's validate/test rows (to 3e-15; the simulator's to one run in 10,000, 2 win totals of 300 move by 1/10,000), every impact model's 2024-25 game margins equal paper_eval's validate rows exactly (same tune pairs), the 2024-25 Rating Tracker re-estimate reproduces `rating_tracker_fit` (λ0 4,918, λq 3,424, λb 11,050, k 0.757, φ 0.890), the shot prices' log loss equals `shot_value_validation`. Judgment calls in the script docstring and the Methodology card (pre-game from 2012-13: the first season with two pairs before it for the home-court constant; impact from 2022-23: the first with a pair to tune on; the three-season window from 2024-25; per possession the ratings times last season's counted/estimated points-per-possession ratio, ~1.02; held-out task, reliabilities, availability odds and Lineup Predictor left off). The simulator's form, chosen per season by build_season_sim's rule, was ratings + last season + back-to-backs every season. **Results (pooled difference, Hartung-Knapp 95% interval; τ = between-season SD of the true difference):** pre-game log loss, 14 seasons: blended ratings + back-to-backs beat Luck & Schedule's projection in 14 of 14 (−0.0113 [−0.0152, −0.0074], clearly in 10), this season's ratings alone in 13 (−0.0099 [−0.0132, −0.0067]), back-to-backs add −0.0017 [−0.0025, −0.0009] (13 of 14); every next-season prediction interval excludes zero: these rankings hold. Simulator halfway, playoff Brier: model vs record carried forward 7-7, +0.0010 [−0.0096, +0.0115], 7 flips (sixty games 9-5, −0.0001); win totals at halfway MAE −0.25 wins [−0.49, −0.01] (9 of 14), 80% ranges cover +15.3 points more teams [9.9, 20.7]. Player impact, game-margin RMSE, 4 seasons: **BPM vs RAPM with a prior 2-2, +0.03 [−0.37, +0.44], τ 0.23, I² 87%, next season −1.09 to +1.16**: the protocol's reversal (prior ahead on tune and validate, BPM on test) is between-season variation; Rating Tracker ahead of BPM 3 of 4 (−0.13 [−0.36, +0.10], p 0.17) and of RAPM with a prior 3 of 4; the prior beats one-season RAPM 4 of 4 (−0.20 [−0.29, −0.11]); shooter-aware xRAPM + prior behind BPM 4 of 4 (+0.20 [−0.03, +0.42]). Per possession (~240k a season) the tracker ranks first in all 4 seasons: vs BPM −0.00016 RMSE [−0.00027, −0.00004], p 0.02; vs RAPM with a prior 4 of 4, p 0.003. Shot model, 5 seasons: shooter-aware beats shooter-blind in 5 of 5 (−0.0027 log loss [−0.0031, −0.0024]), both beat last season's FG% by class and the league rate every season. **Paper-ready (limitation ix answered):** rankings that hold every season (pre-game form, shot pricing) vs ones that flip (simulator vs record, BPM vs RAPM with a prior, where τ is larger than the protocol's test-season gaps): `report_card_pooled`, `report_card_tests`. Checks: `api/tests/test_report_card.py` (7: seasons per task, no choice uses its own season, protocol reproduced, scores recompute from units, pairs = difference of singles and pooled rows recompute, random effects on known cases, endpoints orient a vs b = −(b vs a)); browser-checked (every task, pair panel and URL state, Methodology card, Data Coverage, light/dark, no overflow at 375 px). New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `report_card_units` (26 MB), `report_card_game_sums` (13 MB), `report_card_tests`, `report_card_pooled`, `report_card_choices`, `report_card_meta` (< 1 MB together).

**Just shipped:** Lineup Predictor (2026-10-02, round 6 step 9; see What's in it). Data checked first: the possessions table joins to its starting stint for every tracked possession, giving 95,935 lineup-seasons 2020-21 on (median a dozen possessions; 1,690-2,061 a season with 50+); pre-season ratings exist for ~320 (BPM projection) and ~520 (last season's RAPM / tracker) players a season, Gravity for ~430, roles for ~330, so 10-14% of tune possessions are by players at replacement level. 2020-21 is dropped (no rating season before it), 4,449 one-ended lineups too: 78,205 units. Judgment calls in the script's docstring and the Methodology card (later = first used after game 20; possession weights = harmonic mean of the two sides; replacement levels by the availability model's rule; opponents, home court and familiarity not modelled). **Result, 2025-26 (test), the 12,490 lineups first used after game 20, share of the real spread explained with 95% intervals over team-seasons:** the five BPM projections summed 15.9% [6.0, 27.2] (rescaling: −0.7 points [−2.0, +0.3]); **+ spacing, roles and usage: −0.2 points [−1.9, +1.2]** (tune 0.0 [−1.3, +1.4], validate −0.8 [−3.3, +1.4]): no fit effect beyond the ratings, in any phase; **+ the season so far: +4.3 points [0.7, 9.8], p 0.017**, to 19.3% [10.3, 30.9] (tune +5.4 [2.9, 8.7], validate +5.9 [1.9, 11.4]), carried by the five's own on-court net so far (weight 1.02-1.11), not the team's (−0.04 to −0.12); the team's net so far alone 11.9% [4.7, 21.1]. In real-spread RMSE: 14.3 points per 100 (everyone average) → 13.1 (sum) → 12.8 (full). All 18,212 test lineups: 16.7% sum, 19.1% full (the season so far +3.6 [0.9, 7.2]). Rating sources: last season's RAPM and the Rating Tracker are within ±2 points of the BPM projection in every phase, none outside its interval (BPM chosen on tune by 0.05 of CV error over the tracker). Noise model vs split-half (no noise assumption): real variance 93.5 vs 105.2 over 58,872 lineups, so every share may read ~11% high; the model differences are noise-free. **Paper-ready:** a lineup-level out-of-sample task: player ratings explain ~16-19% of the real spread in new lineups' net ratings, fit (spacing, roles, usage) adds nothing measurable, the season so far adds ~4-6 points of the spread in every phase (`lineup_predictor_metrics`, `lineup_predictor_tests`). Checks: two full runs content-identical; `api/tests/test_lineup_predictor.py` (5: units recompute and add up to the possessions table, players' on-court sums are 5× the team's, no look-ahead, predictions recompute from the phase's fit, metrics recompute, endpoints); browser-checked on Rotations (default five, picking a player, `lu=` restored from the URL, light/dark contrast scan clean, no overflow at 375 px). New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `lineup_predictor_units` (26 MB), `lineup_predictor_players`, `lineup_predictor_teams`, `lineup_predictor_fit`, `lineup_predictor_metrics`, `lineup_predictor_tests` (< 1 MB together). Step 10 (Model Report Card) can add this task.

**Just shipped (previously):** Shot Value Added and the shooter-aware expected-points RAPM (2026-10-02, round 6 step 8; see What's in it). Round 5's expected-points RAPM priced shots for an average shooter, so it removed shooting skill along with shooting luck and predicted next season worse than plain RAPM; it also priced each season with a model cross-fitted over all six (the paper's limitation viii). Now every attempt is priced before its game by a location model fitted only on earlier seasons, moved by the league's level so far, plus the shooter's skill as of the day before (four kinds of attempt, a state carried across seasons and updated game by game; settings by empirical Bayes on 2010-11 to 2019-20), and `paper_xrapm.py` refits RAPM on two new targets: `lf` (no look-ahead, shooter-blind field goals) and `sa` (shooter-aware); both price free throws with the shooter's FT skill before the game, so they differ only in the field goals' shooter term. Data checked first: the reproduced player folds equal `shot_xfg`'s on all 1,281,583 shots; every chart game 2020-21 on has a `game_scores` date and ESPN id; FT history is per-game averages before 2020-21 (player_season_stats), exact game lines after. Found and handled on the way: (1) for threes two of three optimiser starts stall where the carry-over meets its 0.999 bound (18-22 units of −2 log likelihood worse); a profile over the carry-over has its minimum at the interior optimum, three fresh starts reproduce it, and `fit_class` now polishes from the best start; (2) a held-out game's outcome would reach later training stints' targets through the in-season skill updates, so `paper_xrapm_stints.fold_xpts` stores both targets recomputed with each fold's games excluded and `paper_eval`'s held-out task trains on those; (3) **macOS 27 + scipy 1.18 corrupted memory** in the Rating Tracker's Cholesky (below). Checked: `paper_xrapm.py` recomputes every stored price from the stored season-start skills to 9e-8; every round-5 column and row of the five `paper_xrapm_*` tables is byte-identical after the rerun; two `paper_eval --only impact` runs and two `paper_tests` runs give identical tables; `paper_numbers.py --check` passes with no result number changed; `build_shot_value.py` is deterministic (two of its 30 location models refitted give the stored predictions bit for bit, 83,312 shots; everything after the fits is fixed-order arithmetic and Nelder-Mead from fixed starts), and the final build, which differs from the first only by the polish step, moved the settings by at most 3e-7 and changed no quoted number. **Paper-ready results.** *Pricing* (`shot_value_validation`, every season out of sample): log loss per field goal 0.6488 for an average shooter, 0.6466 with the shooter's skill frozen at the season start, 0.6460 updated game by game (−2.8 per 1,000 attempts, 95% −2.9 to −2.7 resampling games; −2.5 to −3.1 in every season, all clear of zero; rim −4.6, other twos −3.2, threes −1.1); free throws 0.5262 → 0.5102 (−16.0, −16.7 to −15.3). Skill per attempt repeats year to year at r 0.92-0.94, beyond at −0.07 to 0.08 (it behaves like luck). *RAPM* (`paper_eval_tests`, next-season game-margin RMSE, paired cluster bootstrap by game): shooter-aware one-season minus round 5's −0.35 [−0.43, −0.27] tune, −0.19 [−0.32, −0.05] validate, −0.18 [−0.31, −0.06] test; minus its shooter-blind twin (the shooter term alone) −0.32 / −0.17 / −0.20, all clear of zero; the look-ahead fix alone moves the blind version by ≤ 0.03; **but against actual-points RAPM it is still +0.11 [0.00, 0.21], +0.66 [0.43, 0.88] and −0.12 [−0.36, 0.12]**, and with the BPM prior +0.05 [−0.05, 0.16], +0.67 [0.44, 0.90], −0.12 [−0.39, 0.14] (vs BPM −0.05, +0.41 [0.14, 0.68], +0.17 [−0.07, 0.42]). So the shooter term is a tested fix of round 5's specific failure, and the expected-points target still doesn't beat the points scored: a better-explained negative. The rating's change from actual-points RAPM no longer tracks shooting skill (r −0.07 with skill per attempt, 1,984 player-seasons; −0.39 for the shooter-blind version) but still tracks what a player made beyond it (r −0.26). New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `shot_value_shots` 111 MB, `shot_value_states` 1.6 MB, `shot_value_added` 1 MB, `shot_value_fit`, `shot_value_validation`; changed: `paper_xrapm_*` (stints 39 → 76 MB), `paper_eval_predictions` / `_metrics` / `_choices` and `paper_eval_tests` (new models and pairs, plus last-digit macOS-27 differences), `rating_tracker_fit` and `rating_tracker_curve` (rebuilt on the new Mac; last digits).

**Just shipped (previously):** Rating Tracker (2026-10-01, round 6 step 7; see What's in it). RAPM whose ratings carry across seasons (state-space ridge: Kalman filter and smoother over 925 players, `scripts/rating_tracker_lib.py`), built, validated and scored under the paper's protocol as model `rapm_tracker`. Two modelling mistakes caught before any result was read: (1) scoring the BPM rows as data in the likelihood let the model "explain" them perfectly (scale 0, infinite precision), so BPM is folded into each season's prior and only the stint rows are scored; (2) even then the marginal likelihood sends the BPM weight to the top of its range, because a season's BPM carries that season's point differential, so the hyperparameters are chosen by the protocol's own criterion (pooled next-season RMSE on the three tune pairs, the same as every other impact model's λ) and the likelihood estimate is stored beside them, not used (it predicts next season worse: 14.414 vs 14.333). Checked: the tracker with no carry-over reproduces build_rapm's BPM-prior ridge to 3 × 10⁻¹³; the stint noise is flat across stint lengths (12,500-13,600 per possession-weighted row); the single-season evidence lands at λ ≈ 3,000 where cross-validation does; `paper_eval.py --only impact` reproduces the stored tune RMSE to 1e-6 and the stored filtered ratings to their rounding, and every pre-existing impact row of `paper_eval_predictions`, `paper_eval_metrics`, `paper_eval_choices` and `paper_eval_tests` is byte-identical after the rerun (md5 of the sorted rows); two full builds give identical tables (no random numbers anywhere). Browser-checked at 1280 px (Paper and Ink) and 375 px (no horizontal overflow). **Paper-ready result** (`paper_eval_tests`, paired cluster bootstrap by game, 10,000 resamples; next-season game-margin RMSE, tracker minus the other model): **vs BPM −0.20 [−0.28, −0.13] on the tune pairs, −0.29 [−0.42, −0.17] on 2024-25, and −0.01 [−0.13, +0.10] (p 0.83) on the test season 2025-26 (15.31 vs 15.33): the first RAPM version never behind BPM out of sample**; vs RAPM + prior −0.10 [−0.16, −0.04] tune, −0.03 [−0.15, +0.09] validate, −0.31 [−0.42, −0.19] test; vs three-season RAPM −0.16 [−0.30, −0.01] tune (one pair), −0.05 [−0.18, +0.09] validate, −0.14 [−0.29, +0.02] test (p 0.08); vs one-season RAPM −0.20 / −0.21 / −0.59, all outside their intervals; vs everyone-average −0.97 / −1.49 / −1.46. Within-season held-out games (BPM sees them): tracker 14.11 vs BPM 13.94 (+0.17 [+0.01, +0.34]) and under every RAPM version (vs prior −0.20 [−0.28, −0.11]). Year-to-year reliability 0.80 on the test pair vs BPM 0.74 (BPM − tracker −0.055 [−0.084, −0.030]), RAPM + prior 0.55, one-season 0.41 — partly by construction (last season is part of this season's estimate). Caveat for the paper: r 0.96-0.97 with BPM; the tracker's edge is the carry-over on top of a BPM-heavy prior, not a different view of the players. New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `player_rating_tracker` (6,318 rows, 1.7 MB), `rating_tracker_fit` (1), `rating_tracker_curve` (15), `rating_tracker_validation` (59); changed: `paper_eval_predictions` (+14,799 rows for `rapm_tracker`), `paper_eval_metrics` (+33), `paper_eval_choices` (+6), `paper_eval_tests` (679 → 757). `paper_manifest.PRODUCERS` + a `rebuild_all.sh` step after `build_rapm.py`; `paper-inputs` rerun (196 tables, digest `06dbaa1db734fc4a`, 923 macros, every claim holds, figures regenerated). Tests 221 → 227.

**Just shipped (previously):** Availability-aware pre-game odds + lineup what-if (2026-10-01, round 6 step 6; see What's in it). Data checked first: `player_game_lines` covers every regular-season game 2020-21 on but one (CHI-LAC 2026-01-20, not in the ESPN play-by-play; it keeps the base odds) and joins to `game_scores` by `espn_id` (the three NBA Cup finals drop out); Marcel BPM projections exist for every season (`projection_backtest_rows`), RAPM with prior only from 2020-21, so the RAPM version starts in 2021-22. Found and fixed before any result was read: (1) averaging only the regular players who played overrated depleted teams (24 team-games with fewer than five rotation players, e.g. COVID call-up nights), so minutes the rotation doesn't cover go to a replacement-level player; (2) "who appeared" includes deep-bench players who enter blowouts (their count correlates 0.36 with the absolute margin), so only rotation players count (the every-appearance version is stored as a check and gains nothing extra); (3) unordered reads made means differ in the last bit between runs, so every read is ordered (two runs now content-hash identical). Checked: Denver's 2026-01-05 win at Philadelphia without Jokić, Murray, Johnson, Braun and Gordon is in the lines with all 265 minutes accounted for (the model's largest swing, 44% → 89% for Philadelphia, which lost); every stored probability recomputes from its season's coefficient; the protocol base equals `paper_eval_predictions` exactly; expected minutes re-derive from `player_game_lines` (150 sampled rows); the roster rows reproduce every team-game's lineup strength; the what-if with nothing changed returns the stored odds. Browser-checked at 1280 px (Paper and Ink) and 375 px (no horizontal overflow). **Paper-ready result** (`pregame_availability_tests`, `pregame_availability_fit`): knowing who played lowers the pre-game model's log loss by −0.0175 (95% −0.0229 to −0.0120) on 2020-21 to 2023-24, −0.0170 (−0.0272 to −0.0068) on 2024-25 and **−0.0173 (−0.0276 to −0.0068, bootstrap p 0.001, Diebold-Mariano p 0.001) on the test season 2025-26 (0.5975 → 0.5802)**; Brier −0.0073 (−0.0118 to −0.0028) on the test season; last season's RAPM instead of the BPM projection −0.0138 (−0.0239 to −0.0038), the two not different (−0.0034, −0.0096 to +0.0026). Upper bound on the value of injury information; answers the paper's limitation (vi). New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `pregame_availability_odds` (7,230 rows, 2.5 MB), `pregame_availability_players` (185,758, 34 MB), `pregame_availability_fit` (129, 56 kB), `pregame_availability_tests` (48, 64 kB). `rebuild_all.sh paper-inputs` rerun. Tests 213 → 221.

**Just shipped (previously):** Coaching Decisions (2026-10-01, round 6 step 5; see What's in it). Four coaching beliefs tested with round 5's popular-beliefs machinery (imported from `paper_beliefs.py`), every time on the corrected clock. Data checked first: ESPN's challenge text decodes cleanly ("Overturned … retain their timeout" = won; "Stands" and "Supported" … "charged with a timeout" = lost; "replaycenter" gives no outcome; a plain "Challenge" is a second record next to an outcome); 5,611 "Full Timeouts" are a challenge's own timeout; ESPN rewrites the log after an overturn, so the challenged call can't be read (a shooting foul by the challenger precedes 1,444 challenges, 5% of them won, because won ones lose their foul) — call type is not reported. Found on the way and fixed before any result was read: (1) a timeout's corrected time can't separate dead-ball timeouts from mid-possession ones — ESPN's timeout stamps match NBA.com's to within 1 s for 77% of the twin games' timeouts but about one in five is late — so timeouts are placed by order, with the clock-based "surely at the dead ball" version as a sensitivity run (−0.17, same direction); (2) a 2-for-1 possession whose first event is a free throw after a non-shooting foul was being called "early", though the defence chose that timing (964 such moments now left out). Spot-checked rows against the play-by-play (a 2-for-1's net points: possessions +2, 0, +3, −2 = +3, as stored). Verified: every stored league, season and secondary effect equals the shared estimator (`api/coaching_lib.py`) on the stored rows (smoke test); two full runs content-hash identical; balance within strata (timeouts: pre-game margin −0.17, margin +0.38; fouling teams up 3 were 1.2 points stronger before the game, disclosed); challenge success 58.8% in 2023-24 against Paraball Notes' 59.5% for that season (no source named there); the second-challenge rule from 2023-24 read on official.nba.com (release of 2023-07-11). Browser-checked at 1280 px (Paper and Ink) and 375 px (no horizontal overflow). **Paper-ready result** (`coaching_decision_tests`, `coaching_decision_summary`): league family **2 of 4 survive** at FDR 5% (0.2 expected): a won challenge vs a lost one +4.2 win-probability points over two possessions (95% 3.9 to 4.5, p < 0.0001; +1.39 net points), shooting early for a 2-for-1 +0.42 net points to the quarter's end (0.32 to 0.50, p < 0.0001; +0.21 on the early possession, +0.21 after, +0.10 possessions); not surviving: a timeout after an 8-0 run −0.13 net points over 6 possessions (−0.26 to +0.01, p 0.056; regression to the mean happens with or without one; 6-0 runs −0.08, 10-0 −0.19), fouling up 3 late +1.3 points of win probability (−3.5 to +5.9, p 0.57; 688 situations, 153 fouls; made threes 2.6% vs 13.5%). Per team: timeouts 0 of 30, challenge success 2 of 30 (Toronto −11.2 points vs the league's rate, Memphis +9.8), 2-for-1 6 of 30 (all positive: the league effect visible in one team's six seasons). Challenge success 48.7% (2020-21) → 64.9% (2025-26). New tables (not on Layerbase, for the round-6 sync): `coaching_decisions` (40,520 rows, ~6.5 MB), `coaching_decision_tests` (124), `coaching_decision_summary` (4), `coaching_decision_meta` (73). `pbp_lineups.load_espn()` gained an optional `game_ids=` (default unchanged). Tests 212 → 213.

**Just shipped (previously):** Corrected clock everywhere it matters (2026-10-01, round 6 step 3b). Step 3 found ESPN's clock late by event type (made shots a median 14 s); now that clock is fixed once at the source and read everywhere it matters. New: `scripts/build_event_clock.py` (~1.5 min) → `pbp_event_clock` (one row per ESPN event, 3,401,830; corrected time, how it was set, anchored) and `pbp_event_clock_meta`; `corrected_clock()` gained an optional `how=` output (times unchanged); `pbp_lineups.Game` gained an optional `clock=` (with `secs(e)` / `event_t()`), `load_espn(conn, clock=True)` joins the stored clock and `game_clock(ev)` turns it into that argument. **Checked unchanged:** without `clock` the parser's output is identical in all 7,232 games (`run()`, `stints()`, the 5:00-split stints, old vs new module side by side); `build_possessions.py` now reads the table (its `chart_clock()` and NBA.com clock check moved to `build_event_clock.py`) and all four possession tables are content-hash identical to before. **Rebuilt on it and diffed against snapshots (dropped after):** Rotations' closing stretch (score at 5:00 changed in 3,354 of 7,220 reconciled games, +0.97 points on average; 352 close-game calls flipped, 2,066 → 2,088 close games), the Play Finder (only `clock` changed, in 80% of rows; made threes in the last 5 s of the fourth/OT 947 → 500) and Best Games (excitement r 0.9998 with the old build, same top five, 49 of the top 50). Game Replay (`/games/wp-replay/{id}` and `/whatif`) reads `pbp_event_clock` for ESPN games and says so (`clock`, `clock_note`, `_source`, the info tooltip); Replay links from Best Games and the Play Finder land on the same second. Win-probability check: Game Replay's model, fitted on ESPN's times, scores slightly better on the corrected clock in every phase (all plays log loss 0.48334 → 0.48326; last five minutes within five 0.4789 → 0.4769; last minute 0.1652 → 0.1628), so no refit. Methodology cards `bestgames` (re-read numbers: overtime swing 9.1/11.1, rank correlation 0.985, 42 of the top 50, best 30-point blowout 3,010th) and `wp` (a limit on the clock); a README sniff test changed (Boston 2023-24's most-used closing five); Data Coverage entry `pbp_event_clock`; Clutch WPA's coverage gap notes it is still on ESPN's clock. Not changed (plan): Clutch WPA (moves with the paper in step 12), the Garbage-Time Deflator (not measured), Situational Splits (no clock). Known real gaps lists which tables use which clock. Smoke test `test_event_clock_table_and_readers`; the Rotations test allows the one game whose glue-back fails (ESPN logs a lone "free throw 2 of 2"). `paper_manifest.PRODUCERS` + a `rebuild_all.sh` step after `train_wpa_model.py`; `paper-inputs` rerun. **Paper-ready result** (`pbp_event_clock_meta`, `rotation_closing_games`): against NBA.com's play-by-play of 418 games, ESPN places 31.9% of events within 2 s of the real time and the corrected clock 93.9%; moving to the corrected clock changes the score at 5:00 left in the fourth in 46% of games (3,354 of 7,220) and flips 17% of ESPN-clock close-game calls (352 of 2,066); Game Replay's model is better calibrated on the corrected times (last-minute ECE 0.0175 → 0.0158). **Tables for the round-6 Layerbase sync:** new `pbp_event_clock` (273 MB), `pbp_event_clock_meta` (32 kB); rewritten `rotation_closing_games` (1.2 MB, two new columns), `rotation_closing_stints` (7.8 MB), `play_finder_events` (201 MB), `play_finder_games` (1.6 MB), `play_finder_seasons` (24 kB), `best_games` (1.9 MB), `best_games_meta` (32 kB); the `possession*` tables were rewritten with identical contents.

**Just shipped (previously):** Possession Explorer (2026-10-01, round 6 step 4; see What's in it). A page on the step 3 tables, no new table: Teams › Possession Explorer (league / team, offence / defence, a season) and a profile block (on/off the floor by start type). Data checked first: possessions after turnovers carry no transition flag (ESPN's turnover times), so transition is reported for timed starts only and steals are measured by points, not speed; the first draft showed 14.6% transition in one place and 15.0% in another (period starts in one denominator, not the other) — both now use `possession_seasons`' definition. Verified: league totals equal a direct count of `possessions` in reconciled games (2024-25: every possession and point); 30 teams' offence and defence possessions each add up to the league's; the player block's on + off equals the 'all' row in every start type; Jokić's on-court possessions (2025-26) are within 2% of On/Off's estimate and the on/off gaps point the same way (offence +0.128 a possession, 95% interval +0.063 to +0.193). Browser-checked at 1280 px (Paper and Ink) and 375 px (no horizontal overflow; chart labels shorten). **Paper-ready result** (`possession_seasons`, live variance from `possessions`): points per possession by start, six seasons pooled — after a steal 1.306, after a defensive rebound 1.155, after a made shot 1.106, after a made free throw 1.095, after a dead-ball turnover 1.089 (1,440,289 possessions in reconciled games); between-team spread beyond chance (average of six seasons) and year-to-year r (five pairs), offence: every possession 83% / 0.47, after a make 68% / 0.41, after a defensive rebound 56% / 0.37, after a steal 25% / 0.10, after a dead-ball turnover 34% / 0.28; defence every possession 77% / 0.51. Transition after a defensive rebound (2025-26): 1.458 vs 1.031 settled. No table created or changed; nothing to sync. Tests 210 → 211.

**Just shipped (previously):** Possessions from play-by-play (2026-10-01, round 6 step 3; see What's in it). Data checked first: `pbp_events` has no possession or shot-clock column and **no fast-break label** (0 ESPN events say "fast break"; the plan's 646 aren't in the database), so transition had to come from time; and the time turned out to be the problem: against NBA.com's play-by-play of the 418 twin games, ESPN logs made shots a median 14 s late, rebounds 6 s, turnovers 5-10 s (see Known real gaps), which made a first version show steals "always" leading to a shot exactly 5 s later. Built: `scripts/pbp_possessions.py` (possession rules and `corrected_clock()`, both on `Game.walk()`; no second parser) and `scripts/build_possessions.py`; `pbp_lineups.miss_three_calls()` now takes an optional precomputed match and its parse moved into a new `chart_matches()` (output checked identical to the committed version on 2020-21 and 2025-26: 2,305 games' calls, 214,412 misses). Fixed on the way: technical free throws logged before a period's first possession were dropped when that possession opened through a rebound (12 games), and a team rebound at the buzzer in a stint `lineup_stints` drops had no stint (8 possessions). **Paper-ready result** (`possession_games`, `possession_seasons`, `possession_meta`): a possession-level dataset of 1,442,608 possessions, 7,220 of 7,232 games reconciled to the final score and team totals; ESPN's clock fixed to within 2 s of NBA.com's for 93.9% of events (from 31.9%), held out on half the twin games; counted possessions vs the box-score estimate −2.3 a team-game (+1.2 to +1.4 once team offensive rebounds are subtracted); team pace and efficiency against ESPN's published numbers r 0.97-0.997; points per possession after a steal 1.31 vs 1.16 after a defensive rebound and 1.11 after a make; transition (first attempt within 7 s, anchored starts) 14.8% of possessions at 1.40 points. RAPM can now be scored per possession (step 10). Tables created (not on Layerbase): `possessions` (1,442,608 rows, 281 MB), `possession_games` (7,232, 1.4 MB), `possession_seasons` (2,033, 0.6 MB), `possession_meta` (5, 32 kB); no existing table changed. `paper_manifest.PRODUCERS` and `rebuild_all.sh` (`build_possessions.py` after `build_lineup_stints.py`) updated; `paper-inputs` rerun (182 tables). Tests 209 → 210.

**Just shipped (previously):** Forecast Ledger live scoring (2026-09-30, round 6 step 2; see What's in it › Forecast Ledger › Live scoring). `scripts/ledger_update.py` scores 2026-27 each morning with the code imported from the tag `ledger-2026-27` itself (never the working tree): ESPN finals → `ledger_results`; each game's odds under the rule locked in `ledger_meta.rule_in_season` for *as is*, *roster* and a record-only baseline, appended to `ledger_game_log` with the time they were computed and whether that was before tip; standings and expected wins against the locked ranges; from 100 games, `paper_tests`' paired intervals for six pairs. Page tab Live scoring. **Checked before the season:** the nightly code run over all of 2025-26 from `game_scores` with the Season Simulator's prior gives back every stored `game_pregame_odds` rating, home court and expected margin to 1e-9 (1,230 of 1,230 games) and the same back-to-backs; with no results it gives back the locked odds of all 1,200 scheduled games (to 2e-6); a rehearsal on the first 535 games of 2025-26 in a throwaway schema ran every piece (tests, calibration, standings, the page) and found one bug before it could hit the real log (a final and a not-yet-final game in one read made an `NaT` timestamp that Postgres refused; fixed). `paper_manifest`: the five new tables are kind `ledger` with producer `ledger_update.py`, and a new `LIVE` set so their growing row counts don't make the manifest stale (schema and table set still checked). `rebuild_all.sh` gained `step fetch net ledger_update.py`. **Paper-ready result:** none yet — no 2026-27 game has been played (first tip 2026-10-20 19:00 UTC); the forward test grows from the first morning after it, and Step 12 reads it from `ledger_game_log` / `ledger_tests` as of a stated date. Tables created (not on Layerbase): `ledger_results` (1,206 rows, 280 kB), `ledger_team_log` (60, 64 kB), `ledger_runs` (3, 32 kB), `ledger_game_log` (0), `ledger_tests` (0); no existing table changed. Tests 203 → 209 (`api/tests/test_ledger_live.py`, 6).

**Just shipped (previously):** Forecast Ledger, the 2026-27 predictions locked before opening night (2026-09-30, round 6 step 1; see What's in it). Two preseason forecasts (the Season Simulator's opening day as is, and a roster-aware one from ESPN's rosters × Marcel projections) locked at 2026-09-30 14:19 UTC, 20.2 days before the first tip, SHA-256 `c1a48402c1363db2f1e247f6931bad5c788a928d36717806aa5d8f89e8483d17` of the 5.9 MB canonical CSV; code committed and tagged `ledger-2026-27` (c9071a8) before the lock ran. Judgment calls, written down before any 2026-27 result existed and stored in `ledger_meta`: the depth-chart minutes rule (a proportional rule was tried first and dropped: with training-camp rosters it depends on how many veterans a team brought to camp, and the hindcast can't tell the two apart, SRS RMSE 3.33 vs 3.35); replacement level −2.0; no injury model; placeholder games for the two the NBA adds after the NBA Cup group stage (Wikipedia "NBA Cup", read 2026-09-30); the fixed 1-8/4-5/3-6/2-7 playoff bracket, 2-2-1-1-1, Finals home court by record (Wikipedia "2025 NBA playoffs", read 2026-09-30). `season_sim_lib.simulate` gained an optional `extra_games` and returns seeds; its default output is bit-identical to before (checked on 2025-26 at opening day and midseason). Checks: projections reproduce the stored table exactly; as-is ratings equal the simulator's opening day to 5e-7; title / Finals / playoff odds sum to 1 / 2 / 16, every team 82 games, mean wins sum to 1,230 in both forecasts; back-to-backs from dates equal the stored rest days in all 38,236 team-games since 2010-11; `api/tests/test_ledger.py` (9 tests) re-exports the rows and reproduces the hash, checks nothing is stamped after the first tip, and that the in-season route (`prior_for` + `ratings_on` + `game_odds`, no games played) gives back every locked game probability to 2e-6. **Paper-ready result** (from `ledger_meta.hindcast`, 2010-11 to 2025-26, 480 team-seasons and 19,118 games; roster-aware leave-one-season-out vs as is; 95% intervals from `paper_tests`' cluster bootstrap by season, 10,000 resamples): final SRS RMSE 3.35 vs 3.82 (Δ −0.48 [−0.63, −0.32]); win total per 82 games MAE 6.79 vs 7.87 (Δ −1.08 [−1.41, −0.73]); every game from opening-day ratings, log loss 0.6320 vs 0.6471 (Δ −0.0151 [−0.0202, −0.0097]), Brier 0.2211 vs 0.2280 (Δ −0.0069 [−0.0093, −0.0044]) — with the caveat that the hindcast rosters know who played. The locked 2026-27 forecasts are `ledger_forecasts` (roster-aware: OKC +11.5, 66.7 wins [58-74], title 52%; as is: OKC +6.7, 57.2 wins, title 19%; the biggest disagreements LAC −4.4 vs +0.6, OKC, DET +1.2 vs +4.6); the forward test starts with round 6 step 2. Tables created (not on Layerbase): `ledger_forecasts` (2,460 rows, 528 kB), `ledger_schedule` (1,206, 232 kB), `ledger_rosters` (607, 152 kB), `ledger_hindcast` (480, 128 kB), `ledger_meta` (44, 80 kB), `ledger_lock` (1, 32 kB); no existing table changed. New manifest kind `ledger` (written once, never rebuilt; `rebuild_all.sh` only runs `ledger_lock.py --verify`); `paper-inputs` rerun: 173 tables, digest `ad7cfcc61ede9d5d`, every claim holds. Tests 194 → 203.

**Just shipped (previously):** The paper rewritten around its contributions, compiled, and given its double-blind and appendix switches (2026-09-30, round 5 step 11; see Running it locally → Paper rewrite, trim and anonymisation). Sections now follow the four contributions (audit; one protocol with a test on every comparison, then the four model sections and the ablations under it; expected-points RAPM as a negative result; beliefs under one test), each model's methods sit in its own section, the award-models section is cut and Platform is one paragraph; the abstract is 265 words (was 624) and the main text about 6,400 (was 9,000), with the detail that came out kept in four appendices behind `\ifappendix`. `\ifanon` builds the anonymised version (`paper/nba_hub_paper_anon.tex`). First real compile of the paper: `tectonic` and `mupdf-tools` installed via brew; 13 pages with appendices, 12 without, no overfull box or undefined reference in any build. Found and fixed on the way: the author block's `\\[City, India]` broke `\maketitle`; the generated audit table could never be placed under `[t]` and stalled every table to the end of the paper (every float is `[!t]` now, in the paper and the three table generators, which are the only repo changes); six tables overflowed the column (footnotesize, tighter padding, the test table and beliefs table full-width, the ablations label column wrapping). No number, figure or database table changed; `numbers.tex` byte-identical; `SHA256SUMS` regenerated for the two re-emitted tables; the Overleaf zip rebuilt with both .tex files, numbers.tex, refs.bib, figures/ and tables/. The owner then asked for all three lengths with both review states: `nba_hub_paper_8p.tex` (8 pages) and `nba_hub_paper_6p.tex` (6 pages) were condensed from the long version and `scripts/paper_build.sh` compiles every version in both `\ifanon` states (six clean PDFs). The venue itself is still unchosen, so the review artifact waits. Tests 194/194. **Layerbase synced after round 5 (2026-09-30):** the 21 tables the round created or changed (the 20 `paper_*` tables, `shot_xfg`, `shot_making_validation`; 4.11M rows) copied and content-hash identical to local; 167 tables, 0 row-count differences, ~3,080MB of the 5GB tier; the full suite against Layerbase passed 193 of 194 in one run and the one failure (a dropped SSL connection at impact_core's import-time schema check, 41 minutes in) passed alone on a re-run.

**Just shipped (previously):** Verified, expanded related work for the paper (2026-09-30, round 5 step 10; see Running it locally → Paper references). The paper moved from a hand-written 18-item bibliography to `paper/refs.bib` with 36 entries, every one checked against its DOI record (30, all fields compared with Crossref) or its own page (6), with a `% verified` line naming the source, the date and the claim the text relies on. Related Work was rewritten around the paper's contributions in five short paragraphs: player impact (APM → ridge RAPM → Bayesian and multi-season variants, reviews, the meta-metrics idea of judging a metric by stability), shot quality and expected-points plus-minus (spatial shooting relative to location, tracking-data models the public chart can't support, the NHL/soccer precedents), forecasting, luck and evaluation (state-space team strength, betting markets, the Pythagorean formula, scoring rules, Diebold–Mariano, cluster bootstrap; stated that we have no betting-market data), popular beliefs (hot hand, clutch, referees, rest), and data quality (NHL rink effects; the datasheet after Gebru et al.). Methods cite the cluster bootstrap, the Wilson interval and the Pythagorean formula where they are used. **Corrections to the old list:** the Marcel reference was dropped with its sentence (projections aren't evaluated in the paper) and its title didn't match the page ("The 2004 Marcels"); Newey–West's page range was confirmed from RePEc because Crossref has only the first page; the Kharrat et al. third author's double surname is now entered as one family name. `scripts/paper_refs_check.py` + `api/tests/test_paper_refs.py` (4 tests). No number changed (numbers.tex, figures and SHA256SUMS identical); no table created, nothing to sync. Tests 190 → 194.

**Just shipped (previously):** Reproducibility package for the paper (2026-09-30, round 5 step 9; see Running it locally → Reproducibility package). Pinned `requirements.txt` + `requirements-lock.txt`, `scripts/rebuild_all.sh` (every pipeline in dependency order, five stages, plan by default; `paper-inputs` regenerates the paper's inputs in one command, ~1 min), `scripts/paper_manifest.py` (row count + order-independent content hash of all 167 tables, 19.9M rows, database digest `b9f5cf00c72f80ff`; `--files` → `paper/SHA256SUMS`), `docs/DATASHEET.md`, `docs/REPRODUCIBILITY.md` (seeds, what differs after a rebuild, the double-blind artifact plan). `paper_numbers.py` gained a manifest section (`\pnMan…`, 923 macros in all) that stops on a stale manifest; the paper's Data and Code Availability section now describes the runner, the pinned environment, the manifest and its digest, the datasheet, and what can't be rebuilt or redistributed, and the Platform section's "every stored table is rebuilt by a script" became true-as-stated (two legacy label tables and three app caches excepted). Every figure and every pre-existing macro regenerated byte-identical. `api/tests/test_reproducibility.py` (9 tests). No table created or changed, nothing to sync. Tests 181 → 190.

**Just shipped (previously):** Figures for the paper (2026-09-30, round 5 step 8; see Running it locally → Paper figures). `scripts/paper_figures.py` → five vector figures in `paper/figures/` (pipeline + protocol; forest plot of every paired difference in all three phases; test-season calibration of the shot and pre-game models; RAPM + prior's sensitivity to prior scale and λ against BPM; hot-streak persistence against its shuffled null), each plotted number the paper prints checked against its macro (204), captions' claims checked, byte-identical reruns; the paper gained the five figures with captions (every number a macro) and a pointer to each in the text. `api/tests/test_paper_figures.py` (4 tests). No table created or changed, nothing to sync. Tests 177 → 181.

**Just shipped (previously):** Ablations for the paper (2026-09-30, round 5 step 7; see Running it locally → Paper ablations). `scripts/paper_ablations.py` takes each model apart under the protocol (RAPM: prior, its scale, λ, possession weights, home term; expected FG: six feature groups; pre-game model and simulator: carry-over, shrinkage, back-to-back flags, per-run rating draw) → `paper_ablation_*` tables and the paper's Table ablations; `paper_numbers.py` gained an ablations section (916 macros in all, 30-odd claims); the paper gained a Methods paragraph, a Results subsection, the table, a clause in contribution 2, one abstract and one conclusion sentence and limitation (xi); `api/tests/test_paper_ablations.py` (7 tests). What it adds to the paper: RAPM rests on its box-score prior, whose best weight and λ move between seasons by more than one season's interval (like the RAPM–BPM reversal); the shot model on location (jointly) and the season; the pre-game model on shrinkage; the simulator's range coverage on its per-run rating draw; and on the test season the simulator's playoff odds would have been better without the carry-over prior or shrinkage. No app table changed. Tests 170 → 177.

**Just shipped (previously):** Popular beliefs tested properly, for the paper (2026-09-29, round 5 step 6; see Running it locally → Paper popular beliefs). `scripts/paper_beliefs.py` puts clutch scoring, hot streaks, situational splits, team luck and referee tendencies under one permutation + false-discovery test → `paper_beliefs` / `paper_beliefs_summary` / `paper_beliefs_meta` and the paper's Table beliefs; `paper_numbers.py` gained a beliefs section (800 macros in all); the paper gained a Methods subsection, a Results subsection, the table, a fifth contribution, four Crossref-verified references and limitation (x); `api/tests/test_paper_beliefs.py` (9 tests). **Results:** nobody is clutch beyond chance (0 of 186 survive), no situational split survives (0 of 73,319 tests in 44 families), team luck has a small persistent component (r 0.14 outside its shuffled range; Minnesota survives at −3.0 wins per 82), and referees' foul rates are the one belief that survives (20 of 84 officials; the null cannot separate tendency from assignment). **The Miller–Sanjurjo check changes how the Hot Streak Checker's numbers should be read:** the "carries on" share has a null centre of 7-86% (nothing carries on in a shuffled season, yet the slope is positive), so shooting-percentage runs carry on 0-6% net of it and points about 19% over 5-10 games; per player, 406 of 13,628 (player, stat, window) tests survive FDR, 156 of 443 for minutes over 5 games and 67 of 443 for points, against 2 of 3,079 for the shooting percentages — role changes and injuries, not hot hands; the Methodology card, its open issues and Known real gaps say so, the page is unchanged. Two artefacts found and fixed on the way (an additive league adjustment that made spurious split survivors, and undefined shuffle draws counted as non-exceedances) are recorded in the script's docstring and the section above. No app table changed. Tests 161 → 170.

**Just shipped (previously):** Data-quality audit for the paper (2026-09-29, round 5 step 5; see Running it locally → Paper data-quality audit). `scripts/paper_data_audit.py` re-measures 17 error classes of the ESPN play-by-play, the NBA shot chart and the season tables into `paper_data_audit` / `paper_data_audit_classes` and writes the paper's Table audit; `paper_numbers.py` dropped its RECORDED counts (all five re-derive exactly: 66 events / 9 games, 8,688 at a 26 ft median, and the tag/text count, now stated exactly as 64, was "about 65") and prints the audit's macros; the paper gained a "Data quality" subsection, the table, and the audit in its contributions. Numbers that moved on re-measuring: on-court +/- not 5× the margin in **25%** of full-minute team-games (9,696 of 12,880 exact), not 29% (the lines have been rebuilt twice since that count, and "full minutes" is now within 1 s); `plus_minus`'s sign disagrees with the win flag in **14** games, not 13 (sign of a nonzero value against the stored flag); the two feeds' clock gap is a median 4 s with a 99th percentile of 26 s ("up to ~26 s" was the tail, not the maximum). No app table or page changed. Tests 155 → 161.

**Just shipped (previously):** Expected-points RAPM for the paper (2026-09-29, round 5 step 4; see Running it locally → Paper expected-points RAPM). `build_shot_making.py` now also writes `shot_xfg` (the per-shot cross-fitted P(make), 2020-21 on; every other output unchanged, checked by hashing), `pbp_lineups.match_coordinates()` also returns the matched `nba_shot_id`, and `scripts/paper_xrapm.py` prices every tracked stint's attempts into expected points and fits RAPM on them; `paper_eval.py` / `paper_tests.py` / `paper_numbers.py` gained the `xrapm_single` / `xrapm_prior` models, their comparisons and 136 macros; the paper gained a Methods subsection, a Results subsection, table rows and four verified references. Honest result: the expected-points target removes 42-44% of the stint-level spread but also removes shooting skill, so the ratings are more stable and predict next-season margins worse (details in the section above). Found on the way: four 2025-26 games missing from the shot chart (Known real gaps). Tests 150 → 155.

**Just shipped (previously):** Significance tests and intervals on every comparison in the paper (2026-09-29, round 5 step 3; see Running it locally → Paper significance tests). `scripts/paper_tests.py` → `paper_eval_tests` (559 rows, 200 kB: 269 paired differences, 290 single-number intervals); `api/tests/test_paper_tests.py` (9 tests); `paper_numbers.py` gained a tests section (\pnEvD… difference/interval/p macros, 472 macros in all; `--check` clean) with 30-odd claims that guard the new sentences; the paper (backup in `paper/versions/`) has a new Methods paragraph (Intervals and tests), rewritten comparison sentences in every Results subsection, a new Table (paired differences on the test season with Δ, 95% CI, bootstrap p and Diebold–Mariano p), two verified references (Diebold & Mariano 1995, Newey & West 1987, both checked on Crossref), and reworded abstract, contributions, limitation (viii) and conclusion. **Where a test changes a published sentence:** (1) RAPM + prior vs BPM: the reversal is not noise — the prior version is ahead on the tune pairs (Δ RMSE −0.10, 95% CI −0.20 to −0.01, p 0.03) and the validation season (−0.26, −0.45 to −0.07, p 0.007) and behind on the test season (+0.30, 0.11 to 0.48, p 0.002; Diebold–Mariano p 0.001), each outside its within-season interval, so the paper now says which estimator wins varies more between seasons than within one and a single test season cannot settle it (the abstract's "slightly better" is gone). (2) Simulator: "narrowly on Brier, clearly on log loss" on the tune seasons was wrong — both are inside their intervals (Brier Δ −0.0013, CI −0.0105 to 0.0078, p 0.79; log loss −0.026, CI −0.069 to 0.011, p 0.19); the real tune-season gains are win-total MAE (−0.33 wins, CI −0.54 to −0.14), RMSE and range coverage (+16 points); on the validation and test seasons every baseline gain is inside its interval (test Brier p 0.06, MAE p 0.95) except the test-season playoff log loss (+0.079, CI 0.011 to 0.161, p 0.02), which comes mostly from teams the simulator favoured at the midpoint that missed (Miami 0.87, Golden State 0.81; a claim checks "mostly"); the coverage gain on the test season reaches zero (16.7 points, CI 0.0 to 36.7, p 0.11). (3) Pre-game: the validation season "keeps the order" but not significantly (chosen − current −0.0064, CI −0.0131 to 0.0003, p 0.06); the test-season "edge" of current-only over the chosen form is noise (+0.0008, CI −0.0060 to 0.0077, p 0.83; DM p 0.82), as is chosen vs baseline (p 0.39); only current-only vs baseline is outside its interval on the test season (−0.0040, p 0.001); the back-to-back flags help on tune (p < 0.001) and validation (−0.0038, p 0.01) and do nothing on test (p 0.94). (4) BPM × 0.71: its validation gain (−0.11, p 0.09) and test loss (+0.02, p 0.70) are both inside their intervals — "helps on validation" is gone. (5) Rescaled on/off vs zero on the test season: indistinguishable (+0.04, CI −0.11 to 0.19, p 0.61); as published, on/off is worse than zero by 3.8-6.6 points in every phase (p < 0.001). (6) Shot model: boosting's gain is small and tight (log loss −0.0083 vs the logistic regression, CI −0.0090 to −0.0076; shots resampled by game), and the logistic regression is indistinguishable from the zone baseline on the test season (−0.0002, p 0.35) though ahead on the validation season; calibration under the protocol: ECE 0.004 (CI 0.003-0.007) on the test season — the raw largest-bin gap was 0.26 from the 7-shot 0.9-1.0 bin, so the stored metric takes bins holding ≥ 1% of shots (0.022). (7) Every step of the year-to-year order BPM > RAPM + prior > one-season > on/off is outside its paired interval on the test pair (smallest: one-season − on/off 0.12, CI 0.03 to 0.21, p 0.006), and quality − shot-making persistence is 0.28 (CI 0.19 to 0.37). Checks: two full runs byte-identical (all 559 rows); the sign-flip p and the bootstrap interval agree at the 5% level on every comparison the paper reports (a claim; the one disagreement in the table is a row the paper doesn't use: validation-season playoff Brier at the 60-game checkpoint, bootstrap p 0.034 vs sign-flip 0.073); 150/150 tests. Also fixed in step 2's `paper_eval.py`: the pooled tune row of `sim_top6` was labelled 2010-11 to 2023-24 while it pools only the four play-in seasons (2020-21 on); relabelled 2020-21 to 2023-24 by rerunning `--only sim` (every prediction and metric value identical, only that label changed). **Not synced to Layerbase** (200 kB, the paper's; `paper_eval_metrics` changed one label). Flag for step 10: Newey & West's page range 703–708 was verified only to its first page on Crossref.

**Just shipped (previously):** One evaluation protocol for every model in the paper (2026-09-29, round 5 step 2; see Running it locally → Paper evaluation protocol). `scripts/paper_eval.py` → `paper_eval_predictions` (1,949,611 rows, ~350 MB), `paper_eval_metrics` (1,162), `paper_eval_choices` (28); `api/tests/test_paper_eval.py` (8 tests); `paper_numbers.py` gained a protocol section (\pnEv… macros) and dropped the platform-validation macros the paper no longer uses, and the paper's abstract, a new Methods subsection (Evaluation protocol) and its three results tables now report tune / validate / test numbers (backup in `paper/versions/`). **Where the protocol changes a published conclusion:** (1) RAPM + prior vs BPM: the paper said "matches BPM" (mean over five next-season pairs, 14.77 vs 14.80); under the protocol the prior version is ahead on the tune pairs (14.44 vs 14.54) and the validation season (14.87 vs 15.13) and behind on the test season (15.62 vs 15.33), where BPM is the best estimator of all — the tuned prior scale is 0.5, not the platform's 0.75-1.0, so the prior version leans less on BPM and its year-to-year r falls from 0.64 to 0.55 on the test pair. (2) Simulator: the paper said it beats the record-only baseline on log loss, MAE, RMSE and coverage (480 team-seasons); that holds pooled over the 14 tune seasons (log loss 0.279 vs 0.305, MAE 3.52 vs 3.85) but on the validation season and the test season the baseline wins Brier, log loss, MAE and RMSE (test: Brier 0.067 vs 0.091, MAE 4.20 vs 4.23; 30 team-seasons each) and the simulator keeps only the range coverage (73% vs 57% at nominal 80%). (3) Pre-game: "chosen < current-only < baseline" holds on the tune seasons (LOSO 0.6142 / 0.6267 / 0.6307) and the validation season, but on the test season this season's ratings alone edge the chosen form (0.5967 vs 0.5975). (4) On/off: even rescaled by its tuned multiplier it does not beat zero on the test season (16.82 vs 16.78); as published it is worse than zero in every phase. (5) The shot model's family was chosen on the test season in the platform (`select_model` scores 2025-26); moved to 2024-25 it is still gradient boosting, and the test numbers are unchanged at the printed precision (log loss 0.6483) because the three configurations tie. Unchanged conclusions: year-to-year order BPM > RAPM + prior > one-season RAPM > on/off in every phase; shot quality far more persistent than shot-making out of sample (test pair r 0.85 vs 0.57, n 260); one-season RAPM worse than the prior version throughout. Where the protocol coincides with the platform's validation (BPM, zero and on/off at scale one predicting 2025-26 from 2024-25) the game RMSEs agree to 0.01 (the protocol keeps 2024-25's intercept and home term instead of refitting them). Checks: three full runs gave identical predictions, metric values and choices (the only difference between the first two was the wall-clock fit time the boosting rows' notes recorded, which is now printed instead of stored; the `run` timestamp row is the one intended difference), 141/141 tests, `numbers.tex` byte-identical across runs (211 macros) and every macro the paper uses defined and used. **Not synced to Layerbase** (the three tables are the paper's, not the app's; ~350 MB if the owner wants them there).

**Just shipped (previously):** Paper numbers generated from the database (2026-09-29, round 5 step 1; see Running it locally → Paper numbers). `scripts/paper_numbers.py` → `paper/numbers.tex` (141 macros), `api/tests/test_paper_numbers.py` (6 tests); the paper now takes every number from the macros (backup of the hand-typed draft in `paper/versions/`). No table created or changed, nothing to sync. Re-reading every value found these numbers in the draft wrong or stale: ESPN play-by-play events 3.61M → **3.40M** (3,401,830; the 3.61M is all of `pbp_events`, which also holds 207,752 nba_api twin rows); zero `shot_distance` on **16% of threes, not 7%** (15,139 of 2024-25's 92,454 regular-season threes; 7% is the share of all shots; 11-17% a season from 1997-98 on) — the same wrong figure was in this README (twice), CLAUDE.md, the shot-making Methodology card and two script docstrings, all corrected; unidentified names a season 100-155 → **104-155**; "assists match exactly" → 4 player-team-seasons off by one; three RAPM table cells were mis-rounded in the draft (exact averages −0.0353, 0.3245, 14.8946, 15.7848 give −0.04, 0.32, 14.89, 15.78, not −0.03, 0.33, 14.90, 15.79); "double-counts in about half" → 56% of 2020-21 to 2022-23 games; SRS r 0.99996 now reads "above 0.9999"; MVP AUC "above 0.99" → up to 0.996 (only one model); "over 140 tables" → 147; λ = 3,000 "in every season" narrowed to every single-season fit (the three-season fit ending 2022-23 chose 4,000). Every other number matched the tables. Checks: 133/133 tests, eslint and vite build clean, generator output byte-identical across runs, every macro the paper uses defined and every macro used. Not compiled here (no LaTeX on this machine); macro expansion was diffed against the old draft in Python instead.

**Just shipped (previously):** Layerbase re-synced after round 4 of the analyst list (2026-09-29, owner's OK). Round 4 was checked as a whole first: all 11 phases and 3 data fixes on `main`, 127/127 tests (100 smoke + 27 known-facts), eslint and vite build clean, every new page loaded in the browser with real data (only the awards service on 8000, not started, logged errors). A full content comparison (every table hashed with both connections in UTC; `player_shots` by count) found 35 new tables (~373 MB) and 22 existing ones whose contents changed with the same columns (~1,025 MB, of which `pbp_events` ~910 MB for the 66 corrected rows of the wrong-player fix; the rest from the 3PA fix, the wrong-player rebuild chain, the reproducible shot-making rebuild and the WPA/leverage reruns). Copied those 57 tables (8,111,985 rows). After: 147 tables, 0 row-count and 0 column differences, 146 tables content-hash identical, ~2,516 MB of the 5 GB free tier. Tests with `DB_TARGET=layerbase`: all 27 known-facts tests pass (including the two 3PA facts that failed before the sync); 79 smoke tests passed and 48 failed with `ReadOnlySqlTransaction` at impact_api's import-time `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` while Layerbase was briefly read-only during the 22-minute run; a fresh connection showed `transaction_read_only = off` and the 48 passed on a re-run, so 127/127 pass. Also removed the aborted first attempt's leftovers (its git stash and the `round4_aborted` schema).

**Just shipped (previously):** Report builder (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 10; see What's in it). Front end only: no new table, endpoint or script, nothing to sync to Layerbase. Surveyed first: every chart already goes through `common/ChartExport.jsx` and every table through `common/TableExport.jsx`, so the Add-to-report button lives in those two components and reaches everything at once (46 `<ChartExport>` and 126 `<TableExport>` placements at the time, `DataTable` counting once). Design choices: (1) snapshots in IndexedDB, one record per report, mutations read the latest copy inside the write transaction (so two tabs don't overwrite each other) and a `BroadcastChannel` refreshes other tabs; (2) a chart snapshot is `chartExport.snapshotChart()`: the theme attribute is flipped to Paper for the length of one synchronous call (transitions frozen so computed values are final, nothing paints in between) so a chart taken in Ink prints on white, and its own background colour is stored with it; (3) a stored SVG is only ever displayed through an `<img>` data URL, where scripts cannot run, and imported files are re-validated item by item; (4) the print stylesheet gives the report Paper colour tokens itself instead of relying on `beforeprint`, which headless printing does not fire. Checked in the browser: added a chart and two tables from RAPM while the theme was Ink: the stored chart carried the Paper background and dark-on-light fills, no `var(--` or `class=` left, and the theme was back to Ink afterwards; all 11 chart/table buttons on Season Simulator stored, both charts decode as images at their on-screen size; Up/Down by mouse and by keyboard (a click, then Enter, moved a chart to the bottom and focus fell back to its Up button); caption and text saved on blur and on a 0.7 s pause; Remove then Undo put the item back at its place; a report export re-imported was skipped (same id); a hand-made hostile file (script inside the SVG, `javascript:` link, `//evil.com` link, a bad colour, wrong item types, ragged table rows) imported with 4 items dropped and the rest cleaned, and nothing ran when it was opened; storage failures simulated two ways (transactions throwing, a put throwing QuotaExceededError) both show the plain message and the next click works. 375 px: no horizontal scroll; contrast of the report's text styles (note, pills, inputs, captions, source line, table cells, buttons) at least 4.5:1 in Paper and Ink. Print: headless Chrome would not run in this sandbox, so the print rules were applied to the live page as ordinary rules at A4 width (794 px): black on white, one caption/table/chart per block, source and date under each, the full URL printed; the computed `break-before`/`break-after` values are the intended ones, but real page breaks were not seen. Also moved the nav entry: a top-level Report item made the top bar 13 px too wide at 1280 px, so it sits in a new **Saved** dropdown next to Saved analyses. Lint clean, production build passes; the smoke suite is unaffected.

**Just shipped (previously):** Known-facts test suite (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 11; see Run the backend smoke tests → Known-facts test suite). `api/tests/test_known_facts.py`, 27 tests, no data or endpoint change. Every real-world fact was read from an outside page first (ESPN box scores, Wikipedia; Basketball-Reference returns HTTP 403 to fetches) and the stored data agreed on all of them except one number: **Adebayo's 83-point game shows 44 FGA in `player_game_lines` against the official 43** (his points, FG, 3P, 3PA and free throws all match); the NBA shot chart in `player_shots` has 43, and the extra attempt is a Q2 heave at 1440.1 s that ESPN logs as a miss, so the test pins the gap at 0 or 1. Invariants were measured before their tolerances were written: on-court plus off-court points differ from the team's in 26 of 3,665 on/off rows (all 2022-23 Pistons and Bulls, the largest 79 of 8,190 points), the league's on-court net is at most 0.06% of points scored, and stint seconds equal game length in all 7,220 reconciled games. **Corrected a stale claim:** the Assist Network entry said every player-season's assists equal the Game Log's (0 of 3,172 differ); it is all but 4, each off by one assist (the 4 made shots with data errors the network drops, already noted in the smoke test). Run against Layerbase: 21 pass, 4 skip (round-4 tables not synced), 2 fail on purpose until the 3PA fix is synced (Adebayo's 3PA reads 19 instead of 22; 2020-21 league 3PA 0.6% short). `pytest api/tests`: 127 passed, exit 0.

**Just shipped (previously):** Shot quality map (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 9; see What's in it). Data checked first: 5,932,761 regular-season shots, 12% (699,565) sitting at exactly (0, 0) (25% a season through 2009-10 and none from 2010-11 to 2019-20, then a steady ~2.7% again since 2020-21: real shots under the hoop, kept), 8,564 beyond half court; a grid finer than about 2 ft would have needed 1.5M+ table rows, so cells are stored as arrays per player-season (15 MB against ~100). The hex code was tested first on a stand-in prediction (zone mean) for reconciliation and sniff tests, then the whole script rebuilt. **Found while diffing player_shot_making before and after: the script wasn't reproducible.** Its unordered `COPY (SELECT ... FROM player_shots)` returned rows in the table's physical order, and the boosting bin thresholds and the logistic-regression subsample depend on row order, so the build stored on 2026-09-28 differed from a rebuild of the same data even though nothing else had changed: two rebuilds in physical order matched each other exactly (14,513 rows identical), but an id-ordered rebuild differed from them. Fixed with `ORDER BY id` (two id-ordered runs are identical, `computed_at` and fit seconds aside). Effect on the stored tables against the 2026-09-28 build: for the 9,026 qualified player-seasons shot-making moved by a median 0.04 eFG points (largest 0.42), correlation 0.9998, the season-level calibration gap went from 0.35 to 0.37 points, the held-out log losses moved by less than 0.0001 (boosting 0.6483, logistic 0.6566, zone 0.6568, constant 0.6915) and 63% of the ranks shifted by a place or a few (2016's top five are the same five players in the same order); every number quoted on the Methodology card and here was re-read (Curry 2015-16 +13.1 → +13.0, +418 → +416 points; Korver +15.1 → +15.0, expected three-point 36.5% → 36.6%; calibration 0.35 → 0.37). One-shot seasons moved most (up to 3 points of x_efg_pct) and are never ranked. Tables created: `player_shot_hex` (9,026 rows, 15 MB), `shot_hex_league` (18,774 rows, 1.3 MB), `shot_hex_meta` (7 rows); rewritten with the new contents and the same row counts: `player_shot_making` (14,513), `shot_making_league` (30), `shot_making_validation` (5): none on Layerbase yet, and the last three must be synced even though counts match. The 3-4 minute build is also why the plan's "~5 min" is now ~3.5. Smoke test 100/100.

**Just shipped (previously):** Best Games & Upsets (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 7; see What's in it).
