Data checked first: `play_finder_events` already holds the reconciled score before every play (only 7 of 7,229 games don't reach the real final; event-id order is time- and score-monotone in all of them), `game_pregame_odds` holds held-out pre-game odds for 19,118 games and `game_scores` links both, so no fetch or model was needed. The excitement score was chosen after trying three forms on the real games (plain swing; swing + lead changes; the stated composite): their rankings agree at ρ 0.985-0.993 and the top 50 shares 40-45 games, so the weights matter less than they look and are disclosed as a judgment call rather than dressed up as fitted. Every sniff test was checked outside the database first (ESPN, NBA.com and press recaps for the 35-point Clippers comeback, both double-overtime classics, the Hawks' 30-point comeback and the Curry-and-Durant-less Warriors loss). The page's Replay link goes to the biggest-swing play (`t=` and `ev=` from the Play Finder's Game Replay parameters). Honest limits kept on the page: weights are a judgment, the win model doesn't know who has the ball, the odds don't know injuries (the top two upsets are partly that), the score used here is the reconciled one and can differ a little from Game Replay's raw chart where ESPN's score fields glitch. Tables created: `best_games` (7,229 rows, 1.9 MB with indexes), `best_games_meta` (7 rows, 32 kB): not yet on Layerbase. Smoke test 99/99 (checks every stored excitement equals the formula on its stored parts, the famous games' places, franchise codes and the whitelist errors). Also stopped a stale impact_api on 8002 that predated the router.

**Just shipped (previously):** Season Simulator (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 6; see What's in it). Data checked first: `game_scores` has every regular-season game with real scores and `team_game_fatigue.rest_days` every side's rest, so the pre-game model needed no new fetch; conference membership had to be pinned for NJN/NOH (the 30 current codes were already in `impact_core.TEAM_META`, unchanged since 2004-05); `team_seasons.playoffs` turned out unusable as the truth for the backtest (20 teams flagged in 2024-25, 3 in 2025-26), and the 2025-26 postseason isn't in `player_shots`, so the real playoff and play-in fields come from a new `postseason_games` table fetched from ESPN's scoreboard (its notes label play-in games and rounds; the 2025-26 play-in went Philadelphia, Orlando, Portland, Phoenix), cross-checked in the build script against the postseason shots and Basketball-Reference where they exist (0 disagreements). Built on `api/luck_lib.py` (SRS, shrinkage, its probit projection is the baseline form) with a Bayesian prior from last season's ratings whose three constants are fitted on the 480 franchise pairs. A prototype found the first tiebreak pass corrupting standings (playoff odds summed to 7.2 a conference instead of 8; column views rewritten mid-swap), fixed before anything was stored; every simulation now asserts nothing of the kind, and the smoke test checks the sums. Honest results kept: the record carried forward matches the model at picking the playoff field by midseason (Brier 0.089 vs 0.090); the model's 80% ranges cover 75% at halfway. New tables (about 5.1 MB in all, none on Layerbase): `game_pregame_odds` (19,118 rows, 3.7 MB), `season_sim_backtest` (3,360, 640 kB), `postseason_games` (1,458, 288 kB), `season_postseason` (510), `pregame_model_fit`, `pregame_calibration`, `pregame_model_seasons`, `season_sim_seasons`, `season_sim_params`, `season_sim_backtest_summary`, `season_sim_calibration`. 98/98 smoke tests (1 new), eslint and vite build clean; checked in the browser light and dark at 1280 px and 375 px.

**Just shipped (previously):** ESPN play-by-play events given to the wrong player, fixed (2026-09-29, the owner's OK). Found while making Hot Streak persistence reproducible: 5 player-dates had lines for two teams. Cause: the ESPN fetch's fuzzy name match (see Known real gaps) turned Keon Johnson (Nets 2023-24, no season row) into Keldon Johnson and Jalen McDaniels (Wizards 2024-25) into Jaden McDaniels. A new rule-based search (`scripts/repair_espn_player_ids.py`, dry run by default) found exactly those two, 66 events in 9 games, and every one's text names the real player; the fetch's matcher now tries an exact name in any season first (checked: Keon and Jalen resolve right, accents and short names still match). Checked before writing anything: the 9 games rebuilt in memory from the unpatched events reproduce the stored lines exactly (206 rows), and from the patched events only those four players' lines change; Keon's five games then match the NBA shot chart shot for shot (8-21, 4-10 from three, per game), lineup fill-ins in those games drop from 6 to 0. Then applied and rebuilt everything on the ESPN play-by-play, diffing each of 41 tables against a snapshot: `player_game_lines` 152,473 → 152,469 rows (Keldon −5 Nets games, 62 min; Jaden −4 Wizards games, 7 min; Keon now has his 5 games, 61 min and 31 points, where he had 3 stubs of 0-22 seconds; Jalen 4 games, 7.5 min, where he had one 32-second stub), and the lines, stints, team totals, closing lineups, assist pairs, WPA and leverage splits changed only for those players and games. Keldon's 2023-24 log now has NBA.com's 69 games; the Garbage-Time Deflator's points check tightened (2023-24 and 2024-25 MAE 0.030 → 0.028). What else moved, and why: fits that pool everyone moved slightly (RAPM, Stat Stability, projections' shrinkage, Situational Splits' league rows, Hot Streak slopes); bootstrap intervals moved for players far from these four (on/off, rim deterrence, stability), because each script draws from one seeded stream and two fewer rows shift every later draw (point estimates unchanged); and 34 `pair_seasons`/`lineup_seasons` rows in unaffected seasons flipped their last rounded digit (0.1 minute, 0.01 rating) because Postgres sums floats in no fixed order (pre-existing; their stints are identical). Every number on the Methodology cards and in What's in it was re-read from both versions: changed are Hot Streak's 20-game usage share (68% → 65%: its previous-season weight flipped from 20 to 10 games, a near-tie on 2023-24), RAPM's 2025-26 test (one-season 15.90 → 15.91, on/off 21.31 → 21.33) and 2024-25 test (BPM-prior 14.85 → 14.84), Stat Stability's 3P% range (420-531 → 422-534, M still 476), On/Off's share clear of zero (11-15% → 10-15%), Rim Deterrence's matched-shot count (302,060 → 302,062) and the stints card's player-game count (152,471 → 152,469); Situational Splits' outlier counts and 3PA year-to-year r were already stale since the 3PA fix and now read 4,961 vs 4,548 (3,667 at a flat 5%) and 0.17. Unchanged at the quoted precision: the projections backtest and sniff tests, RAPM's ranks and year-to-year r, clutch (14 of 186), Jokić's on/off, the assist totals, rim ranks and year-to-year r, every other Hot Streak figure. 97/97 smoke tests (1 new). Rewritten, none yet on Layerbase: `pbp_events` (66 rows), `player_game_lines`, `lineup_stints`, `lineup_stint_games`, `lineup_stint_seasons`, `lineup_seasons`, `pair_seasons`, `player_on_off`, `player_on_off_seasons`, `team_game_totals`, `stat_stability`, `stat_stability_curve`, `hot_streak_persistence`, `player_situational_splits`, `situational_split_league`, `player_projections`, `projection_backtest`, `projection_backtest_rows`, `projection_ranges`, `projection_stats`, `player_rapm`, `rapm_fits`, `rapm_lambda_cv`, `rapm_validation`, `rotation_closing_stints`, `rim_deterrence`, `rim_deterrence_seasons`, `assist_pairs`, `player_assisted_share`, `play_finder_events`, `play_finder_seasons`, `player_wpa_totals`, `leverage_validation`, `player_leverage_splits`, `player_leverage_summary`.

**Just shipped (previously):** Hot Streak persistence is now reproducible (2026-09-29). Two runs of `build_hot_streak_persistence.py` on the same `player_game_lines` differed in 36 of its 42 rows (slopes by up to ~0.00004). The cause was row order, not the seeded bootstrap: `LINES_SQL` (`api/hot_streaks.py`) sorted by player, season and date, and 5 player-dates have two lines (ESPN's feed puts Keldon Johnson on the Nets and Jaden McDaniels on the Wizards on days they also played for San Antonio and Minnesota), so those ties came back in any order and moved the window sums and one 3P% floor check. Added `game_id` as the last sort key (the router's single-player reads use the same SQL). Checked: three reruns are identical row for row (`EXCEPT` both ways empty); reversing the tie order changes all 42 rows, confirming the cause. Against the table before the fix, one window moved (3P% 5-game: 51,802 → 51,803) and every slope changed by under 0.0002; every number quoted on the Methodology card and here is unchanged at its precision, except that the card and What's in it still said 13% for a 10-game 3P% run, stale since the 3PA fix (12.45% both before and after this change), now 12%. Table rewritten: `hot_streak_persistence` (same 42 rows), not yet on Layerbase.

**Just shipped (previously):** Game Log three-point attempts fixed (2026-09-29, the owner's OK after the Play Finder found them short). `player_game_lines` called a miss a three only when ESPN's text said "three point" or gave 23+ ft; the NBA shot chart, matched shot by shot (order within game, shooter and period, the Rim Deterrence match, now `match_coordinates()` in `scripts/pbp_lineups.py`), has 8,688 missed threes the text called twos (median 26 ft) and 1,545 the other way. The shared parser takes an optional `miss_threes` input (`Game(..., miss_threes=)`, from `miss_three_calls()`); without it its output is unchanged, so `lineup_stints` and everything else that doesn't pass it are byte-identical. Rebuilt `player_game_lines` and diffed it against a snapshot: **only `fg3a` changed**, in 9,297 of 152,473 rows (+7,143 attempts net); every other column of every row is identical, and no line has more threes made than attempted. Season 3PA against NBA.com (players with 20+ games) went from 0.981-0.994 to 0.9996-1.0005, the mean per player-season miss from 2.3-4.4 attempts to 1.2-1.7; Bam Adebayo's 83 now reads 7-22 from three, as on the NBA chart. Reran what reads the lines' 3PA and hashed every table before and after: Stat Stability changed only its 3PA row (M per minute 19.7 → 19.2 minutes), `stat_stability_curve` only 3PA, Situational Splits only 3PA and 3P%, Projections only 3PA and 3PA per 36 (their backtest summary is the same to the precision the Methodology card quotes), Hot Streak persistence 3P% (slope moved up to 0.004); `player_on_off`, `team_game_totals`, `lineup_stints`, `assist_pairs`, `rim_deterrence` and `play_finder_events` are identical, and the Play Finder now matches the Game Log on 3PA too (0 player-games differ). Found on the way: `build_hot_streak_persistence.py` wasn't exactly reproducible (two runs on the same input differed by up to 0.00004 in a slope; fixed the same day, see the entry above). Updated: Game Log accuracy note (now live: 3PA total 100.00% of NBA.com's), Stat Stability Methodology card ("within 0.3%"), Data Coverage, Play Finder notes, smoke test (3PA within 0.2% of NBA.com every season, Bam 7-22). Tables rewritten: `player_game_lines`, `stat_stability`, `stat_stability_curve`, `stat_year_to_year`, `hot_streak_persistence`, `player_situational_splits`, `situational_split_league`, `player_projections`, `projection_backtest`, `projection_backtest_rows`, `projection_ranges`, `projection_stats`, `play_finder_events`, `play_finder_games`, `play_finder_seasons`, none yet on Layerbase.

**Just shipped (previously):** Play Finder (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 5; see What's in it). Data checked first: of 3,401,830 ESPN events, 298,126 of the 601,856 made field goals don't say two or three in the text, 42,570 named events carry no id, and the passer, blocker and stealer exist only as names in the description, so a live query on `pbp_events` couldn't tell a made three from a made two or find anyone's assists; one pass of the shared parser writes a compact table instead (3.29M rows, 201 MB; estimated at ~184 MB of heap before writing). Checked after building: 0 of 152,428 player-games differ from `player_game_lines` on anything but 3PA, the score source agrees with `lineup_stint_games` in every game, and Bam Adebayo's 83 on 2026-03-10 comes back whole (20 FG, 7 threes, 36 FT). Found while checking that game against the NBA shot chart (20-43, 7-22 from three): the parser had him 7-19 from three, because it calls 8,688 of the chart's missed threes twos across the six seasons; the finder uses the chart's call for misses (makes keep the score step); the Game Log got the same fix the same day (see above). EXPLAIN ANALYZE on the typical filters showed no index was needed on `pbp_events` (see What's in it). Game Replay gained `t=`/`ev=` link parameters (checked with and without them). Browser: this session's own impact_api; Bam's game, clutch threes, 40-ft makes, a Replay link opening Game Replay with the play marked; Paper and Ink contrast (all text ≥ 5.5:1), 1280 px and 375 px (no horizontal overflow). New tables: `play_finder_events` (3,287,148 rows, 201 MB), `play_finder_games` (7,229 rows, 1.6 MB), `play_finder_seasons` (6 rows, 24 kB), not yet on Layerbase.

**Just shipped (previously):** Assist Network (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 4; see What's in it). Data checked first: 373,226 made shots carry "(X assists)" (60-64% of makes a season, the league's real assisted rate); only 5 of them are events the shared parser doesn't read as shots, so nothing new had to be parsed, and 1,149 assister names (0.31%) match no id — the same two-way/10-day players the lineup phases found. The assist totals were checked against `player_game_lines` (same parser: 0 player-seasons differ) and NBA.com (0.997-1.001 of season totals). Two things turned up while building: the three NBA Cup finals are in the ESPN play-by-play (so some players on the six finalists have 83 games in `player_game_lines`; Game Log already drops them, and so does this), and `player_game_lines` holds one row with team `'NaN'` (Miye Oni, 2021-11-20, 3 minutes; left alone, skipped here; Known real gaps). A first smoke-test draft expected Klay Thompson's 94% assisted threes to rank in the top third; they rank 102nd of 233, because 84% of the league's threes are assisted — the test now compares against the league rate and the Methodology card says so. Browser: temporary harness (deleted) against this session's own impact_api; network, hover and click, tables, profile and team blocks; Paper and Ink, 1280 px and 375 px (no horizontal overflow). New tables: `assist_pairs` (40,681 rows, 5.6 MB), `player_assisted_share` (3,667 rows, 0.6 MB), `assist_seasons` (6 rows, 24 kB), not yet on Layerbase.

**Just shipped (previously):** Rim Deterrence (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 8; see What's in it). Data checked first: ESPN's text gives no distance for most layups, dunks and tips (305,000 of 419,000 rim-type attempts), so the plan's rule "no distance = 0-3 ft" would have placed most rim attempts by assumption; the NBA shot chart (`player_shots`) turned out to hold the same shots with real coordinates, matched by order for 99.0% of attempts, and measured against it the rule is right 85.8% of the time. So coordinates come first and the rule is a counted fallback (2,927 attempts in six seasons). The parser's two-or-three call differs from the NBA's on 0.8% of matched shots (mostly missed ~26 ft threes the ESPN text doesn't call threes), so bands use the NBA's call where matched. The first page default (500 minutes) put 600-minute bench stints at the top, so the default floor is 1,000 minutes (the same as the year-to-year check). A crash on hovering the scatter (a local variable shadowed the formatter) was caught in the browser check and fixed. New tables: `rim_deterrence` (3,646 rows, 1.7 MB), `rim_deterrence_seasons` (6 rows, 24 kB), not yet on Layerbase. Also corrected the stale Known-gaps line that still said the project has no RAPM.

**Just shipped (previously):** Rotation charts (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 3; see What's in it). Data checked first: the stints tell who was on the floor, but only split at substitutions, so the score at exactly 5:00 left in the fourth needed the stint in progress there cut; that is a new optional `split_at` argument on the shared parser rather than a second parser, checked two ways (with the cut, gluing the pieces back gives `lineup_stints` in 7,232 of 7,232 games; without it, `stints()` reproduces `lineup_stints` in 600 of 600 sampled games). Per-team completeness matters more here than the stints' `tracked_ok` (both sides five): 77-99.6% of team-games a season have all five of that team identified in every stint, and the heatmap counts every reconciled game with an Unidentified row instead of dropping partial ones. Player minutes were compared with `player_game_lines` for every player-game: equal except 9, all from a team-less substitution that makes the lines double-count (Known real gaps). New tables: `rotation_closing_games` (7,232 rows, 1.2 MB), `rotation_closing_stints` (37,891 rows, 7.8 MB), not yet on Layerbase. 93/93 smoke tests, eslint and vite build clean; checked in the browser light and dark, 1280 px and 375 px (no horizontal overflow), including the Game Replay link with and without `?game=`.

**Just shipped (previously):** RAPM (2026-09-29, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 2; see What's in it). Data checked first: 284,161 tracked stints give 404,516 side-rows once the 163,806 sides with no possession (substitutions during free throws and the like) are dropped; X'WX is about 1,100 × 1,100 a season, so the normal equations are solved directly and a season fits in about a second, which made 5-fold game-grouped cross-validation over 17 λ values and 300 game bootstraps per fit cheap (the whole build takes 284 s). Two things the first build got wrong and now states instead: the BPM-prior version's cross-validation minimum kept sliding to the top of the λ grid (16,000, then 128,000), i.e. to BPM itself, so that version keeps the single-season λ and records cross-validation's own minimum beside it; and next-season "coverage" counted only possessions where all ten players had an earlier-season rating (38%), which is now stored beside the per-player share (about 90%). Result stated plainly: RAPM with a prior or a three-season window predicts next season's game margins about as well as BPM, plain one-season RAPM a little worse, on/off used as published far worse (its best-fitting scale is 0.09-0.20). New tables about 2.6 MB, not synced to Layerbase.

**Just shipped (previously):** Five-man stints from play-by-play (2026-09-28, round 4 of the analyst list, phase 1; see What's in it). Data checked first: ESPN's score fields are stale in a few hundred games of 2020-21 to 2022-23 (3,847 negative steps in 215 games, jumps over three points in 167), `action_number` is unique per game and holds event order (411 events in 366 games carry a clock later than the event before them; the parser already clamps time forward), one game has only three periods of play-by-play, and 7,229 of 7,232 ESPN games link to `game_scores` through `espn_id` (the 3 NBA Cup finals don't). Measured three ways of crediting points against the real finals before choosing: positive score steps (the lines' method) reconcile in only 512 / 505 / 547 games of the first three seasons, made shots and free throws in 7,052 of 7,229, the running maximum of the score in 7,186; shots first with the running maximum as fallback covers 7,222. Refactor first, then the feature: the on-floor tracking moved verbatim from `build_player_game_lines.py` into `scripts/pbp_lineups.py` (`Game.walk()` replays a game once and drives both `run()`, the player lines, and the new `stints()`), the lines were rebuilt through it and diffed against a snapshot (0 rows changed either way of 152,473), and every player's stint seconds were checked against his line seconds (equal in all but 9 player-games, all with a substitution ESPN tagged to no team). A first full build flagged whole games whenever any stint lacked five identified players (34% of 2020-21 games); the cause was players with no ESPN id and no `player_season_stats` row, so the flag was moved to the stint (a game only loses the minutes such a player was on the floor: 2–6% a season, 0.001% in 2025-26) rather than adding a second name matcher or changing the lines. Reconciliation: 7,220 of 7,232 games match the real final score, the game length and `team_game_totals`' FGA/FTA/OREB/TOV exactly; the 12 that don't are listed with reasons. Sniff tests: each team's five most-used lineups match `lineup_stats`' stored minutes at r 1.000 every season (median gap about a minute; the 2025-26 Thunder's top five 155 vs 167 minutes, the gap one excluded game whose play-by-play is missing six points) and net ratings at r 0.98–0.99; each season's stint minutes equal 48 × games + overtime; 2023-24 Nuggets 57-25 with Jokić and Murray the biggest-minute star pair (1,410 shared minutes, +14.6); 2015-16 Warriors still 73-9 from the stored list. Pair Chemistry, Lineup Chemistry (now stored data with a season selector, floor and order in the link) and the team page's lineups block read the stints from 2020-21 and `lineup_stats` before, and say which. New tables (129 MB, not yet synced to Layerbase — flag for the owner): `lineup_stints`, `lineup_stint_games`, `lineup_stint_seasons`, `lineup_seasons`, `pair_seasons`. Smoke test added (91/91), eslint and vite build clean, light and dark and 375 px checked in the browser.

**Just shipped (previously):** Layerbase re-synced after round 3 of the analyst list (2026-09-28, owner's OK). Round 3 was checked as a whole first (its phases ran in parallel chats in one checkout): 90/90 smoke tests, eslint and vite build clean, every new page loaded in the browser with real data and no console errors. Sniff tests on the combined app: Game Finder's 70+ games are Dončić 73, Lillard 71, Mitchell 71, Embiid 70; Bam Adebayo's 83 (2026-03-10 vs. WAS) is confirmed by the independent stats.nba.com shot chart for that game (20 made field goals, 7 threes; with 36 free throws that is 83); 2023-24 Celtics 64-18 with the league's best SRS (+10.74). Found and fixed: the Projections header said "backtested on 25 seasons" (the backtest covers 26 target seasons, 2000-01 to 2025-26); a running impact_api started before the Luck & Schedule and team-page commits returned 404 for them (code was fine; restarted, and `impact-api` added to `.claude/launch.json`). `--check` found 24 new tables and nothing else: no column differences, and every one of the 86 previously synced tables other than `player_shots`/`pbp_events` content-hash identical once both connections use UTC (6 `timestamptz` tables looked different only because local Postgres runs in Asia/Kolkata); no round 3 script writes to the two big tables and their counts match. Copied the 24 tables (487,095 rows, ~82MB). After: 112 tables, 0 row-count and 0 column differences, 110 tables content-hash identical, all 90 smoke tests pass with `DB_TARGET=layerbase` (no reruns), ~2,114MB of the 5GB free tier.

**Just shipped (previously):** Chart export + hover tooltips (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 10; see What's in it). Surveyed every component with `grep -rl "<svg" frontend/src/components` (36 files) and sorted them into real-data charts to touch, and decorative/game ones to skip (`analytics/ToolPreview.jsx`, `ui/BigStat.jsx`'s sparkline, `pages/LearnTheGame.jsx`'s court diagrams, `pages/GuessTheGame.jsx`, and small in-table CI bars already marked `data-export-skip`/`aria-hidden`). Built the export function first and checked its actual output rather than trusting it: an early version only inlined colors on elements that already had an explicit `fill`/`stroke` attribute or inline style, which missed every chart that colors itself through a CSS class (`.rx-grid`, `.smk-bar`, `.gl-dots`, ...) — those grid lines and bars would have exported invisible. Fixed to always copy `getComputedStyle`'s resolved value (fill, stroke, opacity, stroke-width/dasharray/linejoin, font-family/size/weight, letter-spacing, text-anchor) onto every element and strip its class, then verified in the browser: fetched the exported SVG blob and confirmed zero `var(--` or `class=` left in it, and rasterized the PNG blob to a canvas to confirm real (non-background) pixels were drawn. Checked light and dark themes and 375px/desktop in the browser (PNG/SVG buttons, crosshair hover with the value+n tooltip, and arrow-key stepping all tested live against this session's own two local backends). Smoke tests, eslint and vite build all pass; no Python/table changes, nothing to flag for a Layerbase sync.

**Just shipped (previously):** Real final-score margins everywhere (2026-09-28). Game Log/Game Finder (margin column and the "margin" sort), Schedule Fatigue's rest study and Team Comparison (recent meetings, last-10 form) read `pts_for - pts_against` from `game_scores` instead of `team_game_fatigue.plus_minus` (Known real gaps). A fourth reader turned up while checking: Situational Splits ranked opponents by `AVG(plus_minus)`, which tied NYK and DAL for 10th in 2020-21 (real: NYK +2.31, DAL +2.26), so 11 teams counted as top-10 that season; it now ranks by real margin and `build_situational_splits.py` was rerun (2020-21 top-10 side: 8,157 → 7,427 player-games; pooled top-10 cost 1.08 → 1.09 points per 36; other seasons' gaps unchanged, a few bootstrap/shuffle counts moved slightly because the seeded random stream shifted). Checked before/after on mismatch games: ATL@CHA 2017-10-20 (real 109-91) −22 → −18 in Team Comparison's head-to-head; SAS at DEN 2022-11-05 (101-126) −20 → −25 in Keldon Johnson's game log and head-to-head; UTA at TOR 2022-01-07 (108-122) −9 → −14; LAL-CHI 2026-01-26 (+11) 10.6 → 11 (the game log used to truncate it to 10); rest study 2017-18 back-to-backs −1.866 → −1.873, all seasons −1.986 → −1.991. `game_scores` has exactly the same 40,696 team-game rows as `team_game_fatigue` (0 unmatched either way), and the joins are LEFT joins so a future season loaded before its scores still shows its games (margin blank). With/Without a Star still reads the live stats.nba.com field (it has no stored data to join); disclosed on its Methodology card; the Methodology open issue is removed. Smoke test added. Layerbase: `player_situational_splits` and `situational_split_league` were rebuilt (sync pending with the owner's OK, same as before).

**Just shipped (previously):** Team page (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 3; see What's in it). Data checked first: the app's tables use Basketball-Reference codes before 2009-10 and NBA codes after (PHO/PHX, CHH/CHA, SEA/OKC), so `api/teams_lib.py` maps codes to seasons and franchises, and `team_seasons` (from the Basketball-Reference team files) gives every team-season a record and ratings, including the pre-2009-10 seasons most blocks don't cover; its W-L matches `team_game_fatigue` for all 510 team-seasons 2009-10 on and every code in the player tables resolves. `player_shots` has no team, so `team_zone_mix` places each shot by the game's two teams and the team the shooter appears with most; a first attempt that trusted each player's listed team lost whole games and up to 96% of a team's shots, which found the mislabeled 2024-25 rows (Known real gaps); the final method places 99.98% and agrees with the play-by-play 99.82%. Checked with a contender (2023-24 Celtics: 64-18, SRS +10.75 first, ORtg 123.2 first; Tatum tops the roster by minutes; payroll "Not on file" for 2023-24), a rebuilding team (2025-26 Grizzlies 25-57) and 2024-25 Grizzlies (payroll $166.5M, 13th, with Bane counted for MEM and Anthony/Caldwell-Pope listed as left out), older seasons (1995-96 Bulls 72-10, 2004-05 SuperSonics via OKC + 2005, 2000-01 Vancouver: game-by-game, luck, payroll, lineups and on/off "Not on file" with the reason each). Browser (temporary full-app harness against this session's own API, deleted): all blocks, season switching (incl. into the Vancouver years), franchise-bar clicks, links from Luck & Schedule, Standings, Ctrl+K and the player profile push the right `?page=team` URLs (page transitions freeze in the hidden pane, as CLAUDE.md notes, so each URL was also loaded directly); Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast failures over 800-1,080 text nodes a page, no overflow. Smoke test added. Layerbase not synced: `team_seasons` and `team_zone_mix` are new.

**Just shipped (previously):** Luck & Schedule (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 9; see What's in it). Data checked first, and it changed the plan: `team_game_fatigue.plus_minus` turned out not to be the final margin (Known real gaps), so a new `scripts/fetch_game_scores.py` pulls every final score from ESPN's scoreboard (2,701 dates) and matches all 20,348 games to NBA ids (10 neutral-site games by team pair, 4 that ESPN dates a day off; in 5 neutral-site games `team_game_fatigue.opponent` names the team itself, so the opponent is taken from the game's other row). Checks: winners agree with `team_game_fatigue.win` in every game, no ties, and season points for and against equal Basketball-Reference's (Kaggle export) for all 510 team-seasons. `scripts/build_luck_schedule.py` then fits three expected-win curves (Pythagorean won, exponent 13.94, next to Morey's published 13.91), computes luck, close-game and OT records, and per-season SRS/SOS with a home-court term. Sniff tests: SRS correlates 1.000 with Basketball-Reference's (average gap 0.01, largest 0.26 in the 2019-20 bubble season); 2015-16 Warriors +10.38 and Spurs +10.28, 2016-17 Warriors +11.35, 2023-24 Celtics +10.74, 2024-25 Thunder +12.68 all first; home court +0.9 in the mostly empty-arena 2020-21 against 2.1-3.2 in 2009-10 to 2019-20; the luckiest (2022-23 Bucks +8.1, 2015-16 Warriors +7.6) and unluckiest (2021-22 Celtics −7.9) match their well-known stories. Validation: luck year to year r = 0.14 (0.05-0.22, 480 franchise pairs) vs. 0.59 for margin, close-game win% 0.11; at each season's halfway date, ratings + remaining schedule miss rest-of-season wins by 4.5 (RMS) vs. 4.7 for points so far and 5.0 for the record. Browser (temporary harness against this session's own API on another port, deleted): full season, as-of/Halfway, team history (incl. NOH→NOP), sorting and bar clicks; Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast failures over ~900 text nodes, no page overflow. Smoke test added; full suite, eslint and vite build pass. Layerbase not synced: `game_scores`, `team_luck_schedule`, `luck_schedule_seasons`, `luck_model_fit`, `luck_schedule_validation` are new.

**Just shipped (previously):** Situational Splits (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 5; see What's in it). Data checked first, and it changed two things. (1) Travel: 94% of zero-mile games are home games (home stands), so "long trip vs. none" would mostly measure home court; the short side is under 300 miles instead, and the league effect is also fitted within player-seasons with home/away as a control (points per 36: -0.28 raw, -0.15 controlled). (2) Chance: the plan's "5% expected outside 95%" undercounts with a dozen back-to-backs a season (the per-player interval is a little too narrow for small samples), so the chance count is measured by shuffling each player's own games between the two sides 50 times; e.g. blocks on back-to-backs: 121 standouts, 116 shuffled, 57 at a flat 5%. Sniff tests: home court small but positive and smallest in 2020-21 (no fans); back-to-back minutes rise for players who play both nights, confirmed as selection (a team's three biggest-minute players suit up 5% less often on second nights); Jokić 2024-25's home and away games add back to his game log (70 games, points within 1). Browser: page (all four splits, stats, ranks, team filter, link restore) and the profile block on Jokić, Paper and Ink, 375 px and desktop, no page overflow. Smoke test added (87/87). New tables `player_situational_splits`, `situational_split_league` need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Next-season projections (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 6; see What's in it). `scripts/build_projections.py` (~90 s) projects every target season 2000-01 to 2026-27 from the seasons before it and writes `player_projections` (17,012 rows: 501 players × 34 stats), `projection_backtest` (913 rows), `projection_backtest_rows` (259,919), `projection_ranges`, `projection_stats`. Built on Stat Stability's M values and the Aging Curves; ages from `player_bio` birth dates, so the 2009-10 age-convention break never enters. Found by sniff-testing and fixed before shipping: (1) the population minutes curve under-projected 31+ year-olds playing 28+ minutes by 2.6-3.5 minutes (worse than "same as last season" for them) and over-projected young starters, so minutes get a level-conditional age step measured from the regressed projection's own misses, leave-one-season-out (minutes MAE 4.00 → 3.87, that group's bias now within 0.5 minutes); a first attempt that chained per-year residual steps re-added the regression each year and was dropped; (2) range bins with 1-11 errors gave point ranges, so bins under 50 errors fall back to the reliability bin, then the whole stat; (3) a 3-game call-up came out at 13 points a game because minutes regress toward a starter's league average, so players under 250 minutes over three seasons get no projection and a stated reason. Known and stated, not fixed: the 31 backtest players still scoring 22+ at 34 or older beat their projection by 3 points a game. Browser (temporary harness against this session's own API, deleted): projections page in Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast issues, no page overflow, team/name filters and stat links rewrite the link; profile block "Next season" on Jokić, and the 250-minute reason on a fringe player. Methodology card added; smoke test added. New tables need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Hot Streak Checker (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 4; see What's in it). Data checked first, and it changed the method: the plan said to shrink runs with `stat_stability`, but that reliability is between players (points per game is half-reliable after 0.55 games because roles differ), so for counting stats it would call 90-97% of a run real. Measured directly instead, on 2.1 million windows. A season-to-date baseline alone made 3P% look 55% persistent over 20 games because a 10-20-game baseline is itself noisy (its error sits in both gaps); adding the previous season fixed that and predicted better out of sample, so the weight is chosen per stat on 2023-24 and scored on 2024-25/2025-26, where the rule beats both simple alternatives for all 42 stat/window pairs. Sniff tests passed: shooting runs near-pure noise (and "significant" hot shooters appear at the chance rate), minutes and usage persist most, and on past dates the listed players' actual next games matched the expected share (points 49-70% carried vs. 48% expected; 3P% 10-19% vs. 13%). Browser: league page (hot/cold, stats, dates, link restore) and the profile card in Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, through a temporary standalone harness (deleted; the shared app build was briefly broken by another chat's in-progress import at the time). Smoke test added. New table `hot_streak_persistence` needs a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Expected FG% and shot-making (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 8; see What's in it). Data checked first: `shot_distance` is unreliable (15,139 of 2024-25's 92,454 regular-season threes carry 0 while their coordinates say 23 ft, 11-17% of threes a season; this read "about 7%" until 2026-09-29, which is the share of all shots, not of threes), so distance comes from `loc_x`/`loc_y`; before 2010-11 about a quarter of shots sit at (0, 0) with distance 0 (rim shots without an exact location, FG% 55-60%, the same as the located restricted-area shots of those seasons), kept as they are with the season as an input; 10 shots with impossible locations (`loc_x` −16398) dropped. `scripts/build_shot_making.py` (~5 min) trains on 1996-97 to 2024-25 and scores 2025-26 (218,443 shots) by log loss: constant 0.6915, last season's FG% by zone 0.6568, logistic regression on engineered terms 0.6566, HistGradientBoosting 0.6483 (Brier 0.2285, ROC-AUC 0.652; every reliability bucket with 1,000+ shots within 2.3 points of its observed make rate), then cross-fits the boosting model by player (5 folds; log loss 0.6544 over all 5.93M shots; each season's league expected eFG% within 0.37 points of actual). Sniff tests: Curry 2015-16 +13.0 eFG points (1st of 326, +416 points), Korver 2014-15 +15.0 (1st of 329; 49.2% from three against 36.6% expected), Jokić 2024-25 +11.0 (1st of 333); Gobert 2024-25 and DeAndre Jordan 2014-15 rank 1st in shot quality (expected eFG 66.2% and 73.2%) with shot-making inside one standard error of zero; Westbrook 2022-23 −6.6 (306th of 332), Josh Smith 2013-14 −4.7. Year to year (200+ attempts in both seasons, 6,866 pairs): shot quality r = 0.88, raw eFG% 0.67, shot-making 0.56, so the shot diet is a trait and shot-making only moderately stable. The first run stored the text `NaN` as the team of player-seasons without a season row (pandas 3 keeps a missing string as NaN); fixed and rebuilt. Browser (temporary harness against this session's own API on port 8018, deleted): tab, chart, hover panel, tables, leaderboard controls and link sync, profile block and "Not on file" reason, Methodology card; Paper and Ink, desktop and 375 px. Smoke test added. New tables `player_shot_making`, `shot_making_league`, `shot_making_validation` need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** On/Off for every player (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 2; see What's in it). Data checked first: team-game totals derived from `pbp_events` with the same event rules as `build_player_game_lines.py` reconcile with the lines (the on-court FGA summed over a team's players equals exactly 5× the team's FGA in 78–100% of games by season, the rest off by a possession or two where a lineup was briefly mis-tracked; only 2 free throws in six seasons carry no team; 366 overtime games get their extra seconds), and the derived possessions correlate 0.95–0.96 with `game_team_box.poss_est` per game while running 0.6–1.1% higher. `scripts/build_player_on_off.py` (~16 s) writes `team_game_totals`, `player_on_off` and `player_on_off_seasons`; on + off possessions equal the team's game total over his games by construction (checked: Jokić 2022-23, 6,976.5 both ways), 26 of 152,442 player-games had an on-court total above the team's and were clipped to zero off-court. Sniff tests in the script's output: the league's possession-weighted on-court net is 0.000–0.063 every season; 2023-24 Celtics 64-18 / Nuggets 57-25 / Pistons 14-68; Jokić leads on − off among 1,000+ minute players in 2022-23, 2023-24 and 2024-25 and the Nuggets are a net negative without him every season from 2021-22 on; OG Anunoby's 2023-24 Knicks stint is +30.5 in 23 games; Wembanyama leads 2025-26. Honest result kept on the page: of players with 500+ minutes, 11–15% a season have a 95% interval clear of zero (5% would by chance), so most on/off gaps are noise. Endpoints `GET /lineups/on-off` (team or league view) and `/lineups/on-off/stars` (each team's top-usage regular with and without him) answer in about 20 ms; the profile endpoint gained an `on_off` block. Browser (temporary harness against this session's own API on port 8012, deleted): all three views, sortable headers with the sort in the link, export, Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast issues over 4,800 text nodes, no page overflow (the table scrolls in its wrapper; the range bars hide under 640 px); profile block for Jokić, "Not on file" reason for a pre-2020 player. Methodology card added; smoke test added (`test_on_off_known_cases`). New tables `team_game_totals`, `player_on_off`, `player_on_off_seasons` need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK, after the round).

**Just shipped (previously):** Game Log + Game Finder (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 1; see What's in it). Data checked first: `player_game_lines` has 152,374 played lines in 7,232 games; joined to `team_game_fatigue` on (team, date) every line matches except the three NBA Cup finals (2023-12-09 IND-LAL, 2024-12-17 MIL-OKC, 2025-12-16 NYK-SAS: these were the 25 unmatched 2024-25 lines) and one 2021-22 line with no team; the finals don't count in regular-season stats, so both pages leave them out and say so. Rebuild accuracy shown on the pages is computed live from the lines vs. NBA.com: points within 0.4% for the average player-season (95% within 1.3%), 3PA 1.4% short overall. Sniff tests: the famous games and streaks listed in What's in it match their real box scores; the play-by-play also has Bam Adebayo scoring 83 on 2026-03-10 (MIA 150-129 WAS, matching the stored margin; not checked against an outside box score). Found while checking: per-game on-court plus-minus from the lines is internally inconsistent in 29% of team-games (Known real gaps), so it isn't shown. No new table or index (the existing `(season, player_id)` index covers the log query; finder queries run in 10-150 ms over the 152k rows). Browser (temporary rAF harness, deleted): Game Finder games and streak modes, presets, player filter from a link, paging, sorting; profile Game log with chart and season switch; Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, no page overflow. Smoke test added. Nothing to sync to Layerbase.

**Just shipped (previously):** Aging Curves (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 7; see What's in it). Data checked first: `player_season_stats` has one row per player-season (traded seasons combined), 18,176 consecutive-season pairs in all; `player_bio.birth_date` covers 23,401 of 23,408 player-seasons, and the age it gives on February 1 matches the stored age for 99.5% of pre-2010 rows (Basketball-Reference's convention) but only 56% of 2009-10+ rows (NBA.com's, a year older for the rest), so every age is taken from birth dates. NBA.com's rows for 2009-10 to 2024-25 only exist from 200 minutes, hence the 250-minute floor in every era; rebound %, offensive rebound % and turnover % change definition at the same line (median rebound % 0.091 in 2008-09, 0.079 in 2009-10), so pairs across it are left out for the five rate stats. New `scripts/build_aging_curves.py` (~3 s) writes `aging_curves` (1,455 rows), `aging_curve_summary` (72: 24 stats × 3 eras) and `aging_league_average`. Checked: the peak ages above, and the three eras put the peak within a year of each other for 19 of 24 stats (the 2009-10-on curve is two years off for rebounds, rebound %, offensive rebound %, free-throw % and turnovers); at 34, 34% of qualified players never had another 250-minute season and they were already 2.1 BPM below those who did; LeBron's ages come out 19 in 2003-04 and 25 in 2009-10, Kawhi Leonard's 34 in 2025-26 (matching Basketball-Reference). Browser: Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast issues, no page overflow, examples and a pasted link restore stat, era and player, a bad stat in a link falls back to BPM, Wilt's BPM overlay says none of his seasons can be placed (BPM starts 1973-74). Methodology card added; smoke test added. New tables `aging_curves`, `aging_curve_summary`, `aging_league_average` need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Saved analyses (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 11; see What's in it). Front end only: no new table, endpoint or pipeline script. A **Save** button (`common/SaveViewButton.jsx`) sits next to every tool's `CopyLinkButton` and writes the current URL, an auto-generated title and an empty note to `utils/savedViews.js` (localStorage, same try/catch-and-degrade pattern as `utils/watchlist.js`; nothing sent to the server). The new Saved page (`?page=saved`, own top-nav entry next to Watchlist) lists every save with an inline-editable title and note, **Open** (added `openFullUrl()` to `utils/useUrlState.js`, since the existing `openPage()` rebuilds the query string from a params object and would drop inputs it doesn't know about — a saved URL is replayed exactly instead), **Delete**, and JSON **Export**/**Import** (`downloadText()`, reused from `utils/tableExport.js`; import merges by URL so re-importing the same export twice adds nothing new). Checked in the browser: saved a Stat Stability view with a non-default stat from its Save button, confirmed Open on the Saved page navigated back to that exact URL; edited its title and added a note in place, then deleted it; the Export JSON button ran with no console error (reuses the same `downloadText()` CSV/JSON export already shipped and checked); `importSavedViews()` itself checked directly with a 2-entry file (one new URL, one duplicate of an existing save) returned `{added: 1, skipped: 1}` and the new row appeared on the page. Paper and Ink contrast checked, 375 px and desktop, no console errors. Lint and production build clean. No backend change, so the existing smoke suite is unaffected; no table to flag for a Layerbase sync.

**Just shipped (previously):** Data Coverage page (2026-09-28, round 3 of the analyst list, phase 12; see What's in it). New `GET /meta/coverage` in `api/routers/meta.py`: a hand-maintained `COVERAGE_MAP` (25 tables) paired with a live `_coverage()` lookup that runs `to_regclass`/`COUNT`/`MIN`/`MAX` against each real table, cached per process like the rest of the app's `lru_cache` endpoints. No pipeline script needed — everything numeric comes straight off tables other features already built. Checked before writing the source/gap text for each row by querying real row counts and season spans directly rather than trusting numbers already written elsewhere (`player_season_stats` 23,408 rows 1949-50 → 2025-26, `player_shots` 6,318,078 rows 1996-97 → 2025-26, `player_game_lines` 152,473 rows 2020-21 → 2025-26, `contract_value` non-contiguous 2005-06 → 2019-20 plus 2024-25 only). New page `?page=coverage` (Analytics menu, next to Methodology): summary stats, a group filter (Core/Shooting/Games/Teams/Models/Draft/College/Players), a table with CSV/JSON export, and a "Used by" column linking each table to the page(s) that read it (player profile is named but not clickable there, since it needs a `?id=`). A table present in the map but missing from the database surfaces as a banner instead of a silent zero. Checked in the browser: Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, live counts matched a direct `psql` check, group tabs and the "Used by" links both navigate correctly, no console errors. Smoke test added: every `COVERAGE_MAP` table must actually exist and return a nonzero count. No new table, so nothing to flag for a Layerbase sync.

**Just shipped (previously):** Layerbase re-synced after round 2 of the analyst list (2026-09-28, owner's OK). Before syncing, the whole round was checked together, since its 12 phases were built in parallel chats in one checkout: 79/79 smoke tests, lint and production build clean, every new page loaded in the browser with real data and no console errors. `--check` found 11 new tables missing (`league_season_averages`, `league_zone_mix`, `player_awards`, `player_bio`, `player_game_lines`, `player_team_stints`, `referee_crew_tendencies`, `stat_stability`, `stat_stability_curve`, `stat_year_to_year`, `wpa_clutch_league`) and `referee_tendencies` rebuilt (97 rows vs. 77); a column comparison found `player_wpa_totals` with the same 880 rows but 8 new clutch columns, which row counts alone would have missed. Those 13 tables were re-copied (177,426 rows). After: 88 tables, 0 row-count and 0 column differences, the 13 tables' contents hash-identical to local, all 79 smoke tests pass with `DB_TARGET=layerbase` (no reruns needed), ~2,031MB of the 5GB free tier.

**Just shipped (previously):** Stat Stability (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 5; see What's in it). Data checked first: the project had no per-game player box scores, so `scripts/build_player_game_lines.py` rebuilds them from ESPN play-by-play (~1 min): shots, assists, steals, blocks, rebounds and turnovers parsed from the text, minutes from substitutions (starters of each period inferred; 949 of ~58,700 team-periods needed a fill from the previous period's lineup). Checked against NBA.com season totals for 2,595 player-seasons with 20+ games: games 1.000, minutes 1.000 (mean error 0.3%), points 1.000, FGA 1.001, 3PA 0.986, FTA 1.000, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks, turnovers 0.998-1.000; rebuilt usage/assist/rebound/turnover % correlate 0.999+ with NBA.com's (turnover % only once assists are in the denominator, as NBA.com does), on-court ratings 0.96-0.99. `scripts/build_stat_stability.py` (~45 s) then computes split-half reliability per stat. Sniff tests: 3P% 476 attempts sits between the published 242 (Medvedovsky 2020) and 750 (Blackport 2014, cited by The Ringer); FT% (27 attempts) and rebound % (51 chances) stabilise fast; net rating ~1,000 possessions matches Medvedovsky's "over 1,000" for plus-minus. Browser (temporary harness against this session's own API on port 8012, deleted): table, curve, per-minute toggle, stat links and URL restore; Leaderboard reliability column and greyed rows; Breakout noise markers; Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast issues, no page overflow. Methodology card added; smoke test added. New tables `player_game_lines`, `stat_stability`, `stat_stability_curve`, `stat_year_to_year` need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Player profile page (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 2; see What's in it). New `scripts/build_player_profile_data.py` → `player_bio` (4,938 players), `player_awards` (7,255 rows) and `player_team_stints` (5,829 team rows) from the gitignored Basketball-Reference export, joined through `player_id_map` (players without an NBA id match are skipped and counted). The script asserts well-known facts before it commits: Jordan 5 MVP, 1 DPOY, 1 ROY, 11 All-NBA (10 first team), 14 All-Star; Jokić and Bird 3 MVP each; Harden 2021-22 BKN 44 + PHI 21; Durant 2022-23 BKN 39 + PHX 8. Also checked: Kareem's 6 MVPs are the most; Jokić's MVP finishes from 2018-19 read 4th, 9th, 1st, 1st, 2nd, 1st, 2nd; Wilt 1964-65 SFW 38 + PHI 35; the All-Star file's "replaced" flag means selected but didn't play (Kobe 2014 and 2015, Durant and Embiid 2021). New `GET /player-profile/{id}` (plus `/shot-zones?season=` and `/resolve?name=`); `/players/profile/{name}` was already taken by the older live endpoint, hence the path. The similarity endpoint now takes an optional `player_id` (two current-era names are shared: Chris Johnson, Mike James). The Exploit Guide card's strength/weakness colours were raw hex that failed contrast on Paper; they now use `--positive`/`--negative`. Checked in the browser with a current star (Jokić), pre-2000 legends (Jordan, Wilt), a rookie (Cooper Flagg), a player traded three times (Harden) and a 9-game season (Kawhi Leonard 2017-18, greyed): name links, the search palette, the modal button, Back/Forward between two profiles; Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop, contrast scan 0 issues, no page overflow. Smoke test added. The three new tables need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Watchlist (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 11; see What's in it). Front end only: no new table, endpoint or pipeline script. A star toggle on `common/PlayerName.jsx` (so it appears everywhere a player's name already goes through that shared component — most tables and lists; a few pages that build their own row markup directly, like Stat Leaders, don't get it yet) adds or removes a player from `localStorage` (`utils/watchlist.js`, wrapped in try/catch like `utils/theme.js`; nothing sent to the server, so it's per-browser only). The new Watchlist page (`?page=watchlist`, own top-nav entry) re-fetches each starred player's existing `GET /players/history/{name}` and shows their most recent season's PTS/REB/AST/STL/BLK/TS%/Net Rtg side by side — explicitly their *most recent* season, not assumed to be the current one, since a retired or not-yet-played-this-year player would otherwise show a misleading blank or stale row; a player with no season data at all is named as failed rather than silently dropped. `PlayerName` and the Watchlist page both read the list through `useSyncExternalStore` against a module-level cache (a stable array reference, replaced only on actual change) so every star button and the page itself stay in sync without polling. `<TableExport />` above the table. Checked in the browser: starred LeBron James from the Season Similarity results table, confirmed the button flips to "remove" immediately, then opened the Watchlist page and saw his real 2025-26 line (41, 60 GP, 33.2 min, 20.9/8.1/7.2, 59.4% TS, +2.6 net) with a working CSV/JSON export; removing him from the Watchlist page's own star cleared the row live, back to the empty-state message. Paper and Ink contrast checked, no overflow at 375 px. Lint clean. No backend change, so the existing smoke suite is unaffected.

**Just shipped (previously):** Referee crew tendencies (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 12; see What's in it). `scripts/build_referee_tendencies.py` extended to also build `referee_crew_tendencies`, grouping `game_officials` by the exact real 3-official crew that worked each game (games with any other official count excluded), and running the same season-adjusted fouls/FTA/pace comparison used for single officials. New `GET /referees/crew-tendencies` (impact_api) and a **By Official / By Crew** `SegmentedControl` toggle in `RefereeTendenciesSection.jsx`. Checked before shipping: 7,230 real games with complete two-team box stats feed both tables; 97 real officials and 5,373 real distinct 3-official crews come out; **only 514 of those 5,373 crews ever worked together more than once** (max 4 real games for any exact crew) — real crew assignment is close to random game to game, so the small-sample floor (10 real games) is a genuine finding about crew rarity, stated plainly in the UI and methodology text rather than hidden. Browser: Paper and Ink, 375 px and desktop, toggle switches data and heading text correctly in both themes (checked through a temporary harness page, since other in-progress work had the full app shell failing to compile at the time; harness deleted). Smoke tests added (2 new, 73/73 passing). `referee_crew_tendencies` needs a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Trade Impact view (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 4; see What's in it). No new table and nothing to sync: one aggregating endpoint (`GET /trade/impact`) over `/trade/simulate`, the Spacing Lab's lineup report and `contract_value`. Checked in the browser in light and dark at 375 px and desktop (through a temporary harness, deleted before the commit): the linked NYK–MIA case rendered every block with real numbers, input changes rewrite the link, and the hand-off buttons carry the trade between Trade Analyzer and Trade Impact. Smoke test added (`test_trade_impact_combines_wins_spacing_and_payroll`); the whole suite passes.

**Just shipped (previously):** Role Player Finder (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 3; see What's in it). No new table: one endpoint joins six existing tables per season. The weights are a judgment call, shown in full on the page and on the Methodology card; they were tuned only by checking that well-known role players land near the top (the misses are named, not hidden). Found while checking: `usg_pct` is stored as a fraction, so the usage cap is applied on the percent scale; league-wide z-scores rank a center's three-point volume against guards, which is why the stretch-big preset compares within the chosen positions. Checked in the browser: presets, position chips, usage/salary filters and edited weights all restore from a pasted link (`?page=rolefinder&p=stretch_big&s=2025&pos=C,C-F&rel=positions&usg=22&sal=15&w=…`); contrast 0 issues in Paper and Ink; no overflow at 375 px (the source badge needed the shorter upstream label the other tools use). Smoke test added.

**Just shipped (previously):** Era Translator (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 6; see What's in it). New `scripts/build_league_averages.py` → `league_season_averages` (80 rows, 1946-47 to 2025-26, from the gitignored Basketball-Reference export's "League Average" rows: 53 seasons with pace from full box scores, 23 with Basketball-Reference's estimated pace, 4 estimated here) and `GET /era/translate` + `GET /era/players` (impact_api), computed live from `player_season_stats`. Checked before shipping: league scoring 118.8 a team game in 1961-62, 79.5 in 1953-54 (the last season before the shot clock), 105.6 in 2016-17; the Oscar and Wilt translations above; Westbrook 2016-17 into 1961-62 shows no three-point line and no steals/blocks/turnovers (not recorded then); Mikan 1949-50 says its pace is estimated here and its pool uses games only (minutes not recorded); Wilt's 12-game 1969-70 gets the small-sample warning; Bird's 1979-80 3P% isn't ranked (1.7 3PA a game, under the 2.0 floor). Browser: Paper and Ink, 375 px and desktop, 0 contrast issues, no horizontal overflow, a pasted link reopens the same player, season and target (checked through a temporary harness page, since other in-progress work had the full app shell failing to compile at the time; harness deleted). Methodology card added. Smoke test added. `league_season_averages` needs a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Pair Chemistry grid (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 10; see What's in it). Data checked first: `lineup_stats` has exactly 2,000 lineups in every season 2013-14 to 2025-26 (stats.nba.com's cap), so pair numbers can only cover minutes inside those lineups; Pair Synergy's live 2-man fetch is out while stats.nba.com is unreachable, and reconstructing stints from `pbp_events` substitutions would be a separate, larger pipeline. New `GET /lineups/pair-grid` (impact_api) aggregates on the fly (one team-season is ~50-80 lineups), so no new table and nothing to sync. Pair ratings are weighted by possessions rather than minutes (ratings are per 100 possessions, so this gives the exact rating over those lineups). Sniff tests: record from `team_game_fatigue` matches (2015-16 GSW 73-9, SAS 67-15, PHI 10-72); Curry + Green are the Warriors' most-shared pair (2,179 stored minutes, +19.8) and the top three pairs with 500+ shared minutes all include Iguodala (+26.5 to +28.9); every player's stored minutes stay under their season minutes; Mikal Bridges 2022-23 splits into a PHX part and a BKN part that sum to less than his season total. `team_game_fatigue.plus_minus` averages +10.87 for those Warriors against Basketball-Reference's +10.76 margin, so the page shows only the W-L record, not a margin. Checked in the browser: link restores team, season, rating and floor; changing an input rewrites the link; leaving the tab drops its inputs from the link; text contrast in Paper and Ink at least 4.5:1 (hatched cells) and 9.4:1 (tinted cells); no overflow at 375 px (the grid scrolls inside its card with a sticky name column). Smoke test added.

**Just shipped (previously):** Clutch vs. non-clutch (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 8; see What's in it). `scripts/compute_wpa.py` extended (rerun: ~35 s); the existing `player_wpa_totals` columns came out identical for all 880 players, and clutch + non-clutch plays/WPA add up to the totals. Why not the plan's plain clutch rate − non-clutch rate: clutch plays carry 3.7× the leverage, so the raw difference was positive for nearly everyone; and per all events, role players' clutch plays shift to rebounds and fouls (usage, not performance), so the rate is per scoring chance. Sniff tests: the leverage-neutral unit reads as points — the league's 0.914 per chance outside clutch time sits inside its real points / (FGA + FTA + TOV), 0.90-0.93 in every season 2020-21 to 2025-26; with 50 / 100 / 200+ clutch chances, 24 / 14 / 12 players land outside zero at 95% where 16 / 9 / 6 would by chance, and lift ÷ SE has an SD of 1.09 (1.0 = pure noise), so almost all the spread is noise, as expected; DeMar DeRozan is the most certain "better" at every floor (+0.12 points per chance, 95% +0.05 to +0.20). Checked in the browser (temporary harness, since deleted): floor, sort and the outside-zero filter; Paper and Ink contrast scan 0 issues; no page overflow at 375 px (the table scrolls in its wrapper). Smoke test added. Methodology card added. The new columns and `wpa_clutch_league` need a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Stat Line Finder (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 7; see What's in it). Computed on the fly from `player_season_stats` (about 21,000 player-seasons, cached once per process in `similarity_api._line_matrix()`; restart the similarity service after reloading stats), so no new table and nothing to sync. The plan said to reuse Season Similarity's matrix, but it only has points, TS%, usage, net rating, AST%, REB%, age and minutes (2009-10 on), so "8 assists" couldn't be typed; the new matrix uses the same within-season z-score with the box-score stats people type, back to 1949-50 where recorded. Distance is RMS z-gap rather than cosine because a typed line is about levels: with cosine a smaller copy of the same mix would match. Checked: each sniff-test season's own line returns itself at 0.00; "25 pts, 60% TS, 8 ast" read in 2025-26 gives Archibald 1975-76, Dončić 2020-21, Payton 2000-01, Harden 2025-26; Wilt's 50.4/25.7 read in 1961-62 finds itself, then Shaq 1999-00, Moses Malone 1981-82, Mikan 1950-51; read in 2025-26 nothing is within 3.7 SDs and the page says so. Browser: examples, adding a later-era stat (steals clips the range to 1973-74 and says so), reload, a pasted link and Back all restore the same view; no overflow at 375 px, contrast scan 0 issues in Ink. Smoke test added (70/70). Methodology card added.

**Just shipped (previously):** Shot mix over a career (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 9; see What's in it → Shot charts). New `scripts/build_league_zone_mix.py` → `league_zone_mix` (150 rows, ~7 s to rebuild) and `GET /shots/player/{name}/zone-history` (impact_api), reading stored `player_shots` only (no live fetch), regular season only (`game_id LIKE '002%'`). Sniff tests before shipping: stored shots match box-score FGA (`player_season_stats` fga × gp): season totals within 0.4% in every season 1996-97 to 2025-26, the average player gap at most 1%, and no player-season with 200+ attempts is off by more than 10%; league 3-point share matches Basketball-Reference's 3PAr (0.160 in 1997-98, 0.285 in 2015-16, 0.337 in 2017-18, 0.421 in 2024-25); Curry 2015-16 = 1,598 FGA with 55% threes; Shaq's above-the-break share stays near 0; Dirk's mid-range share sits around 50% for a decade; Harden 2021-22 (traded) is one row with 992 attempts vs. 994 in the box score; Hakeem shows the note that his first 12 seasons predate shot locations; Magic Johnson gets a plain "no shot locations" message. Checked in the browser in Paper and Ink at 375 px and desktop (no overflow). Zone colours are the first five dataviz reference-palette slots, validated on both surfaces; values are always in the legend/tables too. Not done: the Phase 2 player profile page isn't in `main` yet, so the chart isn't on it; it's one line to add there. `league_zone_mix` needs a Layerbase sync (owner's OK).

**Just shipped (previously):** Shareable links for every tool (2026-09-28, round 2 of the analyst list, phase 1; see What's in it). Front end only: no new table or endpoint. Pages moved from React state into `?page=`; seven tools keep their inputs in the query string via one shared hook (`utils/useUrlState.js`). Checked in the browser: each tool reopened from a pasted link with the same inputs and results (e.g. 3P% 2019-20 to 2023-24 with 4+ 3PA: Joe Harris's 47.5% in 2020-21 first; 2016-17 breakouts on points/assist %/BPM: James Johnson, Dion Waiters, Giannis), input changes rewrite the URL without growing history, Back/Forward remount the page with its inputs, old `/#wpa` links become `?page=analytics#wpa`, nav-menu and Command Palette links land on the right Analytics tab (Methodology's links produce the same `?page=analytics#<tab>` URL), and the landing page is still `/`. Invalid values in a link fall back to defaults (a Trade Analyzer player not on that roster is dropped). Copy link: 9:1 contrast in Paper and Ink, no overflow at 375 px. Also fixed on the way: the nav menu, Command Palette and Methodology used to set the tab hash one frame after switching page, which added a second history entry.

**Just shipped (previously):** Layerbase re-synced (2026-09-28, owner's OK): the 4 new tables (`greats`, `greats_meta`, `player_first_season`, `award_chance_calibration`) and the 3 rebuilt ones whose row counts matched but whose contents changed with the ROY retrain (`model_backtest_summary`, `model_backtest_seasons`, `shap_explanations`), 5,306 rows. `--check`: 77 tables, 0 differences; contents spot-checked equal (calibration parameters, ROY backtest 9 of 14, 333 earlier first seasons). All 68 smoke tests pass with `DB_TARGET=layerbase` (2 reran after a dropped SSL connection in the 15-minute run).

**Just shipped (previously):** Breakout Detector (2026-09-28), last item of the analyst list (see What's in it). Computed on the fly from `player_season_stats` (a few hundred rows a season, cached per stat set), so no new table. The persistence line is measured, not asserted: for each season, the same players' standing the following year against the year before the breakout. Found while checking: two age conventions in the table (see Known real gaps). Contrast 0 issues in Paper and Ink, no overflow at 375 px. Smoke test added (68/68).

**Just shipped (previously):** Regression Explorer (2026-09-28), seventh item of the analyst list (see What's in it). Sniff tests: assists vs. turnovers r = 0.80; usage vs. true shooting among 20+ minute players ≈ 0 (95% interval includes zero); threes vs. points positive with Jordan's 1986-87 and 1987-88 furthest above the line; age vs. BPM slightly positive, which the page attributes to who stays in the league. A smoke test checks the fitted slope against r × SD(y) / SD(x). Caught in the browser: at phone width the chart scaled its 11 px labels down to ~4.5 px; the SVG's coordinate system now follows the container width so text renders at real size. Contrast 0 issues in Paper and Ink (SVG text included); no overflow at 375 px. 67/67 smoke tests.

**Just shipped (previously):** Build-your-own-metric mode in the Leaderboard Builder (2026-09-28), sixth item of the analyst list. `GET /leaderboard/composite` (same router and stat catalogue as the single-stat builder) z-scores each chosen stat within its season's qualified pool, so a multi-era ranking is era-relative; lower-is-better stats are flipped; a percentage on fewer attempts than the builder's floor is treated as average rather than dropping the player (before 2009-10, 3P% is empty for players with no attempts, which would otherwise have removed every non-shooter). Checked: 2025-26 points + TS% gives SGA, Jokić, Dončić, Giannis; points + rebounds + assists since 1973-74 gives Jokić 2025-26, Jokić 2024-25, Westbrook 2016-17; blocks + steals + DBPM gives Wembanyama. Found and disclosed rather than capped: threes + 3P% since 1979-80 is led by Darrell Griffith 1983-84, 7.7 SDs above a season in which almost nobody shot threes. Found while checking contrast: the app-wide active tab (`.tab-btn--active`, orange text on a 16% orange tint) was 4.15:1 in Paper; a new `--tab-active-text` token darkens it in light mode only (now 6.4:1; Ink unchanged at 5.5:1), which fixes every tab bar in the app. Long source badges now wrap on phones. Smoke test added (66/66).

**Just shipped (previously):** ROY pool fixed and ROY model retrained (2026-09-27), found while calibrating award chances. A rookie was a player whose first season in `player_season_stats` was the current one, but that table misses many short early stints, so 240 "rookie" player-seasons across 2010-11 to 2025-26 had played in the NBA before (53 in 2025-26: Bronny James, Daniss Jenkins, Sidy Cissoko, Alondes Williams at 12% to win ROY...). New `scripts/build_first_nba_season.py` → `player_first_season`: the earlier of Basketball-Reference's first NBA/BAA season (via `player_id_map`) and the table's own first season; 4,413 of 4,417 players linked, 333 with an earlier first season on Basketball-Reference, none later. The shared pool function (training, backtest, SHAP, calibration) and `/roy/predict` now use it. Checked: all 14 ROY winners stay in the pool (including Blake Griffin and Ben Simmons, whose draft years were lost to injury); the 2025-26 pool went from 156 to 103. Retrained ROY (DPOY retrained too, identical coefficients) and reran the backtest, SHAP and calibration: ROY favourite ranked 1st in 9 of 14 seasons (was 8), top 5 still 14 of 14, ROC-AUC 0.965 (was 0.967); calibrated held-out log loss 1.41 → 1.38, favourite averaged 59.8% and won 9 of 14. 2025-26 ROY: Knueppel 47.2%, Flagg 19.8%, Edgecombe 15.0%. Copy updated in Awards Race, Rookie Class Tracker and Methodology (open issue removed). Smoke test added (65/65).

**Just shipped (previously):** Calibrated award chances (2026-09-27), fifth item of the analyst list. The award models are logistic regressions trained with balanced class weights, which push raw probabilities toward 1: the dashboard showed SGA at 100.0% for MVP with Wembanyama at 99.99% and Dončić at 99.6%, and All-NBA's 15th pick at 92%. Measured on the backtest's held-out seasons, the raw probabilities added up to 6.5 (MVP), 17.0 (DPOY) and 5.0 (ROY) per season, when exactly one player wins, and 27.6 for All-NBA's 15 places. Plain normalised shares were tried first and rejected: they swing the other way (the MVP favourite averaged a 17.5% share but won 7 of 15). New `scripts/calibrate_award_chances.py`: for MVP/DPOY/ROY a one-parameter softmax over each season's candidate pool (adds up to 100%); for All-NBA Platt scaling (adds up to 15.0). Fitted on leave-one-season-out scores and evaluated in a nested loop, so no season is scored by a calibration that saw it. Held-out log loss improves on every award (MVP 1.99 → 1.67, DPOY 3.29 → 2.12, ROY 1.97 → 1.41, All-NBA 0.180 → 0.097), and reliability is close (MVP candidates given 50%+ averaged 68.5% and won 5 of 8; All-NBA players given 10-50% averaged 25% and made a team 28% of the time). Checked first that the served models use exactly the backtest's settings, and left out 2025-26 from the All-NBA fit (teams not loaded yet, which had dragged the sum to 14.1). Served as `mvp_chance`/`dpoy_chance`/`roy_chance`/`all_nba_chance` (raw fields kept in the API) and `GET /awards/calibration`; Awards Race, the Dashboard and Rookie Class Tracker now show chances (2025-26 MVP: SGA 60.1%, Wembanyama 24.0%, Dončić 5.2%). The Prediction Ledger keeps the raw probabilities it logged and says so; new snapshots also store the chance. Methodology shows the calibration table live; the saturation open issue is removed. Also fixed on the way: the ROY tooltip claimed 64% top-1 accuracy, which is the random forest's; the served logistic model's is 57% (8 of 14). DataTable no longer pads decimals (27.7, not 27.700). Found and not fixed yet: some non-rookies are in the ROY pool (see Known real gaps). Smoke test added (64/64).

**Just shipped (previously):** Leaderboard Builder, and a games floor on Raw Impact (2026-09-27), fourth item of the analyst list. Raw Impact had no minimum, so 2025-26 was topped by Colby Jones (1 game, 2.0 PPG); it now uses the BPM leaderboard's 30-game / 20-minute default (top: Wembanyama, Holmgren, Mitchell, Gilgeous-Alexander) and the Impact tab says so; removed from the Methodology page's open issues. The builder ranks player-seasons by any of 31 stats with filters (see What's in it). Checked in the data before building: which stats are per game, and each stat's first well-recorded season, which the endpoint enforces instead of ranking a half-empty pool. Found while testing: 2025-26 3P% with no attempts floor was topped by Mark Williams at 100% on 0.0 3PA a game, so shooting percentages have default attempts floors. Sniff tests against known records: Wilt's 50.4 in 1961-62 (then 44.8, 38.4; Baylor 38.3; Jordan 37.1 in 1986-87), Alvin Robertson's 3.7 steals in 1985-86, Curry's 30.1 on the 2015-16 Warriors, Jokić's 13.7 BPM in 2021-22. Layout bug caught in the browser: `.input-field`'s `flex: 1 1 180px` made every control 180 px tall inside a column; overridden for the builder. Contrast 0 issues in Paper and Ink, no overflow at 375 px. Smoke tests added (63/63).

**Just shipped (previously):** Methodology page (2026-09-27), third item of the analyst list: 19 model and index cards plus 3 open issues, reachable from the Analytics menu. Every written number was re-checked against the live endpoint or its build script before going on the page (MVP logistic backtest 7 of 15, win-probability ROC-AUC 0.830 on 1,447 held-out games, DAD year-over-year r 0.70 over 243 defenders, Spacing +0.074 ORtg per point with p = 0.016, Pipeline r 0.05, All-NBA 77.9% precision at 15 over 16 seasons). Writing it caught three claims that were wrong in my first draft and are corrected on the page: the win-probability model uses score margin and time only (no possession), the Scouting Report tests against the rest of the league (not the player's own baseline), and DPOY/All-NBA do apply a minutes floor to their candidate pools. It also found a stale README line saying DAD, Spacing and Contract Value use the in-house BPM reproduction (fixed in Known real gaps). Live blocks fail on their own without hiding the written content. Contrast scan 0 issues in Paper and Ink (after fixing a source badge whose colour was overridden inside the stats row); no overflow at phone width. eslint clean, build passes.

**Just shipped (previously):** Season Similarity upgrade (2026-09-27), second item of the analyst list. The tab already existed but showed only name, season and a score, from a stored top 10 per season. Checked in the data: a player's own other seasons filled much of that top 10 (4 of Curry 2015-16's 10 were Curry), and 779 stored matches were seasons of under 10 games. New `GET /similarity/season-profile/{player}/{season}` (similarity_api) recomputes the same similarity live, with `exclude_self`, `min_gp`, `one_per_player` and `top_n` (up to 25). The live computation was checked against the stored `season_similarity` table: identical scores (max difference 7e-16) and identical top-10 sets for all 7,279 seasons; unfiltered, it returns exactly the old endpoint's list (now a smoke test). Each match returns its eight inputs, the two playing-style stats it's closest on and the input it differs most on. The page shows the searched season as a pinned first row, every match's stat line, the reason, and the filters (defaults: other players only, 20+ games). Seasons before 2009-10 get a clear message instead of an empty table. Checked in the browser: Jokić 2023-24 (26.4 PTS, 65.0% TS, 79 GP, matching his real line) gives Giannis 2023-24 at 0.961; with one season per player the list is Giannis, LeBron 2016-17, Towns, Butler, Embiid, Sabonis, Harden, Griffin, Westbrook, Randle. Contrast scan 0 issues in Paper and Ink; no page overflow at 375 px. Export file names now keep accented names readable ("jokic", not "joki"). Smoke test added (61/61). The old `/similarity/season` endpoint is unchanged (Rookie Class Tracker uses it).

**Just shipped (previously):** CSV/JSON export on every data table (2026-09-27), first item of the owner's "analyst's best friend" list. A shared `<TableExport />` (`components/common/TableExport.jsx`, logic in `utils/tableExport.js`) sits above 51 tables in 34 components, plus every `DataTable` (Awards Race, Impact Rankings, Season Similarity). It exports the table as rendered, so the reader gets the season, filters and sort they chose, and needs no backend. Info-tooltip "i" buttons, icon ligatures and photos are left out; badges are spaced off (the Hall of Fame "75" badge exports as "(NBA 75th Anniversary Team)"); in JSON, plain numbers ("1,622", "−3.2") become numbers while percentages, records and ranges stay text and dashes become null; the CSV has a UTF-8 BOM so Excel shows Dončić correctly. Left out on purpose: Guess the Player (it would reveal answers), Live Scores, the player modal and the dashboard tile. Checked in the browser by exporting every table that loads by default (Hall of Fame, Stat Leaders, Players (428 rows), Draft Value, Standings, Impact, Model Validation, DAD Index (341), Spacing Lab, Garbage Time, Contract Value, College Pipeline, March Madness, Archetypes): every export has exactly the rows shown and clean column names. That check caught three bugs before shipping: header tooltips leaking "i" into column names ("Points i"), the 75 badge gluing onto names ("LeBron James75"), and file names picking up the source-badge text. Button contrast is 9.1:1 in Paper and Ink; eslint clean, build passes.

**Just shipped (previously):** Greats of the Game page (2026-09-27). The owner asked for a page with the stars' photos as the background; from a question round they chose a new page (Hall of Fame untouched), the 75th Anniversary Team plus today's stars, and a mix of a dithered photo wall and a scrolling marquee. A standalone mockup with real data and real NBA photos was approved first, then extended at the owner's request with data-derived facts and "Did you know?" trivia limited to confirmed claims. Checked before building: which of the 76 have real NBA photos (73; three get the CDN's identical silhouette), photo weight (260x190 ≈ 16 KB each), and every honours count against known records (Jordan 5 MVPs / 14 All-Star / 32,292 points, Kareem 6 MVPs / 38,387, Wilt 31,419 / 23,924; the 83 greats hold 69 of the 70 MVPs ever awarded — Derrick Rose's 2011 is the other). Each sourced trivia line was checked against its Wikipedia article; that caught Kobe's 81 no longer being the second-highest game and dropped a LeBron line the source didn't support. Bug found in the browser: the page's fade-in transform made the fixed-position detail panel and hover card position against the page (opened off screen); both now render through a React portal. Contrast scan: 0 issues in Paper and Ink, with and without the panel open; no horizontal overflow at phone width after the source badge was allowed to wrap. Smoke test added (60/60).

**Just shipped (previously):** BPM/VORP are now Basketball-Reference's published values (2026-09-27, owner's go-ahead).
