This project's own BPM 2.0 reproduction (`build_bpm_vorp.py`) correlated only 0.76 with the published 2024-25 BPM and put 2025-26 SGA at 22.0 / VORP 13.8 against the real 11.7 / 7.8. `scripts/load_bref_bpm_vorp.py` loads `Advanced.csv`'s BPM/OBPM/DBPM/VORP for every 2009-10+ season through the rebuilt `player_id_map` (pre-2010 rows already came from Basketball-Reference): 7,273 of 7,279 rows linked, games played agreeing within one game on every linked row (an independent check of the id map); the 6 unlinked rows are NULL rather than mixing scales. The reproduction is kept in `bpm_repro`/`obpm_repro`/`dbpm_repro`/`vorp_repro` because Pair Synergy's trained model was fitted on its DBPM (it now reads `dbpm_repro`; retraining needs stats.nba.com); `build_bpm_vorp.py` now writes only those columns plus `bpm_position`. Rebuilt and compared: DAD Index year-over-year stability rose (2024-25 0.65 → 0.75, 2025-26 0.53 → 0.70) with its DFG% link unchanged; Contract Value's WAR scale factor is now 0.77–0.80 in every season (it was ~0.53 for 2010+ and ~0.77 for the pre-2010 seasons that already used published VORP, i.e. the reproduction ran ~45% hot); the Spacing Lab effect roughly halved (+0.163 → +0.074 ORtg per spacing point, p = 0.016, R² gain 0.001), so better-measured lineup quality absorbed much of what looked like spacing — the page's verdict now follows the numbers. Copy updated everywhere the reproduction was described (glossary, Impact, DAD, Contract Value, Spacing Lab, API methodology text). Smoke test added (59/59).

**Just shipped (previously):** Player id map rebuilt; pre-2010 seasons reloaded (2026-09-27, owner's go-ahead). The first `player_id_map` matched Basketball-Reference to NBA ids by exact name only, so 233 players had no pre-2010 seasons at all (Gary Payton, Patrick Ewing, Larry Nance, Glen Rice, Tim Hardaway, Steve Smith, Tiny Archibald, the 1991 #1 Larry Johnson...) and four NBA ids held two different players' careers (the 1965-72 and 2005-08 Luke Jacksons under one id, and three pairs whose single NBA id can't be told apart). The matcher from the draft rebuild moved into `scripts/bref_nba_ids.py`, shared by `load_kaggle_historical_seasons.py` and `load_draft_history_bref.py`: names with Jr./II kept, space-insensitive names (Wang Zhi-zhi), first-name forms, five hand-checked nicknames (Lucious "Luke" Jackson, Nate "Tiny" Archibald, Lafayette "Fat" Lever, Johnny "Red" Kerr, Micheal Ray Richardson), 2010+ season overlap, and two NBA id-era rules verified with no exception on every unambiguous match (200000-599999 = entered 2006-07 or later; 76000-79999 = career over by 1995-96), then one person per id with elimination. Result: 4,938 of 5,105 players matched; pre-2010 rows 15,460 -> 16,129 (backup kept locally in `scratchpad_backups/`, gitignored); no NBA id now belongs to two players; checked that the 11 ids with long gaps between seasons are real careers (Anthony Parker in Europe, Cousy's 1970 comeback, Gene Conley's baseball years). Rebuilt downstream: `draft_history` (3,642 of 3,747 picks who played now matched, was 3,622), `player_salaries` and `contract_value` (2006-2009 seasons can match more players). Hall of Fame career leaders read the table live: Ewing is now 28th in career points (24,812; published 24,815, per-game rounding) and Payton 39th. That surfaced one more bug, fixed: `nba75_team` had Patrick Ewing's son on the 75th Anniversary Team (the loader's name lookup kept the last duplicate id); it now takes the long-career candidate, and all 76 members have season rows. Smoke test added (58/58).

**Just shipped (previously):** Player Roles, ten archetypes instead of six (2026-09-27). The owner asked for more archetypes. The old six came from per-game stats, which mostly measure minutes (1,750 player-seasons were one "Bench Role Player" blob), so more clusters on the same inputs wouldn't have meant more roles. `scripts/build_player_roles.py` clusters rate stats plus shot-location shares instead, and picks K by stability rather than by eye: 10 roles reproduce under resampling (ARI 0.83), 11+ don't. The six archetypes stay in `player_clusters` because Pair Synergy's trained model uses them and can't be retrained while stats.nba.com is unreachable; the Archetypes page endpoints (`/clusters/archetypes`, `/clusters/season/{s}`, `/clusters/player/{name}`, `/clusters/evolution`) now read `player_roles` and return the six-way archetype as `family`. Checked: Harden 2018-19 Lead Creator, DeAndre Jordan 2012-13 Rim-Running Big, Klay 2016-17 Above-the-Break Shooter, Gobert Rim-Running Big, Wembanyama Skilled Big. Page: ten-colour palette, each role's description and five different example players, a Family column, player-name links moved off a light blue that failed contrast on Paper, trend-chart labels no longer drawn in the line colour; the contrast scan now also checks SVG text (0 issues, both themes). Smoke test added (57/57).

**Just shipped (previously):** `draft_history` rebuilt and Draft Value Guide fixed (2026-09-27). The table had always been empty (stats.nba.com times out from this machine: full history, one year, and unrelated endpoints), so Draft Value had never shown data. `scripts/load_draft_history_bref.py` now builds it from Basketball-Reference's draft history (8,383 picks, 1947-2025) with NBA ids matched carefully (see Known real gaps); checking the matches caught the existing `player_id_map` giving one id to two people (Luke Jackson 1964/2004 and three more) and two Marcus Williamses landing on one id, both now prevented. Running Draft Value on real data for the first time also showed its metric was unusable: it summed `impact_score_raw`, a per-season z-score tied to team winning, so picks 6-14 scored below 15-30 and Zach LaVine was a top-5 "worst pick". It now uses Basketball-Reference Win Shares in the first five NBA seasons (new `draft_pick_outcomes`), draft classes 1980-2021: 23.6 / 14.4 / 8.4 / 3.9 / 1.9 WS for picks 1-5 / 6-14 / 15-30 / 31-45 / 46-60; best value Jordan, Duncan, LeBron, Barkley; worst Josh Jackson, Tskitishvili, Morrison, Washburn. Checked: Jordan 213.9 career WS (Basketball-Reference 214.0), 14 All-Star selections; Ewing 1985 #1 gets his own id, not his son's. The draft-class view shows first-five and career WS, seasons and All-Star selections. Smoke test added (56/56).

**Just shipped (previously):** March Madness Model (2026-09-26). The owner's Torvik files turned out to be end-of-season for 2013-2025 (tournament games included), so training on them would leak results; Bart Torvik's own site blocks automated requests (not worked around). Instead every D1 game 2013-2026 was pulled from CollegeBasketballData.com (existing free key, 84 calls): the model's inputs are built only from games before each tournament's first tip. Bugs found and fixed on the way: the API's end date is exclusive, so the first fetch silently dropped the last day of every month (2013 showed 65 of 67 tournament games; fixed and refetched); a hung connection (split connect/read timeouts); LIU's split name; the 2021 no-contest being scored as a game; seed-only "ranks" among tied seeds being simulation noise (now tie-aware, e.g. 2014 UConn as a 7 seed is "tied 25th–28th"); seed-only accuracy counting 50/50 calls as misses (now half credit). Model choice was fixed in advance (lowest leave-one-season-out log loss on 2013-2025) and 2026 was run once, after. Checked: all 13 champions agree with the independent Torvik file; each season's bracket is rebuilt from the real games and fails loudly if a team reaches a round without winning its previous game. Dark-mode contrast of the odds heat map was caught by the scripted scan (29 cells under 4.5:1) and fixed with a theme-aware shade. Smoke test added (55/55).

**Just shipped (previously):** College → NBA Pipeline (2026-09-26), from the owner's Kaggle "College Basketball Dataset" (Torvik team ratings, 2013-2026). Real checks before building: all 12 champions in the files match history; the combined `cbb.csv` equals the per-season files; the 2013-2025 files are end-of-season (2024-25 Florida shows its real 36-4, tournament included) while 2026 is pre-tournament. The NBA's own `draft_history` table turned out to be empty (stats.nba.com timed out), so picks and colleges come from Basketball-Reference's draft history, and the outcome is Basketball-Reference Win Shares (same player ids, no name matching on the NBA side; traded players use the combined `2TM` row). College names map to Torvik spellings by rule plus 13 aliases, season-aware because Torvik renamed N.C. State, Detroit Mercy and Louisiana in 2025. The last college season was checked against the CollegeBasketballData.com player table: 560 match the draft year, 3 are earlier (Petrušev in Serbia, Hernandez and Melton sitting out) and two were fixed by hand (Sharpe never played at Kentucky, excluded; P.J. Hairston's last season was 2012-13). Spot-checked: Towns (Kentucky 2015, Torvik No. 1, Final Four), Zion (Duke, Elite 8), Morant (Murray St., Round of 32), Brunson (Villanova champions). The result is a null finding, shown as one (see What's in it). While checking, Basketball-Reference's 2025-26 SGA line (BPM 11.7, VORP 7.8) confirmed this project's BPM reproduction is off (ours: 22.0 / 13.8). Smoke test added.

**Just shipped (previously):** App-wide redesign to match the landing page (2026-09-26). The owner picked, from three mockups of real pages with real data, "Paper" as the default theme, "Ink" as dark mode, and orange poster headers on flagship pages, plus player photos everywhere. How: `styles/tokens.css` now defines Paper (cream `#f1ece1`, ink text, orange `#ff5b14` fills) and Ink (near-black, cream text, orange offset shadows), Anton / Space Grotesk / Space Mono, square corners and a hard offset "sticker" shadow; the theme defaults to Paper (`utils/theme.js`), with System and Dark still in the toggle. A new `styles/theme.css`, loaded after `dashboard.css`, restyles the shared building blocks once instead of editing every page: the ink top nav, page headers, `.dashboard-card` (91 uses), `.data-table` (50), `.stat-card`, bento/analytics/games tiles, buttons, inputs, segmented controls, pills, tooltips and headshots. `PageHeader` adds a section eyebrow on every page and a full-bleed orange halftone band on Dashboard, Analytics, Shot Charts, Games and Hall of Fame (the dashboard's greeting moved into it). Player photos: a shared `PlayerName` cell (square framed headshot + name); `DataTable` uses it automatically for player columns, so Awards Race, Impact Rankings and Season Similarity got photos; also Rookie Class Tracker, Draft Value Guide, Shot Charts' selected player and Learn the Game's stat line. `/impact/raw`, `/impact/star`, `/impact/bpm` and `/learn/basics` now return `player_id`. Accessibility, checked rather than assumed: a scripted scan of every page and every Analytics tab measured each visible text's contrast against its real composited background in both themes, plus horizontal overflow. It found and fixed: brand orange used as text (2.9:1 on cream; text now uses `--brand-text`, 5+:1), lime `--accent-3` text (1.0:1), ~40 hard-coded greens/reds/ambers used for positive/negative text across 22 components (now `--positive`/`--negative`/`--streak`), white letters on pale W/L form badges and news tags (1.5-2.8:1, now ink), a dark tooltip popover whose body text would have been dark-on-dark, dark slate player-search dropdowns, a court map drawn in translucent white, and a 16 px horizontal overflow on Games (the particle canvas wasn't clipped). Final scan: 0 issues and 0 overflow on all pages in both themes. eslint clean, `vite build` passes, 53/53 smoke tests.

**Just shipped (previously):** Removed the last fabricated data from the app (2026-09-26). `services/mockData.js` held hard-coded games and team stats that three pages showed as if real: the Dashboard's "Today's Games" tile and Live Scores started from (and, on a fetch error, fell back to) fake games, which is why the dashboard showed "live" games like BOS 91-87 LAL in Q3 on an offseason day; Team Comparison started from invented team stats and kept them if `/meta/current` failed. All three now start empty with a loading state and say plainly when data couldn't load; with no games today the dashboard shows "No NBA games today" plus the most recent real finals (from the Game Replay list, currently 2026-04-12). The dashboard's games request is also no longer bundled in the same `Promise.all` as news/meta, so a slow scoreboard can't hold up the rest. `mockData.js` is deleted (nothing else used it). Verified in the browser: dashboard, Live Scores ("No games found for 2026-09-26") and Team Comparison (all 30 real teams) show only real data.

**Just shipped (previously):** Learn the Game page (2026-09-26), the owner's idea from mockup F (blueprint). New `GET /learn/basics` (impact_api, new router `learn.py`, cached in-process, ~0.8 s cold): latest-season zone splits via `classify_zone`, the classifier check from `zone_classifier_check`, pace from `game_team_box`, approximate points per team game from `player_season_stats`, and the top scorer's line. Numbers checked by hand before shipping (zone counts sum to all 218,443 shots; 41.5% of shots were threes; points per possession ≈1.14). New nav group "Learn" (also in the mobile menu and command palette automatically) and a "New to basketball? Learn the game" band on the landing page. If the endpoint fails, the page still renders the rules and says the live numbers didn't load. Verified in the browser at desktop and 375 px widths, zone hover/click switching checked. Found while verifying: the top nav overflowed horizontally between 768 and ~1000 px (171 px over at 768 px before this change, 232 px after adding "Learn"), pushing the theme toggle off-screen. Fixed by switching to the hamburger menu below 1024 px instead of 768 px (the mobile menu already carries every nav group and the theme toggle); no overflow at 375, 768, 1000 or 1024 px. Smoke test added (53/53 pass). eslint clean, build passes (Learn page is its own 4 KB gzip chunk).

**Just shipped (previously):** Landing page redesign (2026-09-26). The owner rejected layering onto the old landing and asked for a whole new design; eight throwaway mockups were built (every number in them pulled from the real DB) and the owner picked a combination: mockup B's loud-editorial art style (flat orange, Anton poster type, cream/acid stickers, hard ink borders, dithered glitch ball, score ticker), mockup C's mouse-reactive ribbon, and mockup A's scroll-driven flight through a 3D court of real shots. `LandingPage.jsx` and `styles/landing.css` were rewritten from scratch; `CursorGlow`, `LiveShotHero`, `WelcomeIntro`, `IntroErrorBoundary`, `FeatureChapters` and `TeamRibbons` were deleted (`ParticleField` and `CustomCursor` stay, GamesHub uses them; their CSS moved to `styles/effects.css`). New pieces: `DitherRibbon.jsx` (raw-WebGL fragment shader, 4x4 ordered dithering in the page palette, ripples around the cursor), `DitherBall.jsx` (canvas-2D dithered ball that turns toward the cursor and glitches on hover), `ShotCourtFlight.jsx` (three.js, lazy-loaded; camera keyframes driven by the section's scroll progress: overview, tilted top-down, low at the rim; made shots ink, missed cream). New endpoint `GET /shots/league-sample?n=6000` (impact_api): a fixed random sample (ordered by md5(id)) of real half-court shots from the latest season in `player_shots`, plus that season's own totals; cached in-process (cold ~3 s, then ~20 ms). Checked live: 6,000 of 218,443 real 2025-26 shots, sample FG 46.9% vs. season 47.1%; smoke test added. Every number on the page is fetched live, with neutral fallback copy if a fetch fails: site-stats stickers (77 seasons, 22,739 player-seasons, 105,726 college seasons), today's games or else the latest real finals (currently 2026-04-12), the MVP favourite, the WP model's held-out ROC-AUC (0.83 on 680,617 plays). Fixed on the way: the old landing labelled the MVP model "logistic regression" but showed the gradient-boosting row's backtest accuracy (40%); the new page selects the `logreg` row (the served `mvp_model.pkl` is a `LogisticRegression`) and says "top pick was the actual winner in 7 of 15". Verified in the browser: hero, ticker, all three court chapters, rules grid, games, CTA at 1280x800, 1440x900, 1600x700 (title sized by viewport height so it never clips) and 375 px mobile (no horizontal overflow; on portrait screens the court is lifted above the chapter card via a camera view offset); no console errors. Bugs found and fixed while verifying: the sticky court stage overran its section (negative bottom margin) and covered the rules grid; React StrictMode's double effect run killed the ribbon's WebGL context (cleanup no longer calls `loseContext`). eslint clean, `vite build` passes; the lazy three.js chunk is 532 KB raw / 133 KB gzip, over Vite's 500 KB warning guideline but kept off the first paint. The old "particles become the real data" copy is gone along with the old page. The Lenis setup from Phase 1 carries over unchanged.

**Just shipped (previously):** Landing rehaul, Phase 1 — smooth scroll (2026-09-26). Plan lives in the owner's vault (`Landing Rehaul/Plan.md`): layer onto the existing landing components, not a rewrite. Added `lenis` (1.3.26, ~5KB gzipped), created in a `useSmoothScroll()` hook inside `LandingPage.jsx` and destroyed on unmount, so only the pre-login landing page gets it and the app shell keeps native scroll; skipped entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion`. `anchors: true` makes the "See how it works" link glide instead of jump. ParticleField already reads `window.scrollY` every frame, and Lenis drives the real window scroll, so the particle grid stays in sync with no changes. Verified in the browser: one 300px wheel scroll is spread over 45 frames on an ease-out curve (was a single jump); the anchor link lands exactly on `#how-it-works` (top edge at 0px); leaving via Open Today removes the `lenis` class and the dashboard scrolls natively again; at 375px mobile width, no horizontal overflow and all 6 chapters render. Lint clean, production build passes. Found and fixed a real, pre-existing crash on the way: when the MVP prediction fetch failed (e.g. mvp_api down), `FeatureChapters` read `d.top5.length` on undefined and blanked the entire landing page. Now guarded; re-tested with mvp_api stopped: all 6 chapters render with their fallback copy, no errors. Phases 2 (one WebGL accent) and 3 (typography pass) are not started.

**Just shipped (previously):** F5 — Contract Value (see "What's in it" → Teams & Markets). Built only after checking feasibility with the owner: scraping Basketball-Reference (Cloudflare bot challenge, terms forbid it) and HoopsHype (robots.txt blocks AI agents) were both ruled out, so the owner downloaded two public Kaggle salary datasets instead. Those were checked against real contracts before use, which found inflation-adjusted seasons, incomplete early seasons, stale rows for retired players, a season missing major players, and name changes (Enes Kanter → Enes Freedom, Nene Hilario → Nene, mapped explicitly) — each handled and disclosed (see Known real gaps) rather than shipped. League minimums were sourced per season with citations, not recalled. The first run showed WAR from this project's VORP reproduction runs 1.3–2.0× hot against real wins, so WAR is rescaled per season to real wins above replacement (disclosed). The in-app browser pane was hidden during verification (it freezes the app's page transitions), so the new section and the Trade Analyzer panel were verified by rendering the real components against the live API in a temporary page (since deleted). 51/51 smoke tests passing (1 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** F3 v2 — shot-context splits in the Exploit Guide (see "What's in it" → Player Comparison). `fetch_shot_context.py` pulls closest-defender, touch-time and dribble splits league-wide (12 calls per season, 13 seasons; each dimension's bands sum to 98–100% of real league FGA, checked live) instead of the plan's ~450 per-player calls a season. Two changes made from looking at real output rather than shipping the first run: 2PT context bands were dropped (they restated shot location — rim-heavy scorers looked "strong" in every 2PT band), and the hard-coded "leverage isn't a key" exclusion became a general, data-decided reliability rule (Wilson 95% lower bound on next-season persistence > 50%) that every category — including the new ones, 77–79% lower bounds — has to pass. Top-3 selection now takes the strongest finding per category first. No frontend change needed (the card renders the API's finding text). 50/50 smoke tests passing.

**Just shipped (previously):** F4 — Gravity Index & Spacing Lab (see "What's in it" → Teams & Markets). New real data via `fetch_spacing_data.py`: 13 seasons × 6 league-wide calls (catch-and-shoot, 4 closest-defender bands, 5-man lineups) instead of the plan's ~450 per-player calls a season; the plan's assumed `lineup_stats` table didn't exist, so it's built here. No statsmodels on this machine, so the clustered-SE regression is a small numpy implementation (`scripts/stats_lib.py`) checked by simulation before use. Two real problems caught by checking results rather than shipping them: (1) the plan's shrink-to-league-mean rated non-shooters as average on two of three components, so the prior was changed to the volume-tier rate; (2) the lineup builder would happily extrapolate the regression to a lineup of the five highest-Gravity players (+26 spacing vs. a real max of ~+15), so predictions are now withheld outside the real range. 50/50 smoke tests passing (2 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** F3 — Scouting Report ("Exploit Guide") v1 (see "What's in it" → Player Comparison). No new fetch. Found and fixed a real, pre-existing bug on the way: `api/shots_lib.classify_zone()` — which assigns the zone of every one of the 6.3M bulk-loaded shots (none carry the NBA's own zone), and so drives every shot chart's zone stats — measured the paint as 19 ft from the *hoop* instead of from the *baseline* and used a rounded distance for the restricted area. Checked against the NBA's own real league zone totals it over-counted restricted-area shots by ~7% and under-counted mid-range by ~23% (2023-24: 18,879 mid-range vs. the NBA's 24,589). Fixed with the real court geometry (4 ft restricted-area radius; lane ends 13.75 ft in front of the hoop) — not tuned to fit — after which every zone's real FGA is within 1.3% and FG% within 0.27 pts of the NBA's own totals, stored in `zone_classifier_check`. Two other real issues handled rather than shipped: `player_shots` mixes playoff and play-in shots into each season (filtered to regular season here), and the first run's "Misc" play type (Synergy's catch-all) came up as a "strength" for nearly every star, so it isn't tested. Leverage findings were excluded from the listed keys because the pipeline's own persistence check showed them to be noise. 48/48 smoke tests passing (2 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** Fixed the app-shell live-endpoint stall flagged in Known real gaps (measured 2026-09-25, offseason: `/meta/current` ~136 s, `/games/by-date` and `/leaders/pts` ~45 s each, queuing every other request behind them for up to ~80 s in the browser — see `api/impact_core.py`, `api/routers/meta.py`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`). Two real causes, both fixed without touching what data is shown: (1) every live `stats.nba.com`/CDN call in `fetch_nba_api_standings`, `fetch_nba_cdn_standings`, `fetch_nba_api_team_stats`, `fetch_nba_api_player_leaders`, `fetch_nba_games_by_date` and `fetch_nba_cdn_games_by_date` now has a 6-second request timeout (was 30-45 s) and is cached for 5 minutes per season/date/stat key, so a slow or offseason-flaky call costs seconds once per TTL window instead of tens of seconds on every request; `/meta/current`'s four independent live lookups also now run concurrently (`ThreadPoolExecutor`) instead of one after another. (2) the frontend's shared `getWithCache` helper (`services/api.js`) had no in-flight de-dup, so several components mounting together (landing page alone has 4) each saw a cache miss before the first request landed and fired their own duplicate call; it now shares one in-flight promise per cache key. Nothing here changes what's real or invents a fallback value — every live call still falls back to the real local-DB data (or, for `/games/by-date`, the real CDN scoreboard) on failure or timeout, exactly as before. Verified: `curl` timings before/after — `/meta/current` 136 s → 4.1 s cold / ~1.4 s warm, `/games/by-date` 45 s → 0.56 s, `/leaders/pts` 45 s → 0.56 s; `/defense/dad` measured at 64-84 ms even while 6 concurrent `/meta/current` requests were in flight (was ~80 s queued); confirmed live data (not empty/fabricated) in every response; `/meta/current` fired exactly once across the landing page's 4 callers in the browser pane's network log (was several); 46/46 `pytest api/tests` passing.

**Just shipped (previously):** De-duplicated play-by-play for Clutch WPA, the WPA model, Game Replay and Guess the Game. `pbp_games` stored every one of the 420 nba_api games (2024-25) a second time under an ESPN game_id — 418 match on date + home + away; the other 2 are neutral-site games (2024-12-14 NBA Cup semifinal MIL–ATL, 2025-01-25 IND–SAS in Paris) whose nba_api copy has `home_team` NULL, confirmed as the same games by identical final scores. One shared filter, `scripts/wpa_lib.py`'s `PBP_DEDUP_WHERE`, keeps the ESPN copy (and would still keep any nba_api game ESPN lacks); it's now applied in `train_wpa_model.py`, `compute_wpa.py`, `/players/clutch-wpa`'s game counts, `/games/wp-replay/list` (2024-25 list: 1,651 → 1,231 games, no repeated date+teams) and the Guess the Game pool (7,652 → 7,232 — so the daily mystery game changed once; direct replay links to old nba_api game_ids still work). **Before/after, honestly:** the old split had 128 twin pairs straddling train/test and 25 pairs double-counted inside test. Held-out ROC-AUC 0.8351 → 0.8302 (clutch-only 0.8594 → 0.8496), Brier 0.1625 → 0.1643 (clutch 0.1532 → 0.1576), on 5,785/1,447 train/test games (was 6,121/1,531). That drop is **mostly split noise, not removed leakage**: across 10 random game splits (plain LR, same features) mean ROC-AUC was 0.8325 ± 0.0060 with the duplicates and 0.8328 ± 0.0058 without; clutch-only 0.854 ± 0.007 vs. 0.851 ± 0.008. A 3-feature logistic model can't memorize a game, so leakage barely helped it — seed 42 simply lands on a harder test set now. Clutch WPA leaderboard order is essentially unchanged (DeRozan +79.85 → +75.50 over 469 → 444 games, Jokić +72.86 → +66.69, Fox, Curry; then SGA moves ahead of Trae Young into 5th and Durant ahead of Brunson into 7th; Lillard/Booker/Mitchell reshuffle at 12-14 and Maxey falls out of the top 15, Embiid in), with every 2024-25 regular's games and clutch plays no longer double-counted. `build_leverage_splits.py` (already ESPN-only) was re-run so the Garbage-Time Deflator's Leverage Index uses the retrained model — after merging the 2025-26 refresh, since that refresh had rebuilt it with the old model: bucket shares moved by ≤ 0.2 pts, r/MAE validation unchanged (2025-26 MAE stays 0.028). 46/46 smoke tests passing after merging F2 (an earlier run had 4 live stats.nba.com fetches time out — a network blip, since passing — none of which touch the changed code). The refreshed `player_wpa_totals`, `wpa_model_validation` and the 4 Garbage-Time Deflator tables were copied to the Supabase mirror (row counts verified; cloud DB 216MB).

**Just shipped (previously):** Refreshed 2025-26 (`season = 2026`) in `player_season_stats` with the real final regular season. The stored line had been an earlier in-season snapshot (found by F1's validation). Re-fetched via `fetch_2025_26_season_data.py` (the same `LeagueDashPlayerStats` Base + Advanced PerGame calls, now 582 players, was 579) and reloaded with `load_2025_26_into_db.py`, then re-derived every added column in dependency order: `add_personal_fouls.py` (pf) → `add_shooting_efficiency_stats.py` → `build_bpm_vorp.py` (bpm/obpm/dbpm/vorp/bpm_position) → `compute_impact_score.py` → `upgrade_impact_scores.py`. A before/after diff of the whole table confirmed all 22,157 non-2026 rows are byte-identical. Downstream precomputes re-run: `build_leverage_splits.py`, `cluster_players.py`, `precompute_league_similarity.py`, `precompute_career_similarity.py`. Re-running those last two showed that neither had a season floor, so they'd started pulling in the pre-2010 Kaggle rows (archetypes jumped to 1980–2026). Both now use an explicit `MIN_SEASON = 2010`, which keeps the pools they always had. The archetype refit moved 68 of 5,070 older player-seasons (1.3%) to a different archetype, a normal side effect of refitting a global K-Means. Career similarity grew from 1,404 to 1,560 players because it had never been re-run after the 2024-25/2025-26 rookies were loaded. Award models, SHAP, and backtests were left alone because none of them train on 2026 (award labels only go through 2025). Existing prediction-ledger snapshots were also left alone, since each records what the model said at the time. Verified: Jeremiah Fears is now 82 GP / 14.3 PPG and Branden Carlson 42 / 5.8, matching stats.nba.com. The Garbage-Time Deflator's 2025-26 rebuilt-vs-official MAE fell from 0.127 to 0.028 PPG (r = 0.99998), in line with every other season, so the UI's 2025-26 caveat note was removed. F2 (DAD Index) landed in parallel on the old OBPM, so `build_dad_index.py` was re-run after the refresh. Only 2025-26 DAD values changed (2026 year-over-year r moved from 0.520 to 0.526), and DAD's own 2025-26 caveat was removed from both the API note and Known real gaps. The Supabase cloud mirror was then re-synced with `migrate_to_supabase.py`.

**Just shipped (previously):** F2 — DAD Index (see "What's in it" → Player Analysis). One new real fetch (`LeagueDashPtDefend`, one call per season, 9 seasons), everything else from existing tables. Found and handled while building: the endpoint returns one row per stint for mid-season-traded players (verified live: Corey Brewer 2017-18, 52 + 18 games) — stints are combined exactly (summed makes/attempts, normal FG% weighted by real attempts) rather than keeping one or averaging; and the plain DAD z-score is position-biased (centers +0.95 on average), so a within-position z-score and toggle were added rather than presenting the raw ranking as position-neutral. Also measured, while verifying in the browser, that the app shell's slow live endpoints can queue other requests for over a minute (see Known real gaps — pre-existing, flagged separately). 46/46 smoke tests passing (1 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** F1 — Garbage-Time Deflator (see "What's in it" → Player Analysis for the full description). Built entirely from existing data (ESPN play-by-play + the deployed WPA model); no new fetch. Verified: the rebuilt per-game scoring matches official lines to r = 1.000 / MAE ≈ 0.03 PPG per season, which caught three real data issues on the way — ESPN's NBA Cup finals (excluded), 418 duplicate nba_api/ESPN games (excluded here; since also removed from Clutch WPA and the WPA model — see the latest entry), and a stale 2025-26 `player_season_stats` snapshot (since refreshed — see the entry above). An early bin-averaged Leverage Index gave nonsense values in sparse states (e.g. 2.2× leverage for a 10-point lead late in OT), so LI is instead computed analytically per state from the model plus the real scoring-outcome mix — smooth everywhere, exactly 1.0 on average. Sniff test: deep-bench players (Bronny James 68% garbage-time share, 100th pctile) top "empty calories"; the rebuilt raw line reproduces known seasons exactly (Stephen Curry 2020-21: 32.0 PPG over 63 games); among 2025-26's top 30 scorers, Curry keeps the highest True Production Ratio (0.932; 0.933 after the de-duplicated WPA model retrain). 45/45 smoke tests passing (2 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** Model diversity — advisor feedback (2026-09-25, "it's all Logistic Regression and Random Forest, use different ones"). Both real, evaluated additions, both disclosed honestly rather than cherry-picked to look better:
- **Award models**: `sklearn.ensemble.GradientBoostingClassifier` added as a third model type in `scripts/backtest_models.py`'s `MODEL_CONFIGS` (zero new dependency — neither xgboost nor lightgbm was already installed). Reused the exact real LOSO backtest pipeline unchanged; the only real code change needed was applying balanced class weighting manually via `sample_weight` (`GradientBoostingClassifier` has no `class_weight` param, unlike the other two). `/backtest/{award}/compare` and `ModelValidationSection.jsx` both already generalized over model count — no API or frontend changes needed at all, confirmed live. Real result: Gradient Boosting does **not** clearly beat the other two here — Logistic Regression has the highest real ROC-AUC on MVP (0.996), DPOY (0.939), and ROY (0.967) backtests; Gradient Boosting came in lowest of the three on all three (0.928/0.768/0.925). Found and fixed one real pre-existing bug while rerunning the backtest, unrelated to this change but blocking it: a NumPy 2.0 compatibility issue (`np.float64.__repr__` changed to `"np.float64(0.99...)"`) broke writing `roc_auc` to Postgres entirely — fixed with an explicit `float()` cast.
- **Win-probability (WPA) model**: `sklearn.ensemble.HistGradientBoostingClassifier` (the histogram-binned variant, not plain `GradientBoostingClassifier` — this table has 3.6M+ real events, where the histogram implementation is the one actually built to train at that scale) trained and evaluated on the exact same held-out-by-game split as the deployed calibrated Logistic Regression (`scripts/train_wpa_model.py`), stored under a new `wpa_model_validation.model_type` column and served via a new `GET /validation/wpa/compare` endpoint, rendered as a comparison table on the Clutch WPA tab. **Not deployed** — real result: it performs essentially identically to the deployed model (ROC-AUC 0.8350 vs. 0.8351 all-events, 0.8594 vs. 0.8594 clutch-only; after the de-duplication retrain, 0.8301 vs. 0.8302 all-events and 0.8502 vs. 0.8496 clutch-only — Gradient Boosting now edges ahead on clutch-only Brier 0.1568 vs. 0.1576 and log loss 0.473 vs. 0.488, a gap that was already there before, 0.462 vs. 0.471) because the hand-engineered `margin / √time-remaining` interaction feature already captures the nonlinearity a tree model would otherwise need to learn on its own, so there's little real room left for a more complex model to help — and the deployed Logistic Regression stays deployed on a second, separate real ground: `win_prob()` is called one event at a time in tight loops when replaying a full game, where its closed-form sigmoid is meaningfully faster than scoring a boosted-tree ensemble per event. The existing `/validation/wpa` endpoint's contract is unchanged (explicitly filtered to the deployed model), so nothing that already depended on it broke.

43/43 smoke tests passing (2 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** A6 — Split `impact_api.py` into routers. The single 5,569-line file is now a ~85-line app entrypoint (`impact_api.py`: builds the FastAPI app, wires CORS, mounts every router) plus `impact_core.py` (the shared DB pool, loaded models, and every helper function/constant more than one route needs) plus 35 files under `api/routers/`, one per feature, each holding just its own `@router.get` route(s) moved verbatim out of the original file. Built with a small one-off AST-based script (not committed) that sliced the original file by exact top-level statement boundaries so route bodies transferred byte-for-byte, rather than hand-copying 5,500 lines. Verified two ways beyond the smoke suite: every one of the real 59 endpoints was hit live post-split (0 real 5xx responses — the only non-200s were pre-existing, real data gaps like an unpopulated `draft_history` table, not regressions) and `/openapi.json` was diffed to confirm all 59 real paths are still registered, byte-identical to before. One real internal-only break found and fixed: a smoke test reached into `impact_api._attach_rest_tags` directly (a private helper, now in `impact_core` since it's shared, not a route) — updated the test's import, no route or contract changed. 33/33 smoke tests passing.

**Just shipped (previously):** C6 — Referee Tendencies (owner sign-off given for this session). Real officials per game (`BoxScoreSummaryV2`, no bulk endpoint — one real call per real game) matched to real fouls/FTA/pace from `LeagueGameFinder`, season-adjusted against the real league average, real 95% CI on the difference, small-n warning below 25 real games worked (`scripts/fetch_referee_officials.py` → `scripts/build_referee_tendencies.py` → `GET /referees/tendencies`). The one-call-per-game officials fetch is real but slow, so it's built resume-if-interrupted/skip-already-fetched by design — it doesn't need to finish in one run, real coverage just grows each time it's re-run, and the UI always discloses the real current `n_games`/season span rather than implying full coverage. A known real gap, verified directly against the live endpoint: nba_api's own `BoxScoreSummaryV2` is missing officials for some real games on/after 2025-04-10, logged as attempted (not retried) and simply contributing no officials rows. Wording throughout (methodology text, `InfoTooltip`, this entry) is deliberately descriptive, not diagnostic — a real comparison of totals against a real baseline, not a claim about cause, intent, or bias. 33/33 smoke tests passing (2 new).

**Just shipped (previously):** A2 — Source badges. Every main Analytics endpoint (~20 across all three services — Awards Race, Impact Rankings, Model Validation, Prediction Ledger, Season Similarity, Player Archetypes + Offensive Style, Radar Compare, Trend Analysis, Career Trajectory, Heliocentricity, Clutch WPA, Matchup Finder, Vegas Scanner, Playoff Forecaster, Lineup Chemistry, Game Replay, With/Without a Star, Schedule Fatigue, Draft Prospects) now returns a `_source` object (`api/source_badge.py`) disclosing which real Postgres table(s) backed the response and which real upstream API/dataset those tables came from — live-fetch endpoints (Heliocentricity, Vegas Scanner, Lineup Chemistry, Playoff Forecaster, With/Without a Star) are flagged `live: true` rather than implying a static precomputed table. Rendered as a small `SourceBadge` chip (`components/common/SourceBadge.jsx`) next to each section's existing `InfoTooltip`, with the real table list and live/precomputed status in its native hover tooltip. Purely additive (a new JSON key, a component that renders nothing without it) — no existing endpoint contract changed, all 28 pre-existing smoke tests kept passing untouched, plus 3 new ones spot-checking `_source`'s shape.

**Just shipped (previously):** C8 — Full-season play-by-play via sportsdataverse (ESPN), retrained WPA (real 7,652 stored games — 7,232 unique once the ESPN/nba_api twins are de-duplicated — up from ~420 — see "What's in it" above and the `pbp_games`/`pbp_events` schema row). `scripts/fetch_pbp_espn.py` pulls a real FULL season in one ~30s call via `sportsdataverse.nba.load_nba_pbp` (vs. nba_api's one-call-per-game `PlayByPlayV3`), real ESPN team abbreviations mapped to nba_api tricodes (6 real mismatches: GS/NO/NY/SA/UTAH/WSH → GSW/NOP/NYK/SAS/UTA/WAS), real player names matched to existing `person_id`s by accent-normalized exact match then rapidfuzz fallback (98.6% real match rate). `pbp_games.source` ('nba_api' or 'espn') keeps the two real sources disclosed and never silently mixed, though both were verified to share the identical seconds-remaining/score-margin convention first. Retrained model: real held-out ROC-AUC 0.835 (0.859 clutch-time-only) on 1,531 real held-out games (since superseded by the de-duplicated retrain: 0.830 / 0.850 on 1,447 — see the latest "Just shipped" entry). Found and fixed three real bugs surfaced by the bigger dataset (unbatched `compute_wpa.py` still running after an hour, fixed to ~13s; Game Replay's missed-shot/3PT detection only understanding nba_api's own text vocabulary; ESPN mislabeling the 2024 All-Star Game as "regular season") — see git history for the full writeup.

**Operational note on the Prediction Ledger:** `snapshot_predictions.py` only logs a real point-in-time snapshot when it's actually run — nothing runs it automatically. Run it daily or weekly (manually, or via a cron/launchd entry you set up yourself; see the script's own docstring for an example cron line — nothing is auto-installed) so real trajectory history builds up over the season, and run `resolve_predictions.py` periodically to grade any season that's actually finished.

Throughout: update this README's "What's in it" and "Known real gaps" sections as each item ships, add a smoke test alongside each new endpoint rather than batching test-writing to the end, and keep screenshots reasonably current (not strictly required for every feature, but nice to have for the ones with real visual payoff).

## Advisor demo — status check (5-min read)

*Last checked: 2026-09-25.*

**End-of-session update (same day, later):** everything below this point was true when the demo happened, but more real work landed afterward — Hall of Fame (+ NBA 75 badges), Team Comparison's advanced stats/head-to-head/recent form/rosters, News's team filter/categories/search, Gradient Boosting added to both the award-model and WPA-model comparisons (advisor feedback, both evaluated and disclosed honestly — see the Roadmap section's "Just shipped" entries for the full real results), and the Supabase cloud DB now actually migrated and verified end-to-end (203MB, 944,726 real rows, `DB_TARGET=cloud`). All committed and pushed, 43/43 smoke tests passing, all services healthy as of the last check. This capsule is kept as-is below (a snapshot from the actual demo day) rather than rewritten — the Roadmap section and "What's in it" above are the sections kept continuously current.

**Launch (3 backends + frontend), from repo root:**
```bash
cd api
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m uvicorn mvp_api:app        --port 8000 --reload
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m uvicorn similarity_api:app --port 8001 --reload
/Library/Frameworks/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/bin/python3 -m uvicorn impact_api:app     --port 8002 --reload
cd ../frontend && npm run dev
```

**Checked just now — all green:**
- All 3 backends + frontend healthy (200 on every root endpoint).
- Full smoke suite: **34/34 passing**.
- `git status`: clean, pushed to `origin/main` (up to date through commit `5d44a25`).
- No console errors on the pages exercised below.

**Since the last demo, what changed:**
- Historical depth: `player_season_stats` now spans **1950–2026** (was 2010–2026), via a verified Kaggle import — Trend Analysis, career pages, etc. now show real full-career/full-franchise history instead of stopping at 2010.
- Shot charts: **6.3M real shots bulk-loaded** for 2,842 players (1996-97 through 2025-26). This was the fix for the recurring "impact_api freezes on a shot-chart search" bug from earlier — most real player searches now serve instantly from Postgres instead of a live, flaky stats.nba.com scrape.
- Fixed today: a player-search dropdown that was visually clipping/overlapping content on Player Comparison, Career Trajectory Forecaster, and Playoff Forecaster; missing hover tooltips on the Career Trajectory band/projection line, Game Replay's win-probability line, and the Schedule Fatigue chart; two garbled rotated axis labels; and a real regression the historical import introduced (League Evolution's trend charts had silently drifted out of sync with its archetype chart — caught by the smoke suite, fixed, verified).

**Safe to demo confidently:** MVP/DPOY/ROY prediction + backtesting, Season Similarity, Player Archetypes + League Evolution, Trend Analysis (player and team), Career Trajectory Forecaster, Game Replay (win-probability, now fully hoverable), Playoff Drop-off Forecaster, shot charts for any well-known player (LeBron, Jokić, Giannis, etc. — all bulk-cached).

**One thing to avoid, same as last time:** a shot-chart search for an obscure player who isn't among the 2,842 bulk-loaded (rare now, but possible for a very marginal role player) can still fall back to a live stats.nba.com fetch and be slow. If in doubt, search a recognizable name first.

**Supabase cloud DB — verified working (2026-09-25):** the earlier university-wifi block on outbound Postgres ports was network-specific, not a real code problem — confirmed once off that network. All 40 real tables except `player_shots` and `pbp_events` (959MB + 911MB — the two that would have blown past Supabase's free-tier 500MB cap) are migrated: `scripts/migrate_to_supabase.py`, 944,726 real rows, every table's row count verified to match the local source exactly. Cloud DB sits at 203MB (well under the 500MB cap, room to spare). No `pg_dump`/`psql` exists on this machine, so the script reconstructs each table's schema generically from `information_schema` and streams rows over with `COPY` — a real, disclosed simplification: primary keys are recreated but other indexes and `SERIAL`/sequence defaults aren't, since this is for real read access from a cloud/remote session, not full local/cloud parity. `DB_TARGET=cloud` verified end-to-end through the app's own `db_config.py` (not just a raw psycopg2 test) — real query, real row count, matches local. Local stays the default (`DB_TARGET=local` unless explicitly overridden) and the untouched full source of truth; shot charts and Game Replay/play-by-play simply aren't available when pointed at the cloud DB, since those two tables weren't migrated. Re-run the migration script any time local data changes meaningfully — it's a full drop-and-reload per table, not incremental.

**Layerbase cloud DB — full-parity mirror, verified working (2026-09-26):** Supabase's free tier (500MB) can't fit the whole 2.15GB local DB, so `player_shots`/`pbp_events` were always excluded there. Layerbase's free tier (real Postgres, 5GB, owner created it and added `LAYERBASE_DB_HOST/PORT/USER/PASSWORD/NAME` to `api/.env` themselves) fits the entire DB, so `scripts/migrate_to_layerbase.py` migrates **all 62 tables, no exclusions**. Differences from `migrate_to_supabase.py`: the two big tables (`player_shots` 6,318,078 rows, `pbp_events` 3,609,582 rows) are streamed in 200k-row chunks instead of buffered whole in memory, and every non-PK index is recreated too (from `pg_indexes`, not just the primary key). Every one of the 62 tables' row counts was verified to match local exactly (0 mismatches) — cloud DB sits at ~1,968MB of the 5GB cap. `DB_TARGET=layerbase` verified end-to-end through the real app: `/shots/player/{name}/zones` (impact_api) returned real, sane shot-zone data for LeBron James 2023-24, and `/games/wp-replay/list` (also impact_api, reads `pbp_events`) returned real games — both features that `DB_TARGET=cloud` cannot serve. Same caveats as the Supabase script: no `pg_dump`/`psql` on this machine so schema is reconstructed generically from `information_schema`, and `SERIAL`/sequence defaults aren't recreated (copied rows already have real IDs). Two catches specific to Layerbase's free tier, both fine for a rebuildable mirror: it scales to zero after 15 min idle (first request after that is 1–5s slower) and archives after 14 idle days (needs a manual restore from the dashboard). Local stays the default and the untouched source of truth. Re-run `migrate_to_layerbase.py` any time local data changes meaningfully — same full drop-and-reload-per-table approach, not incremental. The older Supabase mirror (`DB_TARGET=cloud`) is left in place for now rather than retired.
